# Phase 8 - Historical Handoffs

Each row indexes one completed Phase 8 milestone to the commit whose body carries its evidence. Return to the [active Phase 8 definitions](../../Roadmap/PHASE_8.md).

## Milestone 8.30d Light the chosen action's printed icons and integrate the replacement, End Turn, and narration surfaces completion evidence

| ID | Outcome | Commit |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 8.30d | While the player is choosing, the choice itself is lit. Every offered printed action's own icon shines through a per-card overlay tracing that badge's silhouette - steady, brighter under pointer or focus, picking-ignored so hit surfaces, focus order and the UiPlayer drivers are untouched; a Defense offer shines exactly the shield it would defend with at the presenter-typed placement (rail shield for a secondary Defense, primary badge for a Duelist or Block, DECLINE lighting nothing); Fast Travel and Clear hang as card-gold swallowtail ribbons off the Movement and Attack they respectively replace, keeping the retired chips' names, words, anchors and availability semantics; End Turn is a neutral pewter banner - the user's colour, against a board already full of gold - with a modest steady glow and a full shine when ending the turn is the only offer left; and the narration speaks the player's language in two voices, a warm accent-barred lane when the sentence is addressed to the viewing seat and a calm muted one otherwise, with every status-derivation family branch preserved. The overlays are derived per card by `Tools/Prepare-ShineSprites.ps1` beside the ignored private art - donor glyph templates seeded once per action kind, stamped at each card's own panel top, the Attack and Movement value digits re-traced per card, 284 slots matching the typed inventory exactly with the six passive Ultimates declaring and receiving nothing - and `ConfiguredCardShine` resolves them through the artwork catalog's own mapping with the same bounded loader seam and a one-time-diagnostic degradation to the prior treatment, which remains the whole clean-checkout path. Generated fallback faces state the same offers by lighting their typed chips, recolour only. Complete Edit Mode 2068/2068 and complete Play Mode 248/248 at the final revision; the harness stood blocked by `0x800711C7` with nothing it compiles touched. Design approved by the user mock by mock, including the corrections that shaped it: silhouette shine rather than rings, Clear anchored to the Attack it replaces, glyph templates reused across cards, and pewter rather than gold. | `0621b51f` |

## Milestone 8.30b Seat-scope and complete the player HUD prompt flow completion evidence

| ID | Outcome | Commit |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 8.30b | The proven runtime HUD is driven by one selected seat's authorized projection and typed invocations alone. One `IMatchReadModel` serves the Director's projection and a seat's, `SeatProjection` scopes every pending decision, companion envelope and offer to the seat that may act on it, and one `IMatchClient` submits for either; a runtime composed for a seat builds no Undo or diagnostic presenter at all, and `MatchSceneCompositionRoot.NextComposedSeat` is the development-only one-shot selector that can name a seat and change nothing about one. The user-approved amendment moved the contextual narration into the top status bar, widened from 56 to 96 reference units as a 56-unit identity row plus a 40-unit narration lane, and gave End Turn the panel's bottom-right corner as a 300-by-96 control of its own. Every pinned rectangle was re-measured rather than assumed: the Tie Breaker chip, the tray, the compact hand, the Level Up band and the round dock did not move at all, while the rail column went 888 to 848 against the 733 three rails need and the board went 1092x910 to 1044x870. The amendment also found that two of the ten declared pending families were named by no status branch, so a Minion Battle and a paused system transition reached the player under the round's own phase line. | `61ad8def` |

## Milestone 8.30a Add locally testable owner-scoped projections and command authorization completion evidence

| ID | Outcome | Commit |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 8.30a | Six seats are derived from the authoritative participant graph, and each receives its own read model: its own cards, every hero's countable hand and deck sizes, the public board, and the standing decision's answers only when it owns them. Another seat's hand and deck are absent rather than filtered, a facedown commitment has no identity until the reveal, and no Director history, snapshot, diagnostic log or control appears on the player surface at all. Every player submission arrives as a payload plus the trusted seat it came in on, a unique identity and the version it was decided against; the host resolves player, hero and team through `MatchParticipants` and refuses an unknown seat, a stale or future version, a replayed identity, a payload claiming another seat's hero or side, and any command the seat's own projection did not publish - each before the authority is touched. All ten pending families are covered, seven of them observed in a seeded official six-player match answered seat by seat. A development-only harness selects any seat; snapshot restore and session replacement reseat from the restored authority and rebuild every seat identically. | `2d8d76cd` |

## Milestone 8.30 Move match authority behind a transport-neutral session boundary completion evidence

| ID | Outcome | Commit |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 8.30 | Match authority now stands behind one Unity-free serialized host port: every typed command, Undo, restart and snapshot restore is a submission executed one at a time, each accepted one appends a record carrying its sequence, published version and ordered rule events to a bounded stream two independent consumers read from their own cursors, and a consumer that falls outside the window is told to resynchronize instead of being handed a gap. The configured scene composes the host rather than the authority, so the director's controls, presenters and headless driver submit through the door a network host will use; the port names no Unity, UGS or transport type, and the payload carries no caller identity. Nothing about rules, content, schema v34 or the wire changed. | `8496ebbc` |

## Milestone 8.28b Establish a seeded, replayable randomness seam completion evidence

| ID | Outcome | Commit |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 8.28b | `F-ER2-04` is closed on its named check, observed failing before the fix and passing after. **The finding was never that replay produced the wrong side.** It produced the right one, because the outcome was carried into setup state, the scenario, the round and the payload. The finding was that normal setup could only be *remembered*, never *performed again*: the draw happened inside `TieBreakerSetupAuthority.CreateNormalSetupState`, was consumed on the spot, and was written down nowhere - so a future online authority, which must hand a client something it can act on rather than an answer it must trust, had nothing to hand it. **The seam.** New `SetupRandomnessSeed` (a recorded `ulong`, with `Unrecorded` as its named absence in the house idiom of `TieBreakerState.Unrevealed` and `RuleModificationSet.Empty`) and `SetupRandomnessAuthority`, whose `DrawSeed` is now the only unseeded draw in the engine and whose `Derive(seed, purpose)` answers by named purpose so the second consumer to join gets an independent stream instead of silently correlating with the first. The derivation is SplitMix64's finalizer written out in `unchecked` arithmetic rather than taken from a framework type, for the reason milestone 7.9's match loop hand-wrote its generator: `System.Random` differs between Unity's Mono and .NET and `HashCode.Combine` is randomized per process, so either would make a recorded seed mean different things in different places - which is the one property this type exists to remove. `CreateNormalSetupState` now takes the seed and derives; the parameterless unseeded overload is **deleted** rather than deprecated, which is what makes the conformance rule true instead of aspirational. **The two observations.** `SetupRandomnessConformanceTests.NothingButTheSetupRandomnessSeam_ReachesUnrecordedEntropy` scans the compiled IL of every type in the authoritative assemblies for the platform's unrecorded sources, resolved as members rather than named as strings; pre-fix it reported the defect verbatim from the compiled code - `GuardsDigital.Core.Setup.TieBreakerSetupAuthority.CreateNormalSetupState: reaches unrecorded entropy: System.Security.Cryptography.RandomNumberGenerator.Create` - and post-fix it is empty, with violator and compliant twins proving it is not inert and an `UnresolvedTokens` guard proving nothing hid behind a skipped instruction. `SetupRandomnessDeterminismTests.NormalSetup_ReproducesItsTieBreakerSideFromTheRecordedSeedAlone` and its replay sibling were observed failing deterministically rather than probabilistically, by the 6.2c precedent of adding the new vocabulary inert first: the carriers returned `Unrecorded`, so the first reported a normal setup that recorded no draw and the second refused to derive from the named absence. A 50/50 re-draw comparison was considered and rejected as a closing check - a governed check must not pass by luck. **Carriers.** The seed rides `PlaceholderOneVsOneTestConfiguration`, its restore state and `InitialScenarioDescription`, so replay derives the side rather than copying it and `MatchAggregate.RestoreGraphs` writes the recorded scenario's seed back into the restored configuration - the side comes from the payload because it flips during play, the draw cannot, because it happened once and no later state restates it. Focused fixtures reach the scenario through its existing constructor, which now records `Unrecorded` explicitly: a scenario whose side was selected under `DEV-005` genuinely made no draw, and saying otherwise would be a lie the next reader would derive from - which is also why the 154 existing scenario and setup construction sites were left alone rather than mechanically churned. `QuickGameSetupState` deliberately does not carry a second copy: it describes board, team and hero placement and takes its Tie Breaker from the same configuration, so a duplicate seed there would be a second home for one fact and the two could disagree. **No schema move, and this is measured rather than assumed.** The 8.2 re-baseline had predicted one, from the 7.9a audit's expectation. Implementation showed setup state is deliberately an *input* to restore rather than payload content - the payload has never carried the initial Tie Breaker side either, only the running one - so the seed belongs beside the rest of setup and no wire field changes. All nine `CardAggregateInvarianceTests` digests and all eight `SnapshotCompatibilityCorpusTests` fixtures were asserted byte-identical rather than re-pinned (20/20 focused), schema stays v21, and the five-document pinned-constant ceremony was correctly not owed. The prediction is recorded as corrected rather than quietly satisfied. **DEV-005 is untouched** and normal flow still flips randomly from the player's perspective; only the flip's provenance changed. **Validation** T2: complete Edit Mode **1345/1345** and complete Play Mode **37/37** unfiltered, both green on their first run at the final revision; cross-platform harness **1179/1179**, up from 1167 by exactly the twelve new Unity-independent cases; focused Edit Mode 40/40 across the two new fixtures plus the replay, setup, tie-breaker, digest, corpus and configured-scene regressions; focused harness 20/20 on the compatibility set. Presentation compiled through the scratch probe before any Unity launch, since the harness cannot see it. The `Testing/PLAYMODE_AND_SUITES.md` baseline row moved with its delta explained. Living-document validator 127/127 self-tests, 57 governed active leaves, 0 warnings; `git diff --check` clean. | `36cc564` |

## Milestone 8.3 Add verified Wasp definitions and lifecycle data completion evidence

| ID | Outcome | Commit |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 8.3 | Wasp's nineteen printed faces are the project's first `VerifiedOfficial` definitions - `WaspCardContent` at content revision `wasp-8-3-rev-1` - and three of the 7.9a review's findings close on their named checks, each observed failing at the pre-fix working tree and passing after. **The definitions are content, not behavior.** No action carries an `EffectRoot`: each card's composition lands at its own 8.5-8.13 slice under a content-revision advance, and every printed primary states which milestone owes it through the new `PrintedTextComposition` - Crackling Dagger stating instead the 2026-08-21 deferral's named trigger, "first odd-player-count configuration". That statement exists because a missing `EffectRoot` already meant two unrelated things, and milestone 8.4's honesty rule needs to grey exactly the primaries whose text cannot run yet. The faces themselves are checked against the 8.1 inventory rather than against themselves: `WaspVerifiedContentTests` holds a literal nineteen-row table in `Rules/HERO_WASP.md`'s own column order, so a single drifted number, colour, Tier, icon or name fails. p.15's upgrade pairs pass `CardProgressionValidator` with both Tier II cards of a colour naming the same Tier III pair; p.9's supertype gives exactly three basic cards and makes Static Barrier's primary the only basic skill in her set, which is the sole trigger High Voltage can ever read; the dealt eighteen pass `CardInitialSetupValidator` and run the ordinary `CardLifecycleAuthority` including p.16's unlock, and the Handicap card is never instantiated because p.5 boxes it. **F-CARD-08:** `CardSourceContent` gives transcribed text a home and couples it to `CardContentAuthority` in both directions - verified content must state its words or state that the face prints none, a synthetic fixture may state neither - and `CardDefinitionTests.EveryDefinitionStatesItsSourceContentOrAnExplicitAbsence_AndNoRuleReadsIt` also scans the production assemblies to prove no rule reads it back, so the project has a home for card text without a printed-text parser; pre-fix it named all nineteen verified definitions. **F-CARD-06:** the effect-definition validator refuses a node table that is a graph rather than a tree, on `EffectDefinitionValidatorTests.ANodeReachableThroughTwoParents_IsRejected`, with a compliant twin declaring the same work under two ids. Acyclic and reachable were checked and one-parent was assumed; the concrete failure was the dominance analysis unioning each parent's guarantees, so a shared node inherited the guarantees of the path that did not run and could bind an output from a declined `Choose` arm. No existing definition was a graph - the whole suite stayed green when the rule landed. **F-CARD-05:** eleven production sites minted a synthetic catalog when handed none, under a serializer comment claiming "the configured session always supplies the real ones" while `MatchAggregate` supplied neither, so every configured capture and restore silently used a default nobody chose. `MatchContentCatalogs` is now the one declared origin; the card-action dispatcher and the snapshot restore take it as a required argument, the write path reads it from the graph it is serializing, and `EffectSeamConformanceTests.NoProductionCompositionSite_NamesACapabilityCatalogItWasNotGiven` fails the build if any other production type names a catalog factory - including `ForThisBuild` itself, so the defect cannot return wearing the new type's name. The false comment is corrected in the same edit. Pre-fix the check reported seven method/member pairs across the dispatcher and both serializer paths. The **catalog-identity naming decision** is recorded beside the `RISK-COMPAT-001` row: a catalog carries no identity of its own and is deliberately given none, because the payload records the registration list - every capability id and revision, every component definition - which is finer-grained than a name and cannot agree while the contents differ. **F-TEST-03** closed opportunistically: `AnUnavailableAttack_StillOffersTheClearThatReplacesIt` traded its `Assert.Pass` escape for an `Is.Not.Empty` guard, verified non-vacuous at 6/6. Three type-level widenings were needed and each is p.9's or p.14's own sentence rather than a convenience: an Attack or Skill may print either the Range or the Radius icon ("some primary actions have a Range or Radius icon"), `PrintedDefenseResolution` separates p.14's numeric Defense from its Block so a Block card declares a Block instead of a sentinel number, and an Ultimate carries the printed icon its ability reads - which is what lets ledger question 12's approved interpretation reach High Voltage at 8.14. High Voltage's ability is a trigger no `RuleModification` family expresses, so it ships as an explicit composition owed to 8.13 rather than a stand-in rule, and the one query that would have read it skips an Ultimate that supplies none. **No schema cause exists:** the catalogs the configured session now states explicitly are the ones the removed defaults produced, so schema stays v21 with all nine measured digests and all eight corpus fixtures asserted byte-identical rather than re-pinned. **Validation** T3-breadth: complete unfiltered Edit Mode 1367/1367 and Play Mode 37/37 on Windows, cross-platform harness 1201/1201, all zero failed and zero skipped; the +22 Edit Mode delta matches the harness exactly and the `Testing/PLAYMODE_AND_SUITES.md` baseline moved to 1367 with its narrative, the three oldest per-milestone narratives routed to `Archive/TESTING_HISTORY.md` on that shard's own threshold. Edit Mode was green on its second run: the first failed to compile on a `Does.Contain(...).Or.Contain(...)` overload the harness's newer NUnit resolves and Unity's NUnit 3.5 does not, which is `RISK-VALIDATION-001` behaving exactly as written. Living-documentation validator and `git diff --check` green. | `7833472` |

## Milestone 8.4 Load both Wasp hands into the director turn loop completion evidence

| ID | Outcome | Commit |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 8.4 | The configured match deals verified content. `PlaceholderOneVsOneTestConfiguration.Create()` and the saved scene both hand each mirrored seat rulebook p.5's starting five - Magnetic Dagger, Static Barrier, Shock, Lift Up, Stop Projectiles - over p.6's remaining thirteen, and the p.20 Handicap card is instantiated nowhere. **A mirror is one printed set dealt twice**: eighteen definitions, thirty-six instances, seat-prefixed instance identities, and the configuration deduplicates by `CardDefinitionReference` so the same id at two revisions is still the conflict `CardInitialSetupValidator` refuses. **The rule that makes dealing them honest.** Wasp's text lands at the 8.6-8.13 slices, so a printed primary whose `PrintedTextComposition` is still owed is offered with its availability decided false and refuses a start atomically with `PrintedCardTextNotComposed` - stated once as a third clause beside milestone 6.5af's inert Skill and 7.8a1's targetless Attack, so the offer set and the start command cannot disagree, and repeated on all three start paths so the rule holds for every action kind a start can name rather than the two that carry text today. Performing one would carry out the printed values and silently drop the printed sentence, which the match cannot detect afterwards; p.12 makes refusing costless, and p.13's Clear and Fast Travel are still offered against a greyed primary. Recorded as `DEV-006` with its retirement trigger. **Both closing checks were observed failing first, and neither could pass by luck.** `ASkillPrimaryWhoseCardTextHasNoComposition_IsOfferedGreyedAndRefusesTheStart` uses Static Barrier, whose primary Skill carries no `EffectRoot` - the exact shape both existing availability rules answer true for by construction - so pre-fix it was necessarily offered available; `AnAttackPrimaryWhoseCardTextHasNoComposition_IsGreyedEvenWithATargetBesideIt` puts an enemy minion beside the attacker, the position 7.8a1's own fixture exists to prove an Attack stays available in. The first attempt at the Attack case was rejected during implementation because the two Wasps start far apart and the offer was already unavailable for the wrong reason - `AttackActionRejected`, not the missing composition - which is the coin-comparison failure mode the ceremony forbids. Both failed at the pre-fix working tree with the new enum member added inert, and both pass here. **The retirement.** `PlaceholderMovementHand` and `PlaceholderMovementHandRestoreState` are gone: the carrier is `ConfiguredHand`/`ConfiguredHandRestoreState`, which describe a hand rather than claim to be one temporarily, and the showcase cards are `Core/Cards/Synthetic/SyntheticShowcaseHand`, reached only through configuration factories named for the fixtures that want them. The register row is deleted and the name is in `Tools/RetiredStructures.txt`. `PlaceholderOneVsOneTestConfiguration.Restore` stopped naming each seat's Ultimate and reads `Progression.IsUltimate` off the definition instead - milestone 7.3 had to name it because p.16 attaches an Ultimate to the hero rather than to any card, but 7.5 dealt it an instance, and the mirror is what forced the change since one named id can no longer tell two seats apart. `DirectorTestHand` was deleted: it had no caller since 6.1c's `DirectorHandsPresenter` and existed only to wrap the retiring hand. **Schema: no move, and the corpus gains rather than re-pins.** The re-baseline predicted a schema move for verified hand and setup persistence; implementation showed there is nothing to move, because the definition table is a list of id-and-revision pairs and everything a face prints lives in the trusted catalog the reader binds to. All nine measured digests and all ten existing corpus fixtures are byte-identical with two mirrored Wasps in the session, which is the compatibility evidence rather than an absence of it. The corpus gains an eleventh fixture, `verified-hand-setup-v21`, because no payload in it had ever carried verified content, so a later slice advancing Wasp's revision would have moved nothing and said nothing. **What the Play Mode tier cost, and what it bought.** The saved scene is the configured session, so thirteen scene cases that assert showcase-Skill presentation - board cues, effect-choice destinations, generated token views, a minion attack - lost their subject. They did not lose it for long: `MatchSceneCompositionRoot.NextComposedCardContent` is a one-shot request the composition consumes and resets, so five fixtures and two `MinimalHexGridPlayModeTests` cases ask for the mechanics-showcase hands and cannot leave that request on. The reset is proved rather than asserted - the added case loads the scene under a request, then loads it again and finds the verified mirror, thirty-six cards and p.5's starting five - and the other nineteen cases in that fixture were deliberately left on the verified scene, where they passed unchanged, including the two complete rounds and the unavailable-Attack refusal. **A verified Defense answers a real attack.** No Wasp hero can attack until 8.10, by this milestone's own rule, so the one configuration that can prove p.14's reaction runs on printed values pairs a showcase attacker with a verified defender: Red Pursuit's printed Attack 4 against Shock's printed Defense 6, the four in-hand cards printing a secondary Defense icon offered and no others, and the card discarded. **Validation.** Complete Windows Edit Mode 1375/1375 from a baseline of 1367 and complete Play Mode 38/38 from 37, both unfiltered, `total` read from each results XML with zero failed, skipped or inconclusive; cross-platform harness 1209/1209 from 1201, unblocked throughout; player-build smoke gate `GATE PASSED` with exit code 0 and `COMPOSED heroes=2 cards=36 version=0` re-verified against the mirror, zero warnings and zero `NullReferenceException`s; living-document validator and `git diff --check` clean. Play Mode took three runs, the first on the thirteen fixtures the content change reaches and the second on a vacuity guard naming a synthetic card by its instance id rather than its `placeholder-`-prefixed definition id. | `8e0f7fe` |

## Milestone 8.5 Open the general card-text seam on Attack and Defense actions completion evidence

| ID | Outcome | Commit |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 8.5 | The general card-text seam is open, and three of the 7.9a review's findings close on their named checks, each observed failing first. **F-CARD-01.** `ActionDefinition` refused an `EffectRoot` on anything but a Skill, so a printed Attack's "After the attack:" rider had nowhere to live and the whole of rulebook p.14's card-text order was unreachable. A printed Attack or Defense may now declare one, and because that root is card text rather than the action itself it names which of the page's numbered moments applies it - `CardTextTiming`, one of step 1's "Before the attack", step 4's Defense action text, or step 7's "After the attack:" once the target has defended or been removed. The statement is refused in each way it could be false: a root with no moment, a moment with no root, a moment belonging to the other action kind, and a moment on an action whose source-card text p.13 suppresses - **which is the rules consumer the `CardTextApplication` policy had never had**. Movement, Hold, Fast Travel and Clear still carry no root, each being an algorithm rather than effect content, so the seam widened by exactly two kinds. **Where the text runs.** The Attack backend owns p.14's order and emits a `ResolutionStep.ApplyCardText` at each of the three positions - inside `AnswerAttackTarget` before the Attack Total is announced, inside `AnswerDefense` between step 3's discard and step 5's Defense total, and inside `ResolveOutcome` after step 6's check and the Defeat stage - and `TurnResolutionAuthority.MaterializeSteps` composes each one through `CardActionResolutionAuthority.ApplyCardText`, which is where the catalog, the component ports and the items live. The minion arm keeps both attacker moments around the defeat that replaces p.14's steps 3 to 5. **Target policies.** p.13 states an Attack's targeting as a rule about the card - "You may attack enemy heroes and minions", adjacency for a non-Ranged Attack "even if the card has a radius icon", the printed Range for a Ranged one - so every printed Attack now carries a `PrintedAttackTargetPolicy` and `AttackTargetQuery` reads the reach from it instead of switching on `AttackRangeKind`. The default reproduces the page exactly, so no existing Attack changed; the narrowing to heroes alone is Crackling Dagger's shape, and both of the page's sentences are enforced at construction. **F-BOARD-03.** `EffectResolutionContext` and `EffectCapabilityContext` carry the enclosing action's kind as a required argument, and the four handler sites that answered p.18's immunity with the constant `RuleActivity.Skill` ask `context.EnclosingActivity` instead. The restore path derives the kind from the trusted definition the frame names rather than persisting it, so no wire field was added. **F-CARD-07.** A `Conditional` executed its predicate for one boolean and discarded everything else: the typed facts never reached the outcome list, so no later node could bind what the predicate established, and a predicate that paused, refused or declined was indistinguishable from one that answered no. The result is now recorded with its facts at the point the branch is chosen - not at the node's end, because the branch may pause and a second factless record would shadow the first, since an input binding reads the last outcome a node published - and a non-Applied result routes through `ApplyCapabilityResult`. `Resume` gained the matching `Conditional` case so a routed pause cannot create a frame no command could close. **The boundary this milestone did not cross, stated rather than discovered later.** Card text applied inside an Attack cannot pause: the attack's remaining steps are produced in the command that began them and no persisted state can hold them while a question stands, so `EffectDefinitionValidator.ValidateCardText` refuses a card-text root containing p.10's `Choose`, a `may` step or a `RepeatAction` - at content load, before a match runs, with the compliant twin pinned beside each refusal. A Skill is unaffected. The slice that first transcribes a rider needing a question - 8.10's discard riders, 8.11's Block riders - owns the nesting and the schema move it costs. **Closing evidence, all three observed failing at the working tree with the new vocabulary inert.** `ConfiguredCardMatrixTests.APrintedAttackWhoseTextApplies_RunsItsEffectRootInsideItsAttackTimingOrder` reported the ordered log without any `Action.CardTextApplied` fact in it; `ConfiguredImmunitySkillTests.CardTextOperationsAskTheEnclosingActionsActivity` reported `ImpossibleMandatory` where an Attack's text must be offered the unit a Skill immunity protects (`Expected: NeedsChoice, But was: ImpossibleMandatory`); `EffectNodePolicyTests.AConditionalPredicate_PublishesItsFactsAndCannotSilentlyReportFalse` reported `< "then=ImpossibleMandatory" >` with no `root=Applied` at all, which is the binding consumer finding nothing published. All three pass here. **Proven on synthetic content, as the milestone's non-goals require.** Three new fixture cards in their own hands - Red Foretell at p.14 step 1, Red Aftermath at step 7 and narrowed to heroes, Blue Rejoinder whose secondary Defense applies at step 4 - reach the seam without touching the five ordinary cards every pinned digest and the approved card matrix measure. **No Wasp card composed anything**, and the `wasp-8-3-rev-1` revision did not move. **Compatibility: schema stays v21 and nothing was re-pinned.** All nine measured digests and all eleven corpus fixtures are byte-identical, which is the evidence rather than an absence of it: the enclosing action's kind is re-derived on restore, the target policy is definition content the catalog supplies, and the new fixture cards are in a configuration no corpus payload is captured from. **Validation.** Complete Windows Edit Mode 1383/1383 from a baseline of 1375 and complete Play Mode 38/38 unchanged in total, both unfiltered, `total` read from each results XML with zero failed, skipped or inconclusive, and both green on their first run; cross-platform harness 1217/1217 from 1209, unblocked throughout; living-document validator 127/127 self-tests, 0 warnings; `git diff --check` clean. **Review, from the four required perspectives, with no in-scope defect found.** Player experience: nothing a player can see changed, which is the honest claim for a seam proven on fixture cards - no configured card carries text, and the Wasp primaries stay greyed under `DEV-006`. Rules authority: every decision is read off pp.10, 12, 13, 14 and 18 and quoted where asserted, including the two p.13 sentences enforced at construction; the one thing this milestone cannot do is stated as a refusal rather than left to fail later. Maintainability: one statement of p.14's order in the backend that owns the attack, one composition site for the text, one bridge between the printed keyword and the checkpoint vocabulary, and the activity constant that could go stale is gone. Project risk: Phase 8 is open and 8.6 follows, now unblocked. **Material limitations, recorded rather than glossed.** The pausing boundary above is the milestone's largest, and it is a real constraint on 8.10 and 8.11 rather than a theoretical one. The `NeedsChoice` a capability could still return from inside card text is caught at execution and thrown as a content fault, because nothing a definition declares says whether a handler will ask - the validator covers the three node shapes that ask on their own. And the `Conditional` resume path added for a paused predicate is stated but unexercised: no predicate in this build asks a question, and it exists so that routing a pause cannot create an unresumable frame. | `0f46ef3` |

## Milestone 8.5a Show verified card words and action values in the card tray completion evidence

| ID | Outcome | Commit |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 8.5a | Wasp's verified words are readable on her cards. Since milestone 8.3 every face's printed sentence has ridden `CardSourceContent` where no rule may read it, and since 8.4 both mirrored seats have dealt those faces; the tray showed a name, a colour word and a flat value line, so the one thing a player needs to decide a turn on was the one thing not on screen. A tray card is now a card: portrait at 186x344, with rulebook p.7's Initiative on a banner in its own corner, the name centred beneath it, every printed value belonging to an alternate action in a strip of its own, the primary action's kind on a band with its own number attached to it, the transcribed sentence on a light rules panel, p.9's one Range or Radius icon on a badge at that panel's lower left, and the lifecycle label along the bottom. **Every grouping is typed.** `CurrentRoundCards.ReadFace` decides which region a value belongs to from the action it is printed on and its `PrintedValueChannel` - the primary is the action whose origin is `PrintedPrimary`, the secondary strip is every other action the card itself prints, the two qualifiers a label may carry are p.13's Ranged classification and p.14's Block, and p.16's Ultimate reads its icon off the permanent ability because it has no action at all. Nothing reads a card name and nothing parses a printed sentence, which is the rule every card milestone since 4.4 has kept and which `EveryPrintedValue_IsDrawnInExactlyOneOfTheFourHomesItsTypedContentPutsItIn` now holds mechanically: the four regions partition the definition's printed values, each drawn exactly once. Wasp is the proof rather than a fixture built to pass - Static Barrier's text says "radius" three times and its badge reads the printed icon, Stop Projectiles' text says "ranged" and its band reads `DEFENSE - BLOCK` from `PrintedDefenseResolution`. **Three states stay three:** transcribed words are shown verbatim and complete, a read face that prints none says so, and a synthetic fixture with no source face says that instead. **Contrast is measured, not eyeballed:** every plate a card draws is a shift of its own colour *away* from that colour's text colour, so contrast can only rise, and `EveryCardSurface_KeepsItsTextReadableWithoutRelyingOnColourAlone` measures the WCAG ratio for all six `CardColor` values against every plate and against the rules panel. **Five user-approved amendments, all taken on 2026-08-21 during the milestone and all recorded in the roadmap block.** (1) The colour's name came off the card face - a card wearing its colour and also spelling it says the same thing twice in the space the printed text needs - and is carried by the card's hover and assistive reading instead, which is exactly where milestone 7.8a already keeps a hero rail turn slot's colour; the test asserts both halves, that no label draws it and that `AssistiveLabel` contains it. (2) The board framing reserves a share of the viewport width along the left edge, where the tray stands, so the map sits right of centre and the cards get a band of their own: `PlaceholderTestGridView` sizes the map to the band that is left and moves the camera left by half the reserve, widening only when the map no longer fits, which on the official map is barely at all because it is close to square and the viewport is 16:9. The reserve settled at 0.32 after three passes in the running scene, set by eye to where the user wanted the board rather than to the value that would clear the tray completely, so a little of the map is still behind the cards' corner - which is why the Play Mode assertion is the asymmetry the reserve produces rather than an absence of overlap. (3) A hero rail's occupied turn slot reveals the card played on that turn, drawn by `DirectorHandsUnityView.BuildStandaloneCard` - the same builder the tray uses, so one card cannot read as two - with the card also named in the slot's assistive label; an empty slot reveals nothing, a redrawn rail drops the reveal, and the slot gains no command and no focus stop, because eight new tab stops in front of the tray would be a keyboard-order change this milestone has no reason to make. (4) The tray's height bound rose from a third of a tall viewport to 0.46, because the longest verified face needs six wrapped lines that a third of 1080 could not hold without putting a row of five cards behind a scrollbar; the board still keeps the larger of the two surfaces. (5) The tray's `PASS` and `CONFIRM FACEDOWN` controls moved from a row under the cards into the header's right side, where they stay in view and do not move when a card is selected. **One real defect found in the visible pass and fixed here.** `HudElements.MarkSelected` draws the selection outline by widening a border from one pixel to three. That is invisible on a control sized by its own label and wrong on a tray card, whose width is fixed and whose height comes from its content: four pixels of border re-wrapped that card's rules text, made it taller, and - because the row stretches every card to the tallest one - grew the whole tray each time a player picked a card up. `MarkSelectedWithoutResizing` draws the outline always and changes only its colour, and the regression is held by an assertion that every card's border width is 3 in both states. **What did not change,** and is asserted: played-first/available/discarded ordering, the available-card lift, viewed-hero selection, commitment forwarding, unavailable-card refusal, pointer blocking, and the tray's keyboard order. `TrayCardViewModel` construction moved into `CurrentRoundCards.TrayCard` so the tray and the rail build a card the one way. No rule, legality, card lifecycle, command, projection field, or schema value changed anywhere; schema stays v21 and no digest was touched, because nothing in this milestone reaches the wire. **Validation.** Complete Windows Edit Mode 1395/1395 from a baseline of 1383 and complete Play Mode 39/39 from 38, both unfiltered, `total` read from each results XML with zero failed, skipped or inconclusive; cross-platform harness 1217/1217, unchanged and unblocked, because the whole change is in `Presentation` and the harness excludes it by construction. Targeted non-batch Game View inspection at 2560x1440 captured `tmp/milestone-8-5a-card-tray.png` and `tmp/milestone-8-5a-card-tray-selected.png` and compared them against the approved mockup; the Play Mode case that writes them also proves, in the running scene, that each card draws its five named regions, that the words on screen are the verified transcriptions, that no card leaves the tray on any edge, that five cards share one top edge, and that the map's viewport margins are asymmetric - the property the centred framing it replaced made equal by construction. Living-documentation validator 127/127 self-tests with 0 warnings, and `git diff --check` clean. **Review, from the four required perspectives.** Player experience: this is the milestone a player feels, and it was iterated against the running scene rather than against the mockup alone - four rounds of visible correction, of which the selection-resize defect above was the only functional one. The Initiative banner and the card title are a compromise the user accepted rather than an ideal: at 186 pixels a banner in the corner and a centred title cannot both sit on one line without one of them moving off centre, and every arrangement that tried traded a dead gap for a mid-word break. Rules authority: nothing here reads or decides a rule; the p.9, p.13, p.14, p.15 and p.16 readings the regions rest on are quoted where they are asserted. Maintainability: one face reading, one card builder, one tray-card construction, and the plate colours are a stated direction rather than a table of swatches. Project risk: 8.5b follows and extends this layout. **Material limitations.** The HUD is drawn at constant pixel size, so on a 2560-wide screen the whole panel reads smaller than the mockup does; that is a panel scale-mode question for a later presentation milestone and no part of this one. Two of Wasp's five starting names still wrap to two lines, as the mockup's own do. And the tray's height bound and the board's reserve are two numbers tuned against one map at two resolutions; neither is derived from the other, so a very different viewport may want them re-tuned. | `39603a0` |

## Milestone 8.5b Highlight tray cards whose Active effects are live completion evidence

| ID | Outcome | Commit |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 8.5b | A card whose Active effect is still running says so. `MatchSessionProjection.ActiveEffectSourceCardInstanceIds` publishes every card instance sourcing an instance currently in `ActiveEffectPhase.Active`, derived in `MatchSession` from the round authority's own lifecycle - the round holds it and hands the same object to each turn, which is why the answer exists whether or not a turn is running, and an effect that outlives the turn that made it is exactly the kind a player needs to see. Only `Active` counts: a dormant instance has not begun and an ended one is over. The list is deduplicated by source, so one card appears once however many of its instances are live, and ordered by instance id so the sequence is a fact rather than an iteration accident. **Presentation asks and never infers.** `CurrentRoundCards.TrayCard` reads `projection.IsActiveEffectSource(card.Id)`; nothing walks aggregate state, reads a definition to guess that an effect might exist, or reconstructs the answer from events - the third would have given a different answer after a restore than during live play, which is the whole reason the fact is derived in the application. **The indicator.** The card sits in a `gd-card-slot` whose border is the outline, because the card's own border is the shared selection treatment and both states can stand together: the captured `active-and-selected` state shows Red Bulwark spent, outlined violet and labelled `ACTIVE`, beside Red Needle selected in cyan. The slot is always drawn and only its colour changes, so a card never resizes when an effect starts or ends. The accent is violet by elimination - the five printed colours are gold, silver, red, green and blue, selection is cyan and the confirm accent is orange - and it is never the only thing saying so: `ACTIVE` is drawn beside the lifecycle label rather than replacing it, and `AssistiveLabel` carries `ACTIVE EFFECT IN EFFECT`. A test asserts the accent differs from selection, from confirm, and from all six `CardColor` faces. **The producer is synthetic, and it had to be the right one.** The first attempt used the showcase Skill's `MarkActorV1` node and proved nothing: that effect's activation checkpoint is `ActionStarted`, which has already passed by the time the capability creates it, so it never left `Dormant` and every assertion about a live effect was vacuous. The fixture now drives the Bulwark modification Skill - the same effect `ConfiguredEffectLifetimeGateTests` already takes to `Active` - and every case asserts the lifecycle really holds a live instance before asserting anything about the indicator. **Cross-platform coverage was moved deliberately.** The four projection cases live in a new `ActiveEffectProjectionTests` rather than beside the tray, because `MatchHudPresentationTests` is on the harness exclude list and this is application authority whose answer must agree on Mono and on .NET; the harness rose 1217 to 1221 by exactly those four. **The 8.5a correction the user reported in the same session.** The card the current turn is being resolved from was dimmed by the shared unavailable treatment, which says "spent" about a card that is in play. `TrayCardViewModel.IsResolving` is the revealed card for the current turn, and `HudElements.MarkInPlay` keeps it inert - no command, and no focus stop the tray did not have before - at full opacity. The first fix skipped the unavailable treatment altogether and left the card enabled, which would have added eight focus stops the tray never had; the test that caught it asserts both halves. **What did not change:** ordering, the available-card lift (moved onto the slot so the outline rises with its card), viewed-hero selection, commitment forwarding, unavailable-card refusal, pointer blocking, and keyboard order. No Active-effect rule, duration or checkpoint moved, no Wasp card composed anything, and schema stays v21 with nothing new on the wire - the indicator is derived on every path, which is what `ARestoredPosition_DerivesTheSameSourcesRatherThanRememberingThem` proves by moving the position on and restoring back. **Validation.** Complete Windows Edit Mode 1402/1402 from a baseline of 1395 and complete Play Mode 40/40 from 39, both unfiltered with zero failed, skipped or inconclusive; cross-platform harness 1221/1221 from 1217, unblocked throughout. Targeted non-batch Game View inspection captured `tmp/milestone-8-5b-active.png`, `tmp/milestone-8-5b-active-and-selected.png` and `tmp/milestone-8-5b-ended.png` - the three states the milestone asks to see. Living-documentation validator 127/127 self-tests with 0 warnings; `git diff --check` clean. Three Unity cycles were spent on fixture and assembly faults rather than on production behaviour: the dormant-producer above, a `CS0012` from naming `MatchSessionProjection` in the Play Mode test assembly, which does not reference `GuardsDigital.Application` and which the single-assembly compile probe therefore cannot catch, and a Play Mode assertion made against whichever hand the tray happened to be showing rather than the one holding the card. **Review, four perspectives.** Player experience: a persistent effect was previously invisible once its card was spent, and the two-state capture is the evidence that the new mark reads apart from selection. Rules authority: nothing here reads or decides a rule; `Active` versus dormant versus ended is the lifecycle's own distinction, taken rather than reinterpreted. Maintainability: one derivation in the application, one construction of a tray card, one builder for a card face. Project risk: 8.6 follows and is unblocked. **Material limitations.** No configured Wasp card creates an Active effect yet - every printed primary is greyed under `DEV-006` until its own slice - so the running-scene evidence is on the showcase composition, and the first verified consumer arrives at 8.9. The indicator is card-level by design: a card sourcing two live effects shows one mark, and which effects those are is not on the card. | `6716219` |

| ID | Outcome | Commit |

| 8.6 | **Wasp's five telekinetic Skills resolve their complete printed text - the first verified official card text this project runs.** Lift Up, Control Gravity, Center of Mass, Telekinesis and Mass Telekinesis carry `EffectRoot`s at content revision `wasp-8-6-rev-1`, their node tables in the new `WaspEffectContent` so `WaspCardContent` stays the transcription, and each states `PrintedTextComposition.None` - which `ActionDefinition` already refuses to accept beside a missing root, so a composition landing without its row being cleared fails at content load. **No custom handler anywhere**, exactly as 8.2's capability matrix promised: every node names a capability that already existed under a declaration that did not. **The four new declarations.** p.19's straight line is stated whole - `IsStraightLine` accepts a file and the single space the page calls out, `AreInStraightLine` now answers true for a space and itself, and `FilterOutStraightLine` is the complement Telekinesis, Mass Telekinesis and both Boomerangs print; the membership and direction readings part company deliberately, because a line of one space names no file for p.18's Push to walk. `DirectMoveDestinationPolicy` replaces milestone 6.4's `towardActor` boolean with three declared readings - unrestricted, `TowardActor`, and `EquidistantFromActor` for `without moving it away from you or closer to you` - measured by `CloserQuery` and the new `EquidistantQuery`, which negates it in both directions rather than writing a third comparison; direct Move is retired to revision 3, because two live revisions of one declaration would let a definition ask a question the capability can no longer answer the same way. Place is retired to revision 2 and gains the two things p.18 leaves to the card: `PlaceEligiblePieceKind.UnitOrToken`, which admits what `GamePiece` admits and is a separate member for the reason `PushEligiblePieceKind` refuses that name - the name is the content - and `PlaceDestinationAnchor`, so `into a space adjacent to you` is measured from the acting hero, checked against the declaration rather than trusted, and refused as `DestinationAnchorMismatch` when a caller and a policy disagree. `synthetic-range-target` is the printed-Range twin of 7.2b's Radius selection, sharing one handler parameterised by channel, and `RangeValueQuery` was promoted out of `AttackDefenseCardActionStrategyBackend` so the attack's reach and a skill's reach cannot disagree about one icon. **The bounded repeat, and the correction taken inside it.** A `RepeatAction` node declares `MaximumRepeats`, defaulting to the one 6.7's structural bound always meant, so Center of Mass's `May repeat up to two times` runs three instances and the third is offered nothing; `StartRepeatedSkill` now reports a further repeat, which 6.7 could not produce and therefore never read. `on the same target` names the selection node whose chosen **piece** the repeated instance inherits - not the space, which was the first design and which the first fixture refuted immediately: an orbit's whole content is moving the target, so a remembered coordinate names whatever stands where the target used to be. The piece is recorded onto the frame at the moment the selection names it, and the repeated instance looks its current space up and hands it to that node's own `Resume`, which re-derives its candidates and refuses a target that is no longer eligible exactly as it would refuse a stale answer. The definition validator refuses a binding that could not be inherited - a selection this definition does not declare, one publishing no single space, one that has not certainly run by the dominance analysis 8.3 already built, or a second binding in the same definition - on `UnresolvedRepeatTargetBinding`. **Schema v22, decided rather than incidental.** The repeat continuation joins both the pending Skill frame and the owed repeated action; v21 is rejected rather than migrated on the standing ground, because the only reading of its absence is "first instance, nothing inherited", which is right for every payload a v21 build could write and wrong for the first this milestone writes. The full ceremony ran: the six `Tools/PinnedConstants.txt` documents, the two bare `Is.EqualTo` assertions in `DefeatRemovalStageTests` and `MinionAttackResolutionTests`, every corpus fixture renamed `-v22` and re-pinned, `CardAggregateInvarianceTests` re-pinned with its three equality groups unchanged, and a **byte-level delta measured in a throwaway `git worktree` at pre-change revision `3578dda`** rather than assumed. That measurement is why this row can say what did not change: every payload grew by exactly 27 bytes in one contiguous insertion, and those 27 bytes are the new `synthetic-range-target` registration entry rather than the new section - with exactly four bytes differing outside it (the version at offset 30, the capability count 31 to 32, direct Move 2 to 3, Place 1 to 2), plus 54 further single-byte differences in `verified-hand-setup` where Wasp's content revision moves `wasp-8-3-rev-1` to `wasp-8-6-rev-1` across eighteen definition rows and thirty-six instance rows. A twelfth fixture, `paused-orbit-repeat-v22`, was added because the section this milestone put on the wire is written only inside a pending Skill frame or an owed repeat and no earlier payload held either - without it the schema move would have had no version-controlled coverage at all. **Proven on synthetic content first.** Two fixture Skills in their own configurations - the orbit card and the kinesis card - carry all four declarations before Wasp composes them, which is also what keeps `ConfiguredCardMatrixTests`' registered-capability gate honest: `synthetic-range-target` is reachable from a printed card that is nobody's hero. They are where the count above one and the straight-line complement are watched in a configured match at all, because Center of Mass and both Telekineses are Tier II and III cards p.6's setup leaves in the deck; Wasp's own configured evidence is Lift Up, from her printed starting five, orbiting the enemy Wasp and orbiting her again on the repeat. **No check-paired failing observation applies:** 8.6 closes no recorded finding and pairs with no new conformance check; its evidence is the behavioural fixtures above and the measured digests. **Validation.** Complete unfiltered Windows Edit Mode 1438/1438 from a baseline of 1402 and complete unfiltered Play Mode 40/40 unchanged, `total` read from each results XML with zero failed, skipped or inconclusive; cross-platform harness 1257/1257 from 1221, unblocked throughout, and the two tiers agree on the +36 delta exactly because every new case is Unity-independent. One Unity cycle went to an `internal const` fixture value referenced from a test - visible to the single-assembly harness and not to Unity's separate test assemblies - and both suites were green on the run after it. Living-document validator and `git diff --check` clean. **Non-goals held:** no discard rider, no Attack or Defense text, and no Wasp card outside the five. | `0332d66` |

## Milestone 8.7 Consume the declared modifier vocabulary completion evidence

| ID | Outcome | Commit |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 8.7 | **Every declarable modification member now has a production consumer or a by-name reasoned exclusion, closing the 7.9a review's F-CARD-03 and F-BOARD-04.** `ModificationSeamConformanceTests.EveryDeclarableModification_HasAProductionConsumerOrANamedExclusion` enumerates the space from the enums themselves - six value channels, eleven restriction activities, one permission, eleven immunity activities, two effective types and the two new overlay arms, thirty-three members - and requires each to carry a probe whose named production seam answers differently when it is declared, or a reason and the seam its consumer would go in. The probes read seams rather than the modification set, because the set answers every member by construction and the finding was about whether any rule asks; where the asking seam needs a match in flight, `TheConsumingSeams_AreTheOnesTheEngineActuallyUses` supplies the IL link from that seam to the query the probe read, which is the two-link shape `UnitKindQueryConformanceTests` established. **Twenty-two members are consumed and eleven excluded**, and no member may be both. **Restrictions gained the consumer eight of nine activities lacked**: `ActionRestrictionQuery` answers one question for every action kind through `RuleActivityQuery.ForAction`, and the turn's offer set (a fourth `CanStart` clause, not a fourth post-processing site), the three general-rule offers and p.14's Defense response all ask it - so a card that first prints "cannot Attack" is obeyed with no new code, and 6.8f's deferral of Clear to "the milestone that first makes Clear restrictable" is discharged. The five value channels no source modifies stay excluded by name, each naming the single total query that owns it, per this milestone's own non-goal. **Actor-qualified restrictions** are the first of Wasp's two persistent-rule shapes: `RestrictedActorScope` names who is barred - the affected object itself, or heroes in a declared team relation to the source, resolved against the controller side `AppliedRuleModification` already carries - `RestrictionQuery` answers the directed question beside `ImmunityQuery`'s, and the Swap and Place capabilities consume it at target and piece derivation, where an offer is decided rather than at a commit that would refuse what it had offered. p.19's Swap and p.18's Place joined `RuleActivity` as effect families to be nameable, beside milestone 6.4's Object Push, and both capabilities now also ask immunity for their own family as 6.4's Push does. **The conditional obstacle overlay** is the second: `ConditionalObstacleOverlay` is declared on an Active-effect definition beside its modifications and never bound to an object, because it changes what p.18 counts as an obstacle rather than anything about a piece; `RuleModificationQuery` binds it to the source hero's current space (p.17: the Radius moves with its hero) and to the acting hero the set is derived for, so `ObstacleQuery` answers a question about a space alone. That query is the one owner p.18 gives the classification, and `PlaceDestinationQuery` moved onto it, so movement, push and place legality read one closure by construction rather than by three algorithms agreeing. `RuleModificationQuery.Build` and `RuleModificationSet` both gained required arguments for this - the acting object and the bound overlays - with `RuleObject.None` and an empty sequence as the named absences, which is milestone 6.8f's discipline applied to two more rule-affecting values. **F-BOARD-04**: `HasStandingHeavyImmunity` counted "the units in the Battle Zone" with a second derivation of its own beside the shared one; it now consumes `BattleZoneQuery.FindEffectiveMinionsInside`, leaving only what was ever local to p.13's clause - not the Heavy itself, not another Heavy, and standing on the Heavy's side. **Pre-fix observation, both governed checks:** the `UnitKindQueryConformanceTests` link assertion was recorded failing against the unmodified query (`Expected: True / But was: False`), and the consumption rule was recorded failing with the new vocabulary present and inert - four members reported as declared-and-unread (Restriction and Immunity for Swap and for Place) and all four consuming-seam links absent - then passing once the consumers were wired; the pre-fix run is kept at `tmp/8_7-prefix-modification-seam.txt`. The two new declaration shapes are proven on definitions that are nobody's card rather than on new registered capabilities, which is this milestone's non-goal honoured: 8.9 owns Wasp's numbers, and what is proven here is the whole live path from an Active-effect definition through the lifecycle and the derivation to all three obstacle consumers. **Recorded readings:** p.18's after-action correction asks its Place question with no modifications in effect, because the correction is bookkeeping the rules carry out rather than something a hero performs - planner and commit ask identically so they cannot disagree; and adopting the shared Battle-Zone reading also moved one answer, since the old local walk asked the effective-type question of minion miniatures too, which no source can currently observe because none declares `CountsAs Hero`. **Validation** T2 per `Testing/POLICY.md`: complete Edit Mode 1443/1443 and complete Play Mode 40/40, both green on their run after two assembly-boundary compile faults, plus the cross-platform harness 1262/1262; `git diff --check` clean; living-document validator 127/127 self-tests, 57 governed active leaves, 0 warnings. Schema stays **v22** and no pinned digest moved: nothing this milestone adds reaches the wire. | `21e7aab` |

| 8.7a | Each hero rail keeps the card its hero has in play in one dedicated header marker and numbers a turn only once that turn's card has resolved, following the approved current-card rail mockup. **The split is one question asked of the card.** `CurrentRoundCards.TurnSlots` fills a numbered slot on the instance's own `CardResolutionStatus.Resolved` rather than on the round's assignment existing - the round records that assignment inside the same command as p.7's joint reveal, so until now all four slots occupied themselves the instant a card was revealed and presented a turn as history while it was still being played. `CurrentRoundCards.CurrentCard` is the other half: a facedown commitment, or the revealed card whose instance has not resolved, and null otherwise. Resting on a revealed marker shows the same complete passive face the history slots show, through one preview element that can only hold one card. **Four user decisions taken during the Game View review, recorded in the roadmap block**: the revealed marker carries no `RESOLVING` caption (kept in its hover and assistive reading, and on the revealed face's lifecycle band), the facedown marker keeps `FACEDOWN`, the `RED HERO`/`BLUE HERO` line came off the rail onto the hero disc's reading, and the whole Blue rail is now the Red rail mirrored - the numbered slots and the `DISCARD` row excepted. **Validation** T2 per `Testing/POLICY.md`: complete Windows Edit Mode 1453/1453 and complete Play Mode 41/41, both green on the run after two test-side faults - a hover case reading verified printed words from a fixture dealt the showcase hands, and a restart asserted after a snapshot restore had cleared the history a restart replays from.
