| 8.18a | Arien the Tidemaster's complete printed content is source-verified from the extraction and recorded in the new `Rules/HERO_ARIEN.md`, the first hero shard created since Wasp's: nineteen action-card faces (`arien` through `arien19`, 372x520), the dashboard front and back, and both profile-card sides, with every card's colour letter, Tier, Initiative, secondary Defense/Movement icons, primary action type and subtype, primary value, Range or Radius icon, item icon and verbatim rules text. **Each value was read twice** - once from the whole face and again from purpose-built enlargements of the top-right corner, the left ribbon, the primary band, the Range/Radius corner, the item icon and the text box - and the six item-icon types were settled by cross-referencing Wasp's known ones in a single montage, so Initiative was distinguished from Movement and Range from Radius by comparison rather than by eye. **The deck shape matches p.6 exactly**: one Gold basic (Noble Blade), one Silver basic (Spell Break), I/II/II/III/III in each of Red, Blue and Green, one Purple Tier IV Ultimate (Living Tsunami), plus the Gold `H` Handicap card (Ceremonial Blade) that setup returns to the box. The rulebook illustrates the resulting starting five together on p.6 beside the instruction that produces them, so the starting hand is read from the source rather than derived from Tier icons; all nineteen backs are byte-identical and their hash is distinct from all thirty-one other heroes' decks. **Edition confirmed with one caveat, and the caveat is sharper than Wasp's.** The second-printing rulebook illustrates seven Arien faces across four pages - Noble Blade, Spell Break, Dangerous Current, Aspiring Duelist and Liquid Leap on p.6, Slippery Ground on p.7, Expert Duelist on p.11, with three of them repeated on p.11 and p.12 - and five reproduce the extracted faces exactly in name, colour letter, Tier, Initiative, secondary values, band text, primary value, Range or Radius icon and card text. Two differ in wording: Aspiring Duelist's reminder text drops an article, and **Spell Break differs in its operative card text**, the rulebook printing "cannot perform skill actions, except on gold cards" on both pages that illustrate it where the face prints "cannot perform Skill actions, except on the gold cards". Neither difference changes what a card does, but the second falls outside the reminder-text clarification convention that resolved Wasp's single difference, so both are recorded and resolved in the face's favour under the ledger's authority hierarchy. The ledger's first open source question is now answered for Wasp and Arien and open for the other thirty heroes. **One face is unreadable, and the one that was unreadable for Wasp is not.** Arien's dashboard back is the same blank Tabletopia placeholder, recorded as A-01 with the working answer "no tokens or markers" - independently supported here by the profile card carrying none of p.22's `Tokens` role, which that page defines as marking exactly the token-placing heroes, and by no Arien face naming, placing or targeting a token at all, where five Wasp cards act on "a unit, or a token". His profile stat bars, however, resolve to discrete pips against their blue frame where Wasp's gold frame defeated the same measurement: **Attack 8, Defense 5, Initiative 4, Movement 5** on the 1-to-8 scale p.21 states in words, with Defense and Movement each carrying one brighter sixth section that the same page defines as conditional on level-up choices and card texts. Arien therefore has no counterpart to W-02. p.21 additionally uses Arien's own profile card as its anatomy illustration and prints his four role names - Tactician, Disabler, Durable, Pusher - in running text, so the roles are confirmed from the rulebook and not only from icon art. **Two questions are recorded rather than guessed, both owed before the cards they name are implemented.** A-02 asks what "in a straight line directly behind the target" means on the three Red non-ranged Attacks, which reach 2, 3 and 5 such spaces: p.19 defines Straight Line but the rulebook never defines "behind", and a whole-rulebook text search finds the word only inside Arien's own illustration and one unrelated idiom, so the proposed reading is derived from p.18's Push - "move it in a straight line directly away from the pushing unit" - together with p.18's Nearest counting obstacle spaces and p.9's absence of line-of-sight rules. A-03 asks whether Spell Break's "except on the gold cards" means the Gold colour or basic cards generally; the plain reading is the colour, because p.9 lists Gold(D) and Silver(S) as separate colours, and the question is not neutral because p.12 makes a Skill always a Primary action and Wasp's own basic Skill sits on her **Silver** card, so the plain reading stops Static Barrier while a "basic cards" reading would not. **Inventory facts milestone 8.19 will lean on:** Arien's only basic skill action is Spell Break's primary and his only basic attack is Noble Blade's, Ceremonial Blade's when handicapped; he prints no Block card and no combined Defense/Skill card, his three Blue cards carrying ordinary printed Defense values so their text reaches the table only when the card is discarded to defend, per p.14; he uses three different seams of p.14's nine-step Attack/Defense order where Wasp used one, printing "Before the attack:" on five cards (step 1, which no implemented card has used), "After the attack:" on two (step 7), and defender-side text on three (step 4, governing the step-2 minion defense modifiers), while the Ultimate fires before an Attack action is performed at all; Master Duelist prints **This round:**, the first Active effect in the pitch inventory that survives a turn boundary; his twelve item icons span all six p.16 icons and so reach all four dashboard slots, and his dashboard's item legend row is pixel-identical to Wasp's, which is a second independent source for the slot-4 Range/Movement/Radius grouping; and his Ultimate prints neither a Range nor a Radius icon, so ledger question 12's approved interpretation has no Arien instance. **Three printed relationships worth not re-deriving:** Deluge's Radius 1 covers exactly the spaces Slippery Ground's "adjacent to you" covers by p.9, so the Tier III card buys not reach but scaling, since p.16 lets an item widen a printed icon and never a word in card text; Stranger Tide is a strict superset of Liquid Leap and Magical Current by dropping the "not adjacent to an empty spawn point" clause, which the rulebook's own p.12 worked example illustrates against a highlighted board; and the iconless secondary actions are conditional rather than universal, p.11's Fast Travel replacing an entire Movement action and p.13's Clear an entire Attack action, so Arien offers Hold on all nineteen cards, Fast Travel on seventeen and Clear on the seven with an Attack primary. One incidental source correction is recorded in the shard: in the local second-printing PDF the printed page number is the PDF page index minus one, which puts setup step 7's recommended line-up on printed p.5 and the deck-of-eighteen, starting-hand, boxed-Handicap and dashboard-back-tokens instructions of step 11 on printed p.6 - two earlier milestones cited these two pages inconsistently, and every citation in this shard uses printed numbers. The ledger's primary-sources, hero-content-ledger and first-open-question sections now name the new shard, which the roster-wide capability matrix at 8.19 will extend; this milestone claims no capability, no definition and no test, exactly as the shared 8.18 contract scopes it. **Validation** T0 per `Testing/POLICY.md`, the milestone changing only Markdown: living-document validator 127/127 self-tests, 59 governed active leaves, 0 warnings; `git diff --check` clean; no source, test, scene, asset or executable content definition changed, so no Unity suite and no harness run was required or performed. | `4752151f` |

The mismatch is hers alone - the other five roster dashboards were checked in the same pass and reproduce the rulebook's names exactly - so it is not a systematic drift in the extraction. **The usual tie-breaker does not apply**: the ledger's authority hierarchy ranks a component face above the rulebook for *rules*, and an epithet is not a rule, so the hierarchy is silent. Nothing else is in conflict - deck emblem, dashboard, level track, item legend and all nineteen faces agree this is one hero, and p.5's symbol-match step still works - so the roster and the inventory stand either way, and the shard says "Sabina" throughout while claiming neither epithet. Recorded as question **S-01**, blocking the display-name field alone and awaiting a user decision. **Her edition answer is the weakest of the four verified heroes and is recorded as answered-by-convention rather than answered.** A whole-document search for all nineteen card names returns nothing: no Sabina card is illustrated anywhere in the rulebook, so there is no exact-match evidence of the kind that answered the question for Wasp, Arien and Brogan, and the one component the rulebook does name is the one in conflict. Every p.9, p.14, p.16 and p.20 convention holds without exception, which is what the answer rests on (**S-04**). **Two further questions are recorded rather than guessed.** S-02 asks whether Big Sodding Gun's "+2 Range" reaches the Handicap card: her Gold basic is `Basic Attack - Ranged` printing Range 1, so the clause plainly makes it Range 3 and Attack 4, but Bang is a plain melee `Basic Attack` printing no Range icon and no `Ranged` band, and whether the Ultimate gives it a Range - and if so whether that makes it Ranged for every rule that asks - is unanswerable from the faces; p.16's no-matching-icon rule is suggestive but governs items, not card text. Deferred on the same named trigger as Wasp's Crackling Dagger, "first odd-player-count configuration", and unreachable in the six-player pitch where Bang is boxed. S-03 records that her Attack stat bar fills to approximately 7 but shows no crisp base-versus-conditional boundary, so the base cannot be read - the reason is legible even where the value is not, since p.21 makes brighter sections conditional on triggering card texts and Sabina's printed Attack values are the roster's lowest at 1 to 3 while three cards add +3 or +4 and the Ultimate adds +2. Defense 3, Initiative 5 and Movement 5-plus-one-conditional all resolve. The dashboard back needed no question at all: [ledger question 14](../../RULES_LEDGER.md#open-source-and-rules-questions) answers it, and her faces name no token while her profile carries none of p.22's `Tokens` role. **Inventory facts milestone 8.19 will lean on:** her only basic skill is Back to Back's primary and her only basic attack is Point Blank Shot's, Bang's when handicapped; she prints no Defense primary, no Movement primary, no Block and no combined Defense/Skill card, every Red card being an Attack and every Blue and Green card a Skill without exception; **her Gold basic is the first ranged basic attack in the verified inventory**, printing Range 1, while her Handicap card is melee; **she is the first verified hero with no Active effect of any kind**, having neither a `This turn:` nor a `This round:` card; **Quickdraw `2+`, Gunslinger `3+` and Dead Shot `3+` are the first printed increasable values**, using the `+` convention p.14 states beside its own worked example, conditional on the target having played an attack card this turn - the same condition family as Brogan's Shield Bash and Counterattack, applied to raise a value rather than force a discard; Shootout and Bullet Hell **remove** minions rather than defeating them, Shootout restating p.19's no-coins consequence as reminder text; **Ready and Waiting is the first verified card overriding p.13's heavy minion immunity**, which is the whole of what its Tier III printing buys over Listen Up and Roger Roger; Steady Advance and Unwavering Resolve are the second and third verified retrieve cards after Brogan's Bulwark, and p.10 settles their ownership without ambiguity; Covering Fire and Big Sodding Gun make Sabina the **fourth** verified hero printing ledger question 13's "discards a card, or is defeated", with Covering Fire's Tier II sibling printing `if able` in the same sentence position - the Tier-scaled contrast Wasp's Shock and Electroblast also print; and her twelve item icons span all six p.16 icons across all four slots, giving her **the roster's widest Radius reach at eleven cards and narrowest Attack reach at seven**, while her Ultimate prints neither Range nor Radius so ledger question 12 has no Sabina instance. ## Milestone 8.19a Make card text and pre-action continuations fully resumable completion evidence

| ID | Outcome | Commit |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 8.19a | The four continuations the six-hero roster's printed text needs exist, compose through the one action/effect frame, and survive a snapshot at every new altitude. **`attack-target@2`** is a printed Attack's declaration that its own "Before the attack:" text names its target: `PrintedAttackTargetPolicy` gained `AttackTargetBinding`, and an Attack declaring it starts straight into rulebook p.14's step 1 with no target choice and no Attack Total, because the set the page would offer before a charge is the set before the charge. **`set-attack-target@1`** is the node that then names one, publishing a typed fact the Attack resolver reads through `RequestedAttackTargetQuery` - the transport milestone 8.10's Defeat request and 6.9's Lane-Push cause already use, and the reason a binding survives however many questions the text stopped on; the resolver re-derives that unit's legality against the board the text has just changed, and text that names nothing stops the action there under p.10's mandatory-step rule, keeping everything the text already did. **`before-action-trigger@1`** is a continuous rule source's declaration that it runs before its controller performs an action of a named kind, with a frequency: `UltimateRuleDefinition.BeforeActionAbility` carries it, `BeforeActionTriggerQuery` derives the applicable sources exactly as `UltimateTriggeredEffectSourceQuery` derives after-action ones, and the turn holds the offer it interrupted rather than re-deriving one against a board the trigger's own effect may have moved a piece on. **`around-primary-action@1`** publishes which side of a primary action the rest of its text belongs to; the definition declares a root sequence of exactly two nodes - the ordering question and the work it places - and is entered twice, once per side, because a whole Movement with a continuation of its own stands between them. **Milestone 8.5's opposite rule is retired.** Card text applied inside an Attack could not pause at all; 8.10 held p.14's steps 4 and 7, whose remainders follow from the attack's identity; this milestone holds step 1, whose remainder is the rest of the page. The minion arm was the reason it could not: p.13's Defeat was computed *before* the step-1 text, so the facts standing after that step were not reproducible from any identity. `ContinueAfterBeforeAttackText` is now the one generator for everything after step 1 - the target's binding, the Attack Total, the Defense request, the minion Defeat, and the no-target stop - called inline when nothing pauses and by the card-action authority when the text finishes, so a paused page and an unpaused one cannot produce different orders. `EffectDefinitionRejectionReason.CardTextCannotPauseInsideAnAttack` is deleted and `AroundActionShapeIsMalformed` replaces it, refusing a malformed around-action declaration at content load rather than mid-turn. **Schema v25**, with v24 rejected rather than migrated and the decision recorded at the constant and in the corpus. Four things a v24 payload cannot describe: the outer pre-action continuation - a frame standing outside an action that has not started, with the exact validated offer it will start from - the side of a primary action the actor placed that frame's work on, the before-action triggers a turn has already spent, and the space an Attack's target stood on before p.14's consequences. Three of the four have a reading available for their absence that is true of every v24 payload; the target space does not, because every v24 Attack had one and simply did not record it, and recovering it would mean reading a piece off the restored board when the whole reason the field exists is that step 6 may already have taken that piece off. The pre-action block is written *beside* the card-action discriminator rather than as one of its kinds, for a sharper version of the reason 6.9's Lane-Push request is: work placed after a Movement is parked while that Movement is mid-route, so a payload holding two continuations at once is the ordinary case. **Byte delta measured, not assumed**, in a throwaway `git worktree` at pre-change revision `48b1f1a9`: `verified-hand-setup` 6013 -> 6061 bytes, exactly +48 for the two new capability registrations (`set-attack-target` at 22 bytes and `around-primary-action` at 26, each a length-prefixed id plus a four-byte revision); `paused-card-text` 6883 -> 6945, exactly +62, being those 48 plus 4 for the turn's consumed-trigger count, 1 for the absent pre-action flag, and 9 for the pre-consequence target space this payload does carry. All fourteen corpus fixtures are renamed `-v25` and re-pinned, and a fifteenth, `parked-pre-action-v25`, is added for the reason 8.6, 8.10 and 8.11 each added one: it is the only payload holding a pre-action continuation, and the only one holding two continuations at once. `CardAggregateInvarianceTests` re-baselines both halves; the *event* half moves because one payload genuinely changed - p.14 announces the Attack Total at step 2, and step 1's text now stands between the target's validation and that announcement, so `AttackTargetValidated` reports no total where it used to report one the page had not announced yet. No event moved position, none was added or dropped, and the three equality groups hold. **Synthetic evidence, on content that is nobody's card**, which is what milestone 7.9's deferral gate requires before a verified face names a seam: four shapes join the showcase hands - an Arien-shaped Before-Attack move that stops p.14 twice at step 1, a Brogan-shaped charge whose text names the unit the Attack takes, a Tigerclaw-shaped defender rider at step 4, and an Xargatha-shaped primary Movement whose text the actor places either side of - plus the first Ultimate declaring a before-action trigger. `ResumableContinuationTests` drives all five through a real `MatchSession`: the page stopping before the Attack Total and resuming on the answer, a second question inside the same moment, byte-identical restore at the step-1 pause with the restored frame answering the same question, a stale answer refused with nothing mutated, an Attack that opens no target choice and binds one through its text, the pre-consequence space surviving a round trip, the defender's own rider stopping the page on the defender's side, the ordering question asked before the Movement's allowance is spent, both placements, a parked continuation captured and restored while the Movement is mid-route, and the trigger resuming the exact validated offer across a snapshot with its once-per-turn record intact. **Restore was extended where it was not enough**: a card-text pause now restores the attack underneath it at the two moments that attack is still in flight, which no fixture had needed before because the corpus only ever re-captured such a payload rather than continuing it. **Validation** T2 per `Testing/POLICY.md`: cross-platform harness 1378/1378 (baseline 1365 plus twelve continuation journeys and one around-action shape rule; the retired 8.5 rule's case is replaced one-for-one by its opposite); the Windows Edit Mode tier is OWED, not green - a Unity Editor launched by the user's own `PlayCurrentVersion.bat` held the project for the whole milestone and the validation launcher refuses a second one, so it is recorded as owed rather than claimed; living-document validator 127/127 self-tests across 65 governed active leaves, 15 production components and 295 archived bodies with 0 warnings; `git diff --check` clean. Play Mode was not run: this milestone changes no view, no scene and no HUD control, and every new pause reaches the player through the same `PendingEffectChoice` projection every existing one does. | `751ce110` |

## Milestone 8.20 Complete and verify additional pitch hero slot A - Arien completion evidence

| ID | Outcome | Commit |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 8.20 | **This build deals two heroes.** Arien the Tidemaster's nineteen printed faces ship as `VerifiedOfficial` definition content at revision `arien-8-20-rev-1`, transcribed from the inventory milestone 8.18a verified, and the staged one-hero-per-team match now deals Wasp on Red and Arien on Blue where it dealt one printed set twice. Sixteen printed primaries compose; Ceremonial Blade ships as data with its behaviour deferred under the same 2026-08-21 decision that defers Wasp's Crackling Dagger, and Aspiring Duelist carries no effect root because its whole printed text is the minion-modifier suppression its action declares. **The block's prerequisite line was optimistic and this milestone says how.** Three printed sentences needed vocabulary no 8.19 slice built: Spell Break's "except on the gold cards" is a carve-out about the card an action is performed from, which milestone 8.7's `RestrictedActorScope` cannot express because it names actor classes, so `RestrictedSourceCardScope` states it and `ActionRestrictionQuery` now takes the source card's colour as a required argument rather than a defaulted one; the Duelists' "except this attacker" is a hero picked out by the moment the card was discarded to defend, so `ActiveEffectBoundActorSource` declares where an occurrence looks and `ActiveEffectInstance.BoundActorHeroId` carries whom it found; and Slippery Ground's "cannot ... move more than 1 space with a movement action" is a cap rather than a prohibition or a delta, so `RuleModificationKind.ActivityLimit` is an eighth family and `MovementActionStartRestriction.Tightest` composes it with the existing evaluator seam into the one answer the backend holds. Four more followed the same rule: `PlaceSpawnPointExclusion` for the three Green Place cards' spawn-point clauses, a printed-*primary* Defense carrying a value for p.14's own "Primary Defense Value" shape - which also moved `ActionOfferQuery`'s reaction-window test from the Block resolution to the action kind, a strict generalisation that leaves Wasp's three Green cards exactly where 8.11 put them - a Movement primary carrying p.9's one icon for Deluge, and `CardTextTiming.BeforeTheMovement` for p.11's stated default, "Unless it says otherwise, the card text comes into effect before movement", which the model could previously express only as milestone 8.19a's *exception*. **Snapshot schema advances v27 to v28 and rejects v27**, for the bound actor alone: which hero "this attacker" named was decided at p.14's step 4 by an Attack that has ended by the time anything asks, so it cannot be re-derived, and reading the absent field as "bound nobody" is true of a definition that binds nobody and false of a live Master Duelist - which would restore as immunity against the attacker too, a stronger card than the printed one. All fifteen corpus fixtures are renamed `-v28` and re-pinned at a measured uniform **+276 bytes**, which is exactly ten new capability registrations: 226 UTF-8 id bytes plus ten one-byte length prefixes plus ten four-byte versions, checkable against 8.19c1's own figures (`verified-hand-setup` 6336 to 6612, `ended-match` 7722 to 7998). **Milestone 8.19b's roster vocabulary reaches the build's catalog for the first time here**, because milestone 7.9's deferral gate refuses a registration no printed card can start: `choose-target@2`, `behind-target-line@1` and this milestone's own `spaces-adjacent@1` are registered through a named `RegisteredHandlers` subset and the remaining thirteen keep waiting for the hero whose face names them. All three are also proved on showcase content that is nobody's card, so no reusable contract arrives for the first time inside a verified hero's slice. Two capabilities grew an output a node must ask for - the roster selection publishes the chosen piece's space and the printed-icon minion selection publishes the chosen minion's - and `EffectCapabilityContext.DeclaredOutputs` is what makes "a node that does not declare it never sees it" true rather than aspirational: a handler publishing a fact its node did not declare writes a snapshot the build refuses to read back, which is how that rule was found. The roster selection's `distance` parameter is renamed `reach` in the same edit, because `OptionalDistanceConformanceTests` requires every capability declaring a *distance* to read p.10/p.11's optionality through one statement and a selection radius has no such question. **On the map:** `Tools/Prepare-WaspHeroTokens.ps1` is generalised to `Tools/Prepare-HeroTokens.ps1 -Hero <wasp\|arien>` with the source element declared per hero rather than searched for - the corpus names Arien's element `Arien2`, which a name match would have missed - and it measured both of his faces exactly as it measured Wasp's: `R238 G131 B27` is Steam/orange to Red and `R126 G188 B213` is Crystal/light-blue to Blue, both written to the ignored `Assets/Resources/Private/Heroes/`. It also fixes a latent defect in the 8.11b script that nothing had run into: `Get-PngSize` shifted `[byte]` operands, which PowerShell truncates back into a byte, so every 302x302 token measured 46x46 and the `-Check` path could never pass. `QuickGameSetupConfiguration.CreateStagedWaspVersusArienOneVsOne` binds the `arien` content identity to the Blue participant at `blue-hero-01` - the same identity `HeroTokenArtwork` addresses the prepared face by - and the scene composition root pairs each card content with the board that agrees with it, so no mirror board can draw Wasp's face on a hero holding Arien's cards. **Evidence: complete Edit Mode 1700 passed / 0 failed / 0 skipped / 0 inconclusive, complete Play Mode 67 passed / 0 failed / 0 skipped / 0 inconclusive**, both unfiltered at the final revision. The cross-platform harness ran 1453/1453 at session start and then went down mid-milestone under Windows Application Control (`0x800711C7`) for the third milestone running, with `dotnet build` succeeding throughout, so the Windows tier is this milestone's evidence rather than its second opinion. Twelve of the Edit Mode delta are the new `ArienVerifiedContentTests`, whose printed-value assertion is a literal table in `HERO_ARIEN.md`'s own column order so that a drifted number fails against the source rather than against itself. Play Mode moved 66 to 67 in the baseline row and its composition changed rather than grew: four cases that had named Wasp cards for both seats now read what they need off the hand - the cross-team Initiative tie is produced by two heroes who each print an 8 rather than by one card dealt twice, which is how a tie arises at a real table - and one scenario whose subject genuinely is the mirror, the pointer-driven Attack ending on p.14's Block beside numeric Defense choices, names `SceneCardContent.VerifiedMirror` explicitly, because Arien prints no Block at all. **What this milestone does not cover, stated rather than implied:** the permanent hero player-path gate is milestone 8.20a's under the same-day split amendment, so Arien's faces are proved at the engine tier and through the configured projection but not yet through nineteen guarded real-pointer journeys; the scene arms those journeys will load are wired here (`VerifiedEngagedStagedArien` and four progressed stagings) and are so far unread by any fixture. | `6792626` |

## Milestone 8.20a Pass the permanent hero player-path gate for Arien completion evidence

| ID | Outcome | Commit |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 8.20a | **Arien passes the permanent hero player-path completion gate, and passing it required fixing five defects in the content milestone 8.20 shipped.** The gate's mechanical completeness matrix is `HeroPlayerPathMatrix` plus `ArienPlayerPathGateTests`: nineteen face rows and four component rows, where a row's declared interaction shapes are *derived from the printed content* - action kind, origin, printed range, card-text timing, effect-node kind, failure policy, capability identity, progression profile, and the Ultimate's own triggered effect - and compared against the declaration, so a row can claim neither less nor more than its face asks for. A shape cannot be declared without naming the journey that drives it, because a row's shapes *are* its evidence: `PlayerPathRow.Prove(shape, journey)` pairs them and the guard resolves every named journey against the assembly's `[UnityTest]` methods, so a renamed or missing journey fails rather than leaving the matrix pointing at nothing. Ten guard cases hold face completeness, shape derivation, journey existence, journey-orphan detection, the required component shapes, the two declared absences, and the Edit Mode scenarios every row links. **Eighteen configured Play Mode journeys** drive Arien through the input route a player uses - live UI Toolkit pointer pairs on panel-picked controls, and camera screen-point rays after the HUD's own pointer-blocking answer - across the default spawn scene, the engaged staging and four p.15 progressed stagings: commit and reveal, the hero token's spawn/identity/pointer selection/Movement/Defeat/respawn and presentation rebuild, Spell Break's radius lock read as the *enemy's own greyed Skill control on their activation inside the same turn*, Noble Blade's before-attack `may` through confirm/board unit/board space, that same pause answered again after a retained-prefix Undo and after a snapshot restore, Dangerous Current's behind-target line and its discard on the chosen hero's own card face, Aspiring Duelist offered as p.14's Primary Defense Value beside numeric secondaries and spent by clicking its whole face, Liquid Leap's Place onto a lit space, Rogue Wave's ranged Attack and after-attack push with its own distance controls, Violent Torrent's repeat declined on its control, Slippery Ground's text applied before the walk and the enemy's Movement then capped to one space with no Fast Travel, Expert Duelist's `This turn` immunity read on the tray, Master Duelist's `This round` immunity still read on a later turn of the same round, Arcane Whirlpool's swap with a clicked minion, Ebb and Flow's conditional repeat, and Living Tsunami drawn on the rail with its before-Attack step offered as lit spaces and a decline. **Five defects, each found only because a player's own clicks were driven.** Three were one family: milestone 8.20 introduced p.14's Primary Defense Value and widened the single predicate deciding where such an action may be offered, leaving three siblings of that question on the older, narrower one - so `ActionOfferQuery.ToInvocationContext` stamped the offer `PrintedPrimary` while the window offered it in reaction and `PendingDefenseResponseChoice` threw; `PendingDefensePreviewProjection` required every numeric Defense to compose the shared minion contributions, which "Ignore all minion defense modifiers" composes none of; and `CompletedDefenseMatches` re-derived with "a Block is printed primary, every other Defense is a reaction". Together they meant **attacking Arien never asked p.14's question at all** - the click that took him as a target was accepted by the projection, refused by the authority, and fell through to pointing the tray at him, so the board looked as though the click had missed. All four askers now share `ActionOfferQuery.IsPrintedPrimaryDefense`, and the preview carries `SuppressesMinionContributions` from the offer's own declaration because an absent minion modifier has two meanings and only the card tells them apart. The fourth: `DescribePendingEffectChoice` looked in the triggered-effect catalog only when the *triggered* backend held the pause, so Living Tsunami - an Ultimate whose trigger stops the game as a pre-action frame while its effect lives in that catalog - described no candidates and reached the player as four anonymous `OPTION #n` controls instead of four lit spaces. The fifth, at the user's direction ("if it says may we need to be able to just decline it"): a printed `may` had no decline at all, so the Ultimate's optional step was mandatory. A `May` node's selection now carries a floor of zero and `DeclineInvocation` is *derived from that floor* rather than persisted, so a restored, replayed or undone pause offers exactly what the live one did and no schema field moves. It is keyed on `May` alone and on the node's own question, because p.18's batch push already sets its own zero floor for `up to` and is the one node in verified content that asks twice - the `up to` is over the units and the distance is exact - which `ConfiguredKineticSkillTests.KineticBlastsDistance_KeepsItsOwnFloorAndCannotBeClosedWithNothingNamed` now holds permanently. **Closing checks, each observed failing then passing:** `PromptReachabilityConformanceTests` gains `EveryLegalAttackTargetOffer_IsAcceptedWhenItIsChosen` and `EveryLegalDefenseOffer_IsAcceptedWhenItIsChosen`, each run over both the Wasp mirror and the staged Wasp-versus-Arien configuration on a board no fixture had - a Blue melee minion beside the attacker *and* the Blue hero adjacent to him, so one printed non-ranged Attack offers a hero and a minion at once. The mirror arm is green before and after every fix, which is what makes each one a generalisation rather than a change to Wasp's behaviour. **Evidence: complete Edit Mode 1713 passed / 0 failed / 0 skipped / 0 inconclusive and complete Play Mode 95 passed / 0 failed / 0 skipped / 0 inconclusive**, both unfiltered at the final revision, plus one non-batch Game View pass of three journeys at 2560x1440 whose captures show the Primary Defense Value drawn beside two numeric offers, the enemy's greyed Skill control beside a live Secondary Movement with Arien's rail slot wearing the Active outline, and Living Tsunami's four lit spaces beside `CONFIRM CHOICE` and `DECLINE`. Schema stays **v28**: nothing this milestone adds reaches the wire, and no digest fixture moved. **One gate row is a recorded debt rather than a gap:** p.16's item is gained only through p.15's Level Up, which no composed scene stages and no journey can earn its way to, so `arien-items` carries its reason and a guard holds that reason to Arien actually printing item icons. | `d54cdf5e` |

`HeroPiece_StandsAtTheRedSpawnAsWaspAndSurvivesAPresentationRebuild` proves the token's content identity, its prepared official face, its configured Red spawn, the eighteen cards that make it this hero and a `RefreshAll` that returns one piece per hero with the tray still pointed where the player left it; and `ShocksAfterAttackPause_IsAnsweredAgainAfterAnUndoAndAfterARestore` walks her p.14 step-7 rider back through **two** retained-prefix Undos and forward again through a snapshot restore answered on a real printed face. **The two coin flips are the same class of latent flake milestone 8.20 closed in four other cases**: `Scene_PointsTheTrayAtTheOtherHeroThroughAMapClickTheHudDoesNotBlock` clicked whichever seat the tray was not on, which has been Arien half the time since the staged match stopped being a mirror, and `Scene_MovesByTheSecondaryMovementThroughRealControlsAndABoardClick` walked whichever hero the tray opened on; both now name Wasp's seat by her content identity, and the second also asserts that her drawn token followed the authoritative move. **Three machinery refinements, each derived from content and each leaving every Arien row byte-identical.** p.14's Block became `BlockDefenseOffer` beside `PrimaryDefenseOffer`, because the two are one input and two transitions - a Value is compared and a Block succeeds outright or leaves its hero defeated - and a later hero printing a Block would otherwise have inherited a journey in which nobody was ever defeated by answering. And `OpponentRestrictionReading` narrowed to restrictions whose actor scope is unqualified - the ones an enemy meets as their own greyed control, which is all three of Arien's - because a restriction naming its actors is about what may be done *to* a unit and both consumers deliberately filter such a piece out of the offering hero's candidates rather than drawing a control for it. Wasp's Magnetic Dagger is this build's only content of that shape, its enemy-facing half is owned authoritatively by `ConfiguredBasicCardTests`, and **the absence of any cue for a piece the pin has removed from an enemy's candidates is recorded as an observation rather than changed inside a gate milestone**. Eight capability identities Wasp's content is the first to name were classified in the shared table - the four printed-icon selections, the was-adjacent hero, the written-radius Place and the minion Defeat - which the guard requires, because an unclassified capability would otherwise contribute no shape and be proved by nothing. **A seam property worth not re-deriving: one board click on an effect candidate is two accepted commands**, the selection and the confirm behind it, so the retained prefix walks back through both and a journey asserting "the Undo drops the board answer" is off by one until it presses twice. `WaspHeroPieceLifecycleTests` is the gate's authoritative half, five Unity-independent cases over the staged Wasp-versus-Arien seat for identity and over the verified mirror for Defeat, restore, replay, Undo and restart - the mirror because the hero being defeated has to be a Wasp. **Evidence: complete Edit Mode 1718 passed / 0 failed / 0 skipped / 0 inconclusive and complete Play Mode 108 passed / 0 failed / 0 skipped / 0 inconclusive**, both unfiltered at the final revision, plus a targeted non-batch Game View pass of the three new journeys whose captures show the p.14 Block drawn beside three numeric Defense faces in one prompt, Shock's four discard faces each named as the card it would give up, and Wasp's own token on its Red spawn with her five starting faces in the tray. **A second observation came out of that middle capture and is recorded rather than changed here:** an effect choice's controls are appended to the shared contextual row `BuildActionRow` labels ACTIONS, beneath a round status line still reading "choose an action or End Turn", so a player being asked which card to discard is labelled as though they were picking an action. ## Milestone 8.20c Standardize every hero card footprint completion evidence

| ID | Outcome | Commit |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 8.20c | **Every complete hero-card face now has one shared fixed outer size, and the permanent hero player-path completion gate measures every newly configured hero's faces against it.** `CardFootprint` states the contract - 186 by 420 - and `CardFootprint.Apply` is the one way a surface takes it, pinning width and height at minimum and maximum alike with no flex growth or shrink, because a size alone is a preference a container can overrule and containers had been overruling it. The five full-card surfaces now all take it: the ordinary tray, p.14's Defense response, card-valued effect and discard choices, p.15's Upgrade a Card, and the hero rail's complete-face preview. **Only the width had ever been shared, and the pre-fix measurement is the defect stated as data.** Taken on the live panel at the validated 1920x1080 viewport before any production change, the thirty-eight verified faces the two configured heroes print resolved between **344 and 401** pixels tall - Wasp's Static Barrier the tallest at 401, Arien's Violent Torrent at 364, and twenty of the thirty-eight at the old 344 floor - because `DirectorHandsUnityView` gave a tray card a `minHeight` floor and let the row stretch all five to the tallest one's *content*, the Defense and effect prompts set `flexGrow = 1` on the face to fill their own frame, and the preview took whatever its words needed. The same run's third failure is the consequence a player met: `A card in Wasp's own hand is 401 tall rather than the standard footprint` - a whole hand stretched to one card's sentence, so pointing the tray at the other hero resized every card on screen. `CardFootprint.Height` is 420, that measured ceiling with room above it, and it is a stated constant rather than a derived maximum precisely so that the next hero's longest sentence cannot silently resize every card already shipped. **The gate's extension is structural rather than a remembered step:** `PlayerInteractionShape.StandardCardFootprintConformance` joins `HeroPlayerPathMatrix.RequiredComponentShapes`, so every hero's matrix must carry a component row for it and every such row must name a `[UnityTest]` that exists - a new hero cannot complete the gate without one, exactly as it cannot without a spawn or a restore row. The measurement behind it, `StandardCardFootprintCheck`, is hero-neutral and enumerates the hero's own content, so the row naming it cannot narrow what is measured: it stages each verified face on the live panel one at a time, and holds the resolved box to the footprint exactly, the five bands to being present, in order and non-overlapping, every drawn label to lying inside the card, and every label to being at least as tall as `MeasureTextSize` says its text needs at the width it was given - which is the check that catches the failure mode a fixed height introduces, since the card's frame is `overflow: hidden` and a clipped label still reports the whole text it was asked to draw. Failures are collected and reported together, which is why one pre-fix run produced the whole 344-to-401 table instead of one card's number. **Closing checks, each observed failing before the correction and passing after.** Edit Mode `MatchHudPresentationTests.EveryFullCardSurface_DrawsItsFaceAtTheStandardFootprint`, `NoCardStateOrContent_ChangesTheStandardFootprint` and `AtAShortViewport_TheRegionScrollsAndTheCardKeepsItsFootprint` failed 3/3 at the pre-fix revision, the first two reporting a declared height of `0.0f` where the contract asks 420 - a tray card had no height at all, only a floor - and passed 3/3 after. Play Mode `Scene_DrawsEveryWaspFaceAtTheStandardCardFootprint`, `Scene_DrawsEveryArienFaceAtTheStandardCardFootprint` and `Scene_KeepsEveryTrayCardTheSameSizeWhenTheMapPointerSwitchesHeroes` failed 3/3 at that same revision with the measurements above, and passed after. `CardFootprint` itself was added inert at the pre-fix revision - declared and consulted by nothing - so the checks could compile and fail honestly against the shipped surfaces, which is this project's recorded pattern for a closing check that needs new vocabulary. **The state matrix is Edit Mode's and the live layout is Play Mode's, deliberately.** Edit Mode holds every printed face of both heroes and each of the five treatments a card wears - untouched, picked up and committed facedown, revealed and resolving, spent and discarded, and sourcing a live Active effect - each taken from a real position rather than a hand-built view model, because a state no match can reach is not a promise this HUD owes. Play Mode holds what only a live panel answers: each hero's whole set measured on the panel, the map-pointer switch between the two heroes with the tray row and region proved not to move under them, selection and focus proved not to change a card's box, and a short supported 1280x720 viewport where the region's own scroll view holds more content than it can show and the last card in the row is still reachable and pressable after scrolling to it. **Two observations, recorded rather than changed, both outside this milestone's non-goals.** `ConfiguredMatchHudRenderer.Layout` applies the current viewport to every absolutely placed region on a *projection refresh* rather than every frame, so a player resizing the window mid-turn keeps the previous geometry until something refreshes - found while writing the short-viewport journey, which therefore asks for the same `RefreshAll` the presentation-rebuild journey uses. And the standard footprint costs a little more scrolling: an ordinary turn's tray at the validated viewport now stands a 420-pixel card where it used to stand whatever that hand needed, so the region scrolls slightly more often than it did - the bounded scroll is the sanctioned fallback and the trade a shared footprint is paid with, and `CardPanelTallViewportShare` was calibrated when a card was 344, which a later presentation slice could revisit. **Evidence: complete Edit Mode 1721 passed / 0 failed / 0 skipped / 0 inconclusive and complete Play Mode 112 passed / 0 failed / 0 skipped / 0 inconclusive**, both unfiltered at the final revision, plus a targeted non-batch Game View pass of all four footprint journeys, 4/4, whose three captures show Wasp's five faces and Arien's five drawn in the same box at the same size with every sentence whole, and the 1280x720 short viewport drawing those same five cards at that same size behind the tray region's own vertical scrollbar. Schema stays **v28** and no digest fixture moved, because nothing here reaches the wire; no rule, command, event, card or projection shape changed, and `Assets/GuardsDigital` outside `Presentation/Match` is untouched. `DirectorHandsUnityView.CardWidth` and its `CardMinimumHeight` floor are gone rather than forwarded, so the contract has one source and a surface cannot take half of it. | `56fac98` |
