| 8.20d | **Whatever the match is asking, the status line now says which decision it is and the controls that answer it arrive under a heading of their own.** The status derivation ran two phase checks and six pending-family branches and had no `PendingEffectChoice` branch at all, so every effect choice since milestone 8.15 - rulebook p.10's discard chooser above all - fell through to "Initiative N: choose an action or End Turn" while its controls were appended to the row `BuildActionRow` labels `ACTIONS`. Milestone 8.20b's Wasp journey met that and recorded it rather than fixing it, because one branch plus the heading corrects every effect choice at once. **`EffectChoicePromptLabels` is the one place a standing effect choice says what it is**, composing every word from the pause's own typed facts - its kind, its bounds, its candidate count, how many are selected, and whether it projected a decline or milestone 6.5ae's stop - and decoding no option identity, resolution ID, node name or printed sentence, which is the rule `EffectChoiceLabels` has followed since milestone 6.8a. `EffectChoiceSubject` is the one noun the offered candidates are all true of, read from the candidate kinds plus milestone 8.11c's occupancy preview, which is the only thing separating a candidate naming a piece from one naming an empty space: `CARD CHOICE`, `NUMBER CHOICE`, `TARGET CHOICE`, `SPACE CHOICE`, `MODE CHOICE`, `OPTIONAL STEP`, and `CHOICE` where a mixed set makes no narrower noun true. `EffectChoicePromptViewModel` carries the subject and that heading, and the model refuses to carry either control list without it, so a decision's controls and the words naming them arrive together or not at all. **The closing check is structural rather than a written-down list.** Milestone 7.9c left `HasPendingDecision` as one list because two had drifted by one entry; that list now reads nothing of its own and is exactly two halves - `IsAwaitingAnAnsweredDecision`, the seven families a player answers, and `IsInsideAnUnfinishedResolution`, the three frames that ask nothing themselves - and `StatusDerivationConformanceTests` compares the first half against the status derivation by what the compiled code reads, through milestone 6.1k's IL scanner, so both sides come from the type system and a rename moves them together. Three further cases hold the split honest: the pause list may read no projection member directly, the two halves must be disjoint so a new family cannot be excused by calling it a frame, and no scanned instruction in any of the four derivations may be unresolved. **Observed failing at the pre-fix state and passing after**: with the split present but inert and the derivation branch withdrawn, the rule failed with `Expected: <empty> / But was: < "get_PendingEffectChoice" >` at 3 passed / 1 failed, and the same fixture is 4/4 with the branch restored. The split is the typed vocabulary the check needed to compile and was added inert, exactly as milestones 6.2c, 6.5ad and 8.28b added theirs. **`EffectChoiceStatusNamingTests` covers every shape the configured roster reaches**, each driven to a real pause through a real `MatchSession`: Shock's rider for p.10's discard chooser (`Choose 1 of 4 cards.` under `CARD CHOICE`, four whole printed faces), Kinetic Blast for the printed-number choice (`Choose 1 of 2 printed numbers.` under `NUMBER CHOICE`, controls reading `3` and `4`), Lift Up's `May repeat once` for the confirm-or-decline (`An optional step is offered: confirm it or decline.` under `OPTIONAL STEP`), and Shock's own first question for a pause milestone 8.11a answers entirely on the board and which therefore draws no control at all - where the status line is the whole of what tells a player the match is waiting on them. A sweep plays the engaged verified mirror through `DeterministicMatchLoop` and checks every effect choice it meets, so the claim is over the content rather than over four chosen cases; the composer's own arithmetic is pinned separately for exact, `up to`, ranged and refused-every-mode bounds. **Evidence: complete Edit Mode 1737 passed / 0 failed / 0 skipped / 0 inconclusive and complete Play Mode 112 passed / 0 failed / 0 skipped / 0 inconclusive**, both unfiltered at the final revision, plus a targeted non-batch Play Mode pass of `WaspEngagedUiJourneyPlayModeTests` at 2560x1440, 7/7, whose capture `tmp/8-20d-shock-discard-faces-under-their-own-card-choice-heading.png` shows `Choose 1 of 4 cards.` above four faces gutter-labelled `CARD CHOICE` with no action row drawn. The before-picture is milestone 8.20b's own `tmp/8-20b-shock-discard-faces-under-the-shared-action-row.png`. **Play Mode is unmoved at 112 rather than unrun**: no journey was added, and the one that recorded the defect now asserts the correction on the live HUD - the heading is drawn once and every offered face is inside the section it heads. **Schema stays v28 and no digest fixture moved**, because nothing here reaches the wire; no projection field was added, and every word is composed from facts the pause already carried. **One judgement is recorded rather than derived**: the new branch is read directly after the Defense response because that is the order the region itself draws in - a standing Defense response returns before any effect control is built - and the four families under it share the action row with the decision. | `f889f85` |

## Milestone 8.21 Complete and verify additional pitch hero slot B - Brogan completion evidence

| ID | Outcome | Commit |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 8.21 | **This build deals a third verified hero, and one of his cards is the first content that can stop the game on a decision only he can answer.** Brogan the Destroyer's nineteen printed faces ship as `VerifiedOfficial` definition content at revision `brogan-8-21-rev-1`, transcribed from the inventory milestone 8.18b verified; staged matches deal him the Blue seat at rulebook p.6's setup and at both p.15 stages, and every one of his eighteen dealt faces is resolved through the ordinary player pipeline. Leap Forward is the one face no player reaches: p.6 returns the Handicap card to the box and p.20 brings it back only for an odd player count. **The block's prerequisite line was optimistic for the third hero running, and this records how.** Eight pieces of vocabulary no 8.19 slice built: two Active-effect scopes - `MinionsInRadius`, because the Green trio says "any friendly **minion** in radius", and `ControllerAndUnitsInRadius`, because Bulwark says "**You** and friendly units in radius" while `RuleModificationQuery.UnitsInRadius` deliberately excludes its own controller; `DirectMoveDestinationPolicy.NamedSpace`, because "Move into the space it occupied" names a destination rather than narrowing a set, which makes the declared distance a budget and lets one declaration serve Bullrush's "2 or 3"; `SourceTeamRelation.OpposingSourceOrUnaligned`, because "an enemy unit or a token" qualifies one noun and not the other and a token stands on no side - `TargetQuery.StandsInRelationTo` ended in a ternary whose else-branch meant "allied" and is a switch now; a `playedAttackThisTurn` narrowing on `choose-target@2` that filters candidates rather than gating the consequence; and three minted contracts - `discard-owned-card@1` for the positive "if you do" fact a conditional can read where `unableToDiscard` would have needed inverting, `owned-card-present@1` for Throwing Spear's discard-pile condition, and `synthetic-range-unit-target@1`, because "target a unit in range" needed a unit-valued printed-Range selection where only space-, hero- and minion-valued ones existed. **The removal-prevention decision reaches the player, which is the user-approved amendment's core clause.** `RemovalPreventionPrompt` mints one typed command per affordable payment plus the decline the rules always allow, `TurnResolutionAuthority.DescribeRemovalPrevention` pairs the pause with its options in one breath as the effect choice already does, and `MatchSessionProjection` carries it. In presentation it is the eighth pending family: `RemovalPreventionPromptLabels` composes its words from typed facts alone, `FormatStatus` gains a branch read before the effect choice, `IsAwaitingAnAnsweredDecision` gains the family - the half `StatusDerivationConformanceTests` compares by IL - the tray hides while it stands, each payable card is drawn as its whole printed face by the tray's own standalone builder, and the miniature the price would keep is cued on the board rather than named in the prompt. **A second Blue seat, at the user's direction.** War Drummer and Master Skald print "A friendly hero in range gains N coins" and p.10 keeps the acting hero out of his own action, so with one hero per team those two faces were unreachable rather than merely untested; rather than debt them until 8.25, one staged composition deals Brogan beside Arien at `blue-hero-02` - a spawn the reviewed map has declared all along, two spaces from the first and inside both cards' printed Range 4. Composition generates a hero view and pointer selector for any seat the scene does not author and deactivates any view no configuration claims, so the match a player loads is visually identical and 8.25 inherits the mechanism it needs for six. **Schema v29, and its cause is new in kind: not a field, but the pinned registration list itself.** A payload records every registered capability id and revision and a restore compares that list for equality, so the twelve contracts Brogan's faces are the first to reach make every v28 payload name a strictly shorter list than this build registers; v28 is rejected rather than migrated on `RISK-COMPAT-001`'s standing ground, because accepting the shorter list would let a restored match resolve a card against contracts its own capture never had. Measured delta uniform **+329 bytes** on all fifteen corpus fixtures - 269 UTF-8 id bytes plus twelve length prefixes plus twelve four-byte versions - checkable against the previous milestone's own figures: `verified-hand-setup` 6612 -> 6941 and `ended-match` 7998 -> 8327. **THE PERMANENT PLAYER-PATH GATE FOUND THREE DEFECTS IN SHIPPED CODE, all of them in the band between what a tier composes and what a pointer meets.** (1) His three shove faces print "up to N spaces" and declared a zero distance floor beside a *mandatory* node policy, which `AdmittedDistances.ForDeclared` reads only under `UpTo` - so every push travelled its declared distance exactly and the printed choice was never offered; the fix makes the push node `UpTo`, which is what Arien's Rogue Wave already declares over the same capability with the same one-target maximum, and the gate's derived shape table is byte-identical either side of it, which is what says the derivation was right and the content was wrong. (2) `RoundActionPresenter.Submit` refuses any command it did not project and had no arm for the removal-prevention prompt, so the region drew payment faces and a decline that did nothing when clicked - invisible to the projection and presenter tiers alike, because both compose those controls correctly. (3) The decision could be opened at the effect-invoked altitude and never answered: a trigger of that shape runs after its own action has ended, so the actor's card was resolved and acknowledged and the next actor selected while the decision stood, and the second Initiative advance the answer performed was refused *after* the payment had been taken - which the aggregate reported as an atomicity violation and rolled back. The answer now advances only when nobody is selected. **A fourth was this milestone's own new code**: the generated hero view for the third staged seat was configured at radius zero and threw on every load of that scene. **A broader first fix is recorded because it was wrong**: withholding the advance inside `AdvanceInitiative` looked right and broke `DefeatRemovalStageTests.AStandingPreventionDecision_SurvivesASnapshotRoundTripByteIdentically`, whose synthetic fixture opens a decision no turn owns - that same method selects a turn's first actor. **EVIDENCE.** Complete Edit Mode **1766 passed / 0 failed / 0 skipped / 0 inconclusive** and complete Play Mode **140 / 0 / 0 / 0**, both unfiltered and both at the final revision, plus a targeted non-batch Play Mode pass of `BroganEngagedUiJourneyPlayModeTests` at 2560x1440, **8/8**, whose capture `tmp/8-21-shield-offers-to-keep-the-minion.png` is the amendment's own picture: the status line reads `A friendly minion would be removed: discard a silver card to keep it, or decline. 1 card can pay.`, Bulwark's whole printed face stands under a gutter labelled `KEEP THE MINION` beside `DECLINE - LET IT BE REMOVED`, the tray is not drawn at all, no End Turn is offered, and the miniature the price would keep wears its own ring on the board. Two further captures show Onslaught's token standing on the space it emptied and Brutal Jab's two distance controls. The **pre-fix observations are the runs themselves**: the first complete Play Mode run was 128/140 with twelve red, the second 137/140, the third 139/140 and the fourth 140/140, and each fix names the check it turned; the first complete Edit Mode run after the initiative fix was 1765/1766 with exactly the snapshot fixture above red, and 1766/1766 once the fix was narrowed to its own answer site. Twenty-eight of the Play Mode delta are new: seventeen journeys and eleven `BroganPlayerPathGateTests` cases over a nineteen-face, five-component matrix whose rows are derived from the printed content and compared exactly. Twenty-nine of the Edit Mode delta are new: `BroganVerifiedContentTests` (11), `BroganRemovalPreventionTests` (7), `BroganOneManArmyTests` (7), `BroganAlliedHeroCardTests` (3) and the removal-prevention case in `PromptReachabilityConformanceTests`. **Every journey was designed against a headless `MatchSession` probe before it was written** - turn orders, candidate lists and both engine defects were found offline in seconds each - which is why the sequencing lives in the fixtures rather than in a run log. The cross-platform harness stayed down under Windows Application Control (`0x800711C7`) with `dotnet build` succeeding throughout, so the Windows tier is this milestone's evidence rather than its second opinion. Validation: living-document validator green and `git diff --check` clean. `Docs/AI/Testing/PLAYMODE_AND_SUITES.md` crossed its 85% line with the mandatory baseline row and was cleared by routing milestones 8.15's and 8.15a's narratives to `Archive/TESTING_HISTORY.md`; `SESSION_BRIEF.md` was cleared by folding 8.20d into the grouped historical line and retiring the two amendment bullets whose milestones are now complete. **What this milestone does not cover, stated rather than implied:** milestone 8.21a's public coin display is scheduled and unbuilt, p.16's item track remains the one recorded debt on every hero's matrix because no composed scene stages a Level Up, and the six-player setup is still 8.25's. | `f838e151` |

## Milestone 8.21a Show every hero's public coin balance on the fixed side rails completion evidence

| ID | Outcome | Commit |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 8.21a | **Every hero rail now says what that hero has to spend, in space the rail was already reserving and could not use for anything else.** The count is `HeroProgressionProjection.Coins` read straight through - the field rulebook p.13's Defeat reward, p.8's Pity Coin, a card effect's grant and p.15's Level Up have all moved since milestone 7.5 - so there is no new projection field, no schema change and no presentation-owned accounting, and restore, replay and retained-prefix Undo redraw it with nothing of their own: each of those paths rebuilds the ledger it already preserved and the same number falls out. `HeroOverviewViewModel` carries `Coins` and three labels composed from it alone - `COINS`, the amount, and `3 coins`/`1 coin` for the hover and assistive reading - and the rail draws the word in muted ink because it never changes and the amount in primary ink because it is the fact. **Where it stands is the milestone's whole design question, and the answer is the one row in the rail whose content cannot grow.** The `RESOLVED TURNS` caption is a literal at a fixed size, so the space beside it is provably unused rather than unused until some position fills it; every emptier-looking row is not - the item strip is as wide as p.16 has made it, the discard row is as wide as the pile, and the header is as tall as the hero's own name and the current-card marker between them, and each of those grows on a real position a match can reach. The caption keeps its own margins and the count takes the same eight pixels above it while giving up the runtime theme's three around a label, so the row's margin box is exactly the caption's, which is exactly what the caption cost the rail before the row existed: no new row, no added height, no added width, and neither rail's outer box, the standing HUD regions nor the board's framing moved. The count sits inward of the caption on both rails, mirrored like every other row except the two milestone 7.8a deliberately does not mirror. **The pre-fix observation is the pre-fix working tree itself**, with the view model carrying the new members inert and the view still drawing a bare caption: the filtered Edit Mode pass of `MatchHudPresentationTests` ran 68 cases with exactly four red - the two structural cases threw `Sequence contains no matching element` looking for `gd-turn-slots-caption-row`, and the two reading cases reported `Expected: equivalent to < "0", "0" >` and `< "2", "0" >` against `But was: <empty>` - and all four pass at the fixed revision. **EVIDENCE.** Complete Edit Mode **1772 passed / 0 failed / 0 skipped / 0 inconclusive** and complete Play Mode **142 / 0 / 0 / 0**, both unfiltered, both batch and both re-run at the final revision after the last test edit, plus a targeted non-batch Play Mode pass of `WaspEngagedUiJourneyPlayModeTests` at 2560x1440, **8/8**, whose capture `tmp/8-21a-the-defeat-reward-on-the-red-rail.png` is this milestone's picture: the Red rail reads `RESOLVED TURNS  COINS 2` after a clicked Attack defeated the engaged minion, and the mirrored Blue rail reads `COINS 0  RESOLVED TURNS`. Six of the Edit Mode delta are new - five in `MatchHudPresentationTests` and one in `BroganAlliedHeroCardTests`, whose three-seat staging is the only place three rails are drawn at once and the only place a coin moves for a reason that is neither a Defeat nor an end of round, so a rail showing the acting hero's coins or the viewed hero's would pass on two seats and fail there. Two of the Play Mode delta are new: p.13's reward reaching one rail and no other through real tray, control and board clicks, and the count standing inside its rail and clear of every other row at the running viewport and at 1280x720. `HeroRailCoinCheck` is the shared geometry both journeys measure with, and `Describe(HeroOverviewsUnityView)` carries the balance so the existing reconstruction comparisons include it. **Two measurement facts a later rail change will meet again.** A label's *margin* box rather than its border box is what a child costs the column above it - the runtime theme gives a label three pixels above and below, and comparing border boxes reports the theme's own spacing as growth. And a rail's laid-out height legitimately moves by two pixels across a played turn, because the rail wears a two-pixel top border while the tray is showing its hero and the tray follows whoever the match is waiting on, so the journey compares rail boxes net of that marker. The permanent hero player-path gate gained `HeroCoinBalanceReading` and a `wasp-coin-balance` component row naming both journeys; it is deliberately not in `RequiredComponentShapes`, because one shared builder draws every rail from one repeated view model and the gate's own sentence permits skipping permutations that change no input, presentation or transition shape. The cross-platform harness stayed down under Windows Application Control (`0x800711C7`), so the Windows tier is this milestone's evidence rather than its second opinion. Validation: living-document validator green and `git diff --check` clean. **What this milestone does not cover, stated rather than implied:** the coin *ledger* is untouched - no new source, sink or amount - p.16's item track remains the one recorded debt on every hero's matrix, and Arien's and Brogan's gates carry no coin row because the rail they would prove is the same builder drawing the same row. | `2f1b8911` |

## Milestone 8.22 Complete and verify additional pitch hero slot C - Sabina completion evidence

| ID | Outcome | Commit |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 8.22 | **This build deals a fourth verified hero, and closing her found four defects in shipped code that no earlier hero's content could reach.** Sabina the Gunslinger's nineteen printed faces ship as `VerifiedOfficial` definition content at revision `sabina-8-22-rev-1`, transcribed from the inventory milestone 8.18c verified; staged matches deal her the Blue seat at rulebook p.6's setup and at both p.15 stages on both sibling branches, and every one of her eighteen dealt faces is resolved through the ordinary player pipeline. Bang is the one face no player reaches: p.6 returns the Handicap card to the box and p.20 brings it back only for an odd player count. **S-01 was answered by user decision on 2026-08-25** in favour of the rulebook's setup-step-7 epithet, and it governs the display label alone - the content identity stays `sabina`, which is what the prepared token is addressed by and what every rule that asks reads, and `VerifiedContentIsolationConformanceTests` still forbids any rules or application type to spell either name. **Three of her nineteen faces compose no card text at all, and that is the content rather than a gap:** Quickdraw, Gunslinger and Dead Shot print a conditional addition to their own Attack value, which p.14 marks with the `+` on the number itself, so they declare a `RosterValueContributionDefinition` on the action and nothing else - a value is not a step, and composing one would invent a moment the page does not have. **She also prints no Active effect anywhere**, the first verified hero of whom that is true, so there is no `SabinaActiveEffectContent` beside her effect file and no reading shape any face of hers introduces. **Six pieces of vocabulary no 8.19 slice built**, which is the fourth hero running whose prerequisite line was short: `DirectMoveDestinationPolicy.WithinActorRadius` and an optional `actorRadius`, because Troop Movement, Marching Orders and Path to Victory print "in radius" twice and mean it twice - the first bounds who moves and the second bounds where, and `TowardActor` forbids a lateral shuffle p.19 allows while `EquidistantFromActor` forbids stepping inward; `target-is-adjacent@1`, because Point Blank Shot prints "if the target **is** adjacent" and runs at p.14's step 7, so it reads live occupancy where Shootout's "**was** adjacent" reads milestone 8.19b's pre-consequence answer - each face gets the answer its own tense asks for, and the two differ exactly when step 6 removed the target; `UltimateRuleDefinition.Modifications` as a list that may stand beside a trigger, because Big Sodding Gun is the first Ultimate printing two sentences and one `RuleModification` names one channel, so "+2 Range and +2 Attack" is two declarations sharing the Ultimate's own ordering sequence and source; `EffectRoleKind.OpposingHero` as an **input** role, because "that hero discards a card, or is defeated" names a hero the push already picked out rather than selecting one, threaded through `ResolveInputBindings`' three callers so a restored pause rebinds from the rebuilt context; `choose-target@2`'s optional `adjacentToFriendlyMinion`, because Close Support and Covering Fire measure two bounds from two different places - the disk from Sabina and the adjacency from each of her minions - which is asked from each candidate outwards rather than smuggled into the origin; and `presence-count@1`'s optional `minimum`, for "two or more friendly minions in radius". `choose-target-set@1` and `choose-target-pair@1` also publish space outputs when a node asks, the shape milestone 8.20 gave `choose-target@2`'s `chosenSpace`, because her selections name minions while the Swap and the Remove take coordinates. **The silent trap that cost the most is worth the next hero's attention:** a declared consumer on a minion-valued selection makes the printed card *unplayable* with no refusal to read, because `EffectSelectionDeclaration` hands the consumer each candidate and asks `Anticipate`, and a `Minion` candidate is refused by a consumer taking `Coordinates` - Back to Back and Troop Movement were both dead this way, and the definition validator accepts it. **Schema moved twice.** v30 is the pinned registration list again, the same cause as v29: the five contracts her faces are the first content to reach - milestone 8.19b's `choose-target-set`, `choose-target-pair`, `presence-count` and `pre-consequence-adjacency`, and the `target-is-adjacent` her present-tense rider needed minted - make every v29 payload name a strictly shorter list, at a measured +117 bytes uniform. v31 is a field instead: the rest of p.14, owed while a trigger set off by a Push inside an Attack's own card text is standing, which is an altitude that could not set a trigger off at all before this milestone. **Its corpus delta is the first non-uniform one in this build** - +1 byte on fourteen fixtures and zero on `verified-hand-setup` - because the marker rides the card-action section and `WriteCardAction` writes nothing for a payload holding no card-action authority; `ended-match` 8444 to 8445. Both superseded versions are rejected rather than migrated on `RISK-COMPAT-001`'s standing ground. **The four shipped-code defects, and the finding that unifies them.** An effect frame publishes three results on completion - applied pushes, p.8's lane-push cause, and requested hero Defeats - and each altitude carried a different subset: the Skill altitude carried pushes and cause, card text carried Defeats only. Sabina is the first hero whose content needs all three. `F-ATK-01`: `CreateOutcomeComparisonResolution`, the hero arm's rebuild after p.14's Defense, dropped milestone 8.19a's `TargetSpaceBeforeConsequences`, so against a **hero** target `attacked-target-space@1` was impossible and `pre-consequence-adjacency@1` answered no - Point Blank Shot pushed nobody and Shootout and Bullet Hell removed nothing, silently. `F-SKL-01`: a Skill that requested a hero Defeat changed nothing, because `AppendRequestedHeroDefeats` was card-text-only and `ResolveEffectInvokedHeroDefeat` threw without a `PendingAttackResolution`; Covering Fire is the first printed Skill to ask. It is fixed with an `EffectInvokedDefeatSource` carrying id, actor and **activity** - the activity matters, because p.18 judges immunity by it and a Skill's Defeat was being judged as an Attack - which also removes the fabricated `PendingAttackResolution` milestone 8.19c1's interrupt path had used to get past the same problem, and with it a second bug: that path returned early whenever no skill was standing, which is every card-text trigger. `F-ATK-02`: `ApplyCardText` never watched applied pushes, never ran the after-push triggers and never recorded p.8's cause, so Big Sodding Gun's second sentence was unreachable from her only push and a card-text Remove that emptied the Battle Zone recorded no Lane Push. The fix is in `ApplyCardText` **and** in `AnswerCardTextChoice`, because a text that pauses does its work on the resume pass; recording only at the start catches every face that asks nothing and misses every face that asks. A card-text trigger has no Skill resolution to interrupt - what it interrupts is the rest of p.14 - so a `PendingAttackCardTextRemainder` is written outside the continuation discriminator, `IsCardTextPageStopped` replaces the old gates in `TurnResolutionAuthority`, and the remainder is replayed through the same `StepsAfterCardText` generator. That remainder is v31's cause. `F-SNAP-01`: `ReadEffectInterrupt` rebuilt the trigger frame with no opposing hero, so a restored `OpposingHero` binding answered nobody; the interrupt's own `Subject` is that hero and is now passed in rather than persisted. **The permanent hero player-path gate is closed on her, and it needed no new rule.** `SabinaPlayerPathGateTests` carries one row per printed face in the shard's own order plus five hero-owned component rows, and ten guard cases hold the table to the content: every face has exactly one row, every row declares exactly the shapes `ShapesIntroducedBy` derives from `SabinaCardContent`, every reachable face names a journey and the one unreachable face names a reason the content itself confirms, every declared shape is proved by a `[UnityTest]` that exists, no journey in her three fixtures is unaccounted for, every required component shape has a row, the item row is held to her actually printing item icons, her content is held to naming no token or marker capability, and every authoritative-scenario link resolves in the Edit Mode assembly. **The only machinery this hero added is classification, not rules:** nine capability ids join `HeroPlayerPathMatrix.CapabilityShapes` - `choose-target-set` and `choose-target-pair` as board unit selections, and `presence-count`, `pre-consequence-adjacency`, `target-is-adjacent`, `synthetic-remove`, `synthetic-swap` and `synthetic-push-target` as readings and consequences that ask nobody anything - and an offline derivation over all four heroes printed every Wasp, Arien and Brogan row byte-identical afterwards, which is milestone 8.20b's test of whether an addition is a generalisation or a re-fit. **Sixteen journeys across three fixtures drive her through real pointer events**, and one structural fact about this build shaped all of them: a turn is one action, because the movement stop control and the end-turn control are the same command, so a face whose printed sentence needs her somewhere else spends a previous turn walking there. Every such walk, every enemy turn and every enemy Defense rides the headless command surface and is marked in the fixtures; every decision a journey is *about* is a click. The enemy's Defense is answered by reading the outcome off the breakdown the defender is actually shown rather than off a hard-coded number, so a change to minion contributions or to an Ultimate's standing rules moves the journey with the game. **Every journey was designed against a headless `MatchSession` probe before it was written**, on milestone 8.21's terms: turn orders, Initiative ties, candidate lists and the exact Defense face that leaves a hero standing were all settled offline in seconds each, and that evidence is kept in `tmp/8-22-sabina-journey-probe.txt`. **The gate found no defect in shipped code**, which is the second time it has not - Wasp's pass at milestone 8.20b was the first - and the reason is recorded rather than assumed: this milestone's own focused rule tests had already found and fixed all four defects above before a pointer ever met the content, which is what a 47-case authoritative tier is for. The three reds the first Play Mode run produced were all in the new journeys themselves, and two are worth carrying: an unqualified "press whatever confirm is standing" pressed the *next* node's control and accepted Troop Movement's printed repeat instead of leaving it to be answered, so `ConfirmIfStillStanding` now names the node it may confirm; and p.13 offers a respawn at the defeated hero's own next turn, so the card she commits for it has to be able to take that turn. **One acceptance clause is answered by absence, and it is stated rather than implied:** the block asks the up-to-two Remove to prove removal prevention, and it cannot reach it - p.19's Remove takes a piece off the board and collects no coins, milestone 8.19c1's decision hangs off a p.13 **Defeat**'s removal stage, and every guard in this build prints "when any friendly minion in radius **is defeated**". A Remove is not a Defeat, so no guard triggers on hers; the coinless fact itself is pinned by `SabinaAfterAttackRemovalTests.BulletHell_AppliesBothRemovalsInOrder_AndPaysNoCoinForEither`. **T3 totals on this tree: complete Edit Mode 1819/1819 and complete Play Mode 168/168, both in batch.** The Edit Mode baseline moves 1772 to 1819 and the whole delta is Sabina's own cases - her filtered run is exactly 47 - and the Play Mode baseline moves 142 to 168, of which sixteen are her journeys and ten her gate's guards. A non-batch pass ran the same 26 green and took five targeted Game View captures under `tmp/`. The cross-platform harness stayed down under Windows Application Control (`0x800711C7`), so the Windows tier is this milestone's evidence rather than its second opinion. Validation: living-document validator green and `git diff --check` clean. **What this milestone does not cover, stated rather than implied:** no face of hers repeats an Attack and no board her content can reach pushes a minion - a targeted enemy minion is defeated at p.14's step 6 and an immune Heavy is not a legal target while immune - so both absences are recorded rather than tested around; p.16's item track remains the one recorded debt on every hero's matrix; and S-04 stays partial, because whether the dashboard's `SABINA THE COMMANDER` indicates a different printing is still unknown. | `68dca677` |

## Milestone 8.23 Complete and verify additional pitch hero slot D - Tigerclaw completion evidence

| ID | Outcome | Commit |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 8.23 | **This build deals a fifth verified hero, and closing him found SEVEN defects in shipped code - the most any hero slice has surfaced.** Tigerclaw the Cutpurse's nineteen printed faces ship as `VerifiedOfficial` definition content at revision `tigerclaw-8-23-rev-1`, transcribed from the inventory milestone 8.18d verified; staged matches deal him the Blue seat at rulebook p.6's setup and at both p.15 stages, and every one of his eighteen dealt faces is resolved through the ordinary player pipeline. Nimble Strike is the one face no player reaches: p.6 returns the Handicap card to the box and p.20 brings it back only for an odd player count. **Four shared foundations, not one hero slice.** `PrintedBlockCondition` puts p.14's Block condition on the card as its own printed word, with both arms stated rather than one negated, because an action carrying neither classification is not an Attack at all; `MarkerPropertyDefinition` is `TokenPropertyDefinition`'s twin and the one difference is the whole reason it is separate - a token property affects the token's CONTROLLER and a marker property its HOLDER - with `RuleModificationQuery.Build` taking the marker register as a REQUIRED parameter across 32 call sites on milestone 6.8f's rule; p.10's Repeat becomes reachable from OUTSIDE the action it repeats, because Cloak and Daggers stands outside it, and p.10's "cannot repeat a repeated action" is enforced on the SUBJECT rather than the trigger, so a repeated basic Skill is still a basic Skill; and an action may print card text at BOTH of p.14's attacker moments, which is `ActionDefinition.SecondCardTextRoot` plus `CardTextAt(timing)` - the widening `CardTextTiming`'s own doc named as owed to the slice that first transcribed one. **Schema advances v31 to v32 to v33 and rejects both.** v32 is the held repeat subject, at a measured **+1 byte on thirteen fixtures and ZERO on `setup` and `verified-hand-setup`**, because a payload with no card-action authority writes no card-action section at all. v33 is TWO causes at once - a first for `RISK-COMPAT-001` - eight capability ids and two marker definitions in the pinned registration list, and the attacker's space at the start of the Attack that Combat Reflexes' step 7 asks about: **+270 uniform and +279 on the two fixtures holding a paused Attack**, and the arithmetic checks - 8 ids at 244 bytes, 2 markers at 26, and the extra nine are the boolean and the coordinate. **THE SEVEN DEFECTS, each reachable only from his content.** (1) A Move whose own text crosses obstacles: "Move 2 spaces in a straight line THROUGH an enemy unit" was refused by the ordinary reachability walk, so `synthetic-direct-move@3` gained an OPTIONAL `movesThroughObstacles` threaded as a REQUIRED parameter to `ObstacleQuery.BlocksTransit`, ORed with p.18's granted permission exactly as `PushPathQuery` has ORed `pushIgnoresObstacles` since 6.4 - transit only, because a crossing Move still may never rest on an obstacle. (2) An action printing both attacker moments could not resume its page: milestone 8.19a's remainder guard refused ANY card-text step after a step-1 pause and said in as many words that it was stating the rule so a future action declaring two would be caught rather than silently skipped; this is that action, and the refusal narrows to a second step 1. (3) The step-7 question could then be opened and never answered, because the frame lookup matched `ActionDefinition.EffectRoot` alone - now `EffectRootFor(reference)`. (4) A printed-Radius Place measured the printed RANGE: `PrintedReach` always called `RangeValueQuery`, correct while the printed arm was the Range Place and nothing else, so Blend Into Shadows measured zero and reported itself impossible at every space on the board - and its condition arm passed throughout, because the only case driving that face stands away from terrain where p.10 skips the branch. (5) A Defense text that MOVES the defender left p.14 unable to compare: the outcome resolver re-derived the attacker's legal targets and refused a target that had legitimately stepped away at step 4, rolling the whole command back as an engine fault; the prevalidation now asks for a target still STANDING rather than still reachable. (6) A Defense rider naming the attacker resolved to nobody, in two halves - the resume path supplied no opposing hero, so Parry and Riposte opened their discard and refused every answer to it, and the snapshot reader supplied none either, so the payload holding that standing question could not be read back at all; the reader now takes a RULE rather than an answer, because the wire names the attack only after the frame. (7) The Ultimate's repeat decision could not be answered at all: it fires after its own action has ended, so Initiative had advanced past it and both answers were rolled back as atomicity violations - which is milestone 8.21's removal-prevention finding on the other command that finishes a trigger of that shape. **The permanent player-path gate closes on him with nineteen journeys and eleven guards, and it needed one new interaction shape and no new rule.** `ResourceAmountChoice` is the shape: his three thieving Greens are the first printed content to move p.15's coins, and how many is a control the player presses. Six capability classifications joined the table, and the derivation printed every Wasp, Arien, Brogan and Sabina row byte-identical afterwards - milestone 8.20b's test for whether an addition is a generalisation or a re-fit - which was measured OFFLINE by a console probe compiling the real `HeroPlayerPathMatrix` before any Unity run. **Two facts the journeys had to discover and the next hero inherits.** No staged enemy hand holds a ranged Attack at any stage, so his three ranged-condition Blocks are met on the surface as an offer and as a step-4 rider and never as a success - which is exactly the transition milestone 8.20b split `BlockDefenseOffer` out to capture, and the Dodge journey proves it by being defeated for answering. And every Defense journey walks him to one particular space, because p.14 composes the minion contributions standing around a defender into their total and his printed values sit one short of the enemy's Attack Totals: Backstab's printed 5 answers Electrocute's 5 from `(-1, 2)` and is one short from anywhere else beside them. **One gate row is a recorded debt and milestone 8.23a is scheduled to close it.** He is the first verified hero to attach one of p.19's markers and this build draws no held marker anywhere - nothing in `Assets/GuardsDigital/Presentation` reads one, the HUD names an Active-effect source with `ACTIVE` and has no equivalent for an attachment - so Poisoned Dagger and Poisoned Dart lower a hero's Attack, Defense and Initiative with nothing on screen saying so. The user's decision was to record the debt and schedule the display rather than grow this milestone by a presentation slice, which is the shape milestone 8.21a already has. **His token is prepared from the private corpus and measured rather than assumed**: `Tools/Prepare-HeroTokens.ps1` gains `tigerclaw`, whose element sits beside Wasp's at 8597311 rather than beside the other heroes', and the script sampled both faces' rings as `R239 G130 B25` to Red and `R128 G188 B214` to Blue under the reviewed mapping. **EVIDENCE. Complete Unity Edit Mode 1872 passed / 0 failed / 0 skipped / 0 inconclusive and complete Unity Play Mode 198 passed / 0 failed / 0 skipped / 0 inconclusive**, both unfiltered and in batch at the final revision, plus the cross-platform harness at 1604/1604. Play Mode moves 168 to 198 and every one of the thirty is his - nineteen journeys and eleven gate guards. **Every fix was observed failing before it passed**: the crossing Move, the two p.14 moments and the printed-Radius Place through the harness; the three step-4 defects with the four production files stashed, reporting `SnapshotLoadException -> ArgumentException: A criteria owner must be a defined hero` and `'AnswerEffectChoice' was refused: EngineFault`; and the Ultimate's repeat through a filtered Edit Mode pass with the guard stashed, 19 cases with exactly one red reading `'AnswerEffectChoice' was refused: AtomicityViolation`. The five Play Mode runs went 21/30, 24, 26, 26 and 30, and four of their reds are reusable journey facts: a card-valued effect choice is a WHOLE FACE named `gd-effect-card` and never a labelled button, an object-valued candidate is answered through the PIECE where a space-valued one is answered through the space, the Director drawer relabels its own toggle when open so it is closed by label PREFIX, and a `Next Turn` Active effect is deliberately not reported as live while dormant so the reading a journey proves is at the hero's next turn. **The first complete Edit Mode run was 1870/1872**, both reds reading `Property Count was not found` - Unity's NUnit 3.5 cannot read `Has.Count` off an `IReadOnlyList<T>` though the harness's 3.14 accepts it. `StagedHeroBoard.NodeApplied` also learned to read both altitudes in this milestone: card text records `step=<moment>:<node>:Applied` and a Skill records `step=<node>` with the outcome in the event type, so a matcher that knew only the first answered "no" for every Skill node and four `Is.False` assertions were vacuous - all four are genuinely checked now and all four still hold. **What this milestone does not cover, stated rather than implied:** held markers are drawn nowhere, which is milestone 8.23a's; p.16's item track remains the one recorded debt on every hero's matrix; Blink Strike is still OFFERED on a board with no crossable line and performing it attacks nobody, which is recorded rather than changed because greying an offer for a card text that proves there is nothing to do would be a general rule about every hero's pre-attack text; and the six-player setup is still 8.25's. | `e57fb47e` |

## Milestone 8.23a Draw a held marker on a rail that has room for it completion evidence

| ID | Outcome | Commit |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 8.23a | **A hero holding one of rulebook p.18's markers is drawn holding it, and this build had drawn none anywhere.** Milestone 8.23 recorded that as a debt on its own gate row rather than classifying it away: Tigerclaw's Poisoned Dagger and Poisoned Dart lowered a hero's Initiative, Attack and Defense with nothing on screen saying so, because nothing in `Assets/GuardsDigital/Presentation` read an attachment. The reading is one row on the holder's rail carrying the marker's own display name and one signed chip per channel its properties change - `POISON  INI -1  ATK -1  DEF -1` - in the identical chip p.16's items already use, because an item raising and a marker lowering are the same kind of fact about a hero and the sign is on the chip. **It lands on the HOLDER's rail, which is normally the enemy's**, and both journeys assert that in as many words: p.18 gives markers to other heroes and a marker property affects its holder where a token property affects its controller, so a build drawing it on the giver's rail would pass every test about "a marker is drawn" and be telling the wrong player their values dropped. **No schema moved and none was needed.** `MatchSessionProjection.HeldMarkers` hands over the `MarkerAttachmentRegister`'s own attachments - the trusted `MarkerDefinition` object included, so a surface reads the supply, the printed strength and the channels off the definition that states them with no component catalog of its own and no hero-name branch - and the register has been persisted since v33, so restore, deterministic replay, retained-prefix Undo, restart and a presentation rebuild all reconstruct the reading from state they already preserved. The reading's lifetime is the attachment's: it appears when the marker is given and is gone when p.13's holder Defeat or p.8's `Remove Tokens, Return Markers` returns it. **The rail was recomposed to hold it rather than grown by it**, at the user's 2026-08-26 direction, which is the amendment that widened this milestone: milestone 8.21a had established the rail had no free vertical space, so where a held marker goes was not answerable without deciding the layout. `RESOLVED TURNS` is gone and its whole line with it - what the four slots mean now rides their own hover and assistive labels, `Turn 1: Blue card - Static Barrier`, the treatment 8.7a gave the team label and 8.5a gave a card's printed colour; the coin balance moved into the item strip and is drawn as a struck disc with the amount on its face rather than the word `COINS` beside a number, composed from the filled circle, darker rim and centred label the header's hero disc is built from rather than imported; the discard pile shares its caption's line as the Ultimate row already did; and `INIT` became `INI`, the one four-letter token in a set whose own documentation described it as three letters everywhere. **`HeroRailWidth` went 172 to 192 and not further, and the bound is a rule rather than a taste.** It was set to 208 and `TheStandingLayout_KeepsEveryRegionInsideBothValidatedViewports` went red at 416 against a 389 limit, because two rails together must stay under half the narrowest validated viewport so the board remains the dominant surface; the rail was reduced rather than the rule loosened, and the constant records the arithmetic. **The three-per-side measurement is this milestone's deliverable and it is negative.** The acceptance check asked for the recomposed height recorded against a third of the hero-rail rect so 8.25's answer is measured rather than claimed: a rail holding a marker draws **239** at the validated 1920x1080 viewport, where a third of the 491.2 rect - the playfield height, after the status strip's 56 and the card tray's 360 - leaves **155.7**. A stacked column of three does not fit there, the recomposition is not close to closing it, and the journey writes the number to its own run output rather than asserting a pass it does not earn; the gate holds the rail to a 245 ceiling so it cannot grow back silently. That is a fact about a stacked column at one viewport rather than about the roster - the rect is taller at a taller viewport, which the user demonstrated in the Editor the same day - and by their 2026-08-26 decision how milestone 8.25 arranges six rails is left open. **The gate closed on the two Poison faces as well as on the component.** `HeldMarkerReading` joins `PlayerInteractionShape` as a content-derived shape rather than a required component one, so a hero attaching no marker owes nothing and a hero attaching one cannot avoid it; classifying the two marker capabilities made both faces introduce it, and `MatrixRowsDeclareExactlyTheShapesTigerclawsPrintedContentIntroduces` refused the rows until they declared the reading as well as the click - the component row alone was not enough, which is the matrix being derived from content rather than written down. Milestone 8.23's `AbsentComponent` debt row became an ordinary `Component` row with two journeys, and the guard that held it to having something to excuse became one holding it to having a subject. **EVIDENCE. Complete Unity Edit Mode 1874 passed / 0 failed / 0 skipped / 0 inconclusive and complete Unity Play Mode 200 passed / 0 failed / 0 skipped / 0 inconclusive**, both unfiltered and in batch at the final revision, with the Edit Mode run repeated after the last Play Mode-only test edit so both suites stand at one revision rather than one carrying a caveat. The four new cases are the whole delta: two Edit Mode presentation cases driven from a real staged Tigerclaw position that plays Poisoned Dagger rather than from a hand-attached marker, and two Play Mode journeys. **Three of the four reds this milestone paid for were in the tests rather than in the presentation**, and the presentation change itself never failed a run - drawing a held marker needed one projection field, one view model and one row builder. The three are worth carrying: **a restore leaves the session with no accepted command history**, so an Undo attempted after one is refused for having nothing to undo, and the first journey read `Round 1, turn 2: awaiting commitments` with the state right and the history gone - capture, Undo, then restore is the order that proves all three; **`AdvanceToNextTurn` reaches the next TURN and a round is four of them**, so both round crossings stopped inside the round and read the marker still held, which `StagedHeroBoard.CrossTheRoundBoundary` now prevents by delegating to `ConfiguredMatchFixtures.PlayOutTheRestOfTheRound` plus `FinishEndOfRound` and asserting the round index moved; and **a margin box is the wrong unit for "the coin costs the line nothing"** - the strip carries its own 7-pixel top margin and the chips row inside it carries none, so comparing the two margin boxes reported the strip's spacing from the row above as growth caused by the coin. A fourth is a claim that stopped being possible rather than a fault: the coin moved inside `gd-bonus-strip`, so "the count does not overlap the strip" became a claim a child can never satisfy, and the neighbour it must actually stand clear of is `gd-item-chips`. **This milestone also corrects the known-good baseline table**, which milestone 8.23 left reading 8.22's 1819 and 168 although its own paragraph had moved them to 1872 and 198 - the staleness milestone 6.5ac hit, caught here by a milestone that had to touch the row anyway. **What this milestone does not cover, stated rather than implied:** three rails per side do not fit in a stacked column at the validated viewport and the arrangement is 8.25's; p.16's item track remains the one recorded debt on every hero's matrix; no marker content, marker rule or supply changed, and no imported art was added. | `5dfdf652` |

Desperate Cleave is the one face no player reaches: p.6 returns the Handicap card to the box and p.20 brings it back only for an odd player count. **She owns no token, marker, alternate form or statue** - no face of hers names, places, gives or targets one - so there is no component content and milestone 8.23a's `HeldMarkerReading` is owed nothing by her; her petrification changes what an enemy hero already standing on the board counts as, and materialises no statue to stand in their place. **Four pieces of vocabulary her faces needed and the build did not have, and none of them moves the schema.** `UltimateRuleDefinition` had no public multi-modification factory, because Metamorphosis is the first verified Ultimate that is a standing declaration rather than a trigger and states three rules at once - the private constructor has taken a sequence since 6.2a, so `PermanentAbility` is the public way in and adds no field and no lifetime. No minion-class narrowing existed on a selection, though her Green line prints all four readings ("an enemy ranged minion", "ranged or melee", "an enemy melee minion", and no adjective at all): `RosterMinionClass` names them as an optional parameter whose undeclared value admits every class, so every selection declared before this milestone keeps its exact candidate set. No repeat narrowed the CLASS of what the repeated instance may take, which is Cleave's and Rapid Thrusts' "May repeat once on a different enemy hero" after a printed instance targeting "a unit" - **and the narrowing is derived rather than stored**, because a repeated instance already knows which action it repeats, so no snapshot field moves and a tampered payload naming a minion cannot put one back into the set. And Siren's Call prints "not adjacent to you" on a Range UNIT selection, a narrowing only the hero arm carried. Dominate needed no new immunity vocabulary at all: `SyntheticDisplacementCapabilities` has supported a `DirectMove` override since Sabina's Swap needed the Swap one, so "ignore heavy minion's immunity" is declared on both the selection and the move - a piece filtered out of the candidates is not one to offer and then refuse. **Schema advances once, v33 to v34, and its cause is the pinned registration list alone** - her five persistent capability contracts join it, so a v33 payload names a strictly shorter list than an 8.24 build registers and is rejected rather than migrated on the standing `RISK-COMPAT-001` ground. **Nothing about her set needs a field, and that is what closes the roster prediction `TECHNICAL_RISKS` made at 8.22 rather than repeating it**: her Ultimate is a standing declaration rather than a stopped decision, and the class and target narrowings are re-read from trusted definitions when the question is asked. The measured delta is **+135 bytes uniform on all fifteen corpus fixtures** (ended-match 8716 to 8851, verified-hand-setup 7328 to 7463, paused-card-text 8225 to 8360), matching the prediction exactly: five ids at 19, 25, 26, 18 and 22 UTF-8 bytes is 110, plus five one-byte length prefixes and five four-byte revisions; uniform because a registration list is not a continuation, so the two payloads holding no card-action authority carry it like the thirteen that do. The nine aggregate-invariance digests are re-pinned from the same run with all three equality groups unchanged. **The gate gained one interaction shape and one derivation refinement, on milestone 8.20b's terms.** Her three Green Movements choose between two `around-primary-action` placements, and the matrix classified that `Choose` as rulebook p.10's modal - which it is not: no bullet is printed, no sentence is ignored, and the same text runs either way, so each row would have claimed two controls where the player is shown one. A `Choose` is now classified by WHAT ITS BRANCHES ARE, and the new shape is `ActionTextPlacementChoice`, separate from `BeforeMovementTextPause` because that one is a moment the page picks and this is a question with two answers and no way to decline. No earlier hero's content names `around-primary-action`, so the offline derivation printed every Wasp, Arien, Brogan, Sabina and Tigerclaw row byte-identical afterwards. **What the two tiers found, and it is all in the driving rather than in her content.** The Edit Mode tier found that `EffectNodeDefinition` refuses a Capability node naming a chooser - so a printed `you may` belongs on an `Optional` above it, and because the refusal throws in a static initializer, one bad node failed every test that touched `AllDefinitions` and looked like twelve unrelated defects; that a `Next turn:` effect is CANCELLED by crossing the round rather than advanced by it, because p.8's card retrieval fires `CardsRetrieved`, so `StagedHeroBoard` gained `HoldUntilHerNextTurn` and the live window became a Play Mode journey's subject; that a round stopped by a pausing source cannot be driven by `FinishEndOfRound`, whose inner loop advances every stage without looking for a pause between them; and that Constrict's own Defeat is what makes p.8 ask its Minion Battle question, which is the stronger claim - the Defeat lands AT the pre-battle moment rather than merely somewhere before the round ends. The Play Mode tier found that a Defense is offered as a WHOLE FACE named `gd-defense-card` and never as a labelled button, and that her placement side is a modal-style selection whose node stays standing with its own confirm, so `ConfirmIfStillStanding` has to name the node or the press reaches past it into the `you may` behind. **The gate itself found no defect in shipped code**, which has now happened at Wasp's pass and at Sabina's. **A documentation divergence is corrected rather than left standing:** the 8.19 capability matrix mapped Siren's Call through `attack-target@2` and spoke of "before AttackTotal" and "a legal Attack target", but the face prints `Basic Skill - Ranged` with a Range icon and no Attack value, so nothing is attacked and there is no Attack Total for the move to precede; the same section's Green row called the placement decision a `May` when the card offers two sides and no decline. Both rows now state what is composed, by the standing ruling that a hero's cards are the definition. **Validation.** Complete unfiltered Unity Edit Mode 1905 passed / 0 failed / 0 skipped / 0 inconclusive and complete unfiltered Play Mode 225 / 0 / 0 / 0, both in batch, moving the known-good baselines from 1874 and 200; the filtered Xargatha Play Mode run is exactly 25, being fifteen real-input journeys and ten `XargathaPlayerPathGateTests` guards, and the filtered Edit Mode delta is exactly 31. The targeted Game View pass ran non-batch, 4/4, and produced four captures under `tmp/`. `Tools/Validate-LivingDocumentation.ps1` passes. ## Milestone 8.25a Answer an after-action repeat that outlives its actor's Initiative completion evidence

| ID | Outcome | Commit |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 8.25a | **A decision the projection offers is answerable, and rulebook p.10's repeat now stays inside the turn that owns it.** Milestone 8.23 opened Cloak and Daggers' "you may repeat it once" at p.8's `ActionEnded` checkpoint; the action that reaches that checkpoint has finished, so `TurnResolutionAuthority` resolved its card and acknowledged Initiative before the decision was answered and withheld only the advance. The answer settled an *owed* repeat that nothing started - the branch finishing a trigger never asked for one - so the owed instance survived the command that settled it, through the rest of the turn, the end of the round and into the next, and was finally started by whichever action completed next, in whoever's turn that was. Every command the projection then offered for it was refused, because both `TryAnswerMovementChoice` and `TryAnswerEffectChoice` require the pending resolution to be the selected actor's. **The correction is that the action is not over while an ability that may repeat it is standing**: the turn holds its card and its Initiative acknowledgement across that decision, exactly as it already held them for an ability that asked nothing and repeated inside the same command, and the answer starts the second instance there and then. The two commands that can finish a standing trigger - the effect choice and milestone 8.21's removal prevention - now share one `FinishTriggerAnswer`, which starts any owed repeat before deciding anything else, and the prevention answer settles the held subject it never settled (a no-op while the frame still stands, and a permanent hold without it). **Observed failing before and passing after, at `49ddb9ac` and here.** On the staged Wasp-versus-Tigerclaw board that shipped at 8.23, `DeterministicMatchLoop.Play(session, seed: 1, 4000)` was refused `TurnCommandRejected (SkillCommandRejected)` at command 1047 on `after-action-repeat:blue-tigerclaw-blend-into-shadows:primary-skill` while Initiative had Wasp's Electroblast selected; on the official six-player board, seed 3 was refused `TurnCommandRejected (MovementCommandRejected)` at command 2675 on that repeat's `secondary-movement` while Initiative had Brogan selected. Thirteen of the first twenty-four seeded runs across the two boards reached a refusal (staged 1, 3, 5, 6, 9, 11; six-player 3, 4, 6, 7, 8, 9, 12) and eleven reached an ending, which is why the property is stated over a sweep; `AfterActionRepeatContinuityTests` asserts it per board over the first six seeds - measured at 199 s over twelve, past Unity's 180 s per-case timeout - and asserts at every step, whether or not that step draws the failing command, that a standing Movement and a standing repeated instance belong to the hero Initiative selected and to the card it selected them on. **Making the after-action arm run uncovered two shipped defects it had never reached.** `PendingAttackResolution` refused `ActionInvocationContext.Repeated` outright, so milestone 8.23's Attack arm threw `ArgumentException` the first time a match took a repeat of an Attack, and milestone 8.24's `on a different enemy hero` narrowing, written as `resolution.InvocationContext == Repeated`, could never fire. The resolution now admits the third context, which is also what tells the `ActionEnded` checkpoint the instance was a repeat and so what makes p.10's "You cannot repeat a repeated action!" true of an Attack; a repeated instance is looked up against the offer its card prints, because the card prints one action and the repeat is that action again. **One residual is recorded rather than fixed**: milestone 8.10's card-text Attack repeat hands the completed instance's own printed context to the second one, so a Thunder Boomerang or Cleave repeat still reports itself as printed and 8.24's narrowing is reachable only from the after-action path. Correcting that changes which target set two verified cards offer, which is this milestone's stated non-goal, and it belongs to whichever milestone owns that behaviour. **`TurnResolutionAuthority` no longer discards `CardActionResolutionRejectionReason`**: sixteen refusal sites carry the backend's own reason out through `TurnResolutionResult`, `RoundResolutionResult` and `MatchCommandResult`, and `Detail` reads `TurnCommandRejected (MovementCommandRejected: InvalidChoice)` - the diagnosis this milestone opened with took a per-destination retry precisely because that value was dropped one layer down. **Schema stays v34 and no digest moves**: the held subject was already persisted at 8.23 and the owed instance is now created and consumed inside one command, so nothing new crosses the wire; the corrected run replays, restores from a snapshot taken beside the corrected repeat, and survives a retained-prefix Undo into the same bytes. Milestone 8.23's own Edit Mode case is restated rather than left: it could only say the decision closed, and now says that declining ends the action and hands Initiative on while taking it starts the second instance on the same card under the same hero. Complete unfiltered Edit Mode **1917/1917** and Play Mode **226/226**. | `8135cb6c` |

## Milestone 8.25c Scale the HUD with the viewport and identify each rail with its hero portrait completion evidence

| ID | Outcome | Commit |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 8.25c | **The HUD is the same share of every screen, and each rail carries its hero's own face.** `MatchHudPanelScaling` is the single declaration of the panel's coordinate space - `ScaleWithScreenSize`, reference `2560x1440`, `MatchWidthOrHeight` at `0.5` - and every layout number in `ConfiguredMatchHudRenderer` and `CardFootprint` is a reference unit rather than a device pixel. The committed panel-settings asset carries the same four values and an Edit Mode case reads it, so the code agrees with the asset instead of overriding its scale mode as it had since milestone 7.9. **The reference resolution was the whole milestone, and the first answer was wrong.** 1920x1080 was chosen because every layout number had been quoted at it, and captured at both resolutions it was plainly broken: rulebook p.6's three rails draw 733 from the top of the column, so the third ends at 801, and a 1080-tall panel puts the tray's top edge at 571 - burying all but nine units of the third rail on each side, and leaving the tray region a few units too short for a whole 420 card face, so the hand was guillotined too. That had never been visible because before scaling a bigger screen bought real extra room: the rails were a fixed count of pixels while the tray was a share of the viewport, so a 2560x1440 screen had 1360 lines of column and the layout fitted. Declaring 1920x1080 the reference took that room away from every screen at once. The layout needs about 1506 units of height before the third rail clears; 1440 leaves 35, which is the rail's bottom padding and border and is what this build has always drawn under the tray. **`ConfiguredMatchHudRenderer.BlocksPointerAt` converts before it compares.** It took a screen position, flipped it with `Screen.height - y` and measured it against rectangles computed from `Screen.width`/`Screen.height` - an identity mapping, correct only at scale 1, on the path of every board click in the game. `MatchHudPanelScaling.ScreenToPanelPoint` is now that conversion and `StandingRegionsBlockPointerAt` is the seam a fixture with no live panel can ask. **Observed failing at the pre-fix state and passing after**, with the panel already scaled and the conversion absent: `MatchHudPanelScalingTests` 5 total, 4 passed, 1 failed - `At (3840.00, 2160.00) the HUD draws the status strip over the screen position (480.00, 2097.00), which is panel point (320.00, 42.00), and lets the board have the click anyway` - then 5/5. The case samples a grid across each standing region rather than its centre, because a centre point lands inside both the drawn and the unconverted rectangle for every large region and would pass on luck; it runs at 3840x2160 and 1920x1080 so both directions of the defect are exercised, over-blocking below the reference and under-blocking above it. **The reproduced scale formula is measured against Unity's, not believed.** `Scene_ResolvesTheDeclaredPanelScaleOnTheLiveRuntimePanel` runs at 1600x1000 - neither the reference nor its shape, because every 16:9 viewport resolves to the reference whatever the formula - and the live panel measured `(1818.95, 1136.84)` against a predicted `(1818.95, 1136.84)`. It also measured a thing worth keeping: **`RuntimePanelUtils.ScreenToPanel` scales but does not flip Y**, so the flip is this project's own, owed to the bottom-left origin the Input System's pointer arrives in. **A scaled panel snaps every laid-out edge to the device pixel grid**, which is invisible at scale 1 and is why twelve Play Mode cases failed on the first full run: a 186-point card at 0.75 pixels per point is 139.5 pixels, is drawn at 140, and measures back as 186.667. `UiPlayer.PixelSnapping(snappedEdges)` states that tolerance once - one device pixel per independently snapped edge, and `HeroRailMarkerCheck.RailRowCount` for a whole rail's stack - and the footprint gate, the rail-height comparisons, the recorded rail ceiling and one `Rect` equality now use it. The `Rect` equality was the sharpest of them: it failed while printing two identical-looking rectangles. **Milestone 8.25's two geometry facts were re-derived rather than assumed.** Three rails still stack a side, measured at 733 drawn into the 1360-unit column. The tray still meets the third rail, and the overlap is now one number at every screen of the reference's shape instead of 35 on one screen and 230 on another - and `MinimalHexGridPlayModeTests` asserts that **every row a player reads stays clear of the tray as it is drawn**, which is the check whose absence let 8.25 ship two unreadable rails: every assertion there was about rectangles, the rails did fit inside the column the renderer reserved, and the overlap was recorded in the run output rather than judged. It measures the tray's drawn box rather than its reserved rect, because the region is anchored to the bottom and sized to its own content, which is what `BlocksPointerAt` itself prefers. **Each rail's circled initial is that hero's own official token face.** `HeroOverviewViewModel` carries the projected `HeroContentId` and derives `PortraitResourcePath` through `HeroTokenArtwork` - the same private resource the piece on the board is drawn from, addressed by content identity and authoritative team, never by the display name beside it and never by anything read out of the image. The disc keeps the badge's 30-unit footprint exactly, so a rail's height is untouched; it carries no text at all, so there is no fallback to the letter this replaces, and the hero's written name and a words-only assistive reading carry the identity where the private artwork is absent, which is a clean checkout's ordinary state. `HeroRailPortraitTests` proves the six configured heroes address six distinct portraits, that two heroes sharing a display name still address different ones and a renamed hero keeps its own, and that no disc holds a glyph. **Evidence: complete Edit Mode 1926 passed / 0 failed / 0 skipped / 0 inconclusive, complete Play Mode 229 passed / 0 failed / 0 skipped / 0 inconclusive**, both unfiltered and in batch at the final revision. Nine of the Edit Mode delta are the two new fixtures and one of the Play Mode delta is the live-panel measurement plus a two-resolution Game View capture. **No schema move and no digest fixture moved**, because nothing this milestone touches reaches the wire. **What this milestone does not cover, stated rather than implied:** the board camera still frames the map from a serialized `reservedLeftViewportShare` rather than from `CalculatePlayfieldRect`, which the block assumed it already read - the two have never agreed, and making them agree was measured here and rejected, because the official board is close to square and framing it into the 1512x515 band the HUD leaves would halve it and leave voids either side; the HUD's own layout is therefore unchanged in world terms, which is what that check asked for, but by the camera reading nothing rather than by a conversion. The tray region's own bound is unchanged, so a hand of five cards still scrolls by a few units at panels shorter than about 913 reference units. And the private hero artwork stays ignored and undistributed: nothing was added, staged or published, and a clean checkout draws the team-accented disc with the hero's name beside it. | `acc00b5b` |

## Milestone 8.25d Let a card-text Attack repeat say that it is one completion evidence

| ID | Outcome | Commit |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 8.25d | **A repeated Attack instance is one the rules can tell is repeated, and every clause a card prints about its own repeat now applies to it.** Milestone 8.10's `StartRepeatedAttack` built the second instance with `completed.InvocationContext` - the printed context the first instance ran under - so a repeat asked for by a card's own text reported itself as printed everywhere downstream. It now mints `ActionInvocationContext.Repeated`, which is the value milestone 8.23's after-action arm has always passed in, so the two paths that start a repeated Attack agree. The content this moves is exactly the three faces the block named: **Wasp's Thunder Boomerang and Xargatha's Cleave and Rapid Thrusts**, the only verified Attack texts declaring a `RepeatAction` node. **Xargatha's narrowing fires for the first time.** Her "May repeat once on a different enemy hero" is two clauses - an exclusion that travels as `ExcludedTarget` and a narrowing gated on the resolution reporting itself repeated - and only the first had ever run, so her repeat excluded the unit she had just hit and then admitted every other adjacent minion. Observed on a staged board with two enemy minions and the enemy hero all beside her: before, the repeat offered `red-melee-02` and `primitive-hero`; after, the hero alone, with the exclusion on the minion the printed instance took unchanged in both. **p.10's bound was measured rather than assumed, and it did not exist.** A repeated Skill is bounded by the `RepeatContinuation` its own frame carries, counted against the node's declared maximum; an Attack's repeat is asked for by card text applied inside rulebook p.14, and that frame is minted fresh for every instance under a hard-coded printed context with a default continuation, so `RepeatedActionQuery.CanRepeat` was true again inside the repeated instance and `EffectResolutionContext.RepeatIsUnavailable` was set by the triggered-effect path alone. Driven through the real command surface, **one played Cleave started three Attack instances**: the printed one took a minion, step 7 asked and its repeat took the enemy hero, and that instance's own step 7 asked again and opened a third target choice. The bound now lives where the enclosing attack is known - `ApplyCardText` and `AnswerCardTextChoice` derive `repeatIsUnavailable` from the invocation context of the attack the text is running inside, at both of p.14's attacker moments and at the defender's - so the node is skipped in silence exactly as a Skill chain's last instance skips it. **The correction the milestone did not set out to make, which its own fix made unavoidable.** Rulebook p.13's Defeat outcome is identified as `<resolution>:hero-defeat` or `<resolution>:minion-defeat`, and `HeroCoinLedger` refuses to pay one outcome twice, so two instances sharing one resolution identity could not both defeat the same kind of unit. Pre-fix that was reachable as a silent refusal - taking the minion the un-narrowed repeat wrongly offered came back `InvalidChoice` - and post-fix the narrowing makes a second hero Defeat the *only* thing Cleave's repeat can do. Measured on the official six-player board with Arien and Brogan both beside her: the printed instance defeated Arien, the repeat took Brogan, and answering that Defense threw `InvalidOperationException: A prevalidated hero Defeat could not award its p.13 reward: OutcomeAlreadyAwarded`. `StartRepeatedAttack` now takes the repeated instance's `ActionResolutionId` from its caller: the after-action path passes the `after-action-repeat:<card>:<action>` it already minted at 8.23 and is unchanged, and the card-text path derives `<resolution>:repeat` - deterministic under replay, and unique without a counter because p.10 permits exactly one repeated instance per printed one. **Observed failing before and passing after, per check and in three states.** At the pre-fix working tree the three new Xargatha cases and the new Wasp case fail with `Expected: <empty> But was: < red-melee-02 >`, `Expected: null But was: <PendingEffectChoice>`, `OutcomeAlreadyAwarded`, and `Expected: Repeated But was: PrintedPrimary`, while the two Thunder Boomerang cases that predate the milestone stay green in both states. With the invocation context corrected and the p.10 bound absent, the chain case alone stays red, which is what shows the bound is a separate necessary change rather than a consequence. With the bound present and the resolution identity still shared, the six-player case alone stays red. **The compatibility corpus was checked rather than trusted**: `SupportedVersionedCorpus_RoundTripsByteIdentically` and `CardAggregateInvarianceTests` are green with no fixture re-pinned, so no payload holds an Attack resolution mid-repeat - the corpus's one paused repeat is a Skill orbit - and `AuthoritativeSnapshotSerializer.CurrentSchemaVersion` stays **v34** with all fifteen digests byte-identical. **No hero's player-path gate row newly passes or fails.** Both Play Mode journeys that reach Cleave's repeat decline it, so neither changes, and complete Play Mode is unmoved at **229**; the gate tables for all six configured heroes pass unchanged. **Validation: complete unfiltered Edit Mode 1930 passed / 0 failed / 0 skipped / 0 inconclusive in 237 s, and complete unfiltered Play Mode 229 / 0 / 0 / 0**, both in batch at the final revision, moving the Edit Mode baseline from 1926 by the four new cases. The cross-platform harness ran 1651/1651 and carried every measurement; its `Exclude` list had gone stale when milestone 8.25c added two `UnityEngine`-dependent Edit Mode fixtures, which is the tripwire `Testing/DOTNET_HARNESS.md` describes, and both are now listed. `Tools/Validate-LivingDocumentation.ps1` passes. **The warning-threshold ownership review this handoff owes, and what it moved.** `Roadmap/PHASE_8.md` had been warning since milestone 8.25c, at 13598 words against a 12750 line, and the destination the standing growth pattern names - `Roadmap/PHASE_8_COMPLETED.md` - was at 11728, so a move had to shed about 848 words from the active shard without pushing more than 1022 into the completed one. Milestones **8.12 through 8.15a** are the contiguous run that fits: 975 words, moved verbatim under one extended routing summary rather than five separate pointers, which is what keeps both shards inside their own lines, at 12659 and 12717. Their `Historical evidence:` links resolve unchanged because both shards sit in one directory. `Card-System/RESOLUTION_AND_EFFECTS.md` and `Project/CURRENT_IMPLEMENTATION.md` were inspected in the same handoff; the latter is within twelve words of a hard limit, so its two new facts were paid for by compressing two clauses it already stated elsewhere. **What this milestone does not cover, stated rather than implied:** no printed text changed, no ability gained or lost a repeat, `RepeatTargetBinding` is untouched, and milestone 8.25a's after-action path is unchanged in behaviour and in the identity it mints. The shared-identity family is closed for the Attack path alone - a Skill or Movement repeat started from the after-action dispatcher already carries its own - and nothing here revisits `StartOwedAfterActionRepeat`'s four "nothing happened" arms, which are milestone 8.25e's. | `27cfdd78` |
