## Milestone 8.25e Perform the repeat that was offered, or stop offering it completion evidence

| ID | Outcome | Commit |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 8.25e | **A repeat rulebook p.10 offers is one the answer performs, and one it cannot perform is not offered.** Four arms of `CardActionResolutionAuthority.StartOwedAfterActionRepeat` reported "nothing happened": the two kinds it had no case for, and any of the other three whose backend refused the second instance. The offer was decided from `PerformedBasicAction` alone - a fact about the action that *ended* - while whether an instance can start is a fact about kind coverage and about the board as it now stands, so the offer admitted strictly more than the answer could perform, in two independent ways, and Tigerclaw's Cloak and Daggers had no way to know it. **The reading was decided against the printed page and recorded before anything was built**, in the [rule-system ledger](../../Rules/RULE_SYSTEMS.md#rule-system-ledger) and in [HERO_TIGERCLAW.md](../../Rules/HERO_TIGERCLAW.md). p.12's **Hold is repeatable**: it is an action a hero performs on any one of their cards, p.9 makes every action on a basic card basic, and performing it again is performing it - "you simply do nothing" is what that instance does, and it still raises p.8's `ActionStarted` and `ActionEnded` and records p.9's supertype a second time. p.11's **Fast Travel and p.13's Clear are not**, and three printed sentences say so: both exist only as replacements - "instead of your entire Movement action", "instead of your entire Attack action" - p.10's own bullet reads "A repeated instance of an action cannot be replaced with a different action (Such as Clear or Fast Travel)", p.11 adds "You can only perform one Fast Travel action, regardless of the movement value on the card!" and p.13 offers "one Clear secondary action", so the only instance such a repeat could produce is a second replacement all three refuse. **The whole of "is there something to repeat" now lives on one property**, `PerformedBasicAction.CanBeRepeated`, which the ability's `RepeatIsUnavailable` and the held subject both read. **The performability half is asked of the resolver that would start the instance, with the binding the asking node declares.** `EffectResolutionContext.RepeatIsUnavailableFor(binding)` carries an optional probe that `CardActionResolutionAuthority` supplies at the checkpoint; the Attack arm asks the new `CanStartRepeatedAttack`, which is `StartRepeatedAttack`'s own gate extracted into `PlanRepeatedAttack` and asked in both places rather than restated, and the Movement arm asks `CanStartRepeatedMovement` over the extracted `MovementStartRestrictionFor`. The binding matters because Cloak and Daggers prints "if you repeat an attack action, you cannot target the same unit", so the offer has to know what the instance it offers would be barred from; `ExclusionFor` is now the one statement of that, read by the offer and by the settled request. **The three general-rule paths got the hold milestone 8.25a gave the other three.** `TryPerformHold`, `TryPerformFastTravel` and `TryPerformClear` resolved their source card and acknowledged Initiative *before* raising the checkpoint an after-action ability fires at, so the decision stood over a finished turn - nobody selected, card resolved - and an instance started from it had nothing to run on. All three now finish through one shared `CompleteGeneralRuleActionAndAdvance`, and an owed Hold is performed by the turn authority, which is the only place that raises a Hold and is what the dispatcher's `default:` arm had always claimed. **A fifth shape the block had not named, measured rather than reasoned about.** `TryStartOwedAfterActionRepeat` decided whether the turn was waiting from what the resolver reported *before* its steps were applied. A repeated Attack whose text names its own target starts at p.14's step 1, and a board leaving that text nothing to name stops the action at that mandatory step - p.10's own rule - after the turn had already returned, so the hero stayed selected on an unresolved card and the match offered him a second action on it, which p.7 forbids. That is exactly the "choose an action or End Turn with nothing having happened" the block reported from Play Mode. It is decided from what actually stands afterwards now. **Every refusal that reaches the dispatcher faults instead of reporting a completed action**, because after the gate it means the offer and the answer disagree about one instance, which is the defect itself. **Observed failing before and passing after, per arm, at the pre-fix revision in a worktree.** `CloakAndDaggers_RepeatsAHoldByPerformingASecondOne` failed at `Expected: blue-test-hero` for the held actor and now records two `Turn.HoldActionPerformed`; `CloakAndDaggers_DoesNotOfferToRepeatAFastTravel` failed at `Expected: null` for the standing decision; `CloakAndDaggers_RepeatThatStopsAtItsOwnMandatoryStep_StillFinishesTheAction` failed at `Expected: not equal to blue-test-hero`. `PageTensRepeat_MakesASecondInstanceOfEveryBasicKindExceptTheTwoReplacements` is the recorded reading rather than a failing-then-passing check: its whole subject is vocabulary this milestone adds. **The driver-level check the family escaped is one shape stated once**: at every confirm-or-decline either sweep board publishes, both answers are given and rolled back through the director Undo, and the two must not leave the match in the same state and history. At the pre-fix revision it reports `cloak-and-daggers-repeat` on the staged board on seeds 1, 3, 4 and 5; after, that node is gone. It reads the rule-event log beside the authoritative payload, because a second Hold changes no piece and no counter and what makes it a performed action is that it was performed; it forks once per node per run, because Undo replays the accepted prefix and Wasp's `high-voltage-may` alone is reached sixty times in one seed; and it stops where `RuleEventLogSnapshotEnvelope`'s 10,000-entry bound stops Undo. **It immediately found a second family, which this milestone records rather than fixes.** Eight nodes across five heroes - Wasp's `high-voltage-may` and `kinetic-push`, Brogan's `shove-move` and `throw-discard`, Arien's `wave-push`, Tigerclaw's `theft-opening-move`, Xargatha's `green-may` and `converts-may` - are optional card-text steps whose body begins with a mandatory selection, so where the board leaves that selection nothing to name, taking the offer stops at that step exactly where declining leaves it. Closing them needs an optional node to look ahead into a nested selection's candidates, which is a different seam in card text across five heroes; the ruling it would be built against already exists as `RULES_LEDGER.md` question 11. They are the named allowed set, and a ninth node fails the sweep. **Three compatibility digests moved and the schema did not, and the measurement is what says why that is right.** `end-of-round-v34`, `pending-minion-battle-v34` and `paused-level-up-v34` are the three fixtures built out of Holds; each authoritative payload is byte-identical on both sides - 8933, 9138 and 9443 bytes, `cmp` reporting zero differing bytes - and the other twelve do not move at all. What moved is the rule-event log the envelope carries beside the payload: two swapped entries per Hold, four, four and eight of them, each a `Turn.BasicActionPerformed` now raised before `Turn.CardResolved` instead of after it. No payload changes, so every v34 snapshot still loads byte-identically and `AuthoritativeSnapshotSerializer.CurrentSchemaVersion` stays **v34**. **Validation: complete unfiltered Edit Mode 1934 passed / 0 failed / 0 skipped / 0 inconclusive in 246 s, and complete unfiltered Play Mode 229 / 0 / 0 / 0**, both in batch at the final revision, moving the Edit Mode baseline from 1930 by the four new cases and leaving Play Mode unmoved because no journey answers a repeat of one of these kinds. The cross-platform harness ran 1651/1651 before and 1655/1655 after and carried every measurement in this record. `Tools/Validate-LivingDocumentation.ps1` passes. **What this milestone does not cover, stated rather than implied:** no printed text changed and no ability gained or lost a repeat; `RepeatTargetBinding` is untouched; milestone 8.25a's hold and milestone 8.25d's card-text Attack repeat are unchanged in behaviour, and the card-text repeat path deliberately carries no performability probe because it is bounded by its own frame. Two arms of the dispatcher are now unreachable by construction rather than tested through the surface - p.13's Clear cannot be reached at all on shipped content, because no verified card places a token and `CanClearRemoveAnything` withholds the offer where none stands - and a non-text-bound Attack repeat refused for want of a target, and a Movement repeat refused by a prevention, are likewise unreached by any verified face; each is covered by the shared gate and by the recorded reading rather than by a staged board. | `a5373eb1` |

## Milestone 8.34 Add disconnect, reconnect, resynchronization, and host-loss policy completion evidence

| ID | Outcome | Commit |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 8.34 | A temporarily disconnected player now returns to the same private session and rebuilds the correct authorized projection, while every unrecoverable case fails explicitly without corrupting the match. A lost connection leaves its seat reserved and pauses new authoritative progress - no default answer, no timeout mutation, and no other seat advancing the board the away player will come back to - so the pending family, its ordered answers and that board survive by not being allowed to move. The seat returns only to a connection presenting the owner-private reconnect capability the host issued with its grant: the reservation rotates to the next connection epoch, so a frame stamped with the replaced connection's epoch is refused as a protocol violation before any seat-level answer and a frame for the retired connection itself is discarded unread, and the returning seat is answered with a freshly encoded complete owner-scoped projection rather than any client snapshot or delta. All ten pending-decision families are proven at the five required cut points - disconnect before the answer, disconnect after the answer was sent but before its result arrived, the duplicate that retry produces after the return, a stale answer decided against the view the resync replaced, and the successful resume - the seven the deterministic wire walk reaches by walking to them and the three it does not by staging them onto the same wire. The failure policy's first row is now complete in both clauses: an identical repeat of a consumed submission identity is answered from the retained result instead of being refused, which is the debt milestone 8.30a deferred by name, while the same identity carrying a different question is refused as a duplicate and an identity older than the retained result window is refused rather than run again - nothing executes twice in any case. A returning eligible player receives the host's current standing proposal and every teammate's public statement, may revise only its own, and neither a partial nor a conflicting set of statements becomes agreement by way of the return. Reclaims without that seat's capability, of a seat held by a live connection, of a seat nobody has ever held, after an orderly session end, and from an incompatible build are each refused by name before any owner-private byte crosses, with the reservation and its epoch untouched; a stale or future claimed version is refused with nothing mutated and healed by the resync offered beside the refusal; and a connection lost before any claim leaves no reservation at all. Host loss is terminal for a seated client - it keeps the position it was shown, submits nothing, and claims no continuation - while an orderly Goodbye stays an ordinary ending, and the Play Mode surface clears every control the seat could still have pressed without leaving a duplicate HUD, a stale selection, or another player's hand behind. Harness 1,876/1,876, complete Edit Mode 2,250/2,250, complete Play Mode 267/267. | `83f66d32` |

## Milestone 8.35a Add the primitive private host/join lobby completion evidence

| ID | Outcome | Commit |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 8.35a | Invited players now host or join a private session, take one of the six stable seats, become ready, watch the match begin, leave, and start an authorized reconnect - from a primitive lobby with no production styling anywhere in it. The invitation is exactly what the 2026-09-01 transport amendment left: the host's address and port plus the optional pre-shared session key, typed into an entry form beside the six seats this build knows, with no join-code service, account, directory or cloud anything in scope. Readiness and the transition out of the lobby did not exist in the protocol and a surface could not invent them - a lobby deciding locally who is ready would be deciding when six people begin a match, which is authority - so both went into the session layer. `SetReadiness` (kind 17) is one bool from a seated connection about its own seat and names no seat, because the connection-to-seat binding already does; readiness rides the public roster as `SeatRosterEntry.IsReady`, so six players watch each other get ready from the roster alone rather than from anything told to one of them; and when every seat is claimed by a live connection and every one of them is ready, the host derives the transition itself and broadcasts `MatchStarted` (kind 18) exactly once, to seated connections only. The transition is one-way: a later lost connection reserves its seat and pauses the match exactly as milestone 8.34 requires, and does not put six people back into a lobby they have already left. Setting the readiness a seat already holds is accepted and broadcasts nothing; a connection lost before the transition clears that seat's readiness, because a chair nobody is sitting in cannot have agreed to begin; readiness after the transition is refused by name without closing a live match's connection, which would have cost that player their seat; and because a returning player was not there to hear the announcement, the admission answer now carries the session's public phase, so a reclaim learns from its own handshake that it is coming back to a match rather than to a lobby. What a surface draws is a bounded reading of those contracts and of nothing else. Team, occupancy and readiness are words - `RED TEAM`, `SEATED`, `NOT READY` - never a colour; every connection state and every one of the twenty-one refusal reasons has bounded words of its own, and duplicate claim, unauthorized seat change, full session, incompatible build, host loss, join failure and reconnect eligibility are each proven end to end through the session rather than as a label map. Nothing the lobby draws carries another seat's card identity, a reconnect capability, a session key or an address. Host loss keeps the table the player was shown and offers only an authorized return and leaving; the lobby stands aside the moment a started match is being played and returns the moment the session reaches something a player must answer, leaving no control standing that the session would refuse. Its state is presentation-only by construction and mechanically so: no lobby source names a match session, a director command, a submission, an execute or a restore; the model is built from the session client, the session host and the six local seats and from nothing else; every member of it is read-only, so a view cannot write seat ownership into it; and it names no engine type, which is why both test tiers read exactly what a player will. The lobby is an additional interactive path over the same session layer, opted into by `-guardsPrivateLobby` beside the two command-line driver flags and refused alongside them or alongside a command-line invitation - a normally launched build carries no lobby at all, the host and client drivers and their `GUARDS_ONLINE:` marker and exit-code contract are untouched, and the loopback rehearsal passes unchanged. Schema stays v34, the wire protocol version stays 1, and no snapshot payload or compatibility digest moved. Harness 1,925/1,925, complete Edit Mode 2,299/2,299, complete Play Mode 275/275. | `2b07dcb2` |

## Milestone 8.35c Pass a local six-client playtest-build rehearsal completion evidence

| ID | Outcome | Commit |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 8.35c | The exact intended playtest topology now runs as processes on one machine before anyone is invited. `Tools/Invoke-PrivateOnlineRehearsal.ps1 -Topology SixClient -FaultPlan Full` builds one disposable Win64 player and runs two cohorts against it, each one dedicated seatless host process plus six client processes on the six canonical seats, every one of them entering through the real primitive lobby and none of them holding any authority a player does not: the driver may press only lobby controls the model offers and submit only commands and agreement statements its own host-published projection already carries. The match cohort claimed all six seats (`SEATS_COMPLETE claimed=6/6`, `MATCH_STARTED`, `REHEARSAL_TOPOLOGY host=1 clients=6`), handed every seat the owner-scoped HUD, and played a complete match to `REHEARSAL_OUTCOME version=2250 cause=FinalWaveFlipped winner=blue-team` - recorded identically by the host and by all six clients, which is the convergence check, and reproduced across reruns from the fixed seed. Both team-agreement flows were forced rather than waited for: the two Red heroes commit their highest-Initiative card so a same-team tie stands, the tied candidates' owners state conflicting preferences (`initiative-disagreement`, all six seats, version 6), one of them then revises to the other's candidate (`initiative-revision`), the revised preference is what unanimity submits and what the selected actor becomes (`initiative-pass`), and the Tie Breaker then moves exactly as that actor's own deferred flag says (`tie-breaker-pass`). The Minion Battle proposal was agreed by one teammate (`minion-partial`, version 1108), after which the choosing team's controller deselected that removal and selected a different unit, invalidating every recorded agreement (`minion-proposal-invalidated`, version 1109) before the replaced proposal was confirmed unanimously and executed exactly once with removal order unchanged (`minion-pass`, version 1111); no seat can submit either final rules choice directly, and each seat's own authorized set is what says so. Mid-match, `red-player-02` lost its connection deliberately, found every control disabled (`reconnect-input-cleared`), pressed the lobby's own Reconnect, and returned to its reserved seat under a rotated connection epoch with a freshly encoded owner projection (`SEAT_RESERVED`, then `SEAT_RECLAIMED ... epoch=2`, then `reconnect-pass`); while it was away the host refused thirty-four submissions from the other five seats by name as `SessionPaused` rather than advancing the board it would come back to. Every seat audited each projection it received - its own seat, player, hero and side; no other hero's hand, deck or facedown card; no command its own claim does not hold; no agreement statement outside its side's standing proposal - and none ever failed (`privacy-pass`). The second cohort reached the same started match, stood ready, and had its host process terminated, which is the fault plan's own host-termination case and the single deliberate external kill in the runner; every client then observed terminal host loss with its input already disabled and exited zero on its own. Exit codes are never the evidence: the runner reads every log and fails on a missing marker, a divergent outcome, a claimed seat count that is not six distinct canonical seats, a process that had to be stopped, any `ip:port` other than the operator's loopback endpoint, any occurrence of the generated session key, or any degradation pattern - and the invitation reaches each player through that child process's own environment block rather than a command line no banner can hide. The deterministic Full plan itself is the typed `PrivateOnlineRehearsalPlan.FullCases`: eighty stably named cases, each carrying its fixed seed and smallest reproducing message prefix, executed in process at this same revision by the fault-injection, synchronization, compatibility and reconnect suites the matrix row names - drop, bounded delay, duplicate delivery, two-message reorder, completed-submission replay, stale/current/future claimed versions, unknown kind and discriminator, truncation, mismatched length, every declared limit at declared-plus-one, every forged envelope identity field, all ten pending families at all five reconnect cut points, reconnect-eligibility expiry and host termination. `FullPlan_DrivesEveryDeclaredProtocolLimitAtItsDeclaredValuePlusOne` closes five bounds the catalog declared and nothing exercised - the challenge nonce, the rejection detail, the reconnect capability, the command token-selection list and the projection list - and drives the identity window's own boundary, where a replay is recognized inside the declared count and answered by the version check one consumption past it, never executed twice. Five blocking defects were found and each corrected and rerun green. Four were the rehearsal surfaces' own: the driver submitted commands its seat projection does not authorize, because the Director-era offer surface derives the general Hold and a standing decision's contract from public state - the host refused each by name, which is milestone 8.30a working exactly as intended, but at roughly seven refusals and a served resync per accepted command no match could have finished, and the driver now submits only the intersection with its own authorized set; the Minion Battle proposal identity carries the authoritative version, so a seat that began tracking before the selection was confirmable reported the choosing team's first ordinary selection as an invalidation and would have satisfied this milestone's own acceptance check without the flow it exists to prove; the single-advancer rule that stops six seats racing on the choiceless end-of-round command also swallowed a team-owned system transition standing inside end of round, deadlocking a Blue-owned decision on a Red seat that held nothing; and the driver named a socket type in Presentation, which only the complete Unity-free suite catches because no focused filter selects that conformance rule. The fifth was the catalog's own declaration-only coverage above. `AnEndOfRoundDecision_IsAnsweredByItsOwningTeamAndNotByWhoeverAdvancesTheRound` pins the session fact the third correction rests on. Schema stays v34 and the wire protocol stays `guards-private-online/2`; no snapshot payload or compatibility digest moved, and no rules behavior changed. Unity-free 1,951/1,951, complete Edit Mode 2,327/2,327, complete Play Mode 276/276. The rehearsed identity, recorded from the host's own first line, is `protocol=guards-private-online/2 schema=34 map=official-single-lane@1 content=3891bdb8...0d4edf build=1.0+20260903003143-b4a4eb62+6000.5.9f1`. Environment fact, recorded rather than worked around: Windows Application Control refused Burst's backend assembly (`0x800711C7`) on eighty-two consecutive player builds across roughly four hours, and the disposable build behind this evidence is the first one it allowed through; the same outage should be expected at milestone 8.36 and is not a pass. **A same-machine or local-network rehearsal does not prove relay or NAT traversal, external latency behavior, multi-network connectivity, or real-internet reliability; only milestone 8.36's invited two-network match can.** | `1b9fe0ad` |
