## Milestone 8.35d Run the end-of-round sequence automatically and scope each hero's level up to its own player completion evidence

| ID | Outcome | Commit |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 8.35d | Rulebook p.8's end-of-round sequence now runs itself in a seat-served match, and p.15's Upgrade a Card belongs to the one player whose hero bought the level. Both halves are corrections to live multiplayer findings the user reported on 2026-09-03. Milestone 8.30a authorized `AdvanceEndOfRound` to every seat because the command carries no choice; `SeatProjection.AuthorizedCommands` now offers it to nobody, and `MatchHost` runs the stages itself at the end of every accepted seat-path submission - `SubmitForSeat` and the unanimous final of `SubmitAgreementForSeat` - and never from `Submit`, which is the Director's own door. That is the whole opt-in: seat-served play advances itself, Director mode still steps the round by hand, and no composition can be given the policy the wrong way round. The continuation runs inside the caller's own execution window, so a re-entrant submission is still refused; each advance is an ordinary accepted command appended to the one recorded stream, so replay, retained-prefix Undo, snapshots and restore see the sequence they always saw; it stops on a standing decision of any family, on leaving the phase, on the game ending, and on a refusal, which is a defect signal recorded in `MatchHost.EndOfRoundContinuationRefusals` rather than retried. A seat's result now reports the version the answer settled on rather than the one before the continuation, so a seat's next submission is not stale by construction. `SeatProjectionFactory.OwnerOfStandingDecision` reads the level-up transition's own paused item - `HeroLevelUpItem.Read(step.ItemId).HeroId` - so p.15's upgrade is hero-owned while every other transition stays with the side the authority named; nothing persisted or on the wire changed, because the seat projection already carries an optional owner hero id. The seat HUD's `CONTINUE END OF ROUND` is gone with the offer: `IMatchClient` gained `IsDirector`, asked per render because milestone 8.35b's switchable client replaces a local client with an admitted network seat after composition. **The reproduction hunt found both reported symptoms and a third fact behind them.** Pre-fix, with the fixture files in place and only the four behavioural sites reverted, `EndOfRoundContinuationTests` ran 3 passed / 4 failed: a teammate seat's answer to another hero's p.15 upgrade was **accepted** (`RejectionReason = None` where the fixture expects `UnauthorizedCommand`), which is "leveling was skipped for some players"; one seeded seat walk rested inside end of round with no decision standing **112 times**, each one a moment six players shared one button; the upgrade pause reported itself team-owned; and 228 wire-client advance offers were published across the wire walk. The stuck game reproduced on the presentation side rather than in the authority: a seat's scoped surface reports the standing decision it does not own as absent - the privacy rule working correctly - and the continuation control was derived from the phase and those two absences, so while one player answered an end-of-round question the other five were each drawn `CONTINUE END OF ROUND` and every press was refused as unauthorized. `SeatScopedMatchHudTests` recorded that pre-fix at 13/14 in Unity Edit Mode and 14/14 after. The authority itself never deadlocked in any reproduction; the wedge players saw was five dead controls beside one live prompt. New evidence: `EndOfRoundContinuationTests` (7 cases, Unity-free, both tiers) and two `SeatScopedMatchHudTests` cases. Nine existing fixtures moved for one shared cause worth recording: the walk no longer draws the round's own advance, so every seeded trajectory after the first end of round shifted - seed 2 now reaches the Attack target at step 2,536 and the Minion Battle at 2,541 rather than inside 1,500. A twelve-seed sweep re-derived seed 3, which reaches all seven walked families in 268 steps; `DriveThroughSeats` and `DriveThroughWire` gained an optional seed parameter and the coverage fixtures name it. `NetworkSubmissionCodecTests` dropped `AdvanceEndOfRound` from its offered-kind floor because no seat surface offers it; `PendingDecisionReconnectTests` now allows the host's own advances beside one answer's record; the two Minion Battle agreement fixtures assert the appended shape - one confirmation followed only by host-authored advances - rather than a version delta of one; `LocalSixSeatPlayerExperienceTests` skips a recorded Director advance the seat-served host had already run; `MatchFeaturePresenterTests` composes its disabled-view case at a Director resting position, because the state it waited for no longer occurs in a seat-served match, and gained an anti-vacuity arm proving an enabled Director view IS offered the control there. Schema stays v34 and the wire stays `guards-private-online/2`; no snapshot payload or compatibility digest moved and no rules behaviour changed. T2 with the shared-seam escalation, so both complete suites ran: Unity-free harness 1,961/1,961, complete Edit Mode 2,339/2,339, complete Play Mode 278/278, focused Edit Mode 60/60 over the three touched fixtures. Environment facts recorded rather than worked around: Windows Application Control refused the freshly built harness assembly (`0x800711C7`) repeatedly through the session, clearing and re-blocking on almost every rebuild, so the seed sweep was re-derived through the Unity Edit Mode launcher; and a peer session was authoring a different corrective in the same working tree throughout, so this milestone's index was built from its own paths and the complete Edit Mode evidence is from a compile snapshot that did not contain the peer's in-flight fixture. | 848342ad |

## Milestone 8.35e Render the Minion Battle agreement stage on every scoped seat and survive a scoped tray's absent revealed card completion evidence

| ID | Outcome | Commit |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 8.35e | Every seat of a served match now renders the Minion Battle from first selection through milestone 8.30n's public unanimous agreement to the applied removal without a presentation exception, and a seat's hand tray renders a hero whose revealed commitment card the seat's own scoped card list withholds. Both were client crashes recorded by the 2026-09-03 invited playtest's logs on both team sides, and both are presentation defects: the hosting PC's wire lines in the same logs record accepted agreements and submissions advancing the authoritative version from 92 to 97 under surfaces that could no longer draw them. The first crash was structural: `MinionChoicePresenter.CreateViewModel`'s public-agreement branch constructed the identity-guarded removal-prompt `MinionChoiceViewModel` with default end-of-round identities, which that constructor's own guard refuses, so the branch threw on every entry - and it is entered on two reader paths the reproduction pinned. A non-choosing seat entered it on every render of the whole battle, because the seat surface reports the pending choice a seat does not own as absent while the public `TeamAgreement` passes through, and the agreement coordinator opens the MinionBattle proposal at the first selection rather than at confirm. The choosing seat entered it on its own accepted press, because `NetworkMatchReadModel` strips every pending detail while the client is waiting for the host but keeps the public agreement, and a result's projection is read after the send, so an accepted `SubmitUnit`'s and an accepted `SubmitAgreement`'s own refresh both saw agreement-without-detail. Each throw also aborted `MatchFeatureCoordinator.RefreshAll` mid-list, freezing every later presenter - the whole-HUD freeze six players read as a stuck round. The fix is `MinionChoiceViewModel.ForPublicAgreement` - counts, choosing team and side, selected count, empty candidates, no confirm invocation, the agreement HUD, and a derived `IsPublicAgreementStage` - routed through the existing private constructor; the identity-guarded constructor is unchanged and now reachable only from an owned pending choice, and `MinionChoiceUnityView` needed no change because milestone 8.11a had already moved candidate answering onto the board. The second crash resolved each hero's commitment `RevealedCardInstanceId` with `Single` over the seat's scoped card list; the tolerate-the-absence reading held, measured rather than assumed: an offline probe over a real seat walk put the window at exactly `EndOfRound` stage `LevelUpAndUpgrade` in 180 of 180 observations and nowhere else, because the Retrieve stage returns each hero's revealed card to its owner while the commitment record rebuilds only at the next round's start, and the returned cards sit in the hand and deck locations `SeatProjectionFactory.IsPublic` deliberately withholds together - a client that could read an opponent deck could subtract it from the printed set and read the hand - so publishing the card would be a privacy regression and `Single` became `SingleOrDefault`, landing on the tray label's existing null branch exactly as `HeroOverviewPresentation` already reads the same field. New evidence: `MinionBattleAgreementRenderingTests` (six cases, including `SixWireSeatsRefreshTheirWholePresenterSetAtEveryVersionOfAMinionBattle`, which drives six real network clients through a complete battle refreshing every presenter at every observed version across all three entry paths) and one `SeatScopedMatchHudTests` case. Pre-fix observation, taken with the production fix temporarily reverted: 4 failed / 2 passed, each on the recorded defect - the `ArgumentException` from the non-chooser render, from the accepted agreement refresh and from the accepted selection refresh, and the tray's `InvalidOperationException` - and 6/6 after. Provenance fact recorded rather than tidied: a concurrent session completed milestone 8.35d in this same working tree while this milestone was validating, and its commit `848342ad` carried this milestone's production edits with it, so this evidence commit adds only the new fixture, its meta, and the Unity-free harness's exclusion of it (it references Presentation). Complete Edit Mode 2,346/2,346 and complete Play Mode 278/278 at that combined revision - the delta over 8.35d's 2,339 is this milestone's seven cases; focused Edit Mode 212/212 over the twelve touched families. The Unity-free harness answered 1,961/1,961 before the change and then Windows Application Control (`0x800711C7`) refused every freshly built assembly for the rest of the session, so the post-change totals are the Unity tier's. Schema stays v34, the wire stays `guards-private-online/2`, and no snapshot payload, compatibility digest or rules behaviour moved. | `3b682994` |

## Milestone 8.35f Give each seat a bounded undo inside its own turn's card resolution completion evidence

| ID | Outcome | Commit |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 8.35f | Each player now has an undo of their own, and it stops exactly where the user said it stops. During its own turn's card resolution a seat presses one square control and the match steps back one accepted command; pressing again steps back again, as far as the state that turn began in and no further. `MatchHost` keeps the bounds as a watermark brought up to date after every accepted submission rather than searched for in the history afterwards, because the two questions they turn on - which seat answered a command, and whether an Attack action has finished - are answered exactly once, at acceptance, and nothing in the recorded stream records them. The turn floor is set when the marker `round:turn:actor:card` changes; the other-seat wall raises it above anything a different seat answered; and the attack wall rises the moment a `ChooseAttackTarget` is accepted and settles above the whole rulebook p.14 action once nothing of it is still resolving and nothing is being asked, which is milestone 8.29f's rule restated at this layer. The acting seat therefore has no rewind at all while an Attack resolves, and the settled floor stands immediately above it: the defender's answer is information that cannot be un-shown, which is the user's own reason. The rewind is deliberately not a `DirectorCommand` - an undo removes the recorded stream's last entry and can never be inside it - so it follows `TeamAgreementSubmission`'s precedent with its own `SeatUndoSubmission`, its own `ISeatClient.SubmitUndo`, its own host operation and its own result. That submission carries an identity and the version it was decided against and nothing else: no seat, no count, no target, so the host answers all three questions itself, and `OwnerScopedProjectionTests.ThePlayerSurfaceCarriesNoDirectorAuthority` now pins that shape beside its unchanged Director-only absence list rather than being relaxed to accommodate the new method. Idempotency is load-bearing because an undo is not idempotent: the identity is consumed before the version is read, so a repeated identity is answered from what it already did, while the second click of a double click names the version the first replaced and is refused `StaleProjection` without taking a second step. The projection publishes `IsSeatUndoAvailable` to the owning seat alone and the host authorizes from the same rule that published it, so a surface never decides its own undo any more than it decides its own legality; `SeatSubmissionRejectionReason.UndoUnavailable` is one reason for every bound, because every way of reaching it is the same answer to the player, and the detail carries which one it was. On the wire the rewind is two new kinds, `SubmitUndo` and `UndoResult`, under milestone 8.32's envelope, sequence and idempotency discipline; an accepted one republishes every seat's projection because a step back changes the shared board, a stale one serves the asker its own fresh projection, and the reserved-seat pause refuses it by name exactly as it refuses a command. `IsSeatUndoAvailable` rides the seat record, which a client cannot derive - the bounds are facts about the host's recorded history - and because it sits mid-record a protocol 2 decoder would read everything after it at the wrong offsets, so the wire advances to `guards-private-online/3` and both older versions are refused from the bounded header before any payload is interpreted. The HUD control is the user's own placement with one recorded deviation: a square on the status strip's top edge immediately left of END TURN, a counter-clockwise arrow glyph and no words, standing exactly when the host published the rewind and absent from the Director's drawer. The user asked for a square of END TURN's own width; END TURN is 264 wide in a 96-unit strip, and a 264-square cannot stand in one without hanging 168 units over the board, so the side is the largest the strip admits - its own 96 - which keeps the shape, the edge and the neighbour that were named. It carries no invocation, which is the structural half: a rewind is not a command, so the square raises a bare request, the presenter asks its client and the host decides; a view that could compose an undo could compose one for another seat. **The inherited attack-wall case was unobservable as written, and the rewrite is the evidence that matters.** It waited for a seeded walk to stop in a settled post-Attack state, which never happens: the walk only appends and its predicate runs between commands, so a p.14 action that resolves inside one accepted command is invisible to it. Probes over seeds 1 to 5 at 1,500 commands measured the shape - seeds 2 and 4 reach no `ChooseAttackTarget` at all, seed 1 reaches none while still showing nine mid-Attack states because an Attack can be underway before its target is named, and seeds 3 and 5 reach four attacks and one mid-action window each - and printed states pinned the boundary: at phase `ChooseTarget` the actor still holds a rewind, and one command later at `DefenseResponse` it holds none. It is now two cases that cannot pass by luck. `NoSequenceOfUndosEverRevertsAnAttackWhoseTargetWasChosen` states the user's rule as a property of a whole run - a walk pressing the control at every third state that offers one, asserting the count of Attack target selections in the recorded stream never goes down, and failing outright if it observed no Attack or took no rewind; pressing at every offer instead starves the walk, which then undoes itself as fast as it plays and reaches no Attack at all. `AnAttackWhoseTargetWasChosenOffersItsOwnActorNoRewindWhileItResolves` drives the mid-action half by hand, submitting the target itself and asserting at every state until the action settles that the seat is published no rewind and that asking is refused with nothing moved. Two contract updates came with the feature and neither was weakened. The wire growth check now carries `IsSeatUndoAvailable` as a named record field rather than excusing it, so its exclusion floor of seven is unmoved; and the player-surface boundary case records the one crossing there has ever been while keeping every unbounded absence. New evidence: `SeatBoundedUndoTests` (ten cases, Unity-free, both tiers), `SeatScopedMatchHudTests.TheSeatUndoSquareIsDrawnForTheSeatThatMayRewindAndForNobodyElse`, and `MatchHudPanelScalingTests.TheSeatUndoSquare_StandsLeftOfEndTurnOnTheSameStripEdge`, which asserts the 96-square at the reference panel and its clamping at 778x300 and 1280x800 so a later change to either number fails. `RoundActionUnityView.RequestSeatUndo` is the raising seam, following `ForwardInvocation`'s precedent, because an element with no live panel dispatches no pointer event. Pre-fix observation, taken with the one behavioural site reverted so the whole feature is off: 23 passed / 10 failed of 33 - nine `SeatBoundedUndoTests` cases and the new seat-HUD case - and 33/33 after; `TheDirectorsOwnUndoIsUnchangedAndItsViewPublishesNoSeatRewind` passed in both states, which is the anti-vacuity arm proving the Director is untouched, and the geometry case passed in both because a layout function is not what the scaffold reverted. **A pre-existing defect this milestone reached and did not cause, recorded rather than fixed.** The property sweep pressed a rewind the host had published and the session refused it: `Undo replay failed without changing the match: Snapshot payload is malformed or internally inconsistent.` From the identical state the Director's own unbounded Undo is refused identically, so the seat control only reaches it. `Reconstruct` replays from the latest retained checkpoint and deserializing that pre-image throws, with the serializer's generic wrapper hiding the cause; replaying the same 1,262-command stream into a fresh host and round-tripping the public snapshot after every command names it exactly, after command 1,067 - an `AnswerEffectChoice` inside an Attack - as `Snapshot pending effect choice names node 'noble-other', whose capability no longer requests a choice`, which is `DeriveCapabilityCandidates` re-asking a capability on restore and being told there is no choice. A plain seeded walk with no undo anywhere never visits that state (seeds 1 and 3, 1,400 commands, snapshot round-tripped at every one, zero failures), which is why nothing had hit it before. It is milestone **8.29c**'s missing recurrence and it relocates that milestone's defect from the HUD path into shared authority, so 8.29c needs the user's amendment or a new corrective; the seat control's own failure mode is safe and asserted here - refused, with no version, stream or snapshot moved. Schema stays v34 and no snapshot payload, compatibility digest or rules behaviour moved; the wire moves to `guards-private-online/3`, which makes the playtest package built before milestone 8.35d stale on that half of its identity tuple too, and `Testing/FUTURE_GATES.md` records it. T2 with the shared-seam escalation, so both complete suites ran at the final revision: complete Unity Edit Mode **2,358/2,358** against the 2,346 gate - the twelve are this milestone's own cases - and complete Unity Play Mode **278/278**, unmoved. The Unity-free harness answered once early at **1,970** total (1,967 passed and exactly the three contract reds above, against 8.35d's 1,961 baseline) and Windows Application Control then refused every freshly built assembly with `0x800711C7` for the rest of the session across roughly thirty retries, so post-change harness numbers do not exist and every green result recorded here is Unity-tier. A throwaway scratch csproj compiling Core, Application, Network and Presentation against the 6000.5.9f1 editor assemblies carried the compile loop the harness cannot see. The required warning-threshold ownership review moved the 2026-08-28 amendment merging 8.26 into 8.27, and milestones 8.27 through 8.28b, verbatim from the Phase 8 roadmap shard to `PHASE_8_COMPLETED_2.md`: the active shard stood 342 words below its hard limit, which one milestone block does not fit in, and the destination had 9,700 free. Evidence added after the fact, because the record above says the number does not exist: Application Control released the harness at 18:16 the same day, and the complete Unity-free run at this milestone's own revision is 1,971/1,971 green in four and a half minutes - the post-change figure that was unobtainable while the commit was being made, and the +10 over milestone 8.35d's 1,961 is this milestone's own Unity-free cases, its two HUD checks being Unity-only. The outage it ended ran 60 consecutive blocked retries over 46 minutes, the longest recorded on this machine. | `c3763ef5` |

## Milestone 8.35g Hold a finished turn until its own player releases it completion evidence

| ID | Outcome | Commit |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 8.35g | Turns no longer end by themselves. When an acting hero's card resolution completes the match enters a `turn-release` pause owned by that hero's seat instead of selecting the next actor, and END TURN pressed a second time is the release. The user's reason is the whole design: "If user makes the wrong movement, the turn auto ends, so no undo will fix that." Under milestone 8.35f alone the last action of a turn was unreachable, because completing the resolution WAS the hand-off and the rewind's floor moved with it. `InitiativeResolutionAuthority.TryAcknowledgeSelectedCardResolved` is the seam and `TurnResolutionAuthority.AdvanceInitiative` the single funnel every one of the eleven advance paths reaches; `AdvanceInitiative` was split so that everything up to the pause stays and `HandOnToNextActor` - next-actor selection, the turn-only cancellation checkpoint, `TurnEnded` and `IsEnded` - is what the release command runs, which is what puts end-of-turn timing at the release rather than at completion, so an undo inside the window never crosses checkpoint state. Two facts decided where the pause goes: the acknowledgement clears `SelectedActor` and flips the Tie Breaker, so the pause record carries the hero, the card and the side captured BEFORE it by a new `AcknowledgeSelectedCardResolved` helper that all seven completion sites call; and `AdvanceInitiative` runs rulebook p.8's mid-turn Lane Push first and can return early there, so the owed release survives a command boundary as persisted state and the pause check sits after the push, which is what keeps two decisions from standing at once. `TurnResolutionAuthority.Restore` gained a fifth legitimate restore shape - a started, unended turn with no pending choice, no selected actor, no lane push and no triggered effect - the first in which nothing is pending because the turn is COMPLETE rather than interrupted. The pause is the eleventh declared pending family, `turn-release`, with a one-command contract, hero-scoped ownership in `SeatProjectionFactory.OwnerOfStandingDecision`, and the release riding the ordinary `DirectorCommand` path so replay, retained-prefix Undo, snapshots and restore see it as the stream entry it is. Milestone 8.35f's watermark is amended rather than worked around: `MatchHost.TurnMarkerOf` keys on the pause's carried hero and card when one stands and on the selection otherwise, so the marker string is identical across the acknowledgement and the floor does not reset at the exact moment the user wants the rewind to continue; the attack wall gained an explicit exception admitting a standing `TurnRelease` contract, without which an Attack that ended a turn would leave the wall up through the entire pause and the seat would have no rewind there at all. **The undo-through-release requirement is pinned by its own case rather than left as intent**: `SeatBoundedUndoTests.FromInsideTheReleasePause_TheSeatRewindsIntoItsFinishedResolutionAndNoOneRewindsThroughTheRelease` drives a seeded seat walk to a pause whose own seat still has a step to take back and asserts both halves together - the press is accepted, reverts exactly one accepted command and lands back inside the same turn naming the hero and card the pause named; and once the release is accepted no seat is offered a rewind at all, which is what "the accepted release sits behind the next turn's floor" means operationally. The two halves are one case deliberately, because a rewind that reached backwards while letting a later seat revert the release would only trade one crossing for another. **Two conformance guards, not the author, settled the milestone's last design question.** `StatusDerivationConformanceTests.EveryDeclaredPendingFamilyIsNamedByTheStatusDerivation` failed because the eleventh family was a decision the top status bar could not report: a turn waiting on its own player reached the table as the generic "Waiting for the match to continue" fallback, the same defect milestone 8.30c removed for an ended match and 8.20d for the effect choice. `FormatStatus` gained its branch, last of the decision branches because the pause is withheld while anything else stands, and it names the side because one derivation serves every viewer. That required `PendingTurnRelease` on `IMatchReadModel`, which immediately tripped the second guard: the wire-growth rule refused an attempt to DERIVE the pause from the `DecisionOwner` row already crossing, because its exclusion floor is a deliberate ratchet and an excused member is a fact a client cannot see. So the family is carried in full as `NetworkTurnReleaseRow` in the projection detail, the exclusion floor stays at seven, and **the wire advances to `guards-private-online/4`** with protocols 1, 2 and 3 all refused from the bounded header before any payload is interpreted. That is the roadmap's own acceptance check - the family crossing the projection and the wire like the other owned ones - reached by being forced there rather than by being asserted. **Schema v34 to v35 with the full re-pin**: the six documents `Tools/PinnedConstants.txt` names state v35, the four bare `Is.EqualTo(34)` assertions are updated with the two narrative ones extended, the sixteen corpus fixtures are renamed and re-digested, `CardAggregateInvarianceTests` is re-pinned, and the byte delta was measured in a throwaway worktree at the pre-change SHA rather than computed: **+1 byte on eleven fixtures** - the single boolean saying no release is waiting, the whole cost of the field on a turn that is not holding one - **+0** on `setup` and `verified-hand-setup`, which write no turn authority at all, and **-5/-5/+37** on `placed-token`, `attached-marker` and `ended-match`, whose drive loops now include release commands and so capture slightly different positions rather than the same position encoded differently; total **authoritative +38 bytes, envelope +2,922**. **Every seeded trajectory moved, and the reason is worth recording**: `DeterministicMatchLoop` picks with `random.Next(legal.Count)` and `Next(1)` still advances the stream, so the release consumes a draw even as the only legal command and every seeded match is a genuinely different match rather than the old one plus a command per turn. Eleven fixtures were re-derived offline with throwaway `HarnessTests` probes and no Unity launch was spent on a seed: the soak budget 300 to 450 (32 rounds at 300, 40 at 400, 48 at 450 against a floor of more than 40); `ClearReachabilitySeed` 58 to 20; three seat-walk fixtures to seed 6, which one scan answered because all three drive the same official initial state; the wire family sweep to seed 1 at budget 1,200, the only pair in seeds 1-16 that still reaches all seven families; the wire Minion Battle to seed 6; the tied-Initiative walk to a NAMED seed 1 because that case needs a same-team tie and the old default now reaches a cross-team one, which is a different rule; and `LocalSixSeatPlayerExperienceTests` to 51/60/1 at budget 2,400. **The Clear-coverage scare was closed by a seed with nothing relaxed.** `TheCompleteLoop_ReachesEveryPartOfThePage7TurnAndThePage8Round` stopped reaching a `PerformClear`, and the guard was already reading the dedicated `ClearReachabilitySeed` run, so moving it there was not available as an answer. Scanning seeds 1-120 on that fixture found exactly two that take a Clear and only seed 20 also ends the match; seed 51 exhausts its budget. A Clear is rare there for a reason milestone 8.8b makes correct - the offer stands only where a token is beside the actor, so a walk that places none is right to take none - and a narrow search is what the page-coverage property costs. **A defect class this milestone found and did NOT cause, reported rather than absorbed.** A seeded walk died on an `AnswerEffectChoice` the projection offered and the rules refused with `AttackActionRejected: TargetUnavailable`, the class milestone 8.25a closed. Run against a `git worktree` at `6bfe61f9`, the last pre-8.35g commit, the official six-player fixture over seeds 1-40 at budget 4,000 throws identically on seeds 12 and 20 - same command kind, same refusal - so the release re-routed a walk onto a trajectory that reaches a pre-existing offer/refusal gap. It belongs in its own corrective beside the snapshot-restore and rejoin ones and is deliberately not folded in here. **The trap this milestone leaves behind for any bulk edit.** Release guards were bulk-inserted across the fixtures, and five landed in straight-line helper methods carrying a `continue;` - `CS0139`, a hard compile error - in `MatchFeaturePresenterTests` and `MatchHudPresentationTests`, both of which the Unity-free harness excludes. A completely green harness therefore said nothing about an Edit Mode assembly that would not compile at all, which produces no results XML rather than a red case. Four were dead guards at positions where no resolution has completed and no pause can stand, removed rather than converted; the fifth genuinely needed its release AFTER its Hold, because the next line reads the selection the Hold had just emptied. A sixth guard in the same file sits in a real loop and is correct, its budget raised from 8 to 16. The reusable rule: a bulk edit that inserts `continue` must be checked by a compiler that sees the Unity-only test assemblies - the scratch presentation probe extended over `Assets/Tests/EditMode/**/*.cs` answers in about forty seconds - and the compiler, not a grep, is what separates a correct guard from a dead one; a grep found 40 sites and could not tell them apart. Presentation: `CreateEndTurnInvocation` draws the release from the standing contract, so only the seat that owns the pause is drawn one, and the second press is deliberate because an explicit stop is a click that can be misplayed like any other. `MATCH_HUD.md` gained the control and had a claim CORRECTED rather than extended - "A pause exposes neither End Turn nor end-of-round advance" was made false by this milestone. Pre-fix observation: at `5e0e720d` the release pause does not exist, so every new case fails and the interesting half is the shape of the red rather than the count - `AGeneratedMatch_PlaysFromSetupToAnEndingWithoutStallingOrRefusal` reported seed 3 running out of budget after 1,200 commands in round 15 with no stall, no refusal and no unauthorized command, which is a match playing THROUGH the pause for fifteen rounds and needing more commands because every turn costs one more. Post-fix: Unity-free harness **1,973/1,973** against the 1,971/1,971 pre-change baseline at `6df65cb1` - the two are this milestone's own Unity-free cases, and an intermediate run reading 1,974 counted a throwaway scan fixture that has since been deleted, complete Unity Edit Mode **2,361/2,361** against the 2,358 gate - the three are this milestone's own new cases - and complete Unity Play Mode **278/278**, unmoved. The harness stayed unblocked for the entire implementation session, the longest such window since 8.29e, which is why every seed above is measured rather than bisected. | `7c349d5d` |

## Milestone 8.35h Isolate the HUD presenter fan-out so one broken panel degrades alone completion evidence

| ID | Outcome | Commit |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 8.35h | One broken HUD panel now degrades alone. `MatchFeatureCoordinator.RefreshAll` called its seven presenters in a straight line, so the second throwing silently skipped the five behind it on every projection change - the amplifier that turned milestone 8.35e's two view-model defects into Andrej's whole frozen client while the authority kept accepting submissions to version 97. Each panel now refreshes inside its own try/catch; the coordinator publishes `FailedPanelNames` for the latest refresh so a surface can draw a named failed state; and the failure is never swallowed - a composed HUD reports each failed panel and its exception to an injected sink, while a composition supplying none is raised at with an `AggregateException` naming the panels, but only after every panel has had its turn, so isolation cannot become silent swallowing by omission. `ConfiguredMatchSceneRuntime` supplies the production sink, emitting `GUARDS_HUD: PANEL_REFRESH_FAILED panel=` plus the exception where the playtest logs already look. The catch is deliberately broad: the point is that no presenter's defect reaches another panel, and narrowing to the exception types seen so far would only mean the next unfamiliar one takes the client down again. `TeamAgreementHudPresentation` had the second half of Worker C's finding - a bare indexer twice and a `Single` sitting beside a `TryGetValue` fallback in one method - and now reads a candidate it cannot name with the same tolerance, degrading to the card's own identity and the named-absence hero the non-Initiative arm already passes. **The test double is a throwing view rather than a broken presenter**, because the claim is about the coordinator's isolation and not about any presenter's defect; the round panel is chosen as third of seven so the case proves both that the two ahead rendered and that the four behind - exactly what used to be skipped - rendered too. A reusable `DelegatingReadModel` (57 virtual members over a real projection) was added so a case can doctor one fact of a projection; it fails by compile error when `IMatchReadModel` grows, which agrees with the wire-growth rule that already forces a decision on every new member. **Pre-fix observation with the new members left inert**: keeping `FailedPanelNames` and the sink parameter present but restoring the straight-line body and the bare indexer, `MatchFeaturePresenterTests` ran 43 cases with exactly the three new ones failing for their honest reasons - `KeyNotFoundException: The given key 'card-this-seat-cannot-see' was not present in the dictionary` from the indexer, and `InvalidOperationException` escaping the whole refresh in both isolation cases where an `AggregateException` and a clean return were expected. Post-fix, seven Edit Mode fixture families selected by affectedness over the coordinator/RefreshAll path pass **188/188** (`MatchFeaturePresenterTests` 43, `MatchHudPresentationTests` 98, `SeatScopedMatchHudTests` 16, `MinimalHexGridSceneTests` 12, `ConfiguredMatchCutoverArchitectureTests` 7, `ApplicationArchitectureBoundaryTests` 6, `MinionBattleAgreementRenderingTests` 6), and focused Play Mode over the fixtures that construct the composed runtime passes **14/14** (`SeatScopedMatchHudPlayModeTests` 13, `OnlineMatchHudPlayModeTests` 1). **Tier T2 with its Play Mode question answered rather than assumed**: with nothing throwing the fan-out performs the same seven refreshes in the same order, so a journey would exercise a provably unchanged path, and the changed behaviour is an error path a journey cannot reach without breaking a presenter in production code - what is genuinely affected is the runtime's composition, so the owed Play Mode evidence is a regression over the fixtures that build it, with no visual inspection because no visual output changed. **Material limitation**: no unfiltered suite is claimed at this revision, because a peer worker holds uncommitted Core work in the shared tree; the unfiltered gate is deferred to that corrective's completion by the coordinator's evidence ruling. Housekeeping this milestone carried: `PHASE_8.md` stood at 14,457 words of a 15,000 hard limit with 543 free against roughly 2,100 words of queued UX-batch blocks, so milestones **8.25** through **8.25f2a** (5,115 words) moved verbatim to `PHASE_8_COMPLETED_2.md` under one extended routing sentence - the two-sided band was wide (W-P >= 1,707 and W <= 5,798), unlike 8.25d's 170-word squeeze - leaving the active shard at 9,401 and the destination at 12,089, both under the 12,750 warning; and `MATCH_HUD.md` sat at 4,494 of a 4,500 HARD limit with six words free while every milestone of this batch owes it, so a one-time pass bought 113 words by compressing the milestone-journal prose its own maintenance rule forbids and stating the derive-from-projection invariant once instead of five times, which is what paid for this milestone's own HUD sentence. | `f2bc61b4` |

## Milestone 8.35i Restore a snapshot captured while a choice is paused inside an Attack completion evidence

| ID | Outcome | Commit |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 8.35i | A snapshot captured while a capability choice is paused inside an Attack's card text now restores. Milestone 8.35f's rewind sweep reached it as a refused Undo at a history of 1,262 commands whose whole text was `Snapshot payload is malformed or internally inconsistent`, and the diagnosis relocated it out of the undo machinery: `MatchSession.Reconstruct` replays from a retained checkpoint and deserializing that pre-image threw. Replaying the same stream into a fresh host and round-tripping after every command named the cause at command 1,067 - `Snapshot pending effect choice names node 'noble-other', whose capability no longer requests a choice` - which is milestone 8.29c's documented recurrence, and shared authority rather than the HUD path 8.29c assumed. **The cause is a question asked with half of what it reads.** Candidates are never written to a payload: a capability's come from asking the trusted handler again, which is what stops a payload inventing an authoritative option, so a restore rebuilds the question. Arien's Noble Blade binds its selection to `EnclosingTargetBeforeConsequences` - "another unit adjacent to the target of this Attack" - and the restore path rebuilt that question with no enclosing Attack at all. The origin resolved to nobody, the handler offered no choice, and the payload was refused for describing a pause its own content "no longer" requested. The content had never changed. **The fix is where the ordering problem is, not where the throw is.** The Attack is written to the payload AFTER the frame that pauses inside it, so the frame cannot know it while being read; `ReadSkillResolutionAwaitingItsAttack` consumes every byte eagerly in payload order and returns the frame as a FUNCTION of the Attack, which the `AttackCardText` site applies once it has read it. Only the choice's materialization is deferred - re-asking a capability is a reading of state rather than a decode - and a caller with no enclosing Attack passes `EnclosingAttackFacts.None` and gets exactly what it got before. It is the same answer milestone 8.23 gave for the opposing hero, which the payload also names only after the frame: supply what the caller knows when it knows it, rather than reordering the wire or persisting a fact the engine can derive. The facts themselves are built by the authority's own `EnclosingAttackOf`, made `internal` for this, rather than by a second builder in the serializer - that method's own record already refuses "a second place the same fact could be wrong". **No tolerance was widened and nothing is caught more broadly**, which the brief required explicitly: a payload that really is malformed is refused exactly as it was, with the same reason. **No byte moved**: snapshot schema stays v35 and the wire stays `guards-private-online/4`. **A diagnostic change beside the fix, because the refusal text cost a day.** The serializer's generic wrapper now names its cause in the `Message` rather than only chaining it as an `InnerException`: callers surface a refusal by `Message` alone - `MatchSession`'s replay refusal reads exactly that and nothing else - so a chained cause was one nobody could read. The rejection reason and the refusal behaviour are unchanged. **Evidence.** `AttackCardTextSnapshotRestoreTests` is two cases. The focused one asserts the restored pause names the same node, the same candidates and the same selection bounds and then takes its own published answer, because asserting only that it restores would pass a build that restored the pause and forgot what it offered - the same defect one layer down. The second round-trips a snapshot after EVERY accepted command of the reproducing stream rather than at its end, because the defect was invisible until one specific intermediate state was captured, and it fails outright if the stream never passes through a choice paused inside an Attack. **The original 1,262-command seeded walk is deliberately not reproduced**, and the reason is measured rather than asserted: milestone 8.35g's turn release moved every seeded trajectory so that walk no longer reaches the state, and a scan of twenty-four seeds at 2,500 commands each on the official six-player board reaches an effect choice paused inside an Attack **zero times** - it needs Noble Blade played with a unit beside its target and its `may` confirmed. The recorded stream is replayed instead, which is the part of that walk that actually reproduces, and it reaches the state in a handful of commands. Pre-fix observation, taken with the fix off at its one behavioural site so the whole feature is inert: **0 passed / 2 failed**, both on the exact recorded message, and 2/2 after. Post-fix: Unity-free harness **1,975/1,975** against the 1,973 baseline - the two are this milestone's own cases - complete Unity Edit Mode **2,363/2,363** against the 2,361 gate, and complete Unity Play Mode **278/278**, unmoved as a Core-only fix should leave it. Play Mode was run by name rather than argued away on the schema being unchanged, and it earned it: `NobleBladesBeforeAttackPause_IsAnsweredAgainAfterARestoreAndAfterAnUndo` is a journey that restores across this exact pause, and it passes beside five other restore-path journeys. **Milestone 8.29c is not reopened.** What is fixed here is the shared-authority mechanism 8.35f recorded under its name; 8.29c's own HUD-path symptom has still never been reproduced, and fixing a recurrence is not evidence about a symptom. **Reported and left unclaimed**, as the brief directed: the separate offer/refusal defect these scans surfaced - an `AnswerEffectChoice` the projection offers and the rules refuse with `AttackActionRejected: TargetUnavailable` - reproduces at `6bfe61f9` on seeds 12 and 20 of the official six-player fixture at budget 4,000, and is nobody's milestone yet. The Unity-free harness was blocked by Application Control for about thirty-one minutes mid-milestone and cleared on the fourteenth retry, which is the second-longest outage recorded after 8.35f's forty-six. | `e21eb159` |

## Milestone 8.35j Light the whole top bar whenever the match is waiting on the viewing player completion evidence

| ID | Outcome | Commit |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 8.35j | The whole top strip carries the gold treatment exactly while the match is waiting on the viewing player. The user's words were "on players turn, the whole top bar needs to be gold, not just naration bar", refined the same day so the trigger is every moment the match waits on the viewer rather than their action turn alone. **The reading it replaces was not "your turn" and the amendment was right to make it be verified**: `NarrationVoice.YourMove` was a fifteen-way disjunction over the controls the round view model happened to expose, painting the 40-unit lane alone. That approximates well because a seat-scoped model carries only its own offers, and it has one real hole - rulebook p.7's commitment is not one of the eleven declared pending-decision families and exposes no round-action control, because the hand owns it, so the commonest wait in the game reached the player as the calm voice. The user's own list names an awaited commitment first. New `Presentation/Match/AwaitedInputQuery.cs` answers `IsWaitingOnViewer(projection, viewerHeroId)` from three projection facts and the fifteen-way disjunction is deleted: `Voice` and the strip's paint now read that one flag through the same viewer hero, so the two halves of the strip cannot come to disagree. `MatchStatusBarUnityView` paints `Root` - identity row and narration lane together - plus a shine underline; `RoundActionPresenter` gained the optional home-hero parameter on the terms `DirectorHandsPresenter` already takes one. **The design turned on a scoping fact that a plausible reading gets wrong.** Reading `SelectedActor` would light the attacker's bar while the DEFENDER is the one being asked, because the attacker is still the selected actor. It does not, because `AvailableTurnActions` and `AvailableHoldOffer` are published to a seat only while `offering = isActor && !standing` (`SeatProjection.SeatPendingSurface`), so a non-empty offer set already means "my turn AND nothing standing anywhere" - the Application layer's own scoping, not a re-derivation, and `ADecisionStandingOnAnotherSeat_LeavesThisViewersBarCold` pins it. The decision arm reads `DecisionContract`, which a seat projection publishes only to the owner ("null unless this seat owns it"), so one read covers all eleven families and is honest for the Director too. **The conformance hold names no family.** `EveryDeclaredDecisionFamily_ReachesTheBarThroughTheContract` proves by reflection that `PendingDecisionContract.Create` takes one argument per declared `DecisionFamilyInventory` member (11), so "a contract stands" is equivalent to "some declared family stands" and a twelfth family lights the bar the day it is declared, without this query being touched - the rule that makes the status derivation name every family, asked of the other surface that must not miss one. Pre-fix observation, isolated to the milestone's own claim by keeping the query present and removing only the commitment arm and the strip paint: `MatchFeaturePresenterTests` 49 cases with exactly 2 failing - the commitment arm answering False where True was expected, and the strip painting `RGBA(0.031, 0.039, 0.043)` where the warm gold was expected - while the family-conformance case passed on BOTH sides, which is what says the red is this milestone's claim rather than a broken suite. Validation, tier T2: **complete unfiltered Edit Mode 2,376/2,376 and complete unfiltered Play Mode 278/278**, both at the final revision in batch mode. **This pass also discharges milestone 8.35h's deferred unfiltered gate**, which was recorded as owed because a peer worker held uncommitted Core work at that revision; both milestones' breadth is served by this one run by the coordinator's ruling. **The run rode a deliberately frozen tree carrying a peer's uncommitted phase-1 rejoin work** (`RetainedSeatCapability.cs`, `FileRetainedSeatCapabilityStore.cs`, `FreshProcessRejoinTests.cs` and five modified files), harness-green at 1,979/1,979 and held still for the duration; the snapshot is preserved at `tmp/8-35d-8-35e-multiplayer-corrections/worker-b/m2-final-run-snapshot.txt`. **Count attribution, exact and with no residue**: 2,361 at the 8.35g gate, +2 milestone 8.35i, +3 milestone 8.35h, +6 this milestone, +3 the peer's `FreshProcessRejoinTests` and +1 a stale `PrivateSessionSeatClaimTests` case split in two, both uncommitted, giving 2,376; Play Mode is unmoved at 278 rather than unrun, and unfiltered is the point - the strip rides every journey the suite drives, so a moved Play Mode total would have meant the light changed what a player can do rather than how their turn looks. **An earlier attempt at this same unfiltered pass was stopped rather than worked around**: it read 2,372 with a single failure, `PrivateSessionArchitectureConformanceTests.TheSessionLayer_NamesNoSocketAndNoUnityType` naming the peer's `RetainedSeatCapability.cs`, a file this milestone never touched and which did not exist at that run's pre-run snapshot. A standing red blocks the gate whoever owns it; the peer fixed the violation and the pass was re-run green. `Testing/PLAYMODE_AND_SUITES.md` is deliberately NOT updated: its baseline table is frozen at revision 8.30c by its own rule, which routes new totals to the completion commit body and archive row instead. | `a543c266` |

## Milestone 8.35k Give a relaunched player a road back to their own reserved seat completion evidence

| ID | Outcome | Commit |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 8.35k | A player whose game died relaunches it and is returned to their own reserved seat. Milestone 8.34 built the entire return mechanism - reserved seat, host-minted capability, `ReclaimSeat`, rotated epoch - and it worked; what it never had was a road to it. The capability lived in one private field of one live `PrivateSessionClient`, and three ordinary player actions end that object: the process exiting, crashing, and pressing LEAVE. After any of them the lobby could offer only a FIRST join, refused by name because the seat is reserved - the 2026-09-03 playtest report verbatim, "rejoin does not work, if user disconnects, its seat stays away, and connection is refused". `IRetainedSeatCapabilityStore` (Application) states the storage contract and `FileRetainedSeatCapabilityStore` (Network) keeps one file per session and seat under an injected directory, beneath the transport-neutral port for the same reason `CryptoNonceSource` is there - the conformance scan that keeps engine and socket types out of the session layer does not govern the adapter. `PrivateLobbyViews` supplies `Application.persistentDataPath`, which is per-user and outside the installation, so a returned playtest bundle sweeps up nothing. **THE FIX'S OWN WORST DEFECT WAS CAUGHT BY THE NEW COHORT, NOT BY A FIXTURE.** The amendment says the entry is keyed by session correlation and seat, but `PrivateSessionIdentity.Correlation` is deterministic over the content tuple - its own record says two processes composed from the same content derive the same value and draw no entropy - so it names a BUILD and not a session, and a stored entry outlives the session that wrote it. Arming the reclaim on the stored entry alone made every SECOND session on a machine fail: the client sent a reclaim the new host had never minted, was refused `ReconnectNotAuthorized` and closed. All six clients of the rehearsal's host-loss cohort died exactly that way, which is worse than the bug being fixed - a player who finished one game could never join another. The remedy needs no wire field: **the roster already in the Welcome is the evidence that is about THIS session**, read three lines above the claim/reclaim fork, so a reclaim is armed only against a seat the host reports `Reserved` and a leftover entry is dropped. No tier below could have found it - `PrivateOnlineWireHarness` gives each seat a fresh store per instance, so every fixture is a machine's FIRST session, which is the same shape of blind spot as the defect the milestone exists to fix. **LEAVE forfeits and a crash does not**, the user's own rule: `Depart` takes a `forfeited` flag, and the wire already told the two apart because a deliberate leave sends `Goodbye` and a lost link sends nothing - no message kind was added. A forfeited seat returns to `Open` with its capability cleared; a dropped one stays `Reserved`. **A host-side `ReleaseSeat`** is the deliberate escape hatch for the evening a player's machine is gone: manual only, never on a timer - the reservation's absence of any clock is a feature, and an auto-release would hand a dropped player's seat to whoever reconnected first - and it refuses a `Claimed` seat, so a live player is never displaced. **A duplicate enum value found by reading, not by running**: `SeatReleased` was written `= 15` beside `MatchStarted = 15`, which C# accepts in silence, and since every consumer compares values a released seat WAS a match starting - `PrivateOnlineDrivers` would have logged `MATCH_STARTED` for a release and the rehearsal driver reads that same value to decide the match began, so a milestone meant to make the host log readable would have made it lie. Renumbered 16, and `LogHostEvent` gained the `SEAT_RELEASED` line beside `SEAT_RESERVED` where the contrast is the point. No behavioural case catches this cheaply, because the collision makes the two indistinguishable to the ASSERTION as well, so it is asserted about the type. Also fixed: the in-memory store concatenated its key's two parts bare, making `("AB","C")` and `("A","BC")` one entry - unreachable today and failing safe, corrected because "no caller can currently collide" is the wrong thing for a type deciding which secret goes to the wire to rest on. **Diagnostics**: `LOBBY_REFUSED reason=` closes the gap that left the failed playtest's refusal shown on one screen and written down nowhere; it earned itself back within the hour, naming `ReconnectNotAuthorized` in one grep when the cohort first went red. **No wire, protocol, schema or content change**: `PrivateSessionProtocol.cs`, `PrivateSessionFrameCodec.cs`, `PrivateSessionMessages.cs`, `PrivateSessionIdentity.cs` and `AuthoritativeSnapshotSerializer.cs` are untouched; the only frames the changed production code names are `Goodbye`, `ReclaimSeat` and `RosterUpdate`, all pre-existing, and a released seat reaches every client as the roster's existing `Open` occupancy. Protocol stays `guards-private-online/4`, schema v35. `PrivateSessionEventKind` is host-local diagnostics, encoded nowhere. **Privacy, measured rather than asserted**: 18 distinct capability values were read off disk after a three-cohort run and none appears in any of the 33 rehearsal logs; the runner additionally greps every log for the shape a leak would take, beside the session key it already refused. **The rehearsal's third cohort, green.** `-Topology SixClient -FaultPlan Full` now runs a rejoin cohort beside the match and host-loss ones, differing from the match cohort's reconnect in exactly one way: the designated seat's PROCESS exits rather than only its socket. No `Goodbye` is sent, deliberately - `Application.Quit` reaches the lobby's `OnDestroy`, which disposes the transport and sends nothing, so the host sees a crash; a polite exit would have forfeited the seat and the cohort would have proved the opposite of what it is for. The passing run's evidence is a claim no single process can make: `rejoin-process-exiting seat=red-player-02 version=20` and `EXIT 0` in one log; `SEAT_RESERVED ordinal=3 seat=red-player-02` then `SEAT_RECLAIMED ordinal=7 seat=red-player-02 epoch=2` on the host; and in a DIFFERENT log `rejoin-return-pass version=25` followed by `REHEARSAL_OUTCOME version=1831 cause=FinalWaveFlipped winner=red-team`, identical to the host's own. A relaunched process starts with the scripted agreement choreography already behind it, which is a phase and not an assertion to dodge - the first attempt merely skipped the assertions, and because `DriveInitiativeAgreement` branches on `initiativeFinalObserved` to choose between answering ordinarily and forcing the scripted disagreement, the returned process went on forcing one while the other five answered ordinarily and six processes deadlocked at version 347. Two runner defects were fixed on the way: `Get-FirstFailure` indexed `[0]` into a single-match pipeline result, which in PowerShell is a bare string whose `[0]` is a CHAR, so it threw "[System.Char] does not contain a method named 'Trim'" and replaced the real failure with a PowerShell error at the one moment the runner exists to explain what went wrong - and every failure it reports is a first failure, so the single-match case is the common one. One run in five failed on the PRE-EXISTING match cohort timing out mid-match at version 4005 after an accepted submission with no refusals; the passing run reached its outcome at 1831 on identical code, so that is the long random walk's own non-determinism and is reported rather than claimed as fixed. Evidence: harness **1,982/1,982** (the completion commit body says 1,981, which was measured one case before the last of the three structural cases was written; the Edit Mode total, taken on the final tree, is the arithmetic that settles it), complete unfiltered Edit Mode **2,379/2,379** (2,376 committed baseline plus this milestone's three structural cases; seven cases in total from 2,372, the other four having been counted as uncommitted tree content in milestone 8.35j's run), and complete unfiltered Play Mode **278/278**, unmoved as an Application/Network change adding no journey should leave it. Pre-fix observation was taken five times, each with one fix disabled at its own site: the client not consulting the store (`Rejected` instead of `Seated`), the Goodbye reserving instead of forfeiting (3 cases red), `ReleaseSeat` releasing nothing, `SeatReleased = 15` (reported as "15 is MatchStarted and SeatReleased"), and the bare key concatenation (1 entry where 2 were expected). | `04afcedf` |

## Milestone 8.35l Refuse an action a standing rule modification has already barred, and name what barred it completion evidence

| ID | Outcome | Commit |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 8.35l | The SIXTH amendment's corrective half, from the playtest report "I played Sabrinas swap with minion card, and it didnt work beacuse wasps gold. I was still offered the action, which was kinda sus, but even worse i then had it canceled withought knowing why." Worker B's diagnosis measured three faults with one root cause and confirmed the worst of them: the card was **consumed for nothing** - resolved into the turn area with rulebook p.7's one action spent - while every command was ACCEPTED, so no refusal existed for any surface to explain. **Fix 1, the binding.** Milestone 6.8l's narrowing put each CANDIDATE to the consuming node; Back to Back's candidates are minions and its Swap binds the minion's SPACE, so every candidate came back refused, the declaration was recorded as "a correction rather than an omission", and the narrowing was silently lost across five of six heroes. `EffectSelectionDeclaration.ConsumedParameters` now returns `(parameter, sourceOutput)` pairs - the binding always knew which output it read - and the new `IEffectAnticipatedOutcomeCapabilityHandler.AnticipateOutcomes(context, chosen)` says what an answer would publish, lifted out of each handler's own `Resume` so the facts put to the consumer before the offer and the facts it receives after the answer are one derivation. **Fix 1b, the pair, which most constrained the shape.** `ChooseTargetPairHandler` implemented no selection interface and declared no consumer, so narrowing there was UNAVAILABLE rather than undeclared; it now declares both. The diagnosis proposed per-piece narrowing with a shape-specific caveat; what shipped is the general form - a candidate is offered iff SOME COMPLETE ANSWER containing it is accepted - which needs no caveat, degenerates exactly to the single-selection question at a floor of one, is milestone 8.29d's own "at least one complete legal continuation" read at candidate scope, and short-circuits on the first working partner so an all-legal board costs one question per candidate. `LargestNarrowableAnswer = 2` refuses to enumerate above a pair rather than guess. `JudgeSelection` then compares against the selection's own MINIMUM instead of zero, which is the load-bearing clause: a board holding one legal minion is correctly narrowed and still unanswerable, and without it that board still offered, still spent the card, and the fix only appeared to work. **Fix 2, and it needed more than the diagnosis specified.** Carrying `actor-space@1`'s output forward was necessary and insufficient - measured: the walk short-circuits at the first node that can apply, and that node reports `Applied`, so Tigerclaw's Blend Into Shadows was STILL offered after the carry-forward landed. The missing half is that a node which only publishes a fact has not made the action worth taking; handlers declare `PublishesWithoutApplying`, `Judge` reads their `Applied` as `Verdict.Empty`, and the sequence carries what they published to the next step, so the Place behind the bridge is judged rather than skipped. Two nodes on the affected path declare it (`actor-space@1`, `adjacent-terrain-presence@1`) plus the four selections, for which it is the honest statement that selecting mutates nothing. **Fix 3, the reason.** `RestrictionQuery.TryGetRestriction` has always returned the modification "whose source explains the refusal" and every caller discarded it; Swap, SwapWithActor and Place now hand it back through `IEffectRefusalExplainingCapabilityHandler`, the walk records the first in execution order, and `ActionOffer` - which had NO reason channel, only a bare bool - carries `ActionUnavailability` (barred activity, controlling hero, source card, Active effect). `AppliedRuleModification` gained `SourceCardInstanceId` because a player recognises a card, not an effect id. **Fix 4** is deliberately not built: `RefuseCapability` already records `ImpossibleMandatory` with its node, so the fact a fizzle notice needs is already published, and the surface is the UX track's. **Wire `guards-private-online/4` -> `/5`**: offers cross as `NetworkActionOfferRow`, and the reason is CARRIED rather than derived because a seat holds only a flat list of the cards standing effects came off, which cannot say which of them refused this action - deriving it would be a second reading of the rules, free to disagree with the host's. One bool on every offer, five fields only where a reason exists; the decoder refuses a payload claiming a reason on an available offer or a kind outside this build's vocabulary. Content revisions `arien-8-35l-rev-1` and `sabina-8-35l-rev-1` with `VerifiedContentIdentityTests` re-pinned, and **the artwork catalog re-pinned with them** - a content-revision move also owes `ConfiguredCardArtworkCatalog.json`, which the harness cannot see and which only the unfiltered Unity run caught. Tigerclaw needed no content change at all, which is what proves Fix 2 is not selection narrowing in disguise. Two governed checks moved as this milestone's own claim: the modification seam rule follows `TryGetRestriction`, the member the engine now uses, while still asserting the boolean form resolves; and `SeatBoundedUndoTests`' seeded walk moved seed 1 -> 14 after an offline 24-seed scan, because a milestone that stops offering something reroutes a walk that chooses from offers - seed 1 reached no Attack at all and the case's own counter caught that rather than passing vacuously, while 14 observes five. Pre-fix red by the inert-vocabulary technique (types, members and helpers left compiling; only the binding substitution, the minimum clause, the publishing-only reading and the two content declarations reverted): **7 failed / 4 passed**, the four being the controls and the legal-pair case, which correctly hold in both states and are what say the fixture measures the pin rather than the geometry. Post-fix 11/11. Baselines harness **1,982 -> 1,993**. Reported and deliberately NOT folded in: the pre-existing offered-then-refused anomaly, now precisely located - Arien's Living Tsunami `up to 1` node offers both a `Confirm` carrying an empty selection and a `Decline`, the same answer, and the rules accept one and refuse the other with `AttackActionRejected: TargetUnavailable`; seeds 12/20 stopped throwing only because this milestone rerouted the walk, and seed 114 still throws, which is what proves it uncovered rather than fixed. | `abb31e05` |

## Milestone 8.35m Shine every awaited hero's tray and card, and dim the rest of the hand while one resolves completion evidence

| ID | Outcome | Commit |

| 8.35m | The SEVENTH amendment's other two scopes. `AwaitedInputQuery.IsWaitingOnViewer` (8.35j) answers one question at viewer scope; this reads the same question at hero scope (`IsHeroAwaited`) and card scope (`TrayCardViewModel.IsAwaitedAnswer`/`IsDimmedByResolution`) instead of a fresh derivation - "one predicate, three scopes" in the user's own words. **The first duty was to resolve the handoff's own load-bearing open question before building anything, and it did not resolve cleanly.** Hero scope needs, for an arbitrary hero (not only the viewer), who currently owns a standing decision; `IMatchReadModel` carries no such fact - only the concrete `SeatProjection.DecisionOwner` does, and that member is not on the interface every Presentation surface binds to. Reading `IMatchReadModel.cs` in full and `SeatProjection.OwnerOfStandingDecision` (`:483-581`) settled which of the amendment's five named hero-scope cases are reachable: the commitment arm (`Commitments`, public per-hero, the viewer-scope arm generalized), the release owner (`PendingTurnRelease.ActorId`, unscoped by 8.35g's own decision), and a standing team agreement's undecided participant (`TeamAgreement.Participants`, public by its own doc comment - "the proposal is identical for all six seats"). The leveling hero, the defense answerer, the respawn chooser, and the acting hero are NOT reachable - each needs the decision-owner fact that lives only on `SeatProjection.DecisionOwner` - and a naive `SelectedActor` read for the acting-hero arm would reproduce, one level up, the exact bug 8.35j's own `MayTakeATurnAction` exists to avoid: the attacker's tray would keep shining through the defender's own standing decision. Findings recorded in `tmp/8-35d-8-35e-multiplayer-corrections/NOTES.md`; **the coordinator ruled option (B)**: ship card scope and resolution focus in full, hero scope limited to the three derivable arms, the four deferred arms recorded as a non-goal pointing at the Core track's own event-channel milestone, which will add the missing per-hero decision-owner fact to `IMatchReadModel` in the same wire bump as the TENTH amendment's event log. **Card scope reduces to one existing signal plus one new one.** The ordinary hand tray's `IsSelectable` (`BuildTray`'s `canSelect`) already IS "a legal answer to commitment right now", so `IsAwaitedAnswer` for a hand card is that same value, reused rather than re-derived; every offered-card surface - `CreateCardFace` (Defense, effect-choice, and removal-prevention candidates) and `CurrentRoundCards.DeckCard` (p.15 upgrade candidates) - sets it unconditionally true, because being drawn there at all already means the authority is offering the card. **Resolution focus reads the same `resolvingCardInstanceId` `BuildTray` already computed**: the one revealing card gets `isAwaitedAnswer` too (it is the focal point of what the match is waiting on the player to finish, exactly as a clickable candidate is), and every OTHER `Group == Available` card in the same hand gets `isDimmedByResolution`; the two are mutually exclusive by construction (a resolving card's own group is never `Available`) and the constructor throws if a caller ever tries to set both. A mid-resolution Defense candidate shines and is never dimmed for the same reason - it is a different view-model instance on a different surface, never a member of the hand list the dim iterates. Both flags are painted in the one shared `ConfiguredCardFaceView.Build`, so a card cannot shine on one surface and not another: a new outer ring (`gd-card-awaited-shine`, `MatchHudTheme.ShineGlow`) outside the existing black perimeter and cyan selection outline, and `MatchHudTheme.DimmedByResolutionOpacity` (= `HudElements.MarkUnavailable`'s own 0.45, one shared "look elsewhere" figure rather than a second number). The hero rail's own gold treatment (`HeroOverviewsUnityView.BuildOverview`) reads `HeroOverviewViewModel.IsAwaited` the same way the top bar reads `IsAwaitingViewerInput`, tinting the rail's background and outer border while keeping the team-accent side border and the reserved viewed-hero top edge untouched. **A pre-existing test encoded the behaviour this milestone deliberately supersedes, and updating it is the milestone's own claim, not a regression.** `MatchHudPresentationTests.TheCardBeingResolved_IsDrawnAtFullStrengthWhileItsTurnRuns` asserted a held future-turn card's opacity keyword stayed `Null` while a sibling resolved; that is exactly the pre-M3 reading the resolution-focus amendment overrides, so the case was rewritten to assert the dimmed opacity and the shine/no-shine split instead of reverted. **A second, live milestone-id collision, caught by a Unity run rather than by reading the roadmap.** `8.35l` was claimed at definition time exactly as the ceremony requires - neither `PHASE_8.md` nor `NOTES.md` named it - and collided anyway: the parallel Core worker's uncommitted, unstaged swap-feasibility corrective had already bumped `ArienCardContent`/`SabinaCardContent`'s `ContentRevision` to `"arien-8-35l-rev-1"`/`"sabina-8-35l-rev-1"`, invisible on disk and surfaced only when a filtered Edit Mode pass hit `ConfiguredCardArtworkCatalogTests`/`MatchHudPresentationTests`, which compare a card's artwork-catalog revision against its `ContentRevision` and threw a revision-mismatch `InvalidOperationException` naming both strings. Renamed to `8.35m` throughout every file this milestone owns (nine files, sixteen comment occurrences, grepped clean) without touching the parallel worker's uncommitted Core files. **Pre-fix red, the inert-vocabulary technique**: `AwaitedInputQuery.IsHeroAwaited`'s body reverted to `return false`, `BuildTray`'s `isAwaitedAnswer`/`isDimmedByResolution` arguments and `CreateCardFace`/`DeckCard`'s `isAwaitedAnswer` argument reverted to `false`, every new property/method/parameter left compiling and present - ten of this milestone's fourteen new or changed cases went red (the other four are negative-space/privacy checks that correctly hold in both states) beside the same six pre-existing artwork-catalog failures the post-fix run also carries, proving the red was this milestone's own claim and not a broken suite; restored and re-verified green after. **The shared tree's own uncommitted Core work means no unfiltered gate is claimed at this revision** - the same limitation 8.35h recorded for the same reason, deferred until that corrective commits; the `Tools/DotnetTests` harness also would not load a test host this session (`Test host process crashed`), an intermittent, previously-documented environment fact unrelated to this Presentation-only change. Evidence instead is the two throwaway compile probes (`Core+Application+Network+Presentation` and the same plus `Assets/Tests/EditMode`, both `dotnet build -t:Rebuild` clean) and a filtered complete Unity Edit Mode run over every fixture the seam grep named (`MatchFeaturePresenterTests`, `MatchHudPresentationTests`, `SeatScopedMatchHudTests`, `HeroRailPortraitTests`, `BroganAlliedHeroCardTests`, `ClearPresentationTests`, `DirectorHandPresentationTests`, `MinionBattleAgreementRenderingTests`, `ApplicationArchitectureBoundaryTests`): **214/220 passed**, the six failures all the pre-existing, independently-diagnosed artwork-catalog contamination above (none in a case this milestone added or touched), and every one of this milestone's own fourteen cases green. No Play Mode journey is owed: the change is view-model and UI Toolkit rendering only, on the same reasoning 8.35h and 8.35j recorded for their own Presentation-only changes - no new command, no new interaction path, nothing a journey could reach that a rendering case does not already cover more directly. Refusals are classified before acting, each with its own reason (`UnknownSeat`, `SeatOpen`, `SeatOccupied`, `NoSeatNamed`); a `false` return from `ReleaseSeat` itself past that pre-check (a race against the same transport frame the serve loop drains the same tick) is `reason=SeatChanged`, named honestly as a race rather than invented ahead of the operation. **Not a kick**: an occupied seat is refused exactly as `ReleaseSeat` itself refuses it. **The success marker reuses the existing `SEAT_RELEASED` vocabulary rather than inventing a second one**: `ReleaseSeat`'s one call site records its event with connection ordinal 0, verified by grep to be the ONLY one of the event kind's exactly two call sites that does - the LEAVE-forfeit path (`Depart`) always carries a live connection's own ordinal, and `TcpSessionTransport` assigns ordinals starting at 1 (`Interlocked.Increment` from a zero-initialized field), so 0 can never be a real connection. `PrivateOnlineMarkers.LogHostEvent`'s `SeatReleased` case now reads that existing signal to append `reason=manual`, leaving the LEAVE-forfeit line's format untouched. **A real regression of its own, caught by the first complete Edit Mode run and fixed rather than absorbed**: `PrivateLobbyTests.NoLobbySource_ReachesMatchAuthority` scans the lobby's three own sources for a forbidden literal-text list (`.Execute(` among them) guarding the lobby driver off match authority (`IMatchHost.Execute`) directly - `HostConsoleCommands.Execute(...)`'s first name tripped it by a naive text-match collision only, a rule this call was never an instance of.
