## Milestone 8.35p Move a charge before its attack resolves, and wall a card-text attack against its own seat's rewind completion evidence

| ID | Outcome | Commit |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 8.35p | **The NINTH amendment's corrective, and it took three changes rather than the one the diagnosis predicted.** The user, from live multiplayer testing: "Brogan should move for his charge before the attack resolves ... They move as soon as they select, and then they attack." Worker C measured the cause as effect-graph ordering - not a rules defect (targets were already measured from the destination) and not presentation batching (every pause publishes a projection) - and measured the affected set as exactly four playable faces, of which only Brogan's three ask twice. **THE REORDER.** `CreateCharge` becomes `[actor-space, choose-charge-line, move, choose-target, set-attack-target]`: the charge selection asks only where he lands and publishes only `destination`, the Move commits on that answer in the same accepted command, and the unit is named afterwards by an ordinary `choose-target@2` with no declared activity, so immunity still inherits the enclosing Attack. No capability is registered, the persisted schema stays at **v35**, and no wire protocol moves. The showcase `ChargeLineNodes()` follows, making true a summary it had claimed since 8.21. **WHY IT IS A REORDERING**, asserted rather than argued: `ChargeLine_OffersOnlyDestinationsWhoseTargetSetSurvivesTheMove` computes, for every offered destination, the enemy units within 1 of it before the Move and the enemy units within 1 of the hero after it, through the one shared `RosterPresenceQuery`, and asserts set equality - it passes on both sides of the change, which is the point. p.10's stop is unmoved: only destinations with a legal target are offered, and that filter now stands before the Move. **THE DEVIATION, MEASURED.** The plain reorder greyed the charge in exactly the position the card exists for - Mad Dash offered at HEAD in all four staged placements where the only reachable enemy was not already adjacent, and offered in none of them after the reorder alone; offered only where an enemy was ALREADY beside him. These faces bind by card text, so availability runs `EffectOptionFeasibilityQuery.CanCompleteMandatoryAttackTargetBinding`, whose walk judged the new adjacency question from the pre-Move square and reported `Blocked`. The walk now carries `afterAnUntakenMove` and stops JUDGING - never stops walking, so it still reaches the binding - once it crosses a `synthetic-direct-move@3` node, naming the typed Move contract exactly as that walk already names `set-attack-target@1`. It can only suppress a refusal, never manufacture one, which is that query's own standing direction; nothing is offered that cannot bind, because the destination node stands before the Move and is judged normally. Blast radius by grep: the only card-text-bound Attacks holding a Move before their binding are Brogan's three charges and Blink Strike, and Blink Strike has no capability node between its Move and its binding, so its verdict is unchanged - deliberately, per its own declaration that asking twice "would let a player pick a line and then a unit that is not on it". **THE ATTACK WALL, CONFIRMED BY DRIVING BEFORE ANYTHING CHANGED**, which is what the diagnosis demanded and what 8.35f's own lesson requires of a state a walk merely passes through. Driven at HEAD through the real `MatchHost` seat path on the official six-player session: the charge text opened at accepted 2380, both questions were answered with the hero still on `(2, 1)`, the Attack settled at 2384, and the acting seat's rewind was **offered and ACCEPTED** - 2384 back to 2383, taking back the very answer that named the target. `RefreshSeatUndoWatermark` now raises the wall on a standing resolution whose `Target.IsDefined` **and** on the transition where a resolution that was standing is gone; the second clause is not redundant, because naming the victim binds the target and completes the whole p.14 action inside one command, leaving nothing standing to read - a state-only rule was measured doing nothing. Before the target is named the same resolution stands without one, so the committed Move stays rewindable, which is the amendment's own rule. **EVIDENCE.** Three isolated failing-then-passing observations, each against the revision that isolates its own fix, in a `git worktree` at `4bfe6b45`: the reorder (`Expected: Applied But was: NeedsChoice`; `Expected: charge-destination But was: charge-destination-target`), the walk (`Expected: True But was: False`, with the reorder applied and the walk at HEAD - and the other two charge cases then fail too, which is what says the walk change is required by the reorder rather than incidental), and the wall (`Its own seat is published no rewind over a resolved card-text Attack. Expected: False But was: True`, with reorder and walk applied and `MatchHost` at HEAD). **The permanent player-path gate was proved offline in about a second**: a console project compiling `Core/**` plus the real `HeroPlayerPathMatrix.cs` dumped the derived shape set of all **114 faces** before and after, and the diff is EMPTY - the charges keep both board shapes, the space now contributed by `choose-charge-line` and the unit by `choose-target`, so `CapabilityShapes` moved and no row did. No Unity launch was spent on the derivation. Only Brogan's content identity moved (`brogan-8-21-rev-1` -> `brogan-8-35p-rev-1`), with his eighteen `ConfiguredCardArtworkCatalog.json` entries re-pinned in the same edit - the 8.35l trap, that fixture being harness-EXCLUDED - and the five other heroes byte-identical, which is the measurement that says the reorder reached his content and nobody else's. **AN ID COLLISION, RECORDED BECAUSE THE EXISTING RULE DID NOT PREVENT IT:** this milestone was built as 8.35o and renumbered before commit. The pre-claim grep over the roadmap AND the tree - the 8.35l lesson - came back clean, because the parallel worker's own 8.35o existed only in a `tmp/` draft at that moment. Grepping proves an id free of committed and uncommitted work; it cannot prove another agent is not about to take it, and only the coordinator or a re-verification after the peer's commits can. **VALIDATION.** Cross-platform harness 1,993/1,993 at HEAD and 1,998/1,998 at the final revision, unblocked all session at about six minutes a run. Complete Unity Edit Mode **2,435 / 2,435** (baseline 2,430; the +5 is this milestone's own, verified per fixture in the results XML) and Play Mode **278 / 278**, unmoved rather than unrun. The session-start baseline, before any edit here, was 1,992 passed / 1 failed / 1,993 total, the single red being the parallel worker's uncommitted lobby work and recorded as inherited. | `6c516a9b` |

## Milestone 8.35q Fill empty seats with spawned test drivers for solo playtesting completion evidence

| ID | Outcome | Commit |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 8.35q | THIRTEENTH amendment: "I find it really hard to test running 6 clients at once. Can you make AI play the game or something so i just play with one client only?" Exposed the existing rehearsal/SixClient machinery's real-lobby, real-HUD, offers-intersected-with-AuthorizedCommands pattern as a shipped, user-facing practice affordance - explicitly NOT an AI opponent, a durable non-goal left unamended. **Host affordance is a lobby control, not a launcher flag** (`PrivateLobbyControl.FillWithTestDrivers`, "FILL WITH TEST DRIVERS"), offered on the host's own screen exactly while a seat is still Open and the match has not started - grounded in 8.35o's own finding that the interactive lobby (`Tools/PlaytestLaunchers/HOST - Start Server.bat`) is the only host path a real solo player actually uses; the raw `-guardsPrivateHost` CLI role stays CI/rehearsal-only. **Reused the rehearsal machinery's pattern rather than its code**: `PrivateOnlineRehearsalDriver.AuthorizedProjectedCommands` (the offers-∩-AuthorizedCommands intersection the amendment names) bumped `internal` and called directly, zero duplication; a new, deliberately separate `PrivateSeatDriver` (new file) carries the shipped behavior - real lobby join, answers whatever it is offered at a configurable 2 s default think-delay - rather than growing the 1184-line CI-only rehearsal driver into two things at once. New CLI vocabulary (`-guardsTestDriver`, `-guardsTestDriverSeat`, optional think-seconds/seed) with endpoint/key carried only through environment variables, the same command-line-secrecy rule the rehearsal driver already follows, independently satisfied rather than borrowed. **Spawning is the host's own real OS child process** (`System.Diagnostics.Process.Start` on its own running executable, `-batchmode -nographics`, a distinct log path per child - several copies of one build sharing a default log path would otherwise fight over it), reached through `ITestDriverProcessLauncher`, a small seam that makes the spawning DECISION Edit-Mode-testable without ever starting a real process; `RealTestDriverProcessLauncher` is production. Killed on session end (`Leave()` + `OnDestroy()` safety net) - verified against the rehearsal runner's own process-management shape, not assumed. **Labeling is deliberately host-screen-only**: `PrivateLobbySeatRow.IsTestDriverSeat` is sourced from the host's own local spawn record, never the roster - putting it on the wire would be the protocol change the amendment says isn't expected; recorded as a deliberate narrowing rather than silently done. No new session-layer member, no protocol change - verified by grep and by the regression completing under the unmoved wire version. **A real conformance defect found and fixed, not routed around**: `PrivateLobbyTests.TheLobbyModel_IsBuiltFromTheSessionContractsAndNothingElse`, a reflection check enumerating `PrivateLobbyModel`'s factory parameter types against a declared allow-list, correctly flagged `ForHost`'s new `ISet<SeatId>` parameter as outside "the session contracts". Fixed by extending the allow-list with a recorded justification (host-local knowledge, reported not decided - the same shape `string feedback` already has), not by weakening the check. **Two live-fire bugs in the bot's own decision logic, found and fixed via a fast offline reproduction rather than repeated 20-minute live cycles**: (1) the Initiative-tie candidate was picked at random per bot rather than deterministically first, so independently-seeded teammates proposed different tied cards with no revision logic to converge them - a disagreement nobody was scripted to resolve, hanging forever; fixed to always pick `Candidates[0]`, matching the proven rehearsal driver's own "ordinary" branch, since a team AGREEMENT is the one family where legality and convergence are not the same property. (2) A pending Minion Battle choice and a standing MinionBattle team agreement can be simultaneously non-null - the agreement is the standing proposal being confirmed, coexisting with a still-changeable underlying selection - and checking the agreement first (as the code originally did) made every bot on the choosing side wait forever on a turn-condition that could only become true once the choice stopped changing, which nothing was left free to do; fixed by reading `PendingMinionBattleChoice.CanConfirm` first, again matching the proven rehearsal driver's exact priority order missed when first simplifying it. Both reproduced in 9-10 seconds each via a throwaway harness probe replaying the exact decision logic against a LOCAL (no network) `IMatchHost`, versus the ~20 minutes a live process-cohort cycle costs per attempt - deleted before commit, per the harness-probe convention. **A near-miss caught before it cost a Unity launch**: a first Play Mode test pressed the real control through an `internal` test seam that would not have compiled in real Unity - `GuardsDigital.Presentation`'s `InternalsVisibleTo` deliberately excludes Play Mode (recorded at 8.30g) - caught by re-reading the actual grant rather than trusting the one-assembly compile probe, which cannot see this class of error; removed, coverage stays at the Edit Mode presenter/model level (no MonoBehaviour lifecycle needed - `PrivateLobbyPresenter` is a POCO) plus the real end-to-end regression below. **Evidence, all measured in an isolated `git worktree` at HEAD `04d2d090`** (the shared tree carried the concurrent event-channel worker's own uncommitted, transiently-uncompiling WIP touching `IMatchReadModel.cs`/`PrivateSessionHost.cs`/`PrivateSessionClient.cs`/`SeatProjection.cs`/`NetworkMatchClient.cs` and more - confirmed by `git status`, none of it this milestone's; the isolated worktree, built by copying `Library` and this milestone's own diff onto a clean HEAD checkout, sidesteps it entirely rather than racing it, the same technique 8.35n's own notes record): the regression itself, `Tools/Invoke-PrivateOnlineRehearsal.ps1 -Topology Solo -FaultPlan None` (new topology; `ValidateSet` extended, `GUARDS_TESTDRIVER_THINK_SECONDS` set on the runner's own environment before launching the host so a truly random-legal match - no strategy, by design - finishes in a bounded window instead of a human-paced one) - **PASSED**: `REHEARSAL_OUTCOME version=1776 cause=FinalWaveFlipped winner=blue-team`, six distinct `SEAT_CLAIMED` lines from only two launched processes (the scripted seat plus the host), both tracked processes exiting 0 on their own, and a `Get-Process` sweep confirming zero surviving `GuardsDigitalSmoke.exe` after both exited - the host's own proof it killed its five spawned children. **The isolated worktree's own prepared-private-asset gap, closed for full parity rather than footnoted.** A clean `git worktree` checkout carries none of the untracked private board/card/hero-token art the main tree had already prepared locally (`Tools/Prepare-OfficialBoardArtwork.ps1`/`Prepare-HeroTokens.ps1`/`Prepare-CardArtwork.ps1`'s own ignored outputs) - first read as 5 Edit Mode self-skips plus 19 Play Mode failures (every hero's own piece/pointer-target/undo-restart journey, each naming the missing prepared token by name: "Wasp's official circular token is prepared, so the piece must be wearing it rather than the generated prism the fallback draws"), all unrelated to this milestone's diff by their own messages. Copied the three already-prepared private asset directories (`Assets/Resources/Private/{OfficialBoard,Heroes,Cards}`, all untracked, all already present in the main tree, none referenced by this milestone's own diff) from the main tree into the worktree rather than accept a partial or footnoted result. Complete unfiltered Edit Mode **2,451/2,451, 0 failed, 0 skipped**. Complete unfiltered Play Mode **278/278, 0 failed, 0 skipped**, exactly the stated baseline and unmoved, as an Application/Presentation change adding no new journey should leave it. Non-goals honored: no AI opponent, strategy, or difficulty; no wire/protocol change; no in-process second driving path; no change to the CI-only rehearsal driver's scripted cohorts beyond the new `Solo` mode this regression itself exercises. | `d2de7f1a` |

| 8.35r | The TENTH amendment's event channel plus the who-is-awaited fact, gathered into ONE protocol bump as the coordinator's scope ruling required. Three members join `IMatchReadModel`, all **carried, none excused** - the wire-growth ratchet's exclusion floor was already at its limit of 7. `MatchLog` is the seat-public history the amendment asked for, selected per `worker-b/EVENT_LOG_CONTRACT.md` and derived from the retained rule-event log; `DecisionOwner` publishes who the authority is waiting on, which makes the four tray arms milestone 8.35m deferred (levelling hero, defence answerer, respawn chooser, and the acting-hero arm's decision-standing correctness) derivable by every viewer; `UndoMarker` names the standing rewind. **The derivation source was forced, not chosen**: `MatchAggregate.Restore` installs an empty round-event stream while restoring the rule-event log in full, so only the latter survives a snapshot restore - a channel built on round events would come back blank and break the contract's "restore redraws the log for free". **The transport was decided by measurement**: the largest complete projection is 30,575 B against the synchronization sweep's half-bound guard of 32,747, leaving 2,173 B, so the log cannot ride inside the seat record; a first implementation gave it its own message kind and the fault-injection suite falsified that too - a second host frame consumes a gameplay sequence and absorbed the very gap a dropped projection must leave for the client's recovery to see, while the host's per-connection send bookkeeping was silently invalidated by any dropped frame. It now rides inside the seat projection message beside the record, as the whole 256-line window, inheriting the ordered-stream discipline already proven for the projection; all nine reds went green with no fixture edit. Whole projection-and-log frame measured at 47,654 B against a 65,494 B bound, guarded at 80%. **Reconnect-history semantics, recorded**: an arriving seat, a returning seat and one that never left all receive the same bounded recent window, never an archive. **The undo marker is a read-model fact, not a synthetic log entry** - undo appends nothing, so an entry would have to be stored, which would move the snapshot schema and break the channel's derivation invariant, and its sequence would collide with the real event that reuses it after truncation; the recorded limit is that a rewind played past leaves no permanent line. **The blocked-action line names no blocker**: `SkillEffectNodeStopped` translates from a generic `ImpossibleMandatory` outcome with no modification in hand, so naming one would need a second provenance path out of effect execution - milestone 8.35l's offer-side `ActionUnavailability` stays the single channel. Protocol `guards-private-online/5` -> **/6**; **snapshot schema v35 unmoved** (nothing new is persisted). Defect found by re-reading the row table rather than by any run: the Clear row emitted two lines per clear, invisible to every seeded walk because they perform zero Clears. Selection refinements recorded: `Commitment.RevealCompleted` and `Round.MinionBattleSelectionChanged` not carried, and the Move line is a fold over committed steps because `MovementActionCompleted` states neither origin nor destination. Harness 1,998 -> **2,020** (`MatchLogChannelTests` 13, `PrivateOnlineEventChannelTests` 9). | 76bff78f |

## Milestone 8.35t Dial the address the host actually bound, not a hardcoded loopback constant completion evidence

| ID | Outcome | Commit |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 8.35t | Corrective, live-defect-driven. Milestone 8.35q's FILL WITH TEST DRIVERS hardcoded every spawned test-driver child's dial address to `"127.0.0.1"` regardless of what the host actually bound; a host bound to this machine's own real address - the shape the tester instructions' own solo-practice paragraph invites, and the one rehearsing a real port-forwarded setup even alone requires - spawned children dialing an address nothing was listening on, each pressing Join successfully by every measure the game itself published and then sitting forever, since `PrivateLobbyPresenter.Press` returns true the instant the control is offered and says nothing about whether the join it triggered reached anyone; the live report named 34 orphaned `GuardsDigitalSmoke.exe` processes left running after repeated FILL presses tried to work around it. **Three fixes, all root-caused rather than patched over the symptom**: (1) `PrivateSessionEndpoint.ResolveLocalDialAddress()` (Network) resolves a wildcard bind to loopback and returns any concrete address verbatim, confirmed directly against a real `TcpListener`/`TcpClient` pair before writing it - lives in the adapter, not beside its one Presentation caller, because `PrivateSessionArchitectureConformanceTests.SocketTypes_LiveOnlyInTheNetworkAdapter` confines socket-typed decisions there; `PrivateSeatDriverOptions.TryParse`'s matching `IsLoopback` restriction is dropped for the same reason - `-guardsPrivateClient` was already free to dial anywhere with any key, the session key being what actually gates entry. (2) `PrivateLobbyDriver.BeginJoining`'s two silent failure paths (a malformed endpoint, a failed `Dial()`) now set `LastJoinFailure` and log through `PrivateOnlineMarkers.Fail`, which a spawned `PrivateSeatDriver` checks immediately after pressing Join and fails loudly on. (3) `FillEmptySeatsWithTestDrivers` tracks a 12-second per-seat join deadline - a seat still open past it is reported by name and freed for retry, and a seat already carrying a live deadline is skipped by a repeated press rather than sent a redundant child; every spawned driver also carries its parent host's OS process id and polls it independently of `Leave()`/`OnDestroy()`, so a host that dies without a clean shutdown still orphans nothing. **Check-paired, per this repo's own ceremony rule for new vocabulary**: rather than a full revert (which would not compile, since the new tests reference members that did not exist before), `ResolveLocalDialAddress` and the `TryParse` address check were behavior-reverted in place to their exact pre-fix logic with the new vocabulary kept present and inert; a focused Edit Mode run then showed exactly the two cases pinning the defect failing (`ANonLoopbackAddress_IsNowAccepted`, `ResolveLocalDialAddress_ForAConcreteNonLoopbackBind_ReturnsThatSameAddressVerbatim`), 18/20; restored immediately (diff verified byte-identical to the real fix), 20/20. **Full unfiltered Edit Mode**: 2,472/2,501; the 29 failures are attributable entirely to a parallel worker's uncommitted WIP sharing this tree (simultaneous hero leveling and related Core/Application/Snapshot changes - `MatchSession.cs`, `MatchSessionProjection.cs`, `HeroLevelUp.cs`, `SystemTransition*.cs`, `AuthoritativeSnapshotSerializer*.cs`, `SeatProjection.cs`, `NetworkSeatProjection.cs`, `NetworkMatchClient.cs`, `PrivateSessionProtocol.cs`, `RoundResolutionEvent.cs`, `RuleEventLogProjection.cs`): two of the 29 failing fixture files (`PendingDecisionTransportContractTests.cs`, `SnapshotCompatibilityCorpusTests.cs`) are that worker's own uncommitted edits, the rest are match-loop/snapshot/wire-codec/pending-decision fixtures exercising exactly the layers their WIP touches, none reference any type this milestone changed, and the worker was observed actively revising most of those same fixture files while this validation ran. `PrivateSeatDriverTests` itself: 20/20. **Full unfiltered Play Mode**: 274/278; one failure was this milestone's own - `PrivateLobbyPlayModeTests.AJoinToAHostThatIsNotThere_IsDrawnOnTheEntryForm` now trips Unity's unhandled-error-log auto-fail on the very `PrivateOnlineMarkers.Fail` line fix (2) adds, so the fixture gained a `LogAssert.Expect` for it (the assertions on the player-facing entry-form text are unchanged); a focused re-run confirmed 10/10. The remaining three (`MinimalHexGridPlayModeTests`, `Phase4RuntimeBaselinePlayModeTests`, `WaspEngagedUiJourneyPlayModeTests`) all fail on an unrelated `GUARDS_HUD: PANEL_REFRESH_FAILED panel=round`, in files this milestone does not touch, consistent with the same parallel Core/Rules WIP. **`Invoke-PrivateOnlineRehearsal.ps1 -Topology Solo -FaultPlan None`**, extended with three checks: the packaged interactive-lobby command line (both shipped `.bat` launchers' own bare `-guardsPrivateLobby` flag shape, headless flags added only for this box) boots to `LOBBY_BEGIN` cleanly - confirmed. A join dialed at a port nothing is listening on now fails fast and visibly - the first draft of this check used `Start-RehearsalPlayer`, which drives `PrivateOnlineRehearsalDriver` (`-guardsRehearsalRole`), a different, CI-only-choreography component from `PrivateSeatDriver` (`-guardsTestDriver`), the one this milestone's fix and FILL WITH TEST DRIVERS both actually use; the rehearsal driver's own generic timeout masked the real check entirely, caught by the run itself rather than assumed, and fixed by launching a real `PrivateSeatDriver` process directly with the same argument/environment shape `RealTestDriverProcessLauncher` uses - confirmed on the corrected run. FILL WITH TEST DRIVERS pressed twice back to back spawned exactly five `LOBBY_TEST_DRIVER_SPAWNED` lines, not ten, all five naming `address=127.0.0.1` (this run's own loopback host bind), and all six seats claimed before `MATCH_STARTED` - the dedupe fix and the address-resolution wiring both directly confirmed from the host's own log. The run's overall verdict is a timeout, not a pass: around command version 1,794 the host log recorded an uncaught `ArgumentException: A payload is bounded` inside `PrivateSessionFrameCodec.EncodeFrame` during `PrivateSessionHost.PublishProjections`, and the match then never converged - a pre-existing (or parallel-WIP-exacerbated, since `SeatProjection.cs`/`NetworkSeatProjection.cs` are among the files that worker's uncommitted change adds new projection fields to) defect in the host's own projection-send path, in files this milestone does not touch and did not introduce, flagged separately (`task_555cffb9`) rather than fixed here; every assertion 8.35t itself adds is independently confirmed from the log before that point, and no `GuardsDigitalSmoke.exe` process was left running once the script's own cleanup ran. **Governance**: `Docs/AI/Roadmap/PHASE_8.md` measured at 13,462/15,000 words after this block - over the 12,750 warning line (a non-blocking `WARNING`-severity check per the validator's own threshold logic) but 2,462 words under the hard cap; no shard-move ownership review performed for a single milestone's advisory warning. Governance artefacts regenerated clean after the doc edits. **Recorded limitation, shared-tree discipline, matching milestone 8.35s's own precedent**: the same parallel worker's uncommitted WIP named above shared this tree throughout and grew during this validation; none of it was read, absorbed, staged, or committed. Files: `Network/PrivateSessionEndpoint.cs`; `Presentation/Interaction/PrivateLobbyViews.cs`, `PrivateSeatDriver.cs`, `PrivateOnlineRehearsalDriver.cs`; `Assets/Tests/EditMode/PrivateSeatDriverTests.cs` (extended); `Assets/Tests/PlayMode/PrivateLobbyPlayModeTests.cs` (one `LogAssert.Expect`); `Tools/Invoke-PrivateOnlineRehearsal.ps1` (the three Solo-topology additions). Non-goals: no wire, protocol, or schema change; no change to the host's own bind rules (8.35a1's unkeyed-bind refusal untouched); no AI, strategy, or difficulty change to the test driver's own random-legal play. | e48bb238 |


## Milestone 8.35u Level every hero at the same time completion evidence

| ID | Outcome | Commit |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 8.35u | FOURTH amendment: "leveling up should be simultaneous for all players, no need to go one at a time." **This milestone removed a serialization rather than inventing concurrency, and the code said so first**: `HeroLevelUp` has documented since milestone 7.5 that its hero-id order is "a serialization of the page's simultaneity rather than a rule about who goes first", and `SeatProjection` has said since 8.30a that the commitment surface "authorizes every awaiting seat at once". Representation: ONE `PendingSystemTransitionStep` carrying `OpenItems` (a new `SystemTransitionOpenItem` = item + chooser + options), never N pause records - a second pause would be a second copy of the resume location, the exact defect that record exists to make unrepresentable - with `CompletedStepIds`/`CompletedItemIds` unmoved. **The open set is RE-DERIVED on every advance rather than mutated**: `SystemTransitionAuthority.Advance` already re-ran the step rules after each answer, so order-independence is structural rather than tested-for, and "a hero who answers can re-enter the set with its next level" falls out free. The levelling rules became two-phase (self-resolving Ultimates and purchases first, one item per call so the existing loop drains them; then every hero still holding an outstanding purchase opens together). **The single-item accessors `ItemId`/`ChoosingSide`/`Options` THROW when several stand** rather than returning the first - the audit line, and what found four of the six defects below; `Invocations` stays well defined across the whole set because every command names the item it answers, which is why the Director's surface answers all of them with no Presentation change at all. Ownership is evaluated PER ITEM: `PendingDecisionOwner` names every awaited hero and `AuthorizedCommands` filters the contract by each command's own item. A seat is projected only its own open item; another hero's options are ABSENT rather than hidden, because an option id names two cards in that hero's hand. **SIX defects, five found by tests and one by reading, every one a consumer of the changed invariant**: (1) `RoundResolutionEvent` named a `teamSide` a both-sides pause does not have - new non-throwing `AwaitedSide` gives the one side when the open items agree and `None` when they do not, which also keeps the recorded event stream identical for every pause that was never concurrent; (2) **the milestone's own non-local invariant, biting where the design notes predicted** - a snapshot restores the paused transition BEFORE reading Levels, coins and items, so the rules are unbound during the verification pass and the reordered phase one reached `TryPurchaseLevel`; the old order got this free by returning early on an outstanding purchase, and it is now stated (`IsBound`) rather than relied on; (3) `PendingDecisionOwner` is a `readonly struct` that had never declared equality, correct while every field was a value type - giving it `IReadOnlyList<HeroId>` made `ValueType`'s default compare list REFERENCES, so two owners naming the same heroes would have reported themselves unequal everywhere with no error; (4) the rule-event log named "the" chooser for six simultaneous ones; (5) **the seat-authorization defect's second half, and the class of catch this milestone existed to force** - narrowing `AuthorizedCommands` was NOT enough, because a seat's `DecisionContract` is read directly by the prompt presenters, so a seat holding the whole contract would have been DRAWN every hero's upgrade options while the host refused all but its own: milestone 8.35d's symptom at the surface plus a privacy leak, since an option id names cards in another hero's hand. An authorization filter and a projection filter are two different filters; (6) `SystemTransitionStepRuleSet` did not forward the new per-item describer, so a concurrent pause would have published no description at all. **Schema v35 -> v36**, measured in a worktree at the pre-change revision: all sixteen corpus digests move (the version int is in every payload) while fifteen keep their exact byte count, and only `paused-level-up` grows - by exactly **+4 authoritative bytes**, the repeated group's count and nothing else. The prediction was +3 and the measurement is +4; the gap is the useful part, because the count does not REPLACE the per-item present/absent boolean as assumed - it is added and that boolean is kept, which is the offline-probe fidelity rule earning its keep on a serializer. **Wire `guards-private-online/6` -> `/7`, and NOT for the predicted reason.** The paused-transition detail row does not move at all: `NetworkProjectionDetailCodec.Write` takes a `SeatProjection`, and since a `MatchSeat` owns exactly one hero the seat's pause is narrowed to the one item it may answer - milestone 8.32's per-seat encoding discipline absorbed the entire shape change, which is a property of that boundary worth recording rather than luck. What moved is `NetworkDecisionOwnerRecord`: "who is the authority waiting on" is genuinely plural, so one optional hero became a counted group, and a version 6 peer reads that count where it expects a present/absent boolean. The bump is honesty rather than a hole closed - v36 moves in the same milestone and `PrivateSessionIdentity` already refuses admission on `SnapshotSchemaMismatch`. **Three enforcement points AMENDED, never deleted**, and two of the amendments are stricter than what they replaced: `MatchSessionProjection`'s guard still refuses two standing FAMILIES and now says so; `PendingDecisionTransportContractTests` used to assert an owning seat receives the exact whole published contract - true only while one seat owned all of it - and now asserts that each owning seat receives exactly its own share AND that for a hero-owned family those shares PARTITION the contract (a first draft of that claim was wrong and the suite caught it: a team-owned family replicates rather than partitions); `LocalSixSeatPlayerExperienceTests` held a second copy of the same too-strong guard and is amended the same way. **A coverage hole this milestone opens is closed deliberately**: the existing "a non-owner cannot answer" check finds nobody outside the owner set once every seat is being asked, so it would have proved nothing silently - there is now an explicit check that one OWNER submitting ANOTHER owner's answer is refused, which is the 8.35d defect's exact recurrence shape. New `SimultaneousHeroLevelUpTests` (7 cases) drives a rules-produced state rather than a staged one - p.8's pity coin gives every hero a coin and p.15 prices the first level at one, so the round after a barren one is a round everybody must level in - and proves the simultaneous ask, that answering one leaves the others standing and the stage where it is (which is also why a disconnected player cannot be skipped: nothing about their absence empties the set), order-independence asserted deliberately OUT of hero-id order, a partially answered set restoring to the same remaining items while spending nothing to verify itself, the Director answering every item, the throwing accessors, and an answered item refused as stale. `EndOfRoundContinuationTests` gains the seat-authorization regression and `AtEverySeatScope_ASeatIsShownOnlyItsOwnHerosUpgradeOptions`, which asserts the privacy line at every seat across all four surfaces a seat reads a question through - contract, authorized commands, the projected pause including its `Invocations` (the one member every presenter reads), and the description, which carries its own pause. **Two harness-EXCLUDED fixtures failed the first complete Edit Mode run and both were this milestone's own shape**, which is the standing lesson that a green harness is no evidence for the excluded set: the companion-member count moved 9 -> 10 for the per-item descriptions, and `SeatScopedMatchHudTests.ASeatWaitingOnAnothersEndOfRoundDecisionIsDrawnNoContinueControl` threw `Sequence contains no matching element` because p.8's pity coin makes "everybody levels" the ordinary round, so an end-of-round decision can be owned by all six seats and leave no waiting seat to draw - the same coverage hole as above, appearing as a crash instead of a silent pass, and its walk now requires a genuinely waiting seat. **The seeded-constant tax was the largest single cost and is worth naming**: simultaneous levelling adds no commands and removes none, but it changes the ORDER of p.15's questions, so every trajectory diverges from the first level-up and every constant chosen by measurement had to be re-derived - fifteen of them, all scanned OFFLINE with throwaway probes (deleted before committing), never by bisecting Unity runs, with scans retained under `tmp/8-35d-8-35e-multiplayer-corrections/`. Two judgement calls are recorded rather than left silent: the final-wave constant does not take the lowest scanned seed, because seed 3 works but burns 82% of its budget and a constant that close to its ceiling breaks at the next reroute, and the mirror's life-counter seed rejects six lower candidates that end inside one round. **One seed a probe could not predict**, and the lesson generalises: `SeatBoundedUndoTests`' walk submits undos inside its own predicate, so a probe replicating its inputs but not its side effects confidently reported five Attack targets on a seed that reaches none - a probe that reproduces a walk's inputs but not its mutations is not a model of that walk, and candidates were tried against the real fixture instead. **Isolated baseline: harness 2,034/2,034** in a worktree carrying HEAD plus this milestone's files only, isolation verified positively (the peer's `System.Net` absent), because two parallel workers shared this tree throughout and the shared-tree total was never about this milestone. **The id was claimed as 8.35s, and the collision happened exactly as milestone 8.35p's rule says it would**: a clean sweep of code, docs and `tmp/` came back free, the claim was announced, and the parallel Presentation worker committed 8.35s first; 8.35t was also already taken, visible only in a third worker's uncommitted comments. Renamed to 8.35u at a measured cost of 65 occurrences across 22 files - all comments and fixture messages, no content revision and no artwork-catalog entries, which is why a milestone that adds no CONTENT pays only for its prose when its id moves. Files: `Core/Rules/Transitions/SystemTransition.cs`, `SystemTransitionAuthority.cs`, `SystemTransitionStepDescription.cs`; `Core/Rules/Progression/HeroLevelUp.cs`; `Core/Cards/RoundResolutionEvent.cs`; `Core/Diagnostics/RuleEventLogProjection.cs`; `Core/Snapshots/AuthoritativeSnapshotSerializer.cs` + `.Round.cs`; `Application/MatchSession.cs`, `MatchSessionProjection.cs`, `SeatProjection.cs`; `Application/PrivateOnline/NetworkSeatProjection.cs`, `NetworkMatchClient.cs`, `PrivateSessionProtocol.cs`; twenty-two Edit Mode fixtures including new `SimultaneousHeroLevelUpTests.cs`. Non-goals: no HUD work (the tray shine and "waiting for N players" line are the UX track's, consuming `DecisionOwner.HeroIds` and the per-item descriptions published here); p.16's Ultimate unlock stays self-resolving; a hero who can afford several levels still finishes each before buying the next; and milestone 6.11's blocked respawn Place stays one item at a time, because a placement changes the board the next placement reads - the set machinery is opt-in per step, declared by which describing interface a step's rules implement. | 0bc8a5de |

## Milestone 8.35w Bound the projection's completed-round history to a retained window completion evidence

| ID | Outcome | Commit |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 8.35w | Corrective, pre-build, from the live host crash in `tmp/private-online-solo-host.log`, triaged in `tmp/8-35d-8-35e-multiplayer-corrections/worker-codec/DIAGNOSIS.md`. **The defect**: `SeatProjection.CompletedRounds` carried the whole match's round history to every seat on every projection push from an append-only source - the one match-length-linear term in a seat projection, at a measured 1,183 bytes per completed round - so the host walked its own frame into `PrivateSessionProtocol.MaxPayloadBytes` and threw out of `PrivateSessionFrameCodec.EncodeFrame` on its own honest traffic. Terminal for the whole table rather than a dropped frame: `SendProjectionTo` has already advanced the send sequence when the throw lands, so every client reads a Gap, the resync is served down the same throwing path, and the exception unwinds `PublishProjections` so no later connection is served either - six players, nothing on any screen, no recovery. **Measured, not derived**: driving the official six-player configuration through `MatchHost` with the seeded Director walk and computing the real seat frame at every accepted step pinned the throw at 33-34 completed rounds across three seeds, inside the triage's derived 22-38 range and inside the length ordinary matches reach; six of ten scanned seeds crossed 33 rounds. **The fix bounds the growth rather than raising the limit**: `MaxPayloadBytes` is deliberately not raised, because its own doc comment makes it a defensive read-side cap applying to every message kind from every peer, and raising it would weaken that for all of them while leaving the term unbounded - the same freeze at a higher round number. `MatchSessionProjection.RetainedCompletedRounds = 4` is applied at the READ MODEL rather than in the codec, so the host's projection, the `SeatProjection` derived from it and the `NetworkMatchClient` that decodes it all report the same window and byte-for-byte convergence still holds; a cap inside `WriteCompletedRounds` would have made the encoded frame disagree with the projection it was encoded from, which is the one thing convergence is asserted on. `RoundResolutionAuthority` is deliberately NOT windowed and now says so at its own `CompletedRounds`: the rules, the restore state and the authoritative snapshot keep every round, and the new fixture asserts both halves at once. **Why four**: measured by running the window at 2 and at 6 over the same walk and differencing - 35,997 bytes of content that does not grow with match length, plus 1,108 bytes per retained round - and four is also the measured reach of the 8.35r match-log window this same projection already carries (the log fills at the fourth completed round and is flat thereafter), so the two history surfaces a seat reviews cover the same span rather than one silently deciding what "recent" means for both. **No wire and no schema move, both verified by absence**: `PrivateSessionProtocol.cs` and `Core/Snapshots/` are not in the diff, the field is already a count followed by that many records so a shorter list is the same layout, `guards-private-online/7` and `CurrentSchemaVersion` are untouched, and the compatibility-corpus fixtures pass unchanged as the byte-level confirmation. **Pre-fix red**, recorded in the worktree history at `50645044` and cited rather than re-staged: the behavioural line in `MatchSessionProjection`'s constructor reverted with the new constant left declared and inert, per this repo's ceremony rule for a check needing new typed vocabulary to compile - `TheProjectionFrame_StaysInsideItsBound_ThroughAMatchThatRunsToItsOwnEnding` failed with `System.ArgumentException: A payload is bounded.` from `PrivateSessionFrameCodec.EncodeFrame:48`, the same exception at the same site with the same message as the live host crash, and `TheReadModelsRoundHistory_IsTheRecentWindow_WhileTheMatchKeepsEveryRound` failed reporting the whole history where the window's tail was expected. **Three new permanent guards** in `PrivateOnlineEventChannelTests`: the frame guard, sized in ROUNDS and driven until the match ends itself, refusing to pass unless it saw an ending past forty completed rounds and building the frame through the real codec so an over-long frame fails as the host's own exception rather than as a number - the pre-existing guard measured 700 wire steps, about eight completed rounds, which is exactly why a 33-round failure shipped past it; the read-model window guard, asserting the last four rounds on all six seat views AND that the authoritative snapshot still restores every round played, because without the second half it would pass while history was destroyed; and `AReturningSeat_IsServedTheRetainedRoundWindow_OverTheWire`, putting the resync path under the window on a walk that has already outrun it, since a resync is not a separate larger message but is served through the same `SendProjectionTo` that threw. Two existing fixtures were made window-aware deliberately rather than relaxed: `EndOfRoundContinuationTests`' advance-count claim and round-boundary edge detector now derive from `RoundIndex`, because a windowed count saturates and would have silently weakened "every round crosses six stages" to the last four, and `CompleteCoreMatchLoopTests` additionally asserts that a complete match DOES outrun the window so its note cannot go stale. **INTEGRATION**: cherry-picked from worktree `agent-a228a6db649841044` onto `b0d7fbf6`, three milestones ahead of the `1bb44d0c` it was written against. All eight files auto-merged with no conflict, and the merge was verified by reading rather than by git's silence - 8.35u had reworked `SeatProjection` (334 lines) and `MatchSessionProjection` (55) and moved the schema to v36 and the wire to /7, and the single behavioural line lands in the constructor 8.35u also edited without disturbing it, while `NetworkProjectionDetailCodec.cs` is absent from 8.35u's diff entirely. **One integration defect found and repaired rather than relaxed**: the frame guard pinned `seed: 9`, and under 8.35u's simultaneous level-up that walk now ends at 31 completed rounds instead of 43, failing the case's own forty-round precondition. A seeded walk is a pure function of what it is OFFERED, so 8.35u rerouted the trajectory wholesale rather than shortening it. Re-measured across sixteen seeds under the current rules: ten still pass thirty-five completed rounds and four do not end at all inside a 4,200-command budget, at fifty-three - so the match length that made this defect reachable is unchanged and the milestone's premise survives 8.35u intact. The seed was re-pinned to 2 (43 rounds, reaching its own ending, and this fix's own primary measurement seed), and the round floor was NOT lowered, because lowering it would have quietly retired the property the case exists to hold. **The byte arithmetic was re-measured at HEAD rather than inherited**, since 8.35u grew the non-history content of a frame: the worst frame over the complete 43-round seed-2 walk is 39,399 bytes at round 41, against the 65,494-byte ceiling and the 52,395-byte deliberate-resize guard, leaving 12,996 bytes of headroom - slightly better than the 40,431 bytes and 11,964 bytes measured before 8.35u, so 8.35u did not erode the margin. **Evidence**: full dotnet harness 2,037 passed / 0 failed, which is the 2,034 isolated baseline plus this milestone's three new cases; complete unfiltered Edit Mode 2,548 passed / 0 failed / 0 skipped, the 2,510 baseline plus this milestone's three cases and 8.35y's thirty-five; complete unfiltered Play Mode 279 passed / 0 failed / 0 skipped, the 278 baseline plus 8.35y's one new real-HUD case - this milestone adds no Play Mode journey, and the two Play Mode readers of `CompletedRounds` assert rounds {1, 2}, inside the window; a compile probe over Core + Application + Network + Presentation + the entire `Assets/Tests/EditMode` tree clean after the merge, because a green harness is no evidence the Unity assemblies compile; `git diff --check` clean. | `9c51bf09` |

## Milestone 8.35y Make the board's events visible with kinetic cues completion evidence

| ID | Outcome | Commit |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 8.35y | SIXTEENTH amendment, from the user's own playtest reading: "the biggest issue in playtest was that players were not aware whats happening. Can we do some cutscene for attacks or something? Tokens smacking each other?" A primitive-idiom kinetic pass - no production art, no camera seizure, no gated input, nothing a player waits for. **What plays**: a moved piece travels from the space it left to the space it reached instead of teleporting; an attacker leans into the hero it struck carrying a floating damage figure; a struck piece registers the hit and a blocking defender flashes with its block figure; a removed piece shrinks and fades rather than vanishing; a returning piece pops in. Standing and separate in kind: the acting-hero ring breathes while the viewer is being waited on, and an awaited hero's gold tray and card shine carries a slow circling light. **Every cue derives from milestone 8.35r's `MatchLog` and nothing else**, which is what makes it safe: that log admits only events the whole table can already see - its seat filter is an identity rather than a redaction - so a pure function of it plays the same story on every client with nothing sent between them, and a cue can never reveal what the log's own selection contract excluded. `IMatchReadModel` exposes no diagnostic stream, so it is also the only event source Presentation has. **Three rules make a kinetic layer safe on an authoritative surface**: a cue is emitted ONCE, keyed on its log row's own `Sequence`, so a resync redelivering a window cannot replay it; the FIRST projection a client sees primes the watermark and plays nothing, so joining a match in progress does not fire forty smacks at once; and a log that went BACKWARDS - undo, rewind, restore - re-primes, plays nothing and clears the floating figures, because a figure describing an event that no longer happened is the one thing a flourish must never do. **End states are projection-true structurally rather than by testing for it**: the view recomputes each piece's AUTHORITATIVE placement every frame from its own projected occupancy - the same arithmetic the board views already use - and only then adds the kinetic offset. It never integrates and never moves a piece relative to where it currently is, so an interrupted cue, a mid-slide projection change, an undo, a resync and a restore all resolve to authoritative placement on the next frame with no unwind step, and a frame with nothing playing writes exactly what the board view already wrote. **Two findings contradicted the amendment's assumptions and were recorded rather than improvised around**: the events do not carry movement steps - `MatchLogEntry` names a per-hex step as one of its own exclusions and the log keeps only the first origin and last destination - so a slide travels TWO waypoints, with `KineticCue.Route` shaped for N and the view interpolating an arbitrary polyline so a later milestone that carries the real route drops it in with no view change; and the attack rows carry no damage amount, so the figure is the PRINTED value on the card the log named, read from the definition catalog, with its colour taken from that card's own `CardProgressionProfile` through `MatchHudTheme.Face` - the same source the user pinned for colour, never a presentation-side palette guess, and named `PrintedAmount` so nothing downstream mistakes it for a computed total. **Timing against the amendment's own bounds**: slide 0.32 s, lunge 0.28 s, flash 0.24 s, defeat 0.38 s, respawn 0.30 s, every one inside the stated 200-400 ms band and asserted over the whole vocabulary so a kind added later cannot sit outside it; shine sweep 2.4 s, mark pulse 1.2 s at amplitude 0.06. One deliberate departure with its reason in the constant's own doc comment: a floating figure lives 0.90 s, because that band bounds how long a PIECE is displaced from where the projection says it stands, and a figure displaces nothing, gates nothing and has to be READ. **Failure is bounded by construction**: `KineticCueDriver` catches its tick, reports it and disables itself after three failures, leaving exactly the projection-change redraw the board did before this milestone; the coordinator refreshes `kinetics` last and isolated under 8.35h's per-panel isolation; every seam is optional and appended, so every existing composition and fixture keeps its exact call. **Nothing animates that is not awaited**: marks pulse only while the viewer is being waited on and the shine sweeps only for heroes the awaited predicate names, standing animations are periodic functions of accumulated time with no per-element state and no allocation while they run, and cues are capped at six concurrent with a burst keeping only the newest. **INTEGRATION**: cherry-picked from worktree `agent-a98cbd48ed53569bc` onto `b0d7fbf6`, four milestones ahead of the `1bb44d0c` it was written against and across 8.35v's heavy rework of `MatchFeaturePresenters.cs` (329 lines). All ten files auto-merged with no conflict, and the merge was verified by reading rather than by git's silence: the real hazard was that this milestone passes its presenter as the tenth POSITIONAL argument to `MatchFeatureCoordinator`, so a parameter appended by 8.35v would have bound it silently to the wrong slot - confirmed by reading the merged constructor that 8.35v added none and that all ten arguments still line up. **Two integration debts of the worktree were paid here.** The six new `.cs` files shipped with no `.cs.meta`, which this repo requires hand-written beside them so the Unity GUID is stable rather than generated per machine; six were written, plus one for the new Play Mode fixture. And the two new Edit Mode fixtures reference `Presentation.Match`, which the Unity-free harness does not compile - they broke its build outright rather than being silently skipped, which is exactly what its explicit exclude list exists to force, so both were added to it. **The owed Play Mode case was written in the main tree and immediately earned its place.** `KineticCuePlayModeTests.TheRealHud_SlidesThePieceItMovedAndLungesTheAttackItResolved` drives the real configured scene through real controls across two rounds and asserts what only the scene can answer: that the runtime composes a kinetic view at all, that `KineticCueDriver` is attached, that a real movement puts the piece on the two recorded waypoints, that ONE ORDINARY FRAME advances that slide with nothing in the case ticking it - the whole of what "plays on the real HUD" means - and that the piece then resolves to the placement the projection alone decides; then that a real attack on the now-adjacent enemy hero leaves the attacker lunging at it and at no other piece, ending inside the vocabulary's own longest bound. Written first against an attack on a MINION, it FAILED, and the cause is upstream of this whole pass: see the named gap below. **Evidence**: compile probes over Core + Application + Network + Presentation with the entire `Assets/Tests/EditMode` tree, and again with the entire `Assets/Tests/PlayMode` tree, both clean after the merge, because a green harness is no evidence the Unity assemblies compile; full dotnet harness 2,037 passed / 0 failed (this milestone's 35 Edit Mode cases are Presentation-facing and excluded from it by design, so they do not appear in that total); complete unfiltered Edit Mode PENDING_EDIT; complete unfiltered Play Mode PENDING_PLAY; `Phase4RuntimeBaselinePlayModeTests` PENDING_PERF, watched because this pass is the scene's first per-frame work; `git diff --check` clean. **NAMED GAP, found by this milestone's own Play Mode case and deliberately not fixed here**: an attack whose target is a MINION plays no lunge and no flash - the commonest attack in a match, and the one the amendment named. `CardActionResolutionEvent.FromAttackResolution` builds every attack-resolution event with the actor and `TargetHeroId` but leaves `targetMinionId` at its default, so the row reaches the seat-public log with an attacker and no defender and the presenter correctly declines to invent one. The repair is one line - `targetMinionId: resolution.TargetMinionId` - and the receiving code is already in place, since `RuleEventLogProjection` writes `minion=` for any defined value and `MatchLogEntry.SubjectMinionId` is documented as exactly "a target, a defeat, a Minion Battle removal". It was applied experimentally and reverted: it is a Core change whose effect reaches the rule-event log inside the snapshot envelope digest, and measured at this revision it moves the compatibility corpus, so it owes an explicit migrate-or-reject decision and a re-pin - a milestone of its own rather than an integration-time edit made against a build the morning playtest depends on. Two further gaps stand: the awaited hex-corner cues do not pulse yet, only the acting-hero ring, and the circling shine sweep is the least verified surface here and is cuttable in isolation. | `ceab77f0` |

## Milestone 8.35z Lunge at the minion too, and settle what that costs the snapshot corpus completion evidence

| ID | Outcome | Commit |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 8.35z | Corrective, closing the first named gap of 8.35y (`ceab77f0`): an attack whose target is a MINION played no lunge and no flash - the commonest attack in a match, and the one the SIXTEENTH amendment actually asked for. **The defect, precisely**: an attack names ONE defender, and `PendingAttackResolution.Target` already holds whichever it is, exposed as both `TargetHeroId` and `TargetMinionId`. `CardActionResolutionEvent.FromAttackResolution` passed the hero arm and left `targetMinionId` at its default, so every attack-resolution event of a minion attack reached the rule-event log with an attacker and no defender; `RuleEventLogProjection` wrote no `minion=` reference, `MatchLogProjection`'s `Optional(references, "minion", ...)` found none, and 8.35y's kinetic presenter - whose minion arm was already shipped and already correct - declined to invent a target it had never been told. The whole receiving chain was in place; only the field was dropped. **The fix is the one line 8.35y measured and reverted**, `targetMinionId: resolution.TargetMinionId`, and it is a Core change reaching the rule-event log inside the snapshot envelope digest, which is why it owed this milestone rather than an integration-time edit. **THE MIGRATE-OR-REJECT DECISION: ACCEPT UNCHANGED, A COMPATIBLE WIDENING** - schema stays v36, two digests re-pin, nothing migrates and nothing is rejected. It is measured rather than argued, in four parts. (1) **The authoritative payload does not move anywhere.** Fourteen of the sixteen corpus fixtures do not move at all, and the two that do move ONLY in the rule-event log the envelope carries beside the payload - `paused-card-text` envelope 14041 -> 14083 and `paused-level-up` 26364 -> 26427, with their authoritative 8475 and 9448 bytes unchanged. All sixteen decoded authoritative payloads were compared byte-for-byte across the change with `cmp` and every one is identical, so no state was added, removed or reshaped and the version has nothing to move for. (2) **The log delta is exactly the fragment and nothing else.** Five rows changed, all of them rows that already existed, each gaining exactly the twenty-one characters `;minion=blue-melee-01`: two rows in `paused-card-text` (2 x 21 = 42, the whole of that fixture's envelope delta) and three in `paused-level-up` (3 x 21 = 63, likewise). No row was added, removed, reordered or re-worded, and the arithmetic closing exactly is what proves the delta is the fragment rather than something else riding along with it. (3) **The added reference does not change what an old payload MEANS**, which is the half that actually decides migrate-or-reject rather than the byte counts. `minion=` is a REFERENCE, not a fact: it names the board object the resolution had already targeted, and that targeting has always been present in the authoritative payload as `PendingAttackResolution.Target`. A reader of an old row could never have concluded "this attack had no minion defender" from the absence, because the absence was an omission of a reference the writer held, never a claim that there was none - so no old row is re-read differently by this change. Every widening test the corpus discipline asks was therefore answered yes, and the alternative - rejecting v36 payloads written before this revision - would have discarded loadable state to no benefit. (4) **The round trip was measured directly rather than reasoned about.** Every one of the sixteen snapshot strings captured at the PRE-change revision was replayed through the POST-change restore path by a throwaway probe reflecting into the corpus fixture's own `CreateSupportedSnapshots` and `CreateRestoreTarget`; all sixteen, both moved fixtures included, were ACCEPTED and re-captured BYTE-IDENTICALLY. That settles the one thing the byte counts could not: the envelope carries its log verbatim through a restore instead of re-deriving it, so a snapshot in the wild keeps its own historically honest rows, recorded when the writer did not name the defender. **Measurement method**, recorded because it is the cheap path for the next milestone that moves this corpus: the fixture's own failure message prints `name=digest (authoritative N bytes; envelope M decoded bytes)` for all sixteen fixtures at once, so deliberately breaking ONE pinned digest at the pre-change revision yields the entire pre-change measurement line in a single one-second harness run - no `git worktree` and no digest-at-a-time re-pin loop. **Two new permanent guards, both placed at the omission rather than at the symptom.** `MinionAttackResolutionTests.EveryAttackResolutionEvent_NamesTheMinionItTargeted` resolves a real minion attack through the real authority and asserts that all three events built from the pending resolution (`AttackTargetValidated`, `AttackStarted`, `AttackResolutionCompleted`) name the minion they targeted, and that the hero arm stays empty because an attack has one defender - this is the guard that would have caught the original omission, and it is harness-visible so it costs a second rather than a Unity launch. `KineticCueTests.AnAttackOnAMinion_LungesAtIt_CarryingThePrintedFigureOfTheCardThatStruck` pins what the fixed row is worth downstream: the lunge names the minion, the flash lands on the minion, and the damage figure is the striking card's printed value and its own `CardProgressionProfile` colour read from the real definition catalog. **Why the pre-existing minion case was not sufficient and is deliberately kept**: `KineticCueTests.AnAttackOnAMinion_LungesAtTheMinion` passed throughout 8.35y while a real minion attack played nothing, because it hands the presenter a hand-built row that already names a minion - the defect was that no such row was ever produced. A fixture that constructs its own input cannot observe an upstream field being dropped, which is the general lesson: when a presenter is proven against synthesised rows, the seam that BUILDS those rows needs its own guard. `KineticCuePlayModeTests` is deliberately left pinning the HERO arm and its doc comment updated to say why - that arm was always fully described, so it stays the stable "a lunge plays on the real HUD" pin. **Evidence**: full dotnet harness 2,037 passed / 0 failed against the fix and the re-pinned digests with zero fallout, then 2,038 with the new Core guard; complete unfiltered Edit Mode 2,550 passed / 0 failed / 0 skipped, the 2,548 baseline plus this milestone's two new cases; complete unfiltered Play Mode 279 passed / 0 failed / 0 skipped, unchanged from the 8.35y baseline because this milestone adds no journey, run rather than reused because minion attacks now animate on the live board and 8.35y's own defeat-hold regression proved that a kinetic change can move existing journeys; validator green; `git diff --check` clean. | `1dc9aa69` |
