## Milestone 8.35z3 Degrade gracefully when a seat projection outgrows the session frame completion evidence

| ID | Outcome | Commit |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 8.35z3 | Corrective, hardening the failure mode 8.35w's diagnosis (`tmp/8-35d-8-35e-multiplayer-corrections/worker-codec/DIAGNOSIS.md`) recorded but its windows could not remove: the retained-round window bounded the growth term, yet the host's publish path still had no answer for an over-bound frame - `PrivateSessionFrameCodec.EncodeFrame` throws, after `NextEnvelope` has already advanced that connection's send sequence, the throw unwinds `PublishProjections` for every seat behind the first, and every resync is served down the same throwing path: the whole table frozen with nothing on any screen, the live shape in `tmp/private-online-solo-host.log`. **Two defects corrected. (1) The host send path.** `NetworkMessageCodec.TrySeatProjectionFrameWithin` composes the seat projection frame to a caller-stated budget: each match-log row's cost is measured through the one real row writer, the largest NEWEST suffix of rows is kept - the log's own recorded scrollback semantics - and the method returns false, never throws, when the envelope-plus-projection prefix alone cannot fit. `PrivateSessionHost.SendProjectionTo` composes through it against `ProjectionFrameBudgetBytes = PrivateSessionSecureChannel.MaxSealedPayloadBytes` (the tighter of the two bounds every frame must eventually pass, so `Seal` can never throw either), builds the envelope with `GameplaySendSequence + 1` and commits the increment only beside a frame that exists, and answers an unfittable payload with `RejectAndClose(OversizedFrame)` and a named detail for that ONE connection - the seat left Reserved with its reconnect road back - while `PublishProjections` iterates a snapshot so the close cannot unwind the loop. `TrimmedProjectionLogRows` counts degradation observably (zero in any healthy match; the 8.35w guards hold the worst real frame under 80% of the bound), and `ResyncServed` is recorded only on actual delivery. **(2) The client input latch.** A correlated projection judged Stale under a reordered delivery was discarded before the latch-release check, leaving `IsWaitingForHost` latched forever - one seat silently unable to act for the rest of the match, every convergence assertion still green. The stale branch now releases the latch when the frame carries this seat's awaited correlation (the host echoes a correlation exactly once), then discards the copy unapplied as before. **Pre-fix observations**: the host defect's live evidence is the recorded solo-rehearsal crash log above (unreachable through real match state since 8.35w's windows, which is why the new checks drive the compose seam directly); the latch case was falsified in the working tree - with only the client release reverted, `AReorderedCorrelatedProjection_StillReleasesTheInputLatch` fails exactly on `IsWaitingForHost` staying true, and restoring the fix turns it green. **Four new Edit Mode cases**: the over-bound message trimmed to the newest row suffix and decoding under the ordinary read discipline with the raw encoder's `"A payload is bounded."` throw pinned beside it; the payload only the looser plaintext bound could carry refused, proving the budget is the sealed bound; byte-for-byte identity of the bounded compose with the plain encoder for any fitting message; and the reordered correlated echo releasing the latch. **Tier T2** (wire-boundary integration; bug-fix row's automated regression satisfied). Harness evidence at the final code revision `682c768c` (rebased onto 8.35z2): full cross-platform dotnet harness 2,042/2,042 green; private-online/session/network filter 154/154 green. **Unity Edit Mode (complete, unfiltered, Windows launcher)**: 2,559/2,559 passed (`result="Passed"`, 0 failed/skipped/inconclusive; `tmp/8-35z3-editmode-results.xml`, mtime inside this run's own window, log free of every compile/import failure signature) - the 8.35z2 baseline 2,555 plus this milestone's four new cases. **Play Mode omitted by affectedness**: the change touches only `Assets/GuardsDigital/Application/PrivateOnline` production code plus two Edit Mode test files - no Presentation, scene, asset, input, or Play-path file - the wire behavior it alters is unreachable in any healthy match (byte-identity under the bound is itself an acceptance check), and Play Mode holds no private-online wire journey; baseline stays 279. No wire vocabulary, protocol version, or schema movement: `OversizedFrame` is 8.31 vocabulary, the frame layout is unchanged (fewer rows is a smaller legal payload), `guards-private-online/7` and schema v36 stand. `git diff --check` clean; governance manifest/experiment regenerated and living-documentation validator re-run after this milestone's roadmap/archive/status edits. | `aea8dc39` |

## Milestone 8.35z4 Fix the four defects the first live internet playtest found completion evidence

| ID | Outcome | Commit |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 8.35z4 | The first milestone whose defect list was written by a real six-human internet session rather than by a rehearsal. Four Presentation/Match defects, and they were never independent: the first starved the surfaces the second and fourth are read on, which is why the session reported them as one vague "nothing animates and the words are wrong". **(1) The keystone.** `RoundActionPresenter.FormatStatus`'s two team-agreement branches handed an EMPTY owed-set to `NarrationLabels.WaitingOn`, which refuses it by design; an agreement every participant has already answered is an ordinary transient state every seat refreshes through, so from the first one the round panel threw on every refresh (`PANEL_REFRESH_FAILED panel=round`). Milestone 8.35h's panel isolation then contained the throw to that one panel, which is exactly what turned a crash into something quieter and worse - the panel froze at its last good text, the playtest's immortal "YOUR TURN - RELEASE TO CONTINUE", and everything else riding the same refresh was starved, the kinetic cue emission included, hence no animations anywhere. Fixed at the CALLER through the new `FormatAgreementWait`: an empty owed-set is a real state and gets `NarrationLabels.EveryAnswerIn`, while `WaitingOn` stays defensive so the next forgetful caller is still caught. **(2) The identity.** A networked client cannot know its hero at composition time - the lobby grants the seat afterwards - so `ConfiguredMatchSceneRuntime` composed all three seat-scoped presenters with `Seat?.HeroId ?? default` and repaired exactly one; the other two held their identity in `readonly` fields and could not be repaired even in principle. The undefined hero is not a neutral blank: it is the DIRECTOR's identity, and the Director owns every decision at once, so every "is this mine?" predicate read an unrepaired seat as "yes, everything is mine". New `ISeatScopedPresenter`, both fields made mutable behind a validating `SetHomeHero` that drops the per-projection cache, and one `BindHomeHero` fan-out over `SeatScopedPresenters` that refuses loudly rather than leaving a half-bound HUD. Measured while verifying: the two `Seat?.HeroId` reads inside closures re-read the mutable property and were always correct after admission, so only the two constructor-time captures were broken. **(3) The one press.** `SubmitEndTurn`'s compose loop re-reads `session.Projection` after every step, which is exact against a synchronous client and wrong against the wire: `NetworkMatchClient.Submit` returns as soon as the command is SENT, handing back the pre-command projection, and `PrivateSessionClient` refuses every further submission while `IsWaitingForHost`, so the second step of a composition was refused and the player pressed again. Every `SubmitEndTurn` assertion in the repo drove a synchronous client, which is why 2,550 green cases missed it. The press is now remembered and resumed from `Refresh`, bounded to its own actor and to `MaxEndTurnResumes`. **(4) The pace.** `Begin` started the floating figure at `Elapsed = 0`, the same instant as its cue, but a lunge's `Strike` curve does not peak until 34 percent in, so the number preceded its own cause by about 95 ms; the figure now begins one impact-delay behind the clock, derived from the curve's own peak rather than a literal, and `FloatingNumberSeconds` moves 0.90 to 1.5. **THE FIFTH DEFECT, AND THE ONE THIS MILESTONE IS REALLY ABOUT.** The defect batch's own commit body named a live six-client rehearsal as the validation fix (3) most deserved, and it was right. Fix (3)'s bound was UNREACHABLE as first written: `ArmOrClearEndTurnContinuation` reset `composingEndTurnResumes` unconditionally and every resumption re-arms through that method, so the comparison could never be met and a press whose submissions kept being refused resumed on every frame forever. Nothing offline could reach it - against an in-memory wire the composition always completes within two frames, so the state leaves the press's own actor and the continuation disarms at its boundary long before any bound matters; the missing dimension is a host that ANSWERS WITHOUT CHANGING THE MATCH. The first rehearsal caught it in the act: host log 3.59 MB, 17,593 refusals, 17,486 of them one seat, every one `SessionRefused`, all at a single authoritative version 6513 while the match stood still. After the fix - the budget belongs to the press rather than to each resumption of it, so only a fresh press resets it - the same cohort gives host log 451 KB, 126 refusals, ZERO `SessionRefused`, and that seat accounts for exactly 13: one press plus the twelve resumptions the bound now really allows, the live number and the offline assertion agreeing exactly. Pre-fix falsification with only the reset guard reverted: `AnEndTurnPressWhoseSubmissionsAreAlwaysRefused_SpendsItsBudgetInsteadOfResumingForever` expected at most 13 and observed 201 - one submission per frame across 200 frames - with 82 of 83 cases in the fixture still passing, so the red is that defect and nothing else. **MERGE RECORD.** The batch branched at `20658f34`; main had moved to `35f45def` under it. 8.35z3 is entirely Application/PrivateOnline and disjoint by construction, and exactly ONE source file is touched by both sides, `MatchFeaturePresenters.cs`, where the hunks do not overlap - 8.35z2 edits `TeamAgreementHudViewModel` and its `CreateViewModel` around 2808-2925, the batch edits the presenter identities, the end-turn continuation and `FormatStatus` elsewhere. Resolved with no conflict and verified by hand rather than trusted, both marker sets present. The one judgement required: 8.35z2's "WAITING FOR HOST..." and this milestone's `EveryAnswerIn` are complementary, not competing - the first is drawn by the VIEW when `Choices` is empty AND `IsInputEnabled` is false, a statement in flight; the second is returned by the ROUND PANEL when the OWED-participant set is empty, everyone answered. Different predicate, different surface, neither gating the other. 8.35z2's `isInputEnabled` is an OPTIONAL parameter, so the batch's constructions still compile - checked deliberately, because the batch's own probe had already been bitten by a bare `default` ambiguous between a `bool` and a `HeroId` overload. **Also found by integration:** `SeatScopedPresenterBindingTests.cs` was never added to the dotnet harness exclude list, so the harness build failed by design - the csproj's own comment says the list exists so a new Unity-dependent test breaks the build instead of being silently skipped, and the branch never ran the harness. **Two press shapes the batch owed are now driven** over the asynchronous client rather than only the Hold: the movement Stop at two commands, and the open Attack target at three, the deepest composition there is. Measured while writing the second: that press opens with the seat's own REWIND rather than a command, so the deferring wrapper had to defer `SubmitUndo` as well, a property of the composition the batch's shape table did not name. Territory is Presentation/Match plus one harness csproj line; no Core, Application or Network file is touched, and no wire protocol, snapshot schema or content revision moves. Validation at the final revision: dotnet harness 2,042/2,042; complete unfiltered Edit Mode 2,576/2,576 against the 2,559 baseline, the batch's 14 plus this milestone's 3; complete unfiltered Play Mode 279/279, unmoved; six-client rehearsal `SixClient/Full` PASSED across match, host-loss and rejoin cohorts, version advancing 503 to 3440 with all seven `REHEARSAL_OUTCOME` lines identical at `cause=FinalWaveFlipped winner=red-team`, privacy-reviewed logs and every process self-exiting 0. Code revisions `440c9396` (the merged batch) and `42581999` (the corrective). | `34ec1783` |

## Milestone 8.35z5 Close Living Tsunami's empty Confirm, and the whole class it belongs to completion evidence

| ID | Outcome | Commit |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 8.35z5 | Arien's Living Tsunami - "Once per turn, before performing an Attack action, you may move 1 space" - projected BOTH a Confirm carrying an empty selection and a Decline for the one "no" answer, and the rules accepted the Decline while refusing the Confirm as `TargetUnavailable` / `IncompleteSelection`. A player who pressed the offered Confirm was refused for choosing nothing when choosing nothing was legal, just spelled differently. **ROOT CAUSE.** `EffectResolutionSession.ApplyCapabilityResult` forced a `may` node's projected `minimumSelections` to 0 - milestone 8.20a's fix for "Living Tsunami reached the table as four spaces and no way to stay put" - and that same 0 also relaxed `PendingEffectChoice.ConfirmInvocation`'s own gate, legalizing an empty Confirm the handler's floor of one would refuse. Two different questions, "is the whole step declinable" and "how many must a taken answer name", were answered with one number. **PRINTED READING.** "You may move 1 space" leaves the distance exact (p.11: an unqualified number is exact) and makes only the move itself optional, so Confirm keeps the handler's own floor unmodified and Decline is offered from the node's `may` policy directly, through a `declinableRegardlessOfMinimum` flag derived fresh at every pause and restore and never persisted - exactly as `UnavailableCandidates` and `StopInvocation` already are. All four construction sites keep the derivation consistent: the live projection seam, the snapshot restore (deriving from the trusted node exactly as its neighbouring `canStop` does), the network client's wire reconstruction (which passes `row.HasDeclineInvocation` directly, a remote client having no content to re-derive `EffectFailurePolicy` from, and that bit already being the host's own authoritative `DeclineInvocation != null`), and re-selection. **THE CLASS, NOT THE INSTANCE.** Grepping every `EffectNodeKind.Capability` node marked `EffectFailurePolicy.May` found the same shape wherever a handler needs a real floor once its choice opens: Tigerclaw's five "you may move 1 space" faces over direct-move@3 (Hit and Run step 7, Combat Reflexes 1 and 7, Leaping Strike 1 and 7, Sidestep's Defense rider, all through the shared builder), his Evade's retrieve clause, and Sabina's and Brogan's `may`-wrapped Retrieve and owned-card Discard. None of those handlers special-cased an empty Resume either, so the seam fix repairs all of them with no content edit. **PRE-FIX RED** observed on the real Arien fixture before any production change: the paused choice at `node-tsunami-move` carried `<Confirm none @.../node-tsunami-move>` beside a legal Decline, and submitting it is refused by `DirectMoveHandler.Resume`. Regression: the general class on a synthetic `may`-wrapped choose-hero@1 capability, plus `LivingTsunamiEmptyAnswerTests` on the reported card itself. **SEEDED-WALK FALLOUT**, the tripwire this codebase hits at every offer-set change, re-derived offline exactly as 8.35g and 8.35u document: `CompleteCoreMatchLoopTests.LifeCounterMatchSeed` 8 -> 25 (seed 8 no longer ends inside its 2,400-command budget at all; seed 25 ends after 5 rounds and 404 commands, 17% of budget); `AttackLifecycleBoundaryTests.SweepSeeds` {1,2,8,12} -> {1,2,12,15} (seed 8 completes no Attack inside 1,200 commands; seed 15 is the richest replacement at five Attack targets); `PrivateOnlineEventChannelTests`' 40-round frame-bound walk seed 2 -> 5. `FinalWaveMatchSeed`, `ThroneCaptureMatchSeed` and the eight `SoakSeeds` were re-verified unmoved. **INTEGRATION MERGE RECORD.** Cherry-picked from `d853852a`, based on `983aa205`, five commits behind main. The one file both sides touched, `PrivateOnlineEventChannelTests.cs`, auto-merged with no conflict - 8.35z4 appended cases while this branch re-seeded an existing walk, disjoint hunks. The semantic risk was that milestone 8.35z4 had added THREE new seeded walks in `MatchFeaturePresenterTests`, which is harness-EXCLUDED and so runs only under Unity; rather than discover a reroute in a seven-minute run, the walks' reachability halves were replayed in a throwaway harness probe with the fix applied and again with the four production files reverted to `97b03b61`, and measured IDENTICAL both ways - the 600-step sweep walks 600 commands and 4,207 viewer refreshes against its own >100/>700 floors, and the seed-6 Attack-target search reaches `red-player-01` holding its own rewind with 1,457 of 1,800 budget left and zero skipped targets. `WaspEngagedUiJourneyPlayModeTests.BlockedLanePushRespawnPlace` (seed 29) was settled STRUCTURALLY instead of by a sixty-seed scan: the change lives entirely inside `ApplyCapabilityResult`, so only a Capability node marked `May` can move an offer, and the verified engaged mirror deals two WASPS, whose four `May` declarations are one `Optional` and three `RepeatAction` - not one Capability among them. `ArienProgressedUiJourneyPlayModeTests` walks this exact node, is staged rather than seeded, and asserts precisely what the fix preserves (a Decline offered AND drawn, a filled one-space click still confirming), so it is the live confirmation of the fix rather than a casualty. Territory is Core plus one Application wire-reconstruction line; no schema, wire protocol or content revision moves. Code revision `a6c73a3b`. Combined validation for the whole tail batch is recorded on the 8.35z9 row below. | `7534f92b` |

## Milestone 8.35z6 Fade a defeated piece out without holding it on the board completion evidence

| ID | Outcome | Commit |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 8.35z6 | Milestone 8.35y built the Defeat cue's timing and motion but could not deliver the visible shrink-and-fade, and recorded why: holding a defeated piece drawn meant re-activating a GameObject `BoardInteractionUnityView` had already deactivated the SAME frame, behind that view's own back, on an object it pools and reuses. The complete unfiltered Play Mode run at that integration found the cost - five existing removal guards assert a defeated piece's GameObject is inactive the instant Render/RenderMinions drops it, some synchronously with no frame in between (`MinimalHexGridPlayModeTests` x2, `StandardMinionViewTests`, `ConfiguredPointerSelectionTests`, and the Minion-Battle hero-removal case) - and the hold made all five report the piece still present. It was cut rather than traded for five weakened rules guards. **THIS DELIVERS IT WHERE 8.35y'S OWN COMMENT SAID IT HAD TO LIVE: the board view, not the kinetic layer.** `DefeatFadeGhostView` is a bare, non-pooled visual echo - its own MeshRenderer/MeshFilter for a hero or SpriteRenderer for a minion, no `PrimitiveHeroView` or `StandardMinionView` component, no collider, no pointer selector - so it is invisible to every identity-based `FindObjectsByType`/`Single` query the suites run and cannot intercept a click. `BoardInteractionUnityView` spawns one from a per-shape free pool (never Instantiate/Destroy per defeat) the instant its OWN Render finds a piece going from drawn to not-drawn, so the real piece's `SetActive(false)` stays exactly as synchronous as the five guards require - the ghost is a separate GameObject entirely. A piece reappearing before its ghost finishes (Undo, restore, Respawn) cancels that ghost immediately, so a fade can never survive as a stale echo of an event that no longer happened. `AdvanceDefeatFades(deltaSeconds)` ticks every in-flight ghost and is driven by `BoardDefeatFadeDriver`, a per-frame MonoBehaviour isolated exactly like `KineticCueDriver` (self-disabling after three failures) and entirely separate from it: the board's fade never reaches into the kinetic layer and the kinetic layer never reaches into the board's pool. `KineticCueUnityView`'s "hold deliberately not implemented" comment is updated to point here; its code and the five guards are untouched. **SCOPE ADDITION, coordinator-approved and named by milestone 8.35z2's own commit body as recorded-but-not-fixed:** `CalculateOptionalStepControlRect` (the ACCEPT/DECLINE optional-step banners) anchored its left edge at `CalculateCardPanelRect`'s `xMin` - the screen's own left edge, since that full-width tray band reserves the whole viewport - landing the banner on the Red rail's column at every viewport, reference included. Fixed with 8.35z2's own template exactly: bound against `CalculateHeroRailRect` on both sides and `CalculateUnavailableCardGroupRect` instead of the screen edge and a fixed hand-width constant. Retires follow-up chip `d3b5c3d2`. Reproduced offline BEFORE any Unity launch with a net6.0 console probe compiling Core+Application+Network+Presentation against the installed Editor DLLs: the pre-fix formula overlaps the Red rail at reference, narrow and ultrawide sizes; post-fix it clears both rails, the hand tray and the unavailable fan at all three. 18/18 probe checks. **A USER WITHDRAWAL, VERIFIED RATHER THAN TRUSTED.** The resolving-card rail anchor originally planned beside these two was withdrawn mid-work on the user's own call ("Remove card-next-to-the-tray. Its too much + board overlap sucks"), and integration confirmed its absence three independent ways rather than taking the branch's word: a grep over the whole `Assets/` tree for `ResolvingCardAnchor`, `resolvingCardLayer`, `CalculateResolvingCardAnchorRect`, `BuildResolvingCardAnchor` and `PlaceResolvingCardAnchor` returns ZERO hits, and `UiToolkitMatchHudViews.cs` and `MatchHudPresentationTests.cs` are both byte-identical to `983aa205`. The existing bottom-right resolving display and the tray's own resolving-card exposure are exactly as they were. **INTEGRATION MERGE RECORD.** Cherry-picked from `8e959557`; no conflict was possible, main's own movement since that base being Application/PrivateOnline plus the 8.35z4 kinetic and presenter files, which this branch's seven touched files intersect nowhere. The one file where the two could have disagreed is `KineticCueUnityView.cs`, since 8.35z4 reworked the kinetic layer's pace; read rather than trusted, this branch's whole edit there is a six-line COMMENT update and no executable line is touched on either side. `DefeatFadeGhostTests.cs` reads `GuardsDigital.Presentation.Board`, which the dotnet harness does not compile, so it was registered on the harness exclude list in the same commit - the 8.35z4 tripwire paid deliberately, that milestone having found `SeatScopedPresenterBindingTests.cs` left off it. Territory is Presentation only; no rules, schema, wire or content movement. Code revision `85ee4840`. Combined validation for the whole tail batch is recorded on the 8.35z9 row below. | `7534f92b` |

"Better dark than wrong" no longer has to mean dark, now that the owner fact exists. The three 8.35m arms are unchanged. The 8.35m non-goal proof case is REPLACED by five positive cases: a multi-leveler (two heroes via `ForHeroes` shining at once), a defence answerer with the actor beneath suppressed (the M3 trap proved in one case), a respawn chooser, an acting hero, and a real-fixture integration case that doubles as the PRIVACY case - a non-owning seat's projection carries a null `DecisionContract` for a pause it does not own, yet still shines the levelling hero's tray, because who is awaited is public while what they may choose is not. Plus the log region: every named `MatchLogEntryKind` renders its own line or header, counted against the enum so a kind added without a rendering case is caught; the undo-marker line; the closed-drop-down-composes-nothing case; the derived-never-accumulated truncation case; and the presenter's open/close/dismiss machine including the idempotent Dismiss. **INTEGRATION MERGE RECORD, and this was the branch whose territory 8.35z4 moved under.** Cherry-picked from `c67604de`; five files auto-merged with no conflict and all five were read rather than trusted. `IsHeroAwaited` is fully rewritten here while 8.35z4's whole second defect was seat identity - a presenter holding the UNDEFINED hero, which is the DIRECTOR's identity, so every "is this mine?" predicate answered "yes, everything is mine" - and the rewrite could have re-opened exactly that. It does not, and the check is structural rather than a reading of intent: the diff lands in precisely two hunks, and the merged file's own method map puts `IsWaitingOnViewer` and `OwesACommitment` OUTSIDE both, so the viewer-scoped predicate and the deliberate undefined-viewer Director branch 8.35z4's non-goals kept standing are byte-untouched; and `IsHeroAwaited` fails CLOSED on `!heroId.IsDefined` before any arm runs, so an unbound identity goes dark rather than claiming every hero's decision. `MatchLogPresenter` takes no `HeroId`, so it is correctly not seat-scoped, the roster the `BindHomeHero` fan-out binds is unaffected, and the match log is the same log for everyone on the merits. The constructor-parameter ordering flagged as a possible contest with the buttons branch dissolved on reading: that branch touches `MatchFeaturePresenters.cs` on ZERO lines, having composed its own notice in `ConfiguredMatchSceneRuntime` instead, so the only question was placement relative to 8.35z4's own `kinetics` parameter, and this one is APPENDED after it, leaving every existing positional call binding the same arguments. ## Milestone 8.35z8 Give every named control one shared treatment, and say when a step applied nothing completion evidence

| ID | Outcome | Commit |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 8.35z8 | User-directed, the EIGHTEENTH amendment, verbatim: "make these buttons nicer... they need clicking animations, they need to sit nicer in the trays... they look pasted over". **ONE TREATMENT ACROSS EVERY NAMED CONTROL.** `MatchHudTheme` gains `ButtonIdleBackground`, `ButtonCornerRadius` (4, the radius most panels already use), hover and pressed scales, their transition durations, and `Lighten`/`Darken`. `HudElements.ApplyButtonMotion` wires PointerEnter/Leave/Down/Up/CaptureOut to a USS `scale` transition - idle 1, hover 1.035x over ~120 ms, pressed 0.94x over ~80 ms - gated on `enabledInHierarchy` so a disabled control (the existing `MarkUnavailable` opacity/SetEnabled state, the fourth state, untouched) never animates; with `tintBackground` it additionally eases the control's own background toward Lighten/Darken of ITSELF, captured lazily on first hover so a caller that recolours a button after construction (Confirm's orange, Clear's accent) still eases toward its own colour rather than a value captured too early. `HudElements.Control` carries chrome and motion to eleven existing call sites at once - the agreement AGREE/WITHDRAW and Initiative choices, Confirm/Cancel/Clear, printed-action-region forwarding, the upgrade-option confirm, the effect-invocation buttons, and the Director drawer's UNDO/RESET/EVENT LOG. `PewterBanner` (END TURN, the seat's own UNDO) and the card-faced buttons take scale-only motion layered over their own established look - banner cloth glow, card face - rather than a chrome change. `PrivateLobbyViews`' `BuildControl` (HOST/JOIN/FILL/LEAVE) and the seat PICK button drew NOTHING but Unity's stock button skin before this: no themed background, no border at all, very likely the single biggest contributor to "pasted over". Deliberately untouched: the printed-action-region's invisible card-art hotspot and `ReplacementRibbon`, which have their own established identity and are not named controls in the amendment. **THE FIZZLE NOTICE**, the SIXTH amendment's deferred part b. Verified at HEAD before designing rather than assumed: `EffectExecutionResult.ImpossibleMandatory()` is a parameterless factory with no room for a blocking source; `RoundResolutionAuthority`'s `RefuseCapability`/`Stop` record only a node id and the bare kind; milestone 8.35l's `AppliedRuleModification`/`ActionUnavailability` channel is threaded ONLY through the offer-time feasibility walk, never through `Execute()`'s own restriction check at the point a resolution stops; and `MatchLogEntryKind.BlockedAction` already says so in its own doc comment - "The blocker is NOT named here." **The conclusion was RECORDED rather than guessed:** acting-player scoping IS fully derivable (a seat is one hero, `SubjectHeroId` states whose resolution stopped, and an undefined viewer is the Director, who owns every hero's fizzle - mirrored from `OwesACommitment`'s own undefined-viewer branch), while the blocking hero and card are NOT derivable from any fact Presentation can read today. That is an open Core-side gap for a later milestone, not something closed here, and naming a blocker anyway would be exactly the invented reason the amendment's own vocabulary rule forbids. `FizzleNoticeStream` mirrors `KineticCueStream`'s three rules - emit once, prime silently on first sight, re-prime silently on a rewind - kept structurally separate rather than shared so this stays clear of `KineticCuePresenter.cs`; its latch persists across repeated observation, unlike a momentary cue, until acknowledged or rewound. `RoundActionUnityView` gains a SEPARATE additive `RenderFizzleNotice` entry point rather than a branch of `Render`, because Core has already moved past the fizzle by the time the log names it - the notice is a presentation-only courtesy pause, not an authoritative one - drawn as an absolute overlay on the contextual region with an OK button built through the shared `Control`, so it inherits the chrome and motion above for free. **A REAL DEFECT THIS INTEGRATION CREATED, WHICH NEITHER BRANCH COULD HAVE SEEN, AND WHICH 8.35z4's OWN TRIPWIRE CAUGHT.** `FizzleNoticePresenter` takes a viewer `HeroId`, stored it in a `readonly` field, and `ConfiguredMatchSceneRuntime` composes it with `Seat?.HeroId ?? default`. That is, line for line, the second defect of the first live internet playtest in a FOURTH place: this branch was written against `983aa205`, before milestone 8.35z4 defined `ISeatScopedPresenter`, so it reproduced the exact shape that milestone existed to abolish. It is not cosmetic - the stream reads an undefined viewer as the Director, who owns every hero's fizzle, so an unbound networked seat would have been shown a modal acknowledge-to-continue notice naming ANOTHER player's card every time any of the other five had an action blocked. 8.35z4 wrote its roster case as an explicit tripwire "so a FOURTH such presenter cannot be added and forgotten the way two were"; this integration is that fourth presenter, and the tripwire worked. Repaired here on the template the other three follow: the presenter implements `ISeatScopedPresenter`, its identity field is no longer `readonly`, `SetHomeHero` validates the hero against this build's declared setup and refuses an absent or undefined one, and the runtime's `SeatScopedPresenters` roster - the single list `BindHomeHero` fans out over - now names it. The rebinding drops any standing notice through the stream's own `Acknowledge`, which dismisses WITHOUT rewinding the watermark: both halves are wanted, since a notice latched before admission was latched under the Director's branch and may name another player's card, while the watermark must not rewind because a seat just admitted did not watch the fizzles already in the log. The pinned roster case is updated to four with the reason recorded in its own doc comment and renamed so its name need not be re-edited by every future addition; nothing in the repository named the old name, checked across `.cs` and `.md` before renaming, per the rule that a matrix row can link a case by name. **MERGE RECORD.** Cherry-picked from `3602399e`; the two files shared with the match-log branch, `UiToolkitMatchHudViews.cs` and `ConfiguredMatchSceneRuntime.cs`, auto-merged and were read rather than trusted - genuinely additive on each side, with both new statics, the status-bar host method, the appended log view and both new presenters present and composed. The two deliverables compose in this branch's favour without either worker arranging it: the log's chevron and panel controls are built through `HudElements.Control` and so inherit the shared chrome and press motion. `FizzleNoticeStreamTests.cs` and `HudButtonTreatmentTests.cs` both read `GuardsDigital.Presentation`, which the harness does not compile, so both were registered on the exclude list in the same commit. Recorded gap carried forward: the notice names the card and honestly not the blocker; closing it needs a Core change threading the restriction `Execute()` already consults through `EffectExecutionResult`, `EffectNodeOutcome`, the log row and the wire, exactly as 8.35l threaded it through `Anticipate`. Territory is Presentation plus lobby styling; no rules, schema or wire change. Code revision `fd403d6a`. Combined validation for the whole tail batch is recorded on the 8.35z9 row below. | `7534f92b` |

## Milestone 8.35z9 Make the awaited shine sweep reach its hosts, and keep gold for what is genuinely awaited completion evidence

| ID | Outcome | Commit |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 8.35z9 | Three findings from the user's own live testing of the 8.35z4-fix package, relayed mid-integration and taken as one milestone because they are one question - what the HUD's gold and kinetic language says, and to whom - and because all three land on the vocabulary the four tail branches were being merged through. **(A) THE SWEEP ANIMATED NOTHING, AND HAD NOT SINCE IT SHIPPED.** Reported: the board-mark pulse works and a tray lights, but no circling sweep ever appears - not on a lit tray, not on the resolving card, ever. Diagnosed by reading `KineticCueUnityView.CollectSweepHosts` against the elements milestone 8.35m actually marks, rather than by accepting the relayed guess. It asked for `Image` elements NAMED `gd-card-shine`. That is milestone 8.30d's PREPARED PRINTED-ARTWORK overlay - a different family entirely, present only on cards whose art carries a traced shine, and unrelated to whether anyone is being waited on. The awaited-answer gold is a `VisualElement` named `gd-card-awaited-shine` on a card face, and on a hero rail it is not an element at all: `BuildOverview` paints the rail's own border and background gold with no child to find. Wrong NAME and wrong TYPE, so the query could not match either treatment; it returned an empty list, the caller returned early on `sweeps.Count == 0`, and nothing swept - silently, because an empty host set is a legitimate state on a HUD with no shine. Fixed by giving the awaited treatments ONE shared marker, `MatchHudTheme.AwaitedShineClass`. A CLASS deliberately, not a name: these are different element types on different surfaces and always will be, and a class is the one thing all of them can carry. The card ring adds it in `ConfiguredCardFaceView.AddAwaitedShineOutline`; the rail adds it in `BuildOverview` when `hero.IsAwaited`, needing no clearing because rails are rebuilt every render; the sweep queries it and attaches its travelling mark INSIDE the element carrying the gold, so the light traces that element's own border rather than a parent's. A side effect worth recording: the sweep's own doc comment claimed it travelled "each shine overlay that belongs to a hero the match is currently waiting on", which was false - it collected every prepared overlay in the tree and consulted awaitedness nowhere. With the marker it is simply true. **(B) THE GOLD LEAKED ONTO A PLAYER NOBODY WAS WAITING ON.** Reported with a screenshot: during another hero's release pause - narrated correctly in the third person, that hero's own tray correctly lit - the viewer's own raised committed card still wore the gold border that means act now. The relayed guess was legacy committed-card raise styling; it is not, and that is why it was worth diagnosing before touching anything. `BuildTray` sets `isAwaitedAnswer: isAvailableForCommitment || isResolvingThisCard`, and `isResolvingThisCard` is true from the moment a hero's card is REVEALED until it finishes resolving. Every hero reveals at the start of the round and turns then run one at a time in Initiative order, so between its reveal and its own turn - potentially five other turns - a card wore the gold while somebody else acted. `isAvailableForCommitment` was never the leak: it requires the AwaitingCommitments phase, which a release pause is not. Fixed by additionally requiring the turn to be genuinely under way for this hero - `IsTurnActive`, `SelectedActor.IsDefined`, and the actor being this hero - which is the SAME pair `AwaitedInputQuery.IsActingWithNoDecisionStandingOnTop` gates the tray light on, so tray gold and card gold now agree by construction rather than by coincidence. The instruction was to enforce the rule and not merely fix one site, so every other gold treatment was audited and recorded as already correctly gated: the top bar paints on `IsAwaitingViewerInput` (viewer-scoped), the narration accent on `NarrationVoice.YourMove`, the hero rail per hero on `IsHeroAwaited`, the upgrade option's warm tint is a hover state, and the pewter banner's resting glow is that control's own 8.30d cloth identity rather than the awaited language. The card predicate was the only leak. **MEASURED BEFORE CHANGING**, because a lifecycle correction usually breaks the fixtures that characterised the old lifecycle: the two existing cases asserting a resolving card shines both view `Setup.Heroes.First()`, and a throwaway harness probe over both fixtures reported `IsTurnActive=True`, `actor=primitive-hero`, `first=primitive-hero`, `firstIsActor=True` - the viewed hero IS the acting hero in both - so both stay green untouched and the narrowing bites only the multi-hero case those two-hero mirror fixtures never reach, which is the case the playtest hit. **(C) THE FLOATING NUMBERS ARE RETRACTED**, on the user's own call after live testing ("they just don't work"). Removed entirely rather than re-tuned - the figure cues, their printed-colour tinting and the impact timing - keeping the lunge, flash, defeat fade, board-mark pulse and the sweep above. **This deliberately RETRACTS PART OF MILESTONE 8.35z4**, which is recorded here rather than left to be noticed: that milestone's fourth defect was the figures appearing about 95 ms before their own cause, fixed by beginning them one impact-delay behind the clock and lengthening `FloatingNumberSeconds` to 1.5. That timing fix was correct and is not being reverted as wrong - the whole feature above it is withdrawn. A zoom-in spotlight replacement is the user's own separate future design and nothing is built toward it here. Removed: `KineticNumberRole`, `KineticNumber`, `KineticCue.Number` and its two validations, `KineticTiming.FloatingNumberSeconds`, `KineticFloatingNumber`, the playback's number lists and `RebuildFloatingNumbers`, `ImpactMomentFor`, `FadeOutTail`, the view's figure layer and label pooling, and the whole `KineticCardFacts` class - which reading confirmed had no consumer left once `PrintedNumber` went, so it is deleted rather than kept as dead code. `StrikePeak` and `FlashPeak` STAY, shaping the lunge and flash curves themselves rather than only the figure timing. Five figure cases and the fade curve they alone used are removed; every lunge, flash, slide, defeat, respawn, hitch, curve and sweep case stays. **GUARDS ADDED, because the instruction was that the silent failure must be impossible to reship:** `TheShineSweep_FindsItsHostsOnAComposedHudWithALitTrayAndAShiningCard` composes a real hand view and a real rail view under one root and asserts the host set is non-empty, carries one host from each surface, and stays inside `MaxSweptShineElements`, with `FindShineSweepHosts` exposed `internal static` so the case asserts the shipped code rather than a copy of it; and `AHeroWhoIsNotActing_WearsNoAwaitedGoldWhileAnotherHerosTurnStands` reproduces the reported state and asserts both halves - no tray card of the bystander is an awaited answer AND nothing on that hero's whole surface carries the shared marker class - while the acting hero's card still shines, which is what says the grammar was narrowed rather than deleted. The marker from (A) is what makes "zero gold anywhere on this surface" a question a test can ask at all. **ALSO CONFIRMED, as asked:** the tray does light DURING resolution and not only after it - `IsActingWithNoDecisionStandingOnTop`, one of the four arms milestone 8.35z7 added, is true while the turn is active, this hero is the actor and no OTHER hero's decision stands on top, which is precisely the window a card's text runs in, with `IsTurnReleaseOwner` carrying the light through the release that follows; the "lights only after resolution" behaviour was the pre-8.35z7 state. Territory is Presentation only; no Core, Application, wire, schema or content movement in any of the three. Code revision `587fa6ee`. COMBINED VALIDATION FOR THE WHOLE TAIL BATCH, at the final revision `db341c21`, which is what the four rows above point here for. **dotnet harness 2,044 / 2,044** (the 2,042 baseline plus 8.35z5's two Core cases; every other milestone in the batch is Presentation or a Unity-excluded fixture and adds none). **Complete unfiltered Edit Mode 2,620 / 2,620**, 0 failed and 0 skipped, against the 2,576 baseline - plus 44. **Complete unfiltered Play Mode 279 / 279**, 0 failed, baseline unmoved. **Six-client rehearsal `SixClient/Full` PASSED** across the match, terminal host-loss and rejoin cohorts: every one of the seven logs in each cohort - six seats and the seatless host - reports an identical `REHEARSAL_OUTCOME cause=FinalWaveFlipped winner=blue-team`, at authoritative version 1867 and 3192 respectively, with ZERO `SessionRefused` and a 418 KB host log (milestone 8.35z4's own pre-fix runaway was 3.59 MB and 17,593 refusals, so this is the healthy shape that milestone established). **THE FIRST COMPLETE RUNS FOUND FIVE DEFECTS AND THAT IS THE POINT OF RUNNING THEM**: two real code defects this integration introduced - the fizzle notice's OK button standing permanently in the tree as an unoffered control, caught by four reachability and label cases at once, and the new sweep mark hanging 3.5px outside the hero rail's own column, caught by the Play Mode rail guard - and three fixtures that characterised behaviour this batch deliberately changed (the defeat-fade fixture building a hero with no mesh, which the real board never has; and the optional-step banner's left-edge and width assertions, both of which held only BECAUSE of the overlap 8.35z6 corrected). All five are fixed at `db341c21` with the reasoning in its own commit body. Notably, the defeat fade had never been run under Unity at all before this pass - its authoring worktree carried no `Library/` and could not launch the Editor. | `7534f92b` |

Use team trays to show it somehow"). The narration bar moves from a full-width lane to a centred, width-capped bar (`CalculateNarrationBarRect`, preferred width 900, bounded against `CalculateHeroRailWidth` so it is clear of both rails by construction at any viewport), keeping its gold treatment, typography and log chevron; the match-log drop-down now unrolls centred beneath it. Fixed by shrinking the banner to 16 and its own gap to a new `TieBreakerRailBannerGap` (4, not the standing `Margin`), landing the column at 828 - a real, measured 9.33-unit clearance - and fixing a second, independent oversight the same live run found: the banner's own label never got `whiteSpace = NoWrap` carried over from the retired disc's chip label. Measured directly against the verified 372x520 scans with a System.Drawing probe: `arien/liquid-leap.png`'s Movement badge sits at source ~x=10-73, y=208-244 (matching `PreparedActionSourceBounds`' own comment, "x=21..69, y=203..249"), while the Secondary Fast Travel ribbon anchored to the padded 80x82 highlight ring around it - about 15-20 reference units clear of the actual ink. `BuildReplacementChip` now anchors that one branch (Movement, Secondary, prepared artwork) to the tight ink rect `(21,203,48,46)` directly; verified against `arien/noble-blade.png` (Attack/CLEAR), `wasp/magnetic-dagger.png` and `arien/slippery-ground.png` (Movement primary/FAST TRAVEL) that the Primary title-tab placements were ALREADY correct within a few reference units, so they are untouched.
