## Milestone 8.35z11 Give the whole match a motion language: a conductor, an easing layer, and card flight completion evidence

| ID | Outcome | Commit |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 8.35z11 | PLAN.md's TWENTIETH AMENDMENT (user: "animations, not good. Can you look how dune imperium does animations"), built on milestone 8.35y's cue engine and landed as the first of four wave-one integration milestones. Authored in worktree branch `worktree-agent-afe83ab90ba5374cd` at `785a5960`, merged here onto 8.35z10. **FIVE NEW CUE KINDS, AND THE FINDING THAT DECIDED WHERE THEY COME FROM.** Principle 1's very first example - a committed card arcing from hand to slot - had NO SOURCE in the one stream 8.35y derives from: `MatchLogEntry` names "a facedown commitment" in its own list of what no member describes, and that exclusion is load-bearing rather than incidental, because it is why every seat's log is the same log and why its seat filter is an identity rather than a redaction. Adding a commitment row would have put a hand's movements on a wire that has never carried one, for a flourish. So `KineticProjectionWatch` is a SECOND derivation, from the projection, where the fact legitimately lives - and it also covers coins (the log has `PityCoinCollected` but coins also arrive from p.13 defeats) and the round-end retrieval (a STAGE, not an event). The cost is stated rather than hidden: a log-derived cue is identical on every client by construction, a projection-derived one on every client that can SEE the fact, which is the correct rule and not a weaker one - and `KineticCue` REFUSES to carry a `CardInstanceId` for a `CardCommit` (a constructor throw, tested), so the flourish reveals nothing the projection did not already show. **THE CONDUCTOR.** `KineticScene` is one MOMENT, not one cue - an attack's lunge and the flash it caused are one thing that happened and play together, while six reveals are six moments and are staged. `Enqueue` stable-sorts a batch by `Order` with an insertion sort, deliberately: `List.Sort` is introsort and NOT stable, which would let two heroes on the same Initiative swap places between two clients. Backpressure has two stages - past `CatchUpQueueBudget` (6) the beats go to zero and the clock runs at 2.5x; past `MaxQueuedScenes` (24) the OLDEST are dropped and counted. `TheConductor_HoldsNothingAuthoritative` asserts the architecture by REFLECTION: no field is an `IMatchReadModel` or `IMatchClient` and the constructor takes nothing, so "the board never waits on animation" stays true rather than merely being claimed. **THE CORRECTNESS POINT WORTH READING TWICE.** Deferring a cue means playing it against a LATER projection than the one it was derived from. For every kind but one that is harmless - a lunge at a piece that has gone finds no target, a flash on a missing piece finds no piece. The exception is a SLIDE, drawn as an offset back along a route whose end the projection is assumed to stand the piece at; if the piece moved again in between, `current + (partway - arrival)` is a position on no path it ever took. `KineticRouteFreshness.IsRouteStillCurrent` is that check, and a stale slide now travels nothing. **REVEALS ARE RE-RANKED, AND THE REASON IS MEASURED.** `TurnCardCommitmentAuthority.CompleteRevealIfReady` walks `expectedHeroIds`, and `RoundTurnSlot` builds that list as `OrderBy(heroId.Value, StringComparer.Ordinal)` - so the log's own reveal order is an ORDINAL SORT BY HERO IDENTIFIER, stable and meaningless to watch. Staging by sequence alone would have satisfied principle 4's letter and none of its point. Reveals are re-ranked by the revealed card's PRINTED Initiative, highest first, arrival order breaking ties, and the re-ranked span stays inside the span the batch already occupied so a reveal can never jump past a neighbouring moment. It decides nothing: the authority reads a total item bonuses can raise and settles ties by a player's own choice; this is the order cards are TURNED OVER in. A card whose Initiative cannot be read (p.16's Ultimate prints none) sorts last rather than being guessed. **THE EASING LAYER** puts every curve and duration in one enumerable place with an audit that samples each curve against the identity line rather than reading source for the word "linear" - because a curve written as `t` contains no such word and is exactly what the rule forbids. Two declared bands, and the second is a recorded departure argued from the user's own words: travel/appearance at the amendment's 200-450ms, and interaction UNDER 200ms because principle 5 asks a hover to answer "IMMEDIATELY" and 200ms on a pointer is not immediate; the interaction band restates 8.35x's shipped button constants and a case asserts the two sources agree, so the audit covers what actually ships. **THE BRIDGE**, the amendment's one named technical job: `KineticSpaceBridge` converts panel points to board world units through the same mapping every board click already passes through, refusing a point behind the camera rather than mirroring it somewhere plausible. It is genuinely used - coins are won on the BOARD and land in a tray on the HUD - and all the geometry stays Unity-free in `KineticFlightPath`. **AMBIENT LIFE COSTS NO PER-FRAME C# AT ALL:** elements are given UI Toolkit's own USS transitions and have those values moved once, on `Render` (a projection change), never per frame. The HUD rebuilds its elements wholesale on every projection change, so "has this eased in yet?" cannot be answered by marking the element - marking would ease the whole HUD several times a second, which is exactly the "busy" principle 5 forbids; what survives a rebuild is the element's NAME, so entry is a set difference over names. **ONE BEHAVIOURAL CHANGE TO SHIPPED CODE:** `KineticCueStream` no longer truncates a burst to `MaxConcurrentCues`. That rule existed because every surviving cue started at the SAME INSTANT; under a conductor they are queued moments and truncating would have made the queue budget dead code. The constant survives with a truer meaning - the most kinetics played at once is now guaranteed by the SIZE OF A SCENE - and is asserted as such on every scene. **WHAT THE INTEGRATION ADDED.** The one shared-file touchpoint was verified by reading rather than trusted: 8.35z10 reworked the top strip of `UiToolkitMatchHudViews.cs` on main while this branch added one line to `BuildOverview` (`rail.userData = hero.HeroId;`, the identity six same-named rails otherwise lack). Git auto-merged, and the merged file's diff against 8.35z10 is EXACTLY that one line plus its comment, which is the proof the top-strip rework survived intact. Nine hand-written `.cs.meta` files were minted (a worktree has no Unity to mint them). Three fixtures joined the harness Exclude list - all Presentation-typed - so the harness total is unmoved and every one of this milestone's 60 cases is Edit Mode's. The placeholder id `8.35x-motion` was renamed in 45 places across 15 files. Evidence at this revision rather than the branch's: the compile probe (Core+Application+Network+Presentation+the ENTIRE EditMode tree, against the merged tree including 8.35z10) reports 0 errors and needed one reference the earlier recipe lacks, `Unity.Scripting.dll`, for the `[Preserve]` on 8.35z10's `BuildIdentityStamp`; a headless reflection run over this milestone's five fixtures reported PASSED=89 FAILED=0 with four cases unrunnable outside Unity (the known `Resources.Load`/`VisualElement` native-ECall limitation), which REPRODUCES the branch's own recorded 89-pass number exactly - the probe-fidelity rule satisfied before anything else here was believed. Territory is Presentation only; no Core, Application, wire, schema or content movement. Combined validation for the whole wave is in 8.35z14's row below, which is what this row points there for. | `d74c2ff5` |

## Milestone 8.35z12 Frame every attack in a spotlight, ranged pairs included, under one conductor completion evidence

| ID | Outcome | Commit |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 8.35z12 | PLAN.md's NINETEENTH AMENDMENT, the second of four wave-one integration milestones, authored in worktree branch `worktree-agent-aa4647cfc9a3b52fa` at `ca4d76a2` + `192ab121` and merged here onto 8.35z11. **THE FEASIBILITY CLAIMS WERE RE-CHECKED AT HEAD BEFORE ANYTHING WAS BUILT ON THEM, and one came back stronger than assumed:** both cards are already public at the needed moment - the Attack row carries the attacking card and the Defense row the spent defense card, and `SeatProjection.IsPublic` admits any face-up card in `TurnArea` or `DiscardPile` to EVERY seat - so no wire field, no schema bump and no privacy work was owed. The board camera is ORTHOGRAPHIC looking straight down, so zoom is one number and camera distance affects nothing but clipping. **THE RANGED DEFECT, AND IT WAS HIDING INSIDE AN ASSERTION THAT DESCRIBED IT AS CORRECT.** Sabina's Shootout targets "in range", so a spotlight pair can be many hexes apart, and the first version's ONLY ceiling was the resting board frame (`size = min(needed, resting)`): a cross-board attack computed a `needed` larger than resting, took resting, and opened the shot out until it WAS the board view - with the HUD hidden and nothing gained. `TheFraming_ZoomsInAndNeverOut` asserted exactly that as if it were right. **The clamps were then MEASURED FROM THE TREE rather than chosen by intuition:** 210 playable spaces on the official map, a 27.71 x 27.00 playable extent, a maximum board separation of **32.909** world units, one hex step of 1.7320508, and a largest printed Range in the six-hero corpus of **4**. The board is the WRONG bound, because the spotlight frames an ATTACK and an attack's separation is bounded by the attacker's printed Range: `MaximumFramedSeparation = 8 * HexStepWorld = 13.856` (the largest printed Range with its dynamic-Range headroom DOUBLED) and `MaximumOrthographicSize = 11.2` (that separation through the framing solve at the tilt that separation gets). Past it the shot CROPS rather than widening; the resting board size stays a third, softer ceiling so the spotlight is never a zoom OUT. `TheFramedMaximum_ClearsTheLargestPrintedRangeInTheCorpus` reads the largest printed Range back out of the verified content and fails if a future hero prints a longer one, rather than silently cropping a legal attack. **THE TILT TAPERS** from sixteen degrees at melee to the amendment's ten-degree floor at the framed maximum, and the reason is measurable rather than a taste: the tilt shows `depth * sin t` of a piece's side, which reads when the piece fills the frame and is invisible against eight hexes of board, while COSTING framing the whole time because the foreshortening divides the solve by `cos t`. It stops at ten rather than going flat so a spotlight is always visibly a different camera from the board's. **TOKEN DEPTH TURNED OUT TO BE FREE.** Under an orthographic top-down camera, raising a piece's face moves it by exactly zero pixels and the wall added beneath it projects to a line of zero width - so the extrusion is INVISIBLE from the standing board view and appears only when a camera tilts, which is why it could be made permanent rather than switched on and off, and why it cannot move the Phase 4 band: same silhouette, same screen positions, ~128 extra triangles per piece, no new material, renderer or GameObject. Two hazards were checked and cleared without a Unity run (the convex-hull footprint measures the same hull because every extrusion keeps a full perimeter ring at the original y=0 and radius; the existing six-vertex perimeter assertion still finds six because its helper deduplicates by XZ). **The winding is the trap here:** Unity culls back faces, so a wall wound the wrong way is not a subtle shading difference but a piece with NO visible side at all, in the one view nobody looks at until a camera tilts - `ASolidPieceWallsFaceOutward` proves the geometric normal points away from the axis for all twelve wall triangles. **CARD ANCHORING WAS REBUILT ON THE SAME FINDING:** the screen-edge rule was right for the adjacent pair it was written against and wrong the moment a ranged pair spread out, because a card pinned to the far panel edge is nowhere near the combatant it describes. Each face now stands beside its own piece, on the side away from the other, flipping inward when the outer side would leave the panel, with on-screen, mutually disjoint, and clear of BOTH pieces enforced in that order rather than hoped for - the last being the one that actually ruins a spotlight, since the pieces are what the camera just travelled to show. **THE RANGED SMACK WAS HALF A JOB ALREADY DONE:** the cue view has always capped a lunge at 0.60 board units, about a third of a hex, so a cross-board attacker never charged. What was missing was the MIDDLE. `RangedSmackProfile` is a jab that shortens toward 0.18 as the target recedes, a dash crossing 22% of the path at 26 units/second clamped to 0.10-0.34s (a dash and not a beam: a line joining the two pieces reads as a wall between them), and a ring that opens and fades over 0.22s where it lands. It draws on the spotlight's own layer in PANEL space, which under an orthographic camera is not an approximation - the projection is linear, so a straight line between two projected points is exactly where a world-space line would appear. **Where "ranged" lives is a recorded finding:** not on the match-log row, but on `ActionDefinition.AttackRange`, reachable from the card the Attack row already names, so every client derives it from public data with nothing added to the wire - proven end to end against the real Shootout card. It does NOT drive the treatment; the measured SEPARATION does, because a Ranged Attack that landed on an adjacent target crossed no ground. **THE TWO CONDUCTORS BECAME ONE, and this was the integration's largest named task.** 8.35z11 and this milestone independently designed the same seam and the mapping is exact: `KineticConductor` <-> `IConductedScene`, a held scene <-> the `Held` phase, `Acknowledge()` <-> the CONTINUE press. 8.35z11's is the more developed piece - it paces every cue kind, not only this one - so ITS conductor survives and this milestone's stage became a scene it grants: `AutoGrantsStage` is set false in the composition (that flag existed so the spotlight could ship standalone), `KineticConductor.ConductedScene` is the registration - a settable PROPERTY and not a constructor parameter, because `TheConductor_HoldsNothingAuthoritative` asserts the constructor takes nothing and that guarantee had to survive - and the conductor grants a requesting scene and starts nothing behind one that holds the stage, which is principle 3 extended to the one scene not made of cues. CONTINUE is one release for both mechanisms. **THE ONE FLAGGED CONFLICT WAS RESOLVED AS A RULE RATHER THAN A SPECIAL CASE:** 8.35z11 self-releases a held scene after 20 seconds and this milestone's spotlight after 6, and neither number is wrong - they answer different questions. THE VALVE IS SHORT WHEN THE CHROME IS HIDDEN, because twenty seconds of a paused board is merely slow while twenty seconds of a HIDDEN HUD is a client the player cannot use. `KineticScene` gained `HidesChrome`, `KineticStaging` gained `ChromeHiddenHoldSeconds = 6f`, the conductor picks the valve per scene, and a test pins that constant equal to `AttackSpotlightTiming.MaximumHeldSeconds` so no later edit can move one without the other. **WHERE THE INTEGRATION DEPARTED FROM THE BRANCH'S OWN RECOMMENDATION, AND WHY - THE FINDING WORTH CARRYING.** `worker-spotlight/NOTES.md` section 10 step 4 recommended DELETING the `KineticCuePresenter.DeferWhile`/`Resume` pairing, reasoning that "the conductor already holds cues while a scene is on stage, so keeping both would hold them twice". Reading the two predicates side by side shows they are not the same hold: `AttackSpotlightStage.HoldsStage` is `Entering | Held | Exiting` while `AttackSpotlightPresenter.DefersCues` is `Requested | Entering | Exiting`. They differ at exactly one phase - HELD - and that phase is the entire point of the feature. The conductor's hold covers Held, so relying on it alone would have kept the attack's own lunge and flash queued until the spotlight had CLOSED, and the ranged smack is built on those cues being released MID-HOLD, framed, with both faces up ("lose the UI, zoom in, show the cards, show the smack"). Deleting the deferral would have silently moved the smack out of the shot it exists for. The conductor owns WHICH scene plays; the deferral owns WHEN within a granted spotlight its cues fire. Only the first job was ever being done twice. Recorded because the note is otherwise excellent and a later reader would reasonably trust its step 4. **WHAT THE INTEGRATION ADDED:** four new `KineticConductorTests` cases proving the reconciliation (the shared valve constant; a chrome-hiding held scene releasing on the short valve WHILE a chrome-showing one at the same instant is still waiting; a conducted scene granted the stage with nothing starting behind it; and the null-registration path behaving exactly as before), twelve hand-written `.cs.meta` files, four harness exclusions, and the placeholder id `8.35x-spotlight` renamed in 33 places. A headless reflection run over BOTH milestones' fixtures together reported PASSED=144 FAILED=0 with 4 Unity-only - 8.35z11's 93 plus this milestone's 51 plus the 4 reconciliation cases, reconciling exactly, which is the evidence for "one conductor survives and BOTH test suites pass against it". **AND THEN THE FIRST COMPLETE PLAY MODE RUN FOUND A DEFECT IN THAT VERY RECONCILIATION, which is the best argument for running one.** The conductor's new gate asked `IConductedScene.HoldsStage` - "start nothing while a conducted scene holds the stage" - which reads as obviously right and is wrong by one phase. `HoldsStage` is true through `Entering`, `Held` AND `Exiting`, and HELD is exactly the phase the attack's own released cues have to play in: the user's sequence is "lose the UI, zoom in, show the cards, show the smack", and the smack IS those cues, framed, with both faces up. Gating on `HoldsStage` would have kept them queued until the spotlight CLOSED - moving the smack out of the shot the whole milestone exists to build, silently, with every Edit Mode case still green because no Edit Mode case composes a spotlight and a cue layer together. The fix adds a SEVENTH member to `IConductedScene`, `DefersOtherScenes`, true while the scene is TRAVELLING and false while it merely holds; it is defined as `AttackSpotlightPresenter.DefersCuesInPhase`, the same pure function the cue layer's own deferral now calls, so the conductor's gate and the deferral are ONE definition rather than two that happen to agree. `TheConductorsGateAndTheCueDeferral_AreTheSamePhaseSet` walks every phase and pins it, and the shipped `KineticCuePlayModeTests` journey now asserts the lunge reaches the board while the stage reads `Held` - the framed-smack guarantee, stated on the real HUD rather than argued in a note. This is the same shape 8.35z9's own record describes from the other direction: a predicate that is nearly right, over a window nobody had composed end to end. Territory is Presentation only. Combined validation is in 8.35z14's row below. | `d74c2ff5` |

## Milestone 8.35z13 Make the practice bots fight: greedy-legal seat drivers instead of random-legal completion evidence

| ID | Outcome | Commit |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 8.35z13 | Third of four wave-one integration milestones, authored in worktree branch `8.35x-greedybots` at `9e32521f` and merged here onto 8.35z12. `PrivateSeatDriver`'s decision logic gained three deterministic preferences over the random picker, each reading only facts already published on the seat's own `IMatchReadModel` and each falling back to null - random-legal - the instant its own facts are absent. **(1)** Prefer STARTING an Attack over any other offered action, which is safe unconditionally because `TurnResolutionAuthority.CanStartAttack` already refuses to offer an Attack with no legal target; then among legal targets prefer the one on the team with fewest remaining `TeamLifeCounters`, tie-broken by the lowest minion-Defense bonus `PendingAttackTargetDefensePreviews` already publishes. **(2)** Among movement destinations prefer the lowest hex distance (`HexDistanceQuery`, no pathfinding) to the nearest enemy hero or minion - minion position standing in for "the lane battle" because the live Battle Zone id is Director-only board state never published to a seat. **(3)** When the standing `PendingDefensePreview` shows Declining would let the Attack succeed, play the cheapest-sufficient Defense card - the lowest Defense Total whose own preview Outcome is `SuccessfullyDefended` - instead of random-legal. Commitments, leveling picks and team-agreement convergence stay untouched as durable non-goals rather than oversights: a bot that picked its commitment well would change what the practice match is FOR. Every preference is a deterministic filter computed BEFORE the random tiebreak, so per-seed seeded determinism survives, proven directly by a same-seed-twice case. **A FINDING RECORDED AS CONTENT, NOT AS A RULE, ON THE USER'S OWN DESIGN-SIDE DIRECTION.** Building preference 3 surfaced that in the CURRENT content a Decline is unconditionally `AttackResolutionOutcome.AttackSuccessful`. That is CONTENT-DEPENDENT and must never be promoted to an invariant: the user notes that unimplemented heroes may carry decline-triggered saves, and that removal prevention already exists today as a post-hit save - so a rule saying "declining always loses" would be false of the game rather than merely false of this build. No code change was owed, because the bot never baked the assumption in: the guard reads the per-state preview and returns null when Declining already survives, and that guard is the load-bearing line that must not be "simplified" into an unconditional by a later reader who rediscovers the same observation. This is the cards-are-the-definition discipline applied to a bot heuristic - record what the current cards show, never promote it to a rule. **WHAT THE INTEGRATION ADDED:** one hand-written `.cs.meta`, and the harness exclusion the branch added survived a three-way conflict with 8.35z11's and 8.35z12's own additions to the same one-line `Exclude` attribute - all three branches edited it - resolved by hand to the union, alphabetised, with each of the eight new entries verified present exactly once. The 12 cases were EXECUTED here rather than taken on trust: the integration's reflection probe compiles Presentation, which `Tools/DotnetTests` does not, so the fixture the harness must exclude still ran headlessly, 12/12 green. The branch's own offline measurement is carried forward: a headless `MatchSession` probe over 10 seeds converted attackable turns into an Attack 14/38 (36.8%) random-legal versus 24/24 (100.0%) greedy-nudge. Territory is Presentation only; no rules, wire or schema movement. Combined validation is in 8.35z14's row below. | `d74c2ff5` |

## Milestone 8.35z14 Name the blocker in a mid-resolution fizzle, and advance the wire to /8 completion evidence

| ID | Outcome | Commit |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 8.35z14 | The SIXTH amendment's remaining known-problems entry and the last of four wave-one integration milestones, authored in worktree branch `8.35x-blockerid` at `3d358a7d` and merged here onto 8.35z13. A mid-resolution fizzle - a mandatory effect step's `ImpossibleMandatory` stop - named the acting hero and card but never the blocker, even though milestone 8.35l's `AppliedRuleModification`/`ActionUnavailability` channel already answers "who blocked this" at OFFER time; this carries the same answer into the EXECUTE-time stop. `EffectOptionFeasibilityQuery.RecordExplanation`'s two-step question (the refusing node's own `IEffectRefusalExplainingCapabilityHandler`, else the consumer that narrowed a selection to nothing) is extracted into a new internal `TryExplainNodeRefusal`, reused by `EffectResolutionSession` at both sites that produce `ImpossibleMandatory`. **The answer rides the existing `EffectResolutionEvent` as a new `Blocking` property and deliberately NOT `EffectNodeOutcome`/`PendingResolution`:** that record persists into a snapshot only while a resolution is genuinely paused, and `ImpossibleMandatory` always completes the whole resolution (`RefuseCapability` clears the continuation), so no snapshot payload is touched and **the schema stays v36, re-verified at this revision**. `FizzleNoticePresenter.ComposeMessage` prints through the same `ActionUnavailabilityLabels.BlockedBy` vocabulary the offer-time tooltip uses, falling back to the generic line wherever either half is absent - never a guessed blocker. **WIRE VERDICT, MEASURED.** `NetworkMatchLogRow` gained two optional string fields appended after `amount`: a wire-format change, so `guards-private-online` advances **7 -> 8** with the usual "older peer refused before payload interpretation" doc/test pair. It rides the existing BlockedAction row rather than a new message kind - the same reasoning 8.35r's event channel and 8.35z3's frame-degradation corrective record - because the two fields are absent on every other row kind and on an unexplained BlockedAction row. Byte cost: 2 bytes (two false flags) on every row that is not an explained BlockedAction; ~2 + len(hero id) + len(card id) on one that is. `TheProjectionFrameCarryingTheLog_StaysInsideTheSealedBound` - the seeded six-seat walk's own frame-size guard at 4/5 of the sealed bound - passed unchanged with the new fields present. **PRIVACY:** the blocking source is always a standing PUBLIC Active effect (rulebook p.17 - an effect's controller and source card are public), so naming it reveals nothing a viewer's projection does not already carry in `ActiveEffectSourceCardInstanceIds`; the match log's "seat filter is an identity, not a redaction" property is unaffected, since the channel is unchanged and only two of its fields grew richer. **AN ID COLLISION THE INTEGRATION HAD TO FIX, AND IT IS THE TWELFTH ON RECORD.** The branch wrote its own attribution as **`Milestone 8.35x`** in 32 places across 15 files. 8.35x is not a free placeholder - it is an already-COMPLETED milestone (the TENTH amendment's match-log drop-down, the EIGHTEENTH's lobby controls, the button-treatment pass), and three sentences naming it in that sense are still in the tree. Left alone, the blocker-identity channel would have been permanently attributed to the button pass and every future grep for "what did 8.35x do" would have returned two unrelated milestones. The rename could not be a whole-file substitution for exactly that reason: it was done BY LINE NUMBER against `git diff HEAD -U0`, rewriting only the 32 lines this merge ADDED, and verified both ways afterwards - the three genuine references still stand, and no file outside this milestone's fifteen gained an `8.35z14`. **The reusable rule: a placeholder milestone id must be a token that cannot already be a real id.** The other three branches in this wave used `8.35x-motion`, `8.35x-spotlight` and no token at all, and those renamed safely because a hyphenated suffix is unambiguous; a bare `8.35x` is not a placeholder, it is a claim. **REGRESSION:** `EffectResolutionSessionBlockerIdentityTests` (new) proves the engine seam against a handler that refuses-and-explains, one that refuses silently, and the real `SyntheticEffectCapabilities.NoteActorV1` that applies - so `Blocking` is attached exactly where the refusing party could explain itself and nowhere else; `MatchLogChannelTests` gained the Sabina-Back-to-Back-vs-Magnetic-Dagger shape in both stopped-node forms plus a half-stated blocker and the existing no-blocker case now asserted explicitly rather than merely implied; `PrivateOnlineEventChannelTests` round-trips a bare row and a full wire frame with and without a blocker; and `FizzleNoticeStreamTests` gained a real-fixture pair asserting the composed notice prints "WASP" and "MAGNETIC DAGGER" for the pinned case and the plain generic line for the unpinned control. That pair's own remarks record honestly that milestone 8.35l's offer-time gate now refuses this exact pairing before it can reach a live mid-resolution stop - that milestone doing its job rather than a gap here - so the entry is hand-built from the same real, currently-standing restriction to exercise the naming pipeline rather than re-race a now-closed window. **COMBINED VALIDATION FOR THE WHOLE WAVE, at the final revision `60e9cbd8`, which is what the three rows above point here for.** **dotnet harness 2,052 / 2,052** (2,044 plus this milestone's eight; the other three milestones are Presentation-typed or Unity-excluded and add none). **Complete unfiltered Edit Mode 2,762 / 2,762**, 0 failed and 0 skipped, against the 2,622 baseline - plus 140. **Complete unfiltered Play Mode 281 / 281**, 0 failed and 0 skipped, against the 279 baseline - plus 2, the two Play Mode guards the presentation branches owed. **Phase 4 p95 frame gate passed on both complete Play Mode runs of this tree, measuring 2.139 ms and 4.543 ms against its 10.0 ms bound**; the spread is the machine rather than this wave, because on the slower run `avgMapRebuildMs`, `avgSnapshotMs` and `avgAcceptedCommandRefreshMs` all roughly doubled TOGETHER, and both samples sit inside the band this measurement has occupied across the tmp corpus for many milestones. **Six-client rehearsal `SixClient/Full` PASSED** across all three cohorts, and its own identity line is the wire bump riding a real session end to end: `protocol=guards-private-online/8 schema=36 map=official-single-lane@1 content=76a95714...e141f97`. The match cohort's six seats and its seatless host all report an identical `REHEARSAL_OUTCOME version=2970 cause=FinalWaveFlipped winner=blue-team`, the rejoin cohort's seven the same at version 3112, and the host-loss cohort correctly reports no outcome at all because its host is terminated; **zero `SessionRefused` in all three host logs**. **Solo rehearsal PASSED** - milestone 8.35z13's owed cohort - with one scripted seat and five spawned test-driver seats claimed through the host's own real FILL WITH TEST DRIVERS control, playing a COMPLETE match to `MATCH_OUTCOME version=1824 cause=FinalWaveFlipped winner=blue-team`, the dedupe proven by pressing the control twice, and no test-driver process outliving the host that spawned it. **THE FIRST COMPLETE RUNS FOUND THREE THINGS AND THAT IS THE POINT OF RUNNING THEM**: the conductor-gate defect recorded in 8.35z12's row above, a shipped Play Mode fixture that still characterised the pre-conductor lifecycle, and a frame-rate-dependent assertion in that same fixture - all three fixed at `60e9cbd8` with the reasoning in its own commit body, and none of them visible to any Edit Mode run. Governance manifest and access experiment regenerated after the governed-doc edits; `Validate-LivingDocumentation.ps1` PASSED - 73 governed active leaves, 135/135 self-tests, 0 errors and 8 warnings, every one pre-existing and none worsened, with `Roadmap/PHASE_8.md`'s own warning CLEARED by this wave's ownership review and `PRIVATE_ONLINE_ARCHITECTURE.md` improved from 4,422 to 4,315 words by routing its superseded compatibility assessment out under that document's own rule. | `d74c2ff5` |


## Milestone 8.35z15 Stop offering a card-text repeat that has no legal subject completion evidence

| ID | Outcome | Commit |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 8.35z15 | A live user report during play, with screenshot evidence. Xargatha resolved Cleave - "Target a unit adjacent to you. After the attack: May repeat once on a different enemy hero" - against `hero-brogan`, the only enemy hero adjacent to her; he defended with Bulwark (defense 4 against attack 4, `SuccessfullyDefended`); and rulebook p.10's confirm-or-decline then opened for a repeat whose legal subject set was empty, because the one enemy hero in reach was the one the repeat is forbidden to take. The user's words: "why is this being offered when its impossible." **THE FOURTH INSTANCE OF ONE CLASS** - milestone 8.29d greys an Attack whose mandatory text binds no target, 8.29e stops an optional branch that can apply nothing from opening confirm-or-decline, 8.35z5 closed the up-to-N floor-0 double path, and this is the after-action repeat family answering milestone 8.25a's own reusable question ("is the set an offer admits a subset of what the answer can perform?") wrongly. **THE CAUSE IS AN UNWIRED PROBE, NOT A MISSING RULE.** Milestone 8.25e extracted `AttackDefenseCardActionStrategyBackend.CanStartRepeatedAttack` out of `PlanRepeatedAttack` precisely so a repeat's offer and its answer could not drift, and `Rules/RULE_SYSTEMS.md` row 48 has recorded the ruling for every path ever since - "whether the second instance can start is asked of the board as it then stands, under the target binding the asking node declares, rather than of the action that ended". `EffectResolutionContext.RepeatIsUnavailableFor(binding)` is where a `RepeatAction` node asks it, and it reads `RepeatIsUnavailable \|\| (repeatCanBePerformed != null && !repeatCanBePerformed(binding))` - so a frame supplying no probe answers "available" unconditionally. Only `TriggeredEffectResolutionAuthority` supplied one. Both card-text sites in `CardActionResolutionAuthority` - `ApplyCardText`'s start half and `AnswerCardTextChoice`'s resume half - supplied none, so **every repeat printed inside rulebook p.14 had been offered unjudged since milestone 8.10**. Both now supply `binding => CardTextRepeatCanBePerformed(...)`, and the exclusion the offer is judged against is extracted as `CardTextRepeatExclusionFor` and read by `RecordOwedAttackRepeat` too, so the offer and the answer read one statement rather than two copies that can drift. **THE GATE IS WHETHER THE TARGET IS KNOWN, NOT WHICH MOMENT THE TEXT PRINTS AT - AND THAT WAS MEASURED RATHER THAN REASONED.** The first draft gated on `CardTextTiming.AfterTheAttack`, on the argument that every binding here excludes the Attack's own target and p.14 does not choose one until step 2. Arien's Violent Torrent prints its repeat at "Before the attack:" and was excused by that gate. Driven on a board holding one adjacent minion, its node turns out to be reached **with the target already chosen**: it opened p.10's confirm, accepted the confirmation, and started nothing at all. So the gate is now "the enclosing Attack's target is defined", which covers both moments, and Violent Torrent is the second member of the class rather than an exception to it. Judging it also required telling `PlanRepeatedAttack` that the Attack occupying `pendingAttackResolution` is the SUBJECT of the question rather than an obstacle to it (`theAskingInstanceIsStillUnderWay`, which excuses only the named instance); that guard exists so `StartRepeatedAttack` cannot clobber a live resolution, and asked as a question it would have refused every Violent Torrent repeat - measurably worse than the defect being closed. **PRINTED READINGS, SOURCED RATHER THAN ASSUMED.** (1) *A defended Attack still offers its repeat.* p.10: "After you perform this action, you may perform the entire action again (with the same range, restrictions etc.)" - nothing conditions on the outcome, and no code couples a repeat to the attack's result; the reported case was a successful defence, so the fix keys on the empty subject set and nothing else, and `CleavesRepeat_WithAnotherEnemyHeroInReach_IsStillOffered` is the same defended Attack with somewhere to go, still opening. (2) *Minions do not satisfy Cleave's repeat.* The verified content already states it - the printed instance admits p.19's units and the repeated one admits enemy heroes, which `RepeatTargetBinding.DifferentEnemyHero` narrows to `AttackTargetKind.Hero`; her parenthesis ("You may repeat even if the original target was a minion") is about the FIRST target, not the repeat's. (3) *Suppression, not milestone 8.35s's grey-with-reason vocabulary.* That grammar attaches a cause to an `ActionOffer` a player can hover before choosing; a `RepeatAction` node publishes no offer - its only states are a pause that stands and a pause that does not - and the node's own rule has said since milestone 8.23 that an ineligible repeat is skipped in silence, because "asking, and then doing nothing when the player confirms" is the one outcome a `may` must never produce. A greyed ACCEPT nobody can answer would be that outcome with a label on it. **THE CLASS, MEASURED IN BOTH DIRECTIONS.** New `RepeatOfferFeasibilityTests` enumerates every `RepeatAction` node the dealt roster's action definitions print - **twelve**, keyed on the kind of action each repeats, because for an Attack the action's effect root IS its card-text body and a walk naming the property it arrived by reports each one twice. **Four repeat an Attack** and are this milestone's family (`xargatha-cleave` and `xargatha-rapid-thrusts` on `DifferentEnemyHero`; `wasp-thunder-boomerang` and `arien-violent-torrent` on `DifferentTarget`); **seven repeat a Skill** and are untouched, because starting a Skill instance needs nothing from the board and p.10 stops it at whatever mandatory step it cannot take. A second walk reads the printed transcriptions instead, so a face that prints "may repeat" and composes nothing cannot hide: it reconciles exactly, the one dealt card printing a repeat with no node being Tigerclaw's Cloak and Daggers, a permanent after-action ability no walk over `ActionDefinitions` reaches and which milestone 8.25e already judges through the same query. **A guess the measurement corrected**: Xargatha's Desperate Cleave prints the clause word for word and composes nothing, so it looked like a second exception - but it is p.20's Handicap card, which p.6 returns to the box, so it is not in the dealt configuration at all. **ALL FOUR ATTACK REPEATS WERE DEFECTIVE, not just the reported one.** Pre-fix observation with the two production files reverted and the new fixtures in place - exactly the four empty-subject cases fail and nothing else does: `ThunderBoomerang_OffersNoRepeatWhenItTookTheOnlyUnitInReach`, `CleavesRepeat_WithNoOtherEnemyHeroAdjacent_IsNotOfferedAtAll`, `RapidThrustsRepeat_WithNoOtherEnemyHeroInReach_IsNotOfferedAtAll` and `ViolentTorrentsRepeat_WithNoOtherUnitInReach_IsNotOfferedAtAll` red, while `CleavesRepeat_WithAnotherEnemyHeroInReach_IsStillOffered`, the three existing Thunder Boomerang cases and both structural tables stayed green throughout - which is what proves the new cases test the empty set rather than the offer as such. Post-fix 11/11 on the same filter. **THE FINDING THE COMPLETE PLAY MODE RUN PRODUCED, AND IT IS THE MILESTONE'S MOST REUSABLE ONE.** The first complete run came back 278/281 with three journeys red - `Cleave_AttacksAnAdjacentMinionAndIsOfferedItsRepeatOnAHero`, `CleavesRepeat_IsAnsweredAgainAfterAnUndoAndAfterARestore` and `ViolentTorrent_RepeatsItsBeforeAttackTextOnADifferentUnitByItsRepeatControl`. Measured offline on the exact scene boards before changing anything: Xargatha stands at `(-3,1)`, the enemy hero at `(-2,2)`, and Cleave's legal hero set is EMPTY; Arien's Tier III board admits exactly one Violent Torrent target. **`EngagedMirrorStaging.Restage` hard-requires the two heroes to be exactly two spaces apart** - it skips any candidate pair whose distance is not 2, deliberately, so a Radius 2 disk and a bent ranged line are meaningful - **so a melee repeat narrowed to a different enemy HERO is unsatisfiable on every staged scene by construction.** All three journeys had only ever pressed the prompt's DECLINE; none had ever taken a repeat. They were not collateral damage, they were three shipped fixtures encoding the defect, and their names claimed coverage the staging guaranteed they could not have. Under a coordinator ruling (no production affordance added to serve fixtures, and the permanent gate not diluted) they were rewritten to reach a board where the repeat is real: Wasp's Magnetic Dagger prints Initiative 12 against Cleave's 11, so the enemy seat takes its turn first and spends its secondary Movement stepping onto `(-2,1)` - the only square adjacent to both her and the staged enemy Ranged minion - and Arien's reuses the fixture's existing `CloseToAdjacency`. Nothing is placed by hand; it is a position the rules produce, the same shape milestone 8.29e used when it let Wasp's Dagger clear a square before Arien acted. **All three now press CONFIRM and drive a second instance through the real controls** - Cleave's repeat offers exactly `[primitive-hero]` with no minions, the hero is clicked on the board, and p.14's Defense question opens for the repeated instance - which is the first live coverage in this build that has ever TAKEN a repeat rather than refusing one. **All three method names were kept**, because they become true rather than needing renaming, which also avoided the rename-versus-matrix trap that cost milestone 8.29e a whole run: every one of them is named by a `PlayerPathRow` constant in `XargathaPlayerPathGateTests` / `ArienPlayerPathGateTests`, and the permanent gate needed no change at all. **NO SCHEMA MOVE, NO WIRE MOVE, NO CONTENT REVISION, NO SNAPSHOT FIELD**: the probe is a question asked of state that already exists, `SnapshotCompatibilityCorpusTests` round-tripped unchanged so no digest was re-pinned, and no artwork-catalog obligation arises. **VALIDATION.** dotnet harness **2,059 / 2,059**, 0 failed and 0 skipped, against the 2,052 baseline verified at `bc7f2c3e` before any edit - plus 7. Complete unfiltered Edit Mode **2,770 / 2,770**, 0 failed and 0 skipped, against the 2,762 baseline - plus 8 (seven new `RepeatOfferFeasibilityTests` cases and one new `ConfiguredAttackCardTests` case). Complete unfiltered Play Mode **281 / 281**, 0 failed and 0 skipped, against the 281 baseline - **unmoved**, because the three journeys were rewritten rather than added. Schema stays **v36** and the wire stays **`guards-private-online/8`**. **REUSABLE TOOLING THIS MILESTONE BUILT**: a scratch Play Mode compile probe (Core + Application + Network + Presentation + `Assets/Tests/PlayMode/**` against Unity's own DLLs plus `nunit.framework` from `Library/PackageCache/com.unity.ext.nunit`), which compiles in seconds and catches a broken journey before a fourteen-minute Unity launch; it reports **three permanent false errors** under `net472` (`System.Index` is unavailable there, hitting `SeatScopedMatchHudPlayModeTests` twice and `RoundResolutionAuthority` once) and those three should be ignored rather than chased. Also measured for planning: the complete harness now takes **6-8 minutes**, complete Edit Mode about **14**, and complete Play Mode about **3**. | `db41f6f2` |

## Milestone 8.35z16 Add rulebook pp.5-6's printed 2v2 setup and both game lengths, and advance the wire to /9 completion evidence

| ID | Outcome | Commit |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 8.35z16 | The ELEVENTH amendment's first half, authored in worktree branch `8.35x-formats` at `1c7aed19` and merged here onto 8.35z15. **THE PRINTED READING CAME FIRST AND NOTHING WAS INVENTED.** p.5's setup chapter is titled "GAME SETUP (4 or 6 players)", p.4 calls 4 or 6 the recommended counts, and p.20 confirms it from the other side by covering the ways to play "outside of the recommended 4 or 6 player count described on pages 5-6" - so a 2v2 is one of the two PRINTED setups rather than a variant of one. Step 2 splits players into two equal teams; step 4 makes the Wave count a function of the game length alone (Quick 3, Long 5); step 5's table crosses length with count for Life counters (4 players 4 and 6; 6 players 5 and 8); step 7 names the four-player dashboards - Wasp the Warmaiden and Xargatha the Changed for Red, Arien the Tidemaster and Brogan the Destroyer for Blue - and builds the six-player line-up out of them by adding Tigerclaw and Sabina "for the third players", so a 2v2 deals four of the same six printed sets this build has verified and reads nothing new. Step 10 places one Ranged, four Melee and one Heavy per team with no mention of the player count, so all four formats share milestone 8.25's existing twelve placements; step 11 returns the Handicap card to the box and p.20 brings it back only for an ODD count, so no printed format deals one and Wasp's Crackling Dagger and Sabina's `S-02` stay deferred on their own trigger. **THE ONE THING p.6 DOES NOT PRINT, recorded rather than invented:** which of a Throne's three marked hero spawn points a two-hero team occupies. Answered the way milestone 8.25 answered the identical question about which dashboard goes to which player - the points are interchangeable at setup and a draw between them changes nothing any rule, projection or snapshot can observe - by stating it once: each hero keeps the spawn point it already stands on in the six-player board. **Ledger row `DEV-008` records it as an approved deviation at integration**, with its own review trigger. **WHAT WAS ADDED.** `MatchFormat` is the printed menu as four rows (`2v2-quick`, `2v2-long`, `3v3-quick`, `3v3-long`) with `WaveCount` derived from the length and `TeamShape`/`HeroesPerTeam` from the count, so the stored numbers cannot disagree with the derived ones; `Default` is `3v3-quick`, so **every existing caller, fixture, script and shortcut composes exactly the match it composed before**. `OfficialFourPlayerParticipants` is step 7's four seats reusing the six-player id constants, and `QuickGameSetupConfiguration.CreateOfficial(format)` takes each team's first `HeroesPerTeam` rows off one `OfficialHeroRows` table - step 7's own construction rather than a truncation invented here - so one source still owns every hero's set, epithet and spawn point. `-guardsMatchFormat` is accepted beside EVERY role including the lobby, deliberately: the lobby refuses an address, bind, port, seat or key because those are typed into it, while a format is not an invitation but the match this process composes, settled before any front door opens. **THE FAILING OBSERVATION, RUN AT `bc7f2c3e` BEFORE THE FIX.** A console probe over the pre-fix Core/Application/Network sources in a throwaway worktree (the pre-fix constructor cannot compile the closing check, so the observation is a run rather than a red test): Quick and Long of the SAME player count produced **byte-identical admission tuples** - `guards-private-online/8 build=same-build schema=36 map=official-single-lane@1 content=76a95714...e141f97` for both - `FirstMismatch(quick -> long) = None`, and the two were **admitted as compatible**. Post-fix the same pair reports `MatchFormatMismatch`. **WHY THE ELEMENT IS ITS OWN ELEMENT, MEASURED IN BOTH DIRECTIONS.** Post-change over all four formats: `2v2-quick` heroes=4 defs=72 waves=3 lives=4 digest=`b101a417aacb5d3c`; `2v2-long` heroes=4 defs=72 waves=5 lives=6 digest=`b101a417aacb5d3c`; `3v3-quick` heroes=6 defs=108 waves=3 lives=5 digest=`76a95714224331fb`; `3v3-long` heroes=6 defs=108 waves=5 lives=8 digest=`76a95714224331fb`. Two things at once: the six-player digest is **byte-identical to the pre-change measurement**, so the refactor moved nothing about the configuration a player already loads; and the digest is equal WITHIN a player count across lengths and different ACROSS counts, which is exactly why the count half needed no new element and the length half did. Folding the length into the content digest would have closed the gap with no wire move but falsified that digest's own stated contract - it "agrees when and only when a snapshot written by one build would rebind on the other", which stays TRUE of a Quick/Long pair - and `RISK-COMPAT-001` explicitly refuses a second, coarser content identity beside the snapshot's own. **It is DERIVED, not declared:** `PrivateSessionIdentity.ForComposition` now takes the `QuickGameSetupState` and reads the participant count, Wave count and agreeing per-team Life count off it, because a caller that composed one setup and declared another is indistinguishable at the wire from the failure this element exists to catch. `DescribeComposition` is TOTAL - a composition matching no printed row (every staged mirror, one-hero and ally fixture) is named `custom-2p-w3-l5` rather than failing - so no composition has an undefined format element and no path exists on which the check silently does not happen. It is checked BETWEEN the map revision and the content digest though its enum value (22) is appended after every existing one, because wire codes are not check order; both facts are documented at both sites, and the consequence is that a 2v2-vs-3v3 pair now reports a fact a player can act on instead of `ContentDigestMismatch`. **WIRE `guards-private-online/8` -> `/9`**, reconciled once at the final revision on top of 8.35z15's `/8`: the Hello payload carries one more string, so a version 8 peer reads it as the content digest and then finds the key proof missing and fails to DECODE rather than mis-comparing, and version 8 is refused from the bounded header before either side interprets a payload. Both hard-coded version pins read 9 and the older-version sweep now refuses 8 as well - both tripwires EXTENDED rather than renumbered. **SCHEMA v36 DOES NOT MOVE, and this was measured rather than assumed:** the three numbers a format changes are already ordinary counted values in the payload - the Wave total as an `Int32` in the round section, each team's Life counters beside it since v20, and the participants as a counted list - so a 2v2 payload is a v36 payload of different VALUES, not of a different SHAPE. No corpus re-pin, no six pinned docs, no four bare version assertions, no artwork catalog. **MEASURED BLAST RADIUS:** a sweep for `== 6`, `!= 6`, `Count == 3`, `Take(3)` and `Take(6)` over Core and Application production returns nothing - seats are derived from the participant graph and `PrivateSessionProtocol.MaxSeats = 6` is an upper bound only; `PrivateLobbyModel` was already seat-count-agnostic; milestone 8.21's `PlaceConfiguredHeroes` already DEACTIVATES the hero view of any seat a configuration did not deal, so the largest anticipated Presentation risk was already paid; and the permanent player-path gate does not move, being content-derived per hero over four of the same six verified sets. Team agreement does NOT degenerate at 2v2, contrary to the amendment's watch item - that concern belonged to the dropped 1v1, since `TeamAgreementCoordinator` quantifies universally over `participantHeroes` and a two-hero team is a real two-way agreement. **INTEGRATION FINDING:** the branch predicted +36 harness cases and delivered exactly +36 - but main's true pre-wave baseline was **2,060**, one above the 2,059 the handoff carried, which is why the first post-merge total read 2,096 rather than 2,095; measured directly by running the harness at `d79248c2` in a throwaway worktree rather than assumed. **Combined validation for the whole wave is in 8.35z19's row below.** | `26e27e89` |

## Milestone 8.35z17 Stage a situation by hand and play it with the real rules: the Director sandbox completion evidence

| ID | Outcome | Commit |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 8.35z17 | The TWELFTH amendment's first half - the user's "I want a test environment where I can spawn stuff and test it" - authored in worktree branch `8.35x-sandbox` at `0c846c91` and merged here onto 8.35z16. **THE AMENDMENT'S OWN OPEN CHOICE IS CLOSED BY THIS BUILD'S CONFORMANCE, NOT BY PREFERENCE.** The amendment requires a deliberate decision between staging-as-recorded-commands (giving the staging its own replay/undo) and a load-a-staged-description entry point, and says either is honest. In this build only one is available, and the conformance suite is what says so: a recorded staging operation would have to be a `DirectorCommandKind` member; `NetworkSubmissionCodecTests` asserts `covered Is.EquivalentTo(vocabulary)` over `Enum.GetValues(typeof(DirectorCommandKind))` - "Every command kind has at least one specimen, or a kind could ship with no round-trip evidence at all" - and `SeatAuthorizedSubmissionTests` classifies every kind for seat authorization. So a staging command kind either **breaks two governed ratchets** or **is carried on the wire and offered to seats**, which is exactly what the amendment forbids. **The description option is also strictly better, for a second measured reason:** the snapshot-restore analogue would have DESTROYED the Undo the amendment requires, because `MatchSession.RestoreSnapshot` runs `history.Clear()` and sets `canRecordHistory = false`. Composing the staging into `MatchSessionInitialState` instead means every downstream mechanism is the ordinary one - and **a restart returns to the STAGED board rather than to a dealt one**, which is what a tester setting a situation up and replaying it actually wants. It costs no new code, because `RebuildInitialState` is already what restart and a retained-prefix Undo re-run. **WHAT STAGES:** hero placement; minion spawn, removal and relocation; a hero's chosen hand; a hero's Level and coins; p.8's Wave counters and p.13's Life counters. The board half follows `EngagedMirrorStaging`'s stated terms exactly - it relocates the setup's own pieces before the match starts, "a staging decision the rules never see", on `DEV-004` terms - and the hand half moves cards only through `CardLifecycleAuthority`'s own transitions, so nothing invents card state. **THE TWO REFUSALS, with measured reasons rather than workarounds.** (1) **Placed tokens and markers.** `AdjacentMoveAuthority`'s token-carrying constructor arm is private and says why: "Tokens enter only through a restore. No configured setup places one: a token reaches the board when an effect places it." `RebuildInitialState` hard-codes an empty marker register, `InitialScenarioDescription` carries no components at all, and `ComponentLifetimeOwnershipConformanceTests` pins component lifetime to the capability handlers - so a staged token would be a second origin for a board object no rule authored, which is the rules path the amendment forbids. If staged tokens are wanted, that is a deliberate separate decision about component provenance, not an oversight here. (2) **The mid-round cursor.** A composed match begins at round 1, turn 1; a round index is not a setup decision but where the rules have got to, so staging one would assert a history that did not happen. The Wave and Life counters, which ARE setup decisions the composed setup already records, are staged. **THE ONE NEW SEAM, and why it is composition rather than a rules change.** Coins and Level had no composition-time route at all: `RoundResolutionAuthority` constructed `HeroProgressionState` and `HeroCoinLedger` itself, always Level 1 and always zero. The seam follows a precedent whose own doc comment makes the argument verbatim - `RoundResolutionComposition.TeamLives` says p.13's Life counters travel in the composition "rather than being created by the authority because the starting count is a setup decision ... and the composed setup is the one record of it". Everything added is optional and defaults to today's behaviour: `StartingProgression` and `StartingCoins` on `RoundResolutionComposition` reuse the two EXISTING restore-state types, so there is **no new persisted vocabulary and no schema move**; `EnsureCoinLedger(participants, seed)` reads its seed only on the creation the lazy singleton would have made anyway, so a ledger a match has already moved can never be overwritten and there is still exactly one ledger. **No rule changed** - `HeroProgressionState.TryRaiseLevel` remains the one transition and `HeroLevelUpStepRules` still owns whether a purchase is owed, what it costs and what it upgrades. **FAILING-THEN-PASSING BY KNOCKOUT:** with `MatchAggregate`'s two staged-progression arguments replaced by `null`, exactly **3 of the 21 cases fail** - `AStagedLevelAndPurse_AreWhatTheHeroBeginsWith`, `ARestartReturnsToTheStagedBoard_AndNotToTheDealtOne` and `TheSandboxComposes_TheOrdinaryDirectorClientOverAStagedInitialState` - while the other 18 stay green, which is what says the board-staging half and the progression half are independently covered rather than one implying the other. `AnEmptyStaging_ComposesTheDealtMatchByteForByte` is the guard that the seam is inert, comparing a sandbox-composed empty staging's snapshot against an ordinarily composed match's byte for byte. `AThoroughlyStagedBoard_IsPlayedOutByTheOrdinaryRules` drives a seeded walk through the seats over a board no dealt setup would produce - two heroes relocated, a minion removed, a minion spawned, one hero at Level 4 with coins to spend and a hand of two - because staging bypasses p.6's setup restrictions deliberately, so "is a staged board still one the rules can run" is a real question rather than a formality. **INTEGRATION FINDINGS.** The branch's own NOTES recorded **20** cases and its commit body **21**; measured here as the harness delta 2,096 -> 2,117 with nothing else moving, the commit body was right and the notes undercounted. The two files this branch shares with 8.35z15 (`CardActionResolutionAuthority.cs` and `AttackDefenseCardActionStrategyBackend.cs`) **auto-merged with no conflict**, and both intents were then verified present rather than trusted: z15's repeat-feasibility machinery and this milestone's seeded `EnsureCoinLedger` overload coexist, confirmed by grep and by both compile probes. **NOT COVERED AT THIS TIER:** that a spawned minion's borrowed spawn point behaves correctly through a full p.8 Lane Push respawn - the fixture proves spawn points stay unique but does not drive a Wave to exhaustion. `DEV-004`'s ledger row was EXTENDED at integration rather than merely cited: its subject list named Undo, restart, snapshot tools, the event log, animation speed and hidden-state inspection, and did not name board staging, so the branch's "already covers it" was a slight over-claim and the row now names the sandbox, its two refusals, and the parser-level enforcement 8.35z18 adds. **Combined validation for the whole wave is in 8.35z19's row below.** | `26e27e89` |

## Milestone 8.35z18 Reach the sandbox from a launch flag, and say on screen which board it staged completion evidence

| ID | Outcome | Commit |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 8.35z18 | The TWELFTH amendment's second half - the sandbox HUD affordances milestone 8.35z17 deliberately left owed, because they are Presentation and a worktree can neither compile-probe Presentation cheaply nor run Play Mode. **Built at integration rather than merged**, following the recipe 8.35z17's own notes left. **THE COMPOSITION SLOT.** `SandboxMatchComposition.Compose` is called beside `EngagedMirrorStaging.Restage` in `MatchSceneCompositionRoot.Compose()` - exactly where the branch's notes said - because both answer the same question, what board does this scene begin from, and both answer it before any view is generated or any authority exists. From there **the staged setup IS the setup**: `EnsureViewsForConfiguredSeats`, the board rebuild and the runtime all read it without knowing a staged board from a dealt one, which is what makes Undo, restart, the event log and replay work unchanged. The composed `MatchSessionInitialState` is carried into `ConfiguredMatchSceneRuntime` as an optional appended parameter rather than recomposed there, because staging MUTATES the configuration in place (a staged hand is a real card moved by `CardLifecycleAuthority`) and composing it twice would deal the hands twice. **`DEV-004` IS ENFORCED IN THE PARSER RATHER THAN ASSERTED IN A COMMENT.** The ledger row requires the affordance never be exposed in a real online match, and a staged board reaches one by exactly one route - a host composing it before its seats join - so `-guardsSandbox` is refused beside the host, client and lobby roles, before any surface exists that could decide otherwise. "Director-only" is therefore a property of what will PARSE, not of what a caller remembers to check. This is the deliberate opposite of `-guardsMatchFormat`, which IS accepted beside every role, and the two sites document each other: a format is the match a whole table agrees to play and is checked into the admission tuple, while a staging is one machine's private experiment that no admission tuple describes. The banner is Director-only a second, independent way - it hides with the rest of the Director tools when the lobby hands the HUD an online seat. **THE BANNER.** `SandboxStagingSummary` is engine-free and lives in Application beside `SandboxMatchComposition`, following `PrivateLobbyModel`'s precedent, so **the Unity-free harness covers it** while the view holds nothing but the strings it produces. Its lines are DERIVED from the composed staging rather than recorded beside it, so the banner cannot describe a board other than the one on screen. It sits OUTSIDE the Director drawer deliberately: inside it, a tester would have to open DIRECTOR to discover which board they are on, and the one thing this surface exists to guarantee is that they never have to ask. It is laid out by its BOTTOM edge so it grows upward as staged lines are added rather than needing a height the layout would have to predict, and it clears the DRAWER's rect rather than the toggle's, unconditionally - the drawer opens upward from the toggle, so clearing only the toggle would put the banner underneath it, and reserving the space either way keeps the banner still when a tester opens DIRECTOR instead of making it jump. **TWO DEFECTS THIS MILESTONE'S OWN CONSTRUCTION FOUND.** (1) `SandboxHeroStaging.Hand` is **null** when a hand was not staged and an EMPTY list when one was deliberately staged with no cards - two different facts - so the first draft's `hero.Hand.Count > 0` would have thrown on the commonest staging of all; the summary now reads `Hand != null` and the distinction is recorded at the site. (2) The banner is drawn from RETAINED state and re-rendered at the end of `Build()`, because the composition root learns the staging before it configures the HUD and `Configure` rebuilds the whole tree - a banner drawn only at the moment it was announced would be discarded by the very next rebuild, "which is exactly the shape of a defect that survives every headless test and shows up as a blank panel in a live run". **REGRESSION:** new `SandboxLaunchAndSummaryTests`, 11 cases, all harness-visible - the flag names a staging and marks the process a sandbox, an absent flag reads as the ordinary dealt match, a flag with no path is refused, the flag is refused beside the host, lobby and client roles each naming "Director-only", the sandbox and format flags still compose on the one line where both are legal, an empty staging is NAMED as the dealt board rather than drawn blank (a tester shown a blank panel would read it as a bug), counts agree with their nouns, and a staging that round-trips through its own `guards-sandbox-staging/1` file format summarises identically, which is the join between the flag and the banner. **Combined validation for the whole wave is in 8.35z19's row below.** | `26e27e89` |

## Milestone 8.35z19 Make a tooltip render in a Player build at all completion evidence

| ID | Outcome | Commit |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 8.35z19 | From the user's own live evidence - no packaged build has ever shown a tooltip - authored in worktree branch `8.35x-tooltiplayer` at `a7a94680` and merged here onto 8.35z18. **THE PREMISE WAS VERIFIED RATHER THAN ASSUMED**, before any code was written: Unity's manual page for the Tooltip event states "This is an Editor-only event", and a Unity staff reply on Unity Discussions ("Does VisualElement.tooltip work at runtime?") states that tooltips are Editor-only and that a custom runtime solution is needed. That matches the user's evidence exactly. A grep of `Assets/GuardsDigital` for `.tooltip =` finds **29 assignment sites** across just two files (`UiToolkitMatchHudViews.cs` 27, `ConfiguredMatchHudRenderer.cs` 2, the TIEBREAKER rail banner) - including milestone 8.35s's greyed-control blocked reasons, which had been written, shipped and invisible to every player. **None of them changed**: `RuntimeTooltipLayer` is one popup plus one root hook that serves every existing string as it stands. **THE DISABLED-ELEMENT WRINKLE, and why the solution does not depend on the research being right.** A blocked reason lives on a control `HudElements.MarkUnavailable` has already called `SetEnabled(false)` on. Research indicates only the click/press family is withheld from a disabled element's own callbacks, corroborated IN THIS CODEBASE by `HudElements.ApplyButtonMotion`'s own `PointerEnterEvent` handler guarding on `enabledInHierarchy` - dead code if the event never reached a disabled target. **The layer does not lean on that**: it never registers anything on the hovered element at all. One capture-phase (`TrickleDown`) `PointerMoveEvent` handler on the HUD root - the identical pattern the match-log drop-down's click-away handler already ships, "a trickled event still walks every ancestor of its real target on the way down, root included" - resolves `evt.target` and reads `.tooltip` as a plain property. Picking, which decides `evt.target`, is governed by `pickingMode` alone and is unaffected by `enabledInHierarchy`, so a disabled control is still the resolved target and its string is still readable without asking it to handle anything. **DESIGN:** a pure Hidden/Pending/Shown hover state machine, pure clamped anchor math in the same reference-unit space `MatchHudPanelScaling` already uses, and an ancestor walk for "closest tooltip wins" matching the Editor's own default bubbling - so timing and geometry are provable with no live panel. No per-frame cost when idle: the delay is one `schedule.Execute(...).StartingIn(400)` and a stale timer is a harmless no-op checked by candidate identity. Positioning happens in two steps - provisional coordinates and `display: Flex` force one layout pass, then the real `resolvedStyle` size gives the clamped anchor and only THEN does opacity rise - so nothing is ever visible at the unclamped spot. It eases in on opacity alone over `KineticMotionDurations.PanelEnterSeconds` and `HudAmbientLife.EnterEasing` ("ease in, no pop") and opts out of the ambient sweep both structurally (its own pre-set `transitionProperty` makes `HudAmbientLife.Qualifies` return false, traced through that method's source) and explicitly (`HudAmbientLife.OptOutClass`). **THE BRANCH EXECUTED WHAT IT COULD AND SAID SO:** a standalone reflection runner over the real Unity DLLs ran **12 of 17 cases green**; the 5 touching a live `VisualElement` threw `SecurityException: ECall methods must be packaged into a system module` outside a real Unity process - independent, concrete confirmation that `VisualElement` genuinely requires Unity's own runtime rather than a defect in the test code. **Those 5 were the Unity-owed debt, and they ran here.** **INTEGRATION FINDING the branch missed:** `RuntimeTooltipLayerTests.cs` uses `UnityEngine` and `UnityEngine.UIElements` but was NOT added to the Unity-free harness's `Exclude` list, so the harness build would have failed on CS0234/CS0246 for that file alone - the documented stale-`Exclude` tripwire, now listed. The fixture contributes **+0** to the harness and its 17 cases are Edit Mode only. **COMBINED VALIDATION FOR THE WHOLE WAVE, at the final revision, which is what the three rows above point here for.** **dotnet harness 2,128 / 2,128**, 0 failed and 0 skipped, against a baseline of **2,060** - and that baseline is itself a correction. The handoff carried 2,059, the first post-merge run read 2,096, and the formats branch predicted +36; rather than accept one unexplained case the baseline was MEASURED by running the harness at `d79248c2` in a throwaway worktree, which returned 2,060. The branch's arithmetic was right and the handoff was one low. The +68 decomposes exactly: +36 formats, +21 sandbox core, +11 sandbox HUD, +0 tooltips (harness-excluded). **Complete unfiltered Edit Mode 2,858 / 2,858**, 0 failed and 0 skipped, against the 2,770 baseline - plus 88, which also corrects a branch's own count: the tooltip fixture is **20** cases under Unity, not the 17 its notes recorded, because NUnit expands its `[TestCase]`s, so 2,770 + 36 + 21 + 11 + 20 = 2,858. **All 20 pass, including the five that threw `SecurityException: ECall methods must be packaged into a system module` outside a real Unity process** - the Unity-owed debt the tooltip branch recorded, now paid. **Complete unfiltered Play Mode 284 / 284**, 0 failed and 0 skipped, against the 281 baseline - plus 3, this wave's three live journeys and no rewrites. **Six-client rehearsal `SixClient/Full` PASSED** across all three cohorts, and its identity line is the wire bump riding a real session end to end: `protocol=guards-private-online/9 schema=36 map=official-single-lane@1 content=76a95714...e141f97 build=1.0+20260905222745-3615485f+6000.5.9f1`. The match cohort's six seats and its seatless host all report an identical `MATCH_OUTCOME version=5257 cause=FinalWaveFlipped winner=blue-team`, the rejoin cohort's seven the same at version 2704, and the host-loss cohort correctly reports no outcome at all because its host is terminated; **zero `SessionRefused` in all three host logs**. **STAGED SANDBOX SMOKE PASSED**, run as one continuous sequence rather than as separate assertions because that is what a tester actually does: a staged board composed through the surface a launched build now uses, **160 commands driven by the deterministic loop to round index 3**, then one undo and a restart returning the staged origin **byte for byte** with the staged Level and coordinate both back, and the banner derived from the same description reading `1 hero placed | 1 hero given a Level or coins | 1 hero given a chosen hand | 5 Wave counters`. (A first draft of that probe committed cards directly and stalled the moment all six seats had committed - it could not resolve a turn, which is a fact about the driver and not about the board - so the recorded run drives the real command surface.) **THE LIVE RUN FOUND ONE THING, AND IT IS THE REASON THESE THREE JOURNEYS EXIST.** The first complete Play Mode run came back 283/284 with `TheTooltipPopup_RendersOverADisabledControlAndClampsInsideThePanel` red: the popup DISPLAYED and carried the disabled control's exact tooltip string - the milestone's whole claim, proven - but `resolvedStyle.opacity` read 0.0 at the instant `display` flipped to `Flex`. Diagnosed rather than patched away: that is the layer working as designed, because `Show()` deliberately flips display at a PROVISIONAL position with opacity still 0 so that the forced layout can fire `GeometryChangedEvent`, and only `PositionAndReveal` then clamps the anchor and raises opacity over the 0.22 s eased transition - which is exactly what stops the popup ever being painted at the unclamped spot. **Displayed is not the same as visible**, and the assertion had sampled the one frame where they differ by construction. The journey now waits for the reveal on its own and says why, so a popup that displayed and stayed invisible - as indistinguishable to a player as the Editor-only tooltip this milestone replaces - would still fail it. Re-run filtered: 3/3. **THE 2v2 BOARD VERDICT: it draws correctly and nothing was owed.** Four pieces stand, two Red and two Blue read off the drawn views; both hero rails lay out with real width and height and do not overlap, two a side being strictly slacker than the measured three-rail fit; the tray keeps its footprint; and milestone 8.35z10's tie-breaker rail banner is present on the shorter roster. Milestone 8.21's `PlaceConfiguredHeroes` had already paid the largest anticipated Presentation risk by deactivating any seat's view the configuration did not deal, and this is the measurement that had never been taken. **THE FOUR-SEAT REHEARSAL COHORT WAS NOT RUN, and that is recorded rather than skipped quietly.** `PrivateOnlineRehearsalPlan.ClientProcessCount` is a `const` equal to `PrivateSessionProtocol.MaxSeats`, and `PrivateOnlineRehearsalContractTests` asserts it equals 6 - a governed conformance assertion deliberately pinning the six-process gate. A four-seat cohort therefore means parameterising the plan AND revising that assertion, which is a milestone-sized decision about what the rehearsal gate means rather than a flag; the 2v2 is proven playable by `OfficialFourPlayerSetupTests` and by the live board journey instead. **NO PACKAGE WAS CUT**, by explicit user direction at integration time; `Testing/FUTURE_GATES.md` records that the next cut carries everything since `Playtest-8.35z14-wave.zip` and must be a fresh build, because that package speaks `/8` against the current `/9` and can therefore neither admit nor be admitted, so it is no longer 8.36 evidence. Schema stays **v36** and the wire is **`guards-private-online/9`**. Governance manifest and access experiment regenerated after the governed-doc edits; `Validate-LivingDocumentation.ps1` PASSED - 135/135 self-tests, 73 governed active leaves, 27 production components, 295 archived bodies, 0 errors and 8 warnings, every one pre-existing and none worsened. This wave's required ownership review CLEARED `Roadmap/PHASE_8.md`'s own warning by moving 8.35y-8.35z2 to `PHASE_8_COMPLETED_4.md` (active 11,948 / 79.7%, destination 12,262 / 81.7%), and `Project/CURRENT_IMPLEMENTATION.md` improved from 4,499 to **4,416** against its 4,500 HARD limit by consolidating the per-version schema journal its own maintenance rule forbids - 84 words of headroom where the next milestone would have had one. | `26e27e89` |

| 8.35z20 | Found by the Misa inventory worker's Silver-cohort calibration montage during the roster-expansion inventory pass and corroborated independently by the Whisper worker's six-hero Silver control montage and the coordinator's own 5x crop (reviewed by the user): Tigerclaw's Blend Into Shadows shard row and content have recorded `Sec Mov 2` since milestone 8.25e5b, but the face (`tigerclaw2__7379736__front.png`) prints ONE secondary ribbon icon - a riveted Defense shield reading 2, in the ribbon's lower slot - and no boot anywhere. **THE MECHANISM is the one-icon slot-position trap**: on a two-icon card the upper slot is always Defense and the lower always Movement, but a ONE-icon card's lone icon sits in the lower slot regardless of its own type, so a reader trusting position rather than glyph shape misreads a shield as a boot.
