## Milestone 8.35z21 Fire the last-minion Lane Push at the end of each action instance, repeats included completion evidence

| ID | Outcome | Commit |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 8.35z21 | Landed by squash-merging the isolated worker branch `worktree-agent-a47d98396073bd0d2` (`7d15fc05`, sixteen files, +1169/-120) onto main at `9b24698f`. **The ruling.** Official designer answer of 2026-06-18 in the rules channel `#rules-questions-goa2`, harvested 2026-09-06 and cross-checked against the shipped implementation before any code moved: p.8's "immediately Pushed" fires "At the end of the action. However, repeat instances are separate actions." The first half conformed already. The second did not - the mid-turn push was recorded at the Attack's outcome and drained at exactly one site inside `AdvanceInitiative`, which every repeat family reaches only after its LAST instance, so one push fired after all of them and a p.10 repeat chose its target on the pre-push board. The build stated the wrong ordering deliberately, in a comment whose justification ("the turn is stopped from the moment the first one is recorded, so a second cause could only arise inside the same action instance") is false across a repeat, because a running repeat instance keeps `HasPendingResolution` true. **Design.** The drain body moved out of `AdvanceInitiative` into one `ResolveOwedImmediateLanePush`, returning null for "carry on" and a result for "the turn stops here". It is called first at every action-instance completion boundary - `CompleteAttackOrStartItsRepeat`, `CompleteAttackAndAdvance`, `CompleteMovementAndAdvance`, `CompleteSkillStep`, `CompleteGeneralRuleActionAndAdvance`, and `FinishTriggerAnswer` only where an action is held - and last at the old site as an unchanged backstop. `TheTurnsActionIsUnderWay()` is asked inside the drain rather than at each call site, so the drain and the six Attack completion points cannot disagree about whether p.14's page is open. The mid-action resume adds no state: `ContinueAfterImmediateLanePush` branches on the still-selected actor, which is impossible at any non-boundary drain site because acknowledging Initiative is what clears the selection - verified against all seven `AdvanceInitiative` call sites. `CompleteHeldActionAfterItsTrigger` was renamed `CompleteTheActionTheTurnHeldOpen`, a trigger no longer being the only thing an action can be held across. The one-push cap becomes per instance rather than per card resolution, and `DeriveImmediateLanePushAtEndOfAction` re-asks the recorded cause against the board as it then stands so the winner is read at the end of the action; it was widened to public rather than internal because a Core internal compiles in the dotnet harness and fails under Unity's real assembly split (the 8.12 trap). **Schema 36 to 37, required and not incidental.** Draining inside an action creates a state v36 could not encode: a push stopped on p.8's sidebar Place with a repeat instance decided and not started. The four owed-repeat fields (`owedAttackRepeat`, `owedAttackRepeatExclusion`, `owedAfterActionRepeat`, `owedAfterActionRepeatExclusion`) were never persisted, because a v36 build always started an owed repeat inside the command that decided it. Not only a reload concern: `MatchAggregate.Commit` captures a pre-image on every command and rolls a refused one back by deserializing it, so the decision vanished at the next REFUSED command. v36 is rejected rather than migrated on `RISK-COMPAT-001`'s standing ground - the absent field's only reading, "no repeat is owed", is false in exactly the state a migration would have to be right about. Byte delta measured at the pre-change revision, on fixtures the change does not itself move: +10 bytes on every payload whose turn carries a card-action authority (coin-earning session 9,035 to 9,045) and zero on four fixtures carrying none; the paused-push fixture's further +5 is this milestone's behaviour rather than its format, the push now stopping with the card unresolved and its actor still selected. In passing this closes a gap milestone 8.22 opened without knowing: its after-push trigger already stopped the page after an owed repeat was recorded. **Pre-fix observation and the new fixture.** `MidTurnLanePushInstanceTimingTests` (six cases) drives Arien's Violent Torrent on the official map. Case 1, `APushOwedByTheFirstInstance_ResolvesBeforeTheRepeatAsksForItsTarget`, is recorded failing before the change and passing after; the measured event stream is the whole corrective - `Turn.ImmediateLanePushRequested`/`Resolved` now land at rows 24-25, between `Action.AttackResolutionCompleted` and the repeat instance's own `Action.AttackTargetChoiceRequested`, where they previously followed both instances. The other five pin the pushed-board target question (composed against 8.35z15), the byte-identical snapshot of a push stopped inside the action, the resume that starts the repeat on the pushed board, and BOTH winner readings - a winner the rest of the action changed, and an end of action naming no winner leaving the recorded answer standing. Test support gained an optional `obstacles` parameter on `StagedHeroBoard.Create`/`ArienBoard.Create`, because p.8's sidebar owes a Place only when a respawning minion's own Spawn point is taken. **Re-pinned, each with its reasoning extended in place rather than overwritten:** `CardAggregateInvarianceTests`' nine digests with its three equality groups surviving exactly; `SnapshotCompatibilityCorpusTests`' sixteen fixtures renamed `-v36` to `-v37` with digests re-pinned and the reject decision recorded; and the five literal version assertions in `DefeatRemovalStageTests`, `MatchFormatAdmissionTests`, `MinionAttackResolutionTests`, `RosterPausingSourceTests` and `RosterStateOperationCapabilityTests`, the two narrative ones extended rather than renumbered. **THE INTEGRATION CHECK THAT MATTERED.** The branch measured 2,134 against PRE-8.35z20 main, and 8.35z20 had re-derived twenty seeds and digests across ten files. Neither side's numbers were trusted: the merged tree was rebuilt and the complete harness re-run on it, returning 2,134/2,134 with zero failures, which is 8.35z20's 2,128 baseline plus exactly this milestone's six new cases and no re-derivation owed. The squash merge itself was textually clean - 8.35z20's twenty-six files and this branch's sixteen do not intersect - and the semantic risk (Tigerclaw's `tigerclaw-8-35z20-rev-1` content revision moving a pinned digest this branch also re-pinned) is what the re-run disproves rather than an argument. **Three tiers, complete and unfiltered, on the merged tree:** dotnet harness 2,134/2,134; Unity Edit Mode 2,864/2,864; Unity Play Mode 284/284 (104 s, in line with the previous complete run's 98 s, so a real full suite and not a truncated one). Baselines move 2,128 to 2,134 and 2,858 to 2,864 for the six new cases; Play Mode is unchanged at 284, no journey added or renamed. `Tools/Validate-LivingDocumentation.ps1` is green with the eight pre-existing threshold warnings and no new one. **Ceremony.** Five rules-ledger rows land with it, four drafted by the read-only cross-check and a fifth this milestone creates: the mid-turn push timing amendment and the end-of-action winner derivation (with its no-winner reading marked as this project's and not the designer's) on `Rules/RULE_SYSTEMS.md`'s Lane Push row; the `if able` corroboration on its ordered mandatory/optional row, cross-referenced from ledger question 13; and two new ledger questions - 16, the board edge as multiple virtual hexes, ANSWERED and measured (82 terrain spaces forming a complete ring, zero playable spaces with an off-map neighbour, one-to-three terrain neighbours per coastal space, reproducing the designer's own figure) with a standing per-hex constraint recorded; and 17, the boot item movement bonus, left OPEN because the designer's answer was an uncaptured image and a guess is not an answer. Six governed documents carry the schema mirror `Tools/PinnedConstants.txt` names; the `TECHNICAL_RISKS.md` and `CURRENT_IMPLEMENTATION.md` halves are prose rather than token swaps, and the risk row paid for its new sentence by dropping a clause `CURRENT_IMPLEMENTATION.md` already owns in full, landing at 4,491 of a 4,500 hard limit. The required warning-threshold ownership review moved milestones 8.35z3 through 8.35z5 verbatim to `PHASE_8_COMPLETED_3.md` - the destination changing for the first time since the fourth shard opened, that shard now being at 12,262 words itself - leaving the active shard at 11,964 and the destination at 11,154. **Named residual, recorded rather than decided:** the push is ordered against the repeat INSTANCE and not the repeat OFFER. Both shipped repeat families print their offer before a push can exist - Violent Torrent prints its repeat as "Before the attack", answered at p.14 step 1 (measured, not assumed), and the after-action family's trigger stands at `ActionEnded`, the same instant "the end of the action" names - and the rulebook does not order the two either. | `949a86f4` |

## Milestone 8.35z22 Give the practice bots a brain a seat can actually run completion evidence

| ID | Outcome | Commit |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 8.35z22 | Landed by squash-merging the isolated worker branch `worktree-agent-a3f006dead810f63e` (`40a50308`, fourteen files, +2389/-174) onto `a2dce200`. The branch was built at schema v36 off `d79248c2`; it pins no schema version and no digest anywhere, so the v37 tree it merged onto needed no re-derivation, and the merge was textually clean because nothing on main had touched `PrivateSeatDriver.cs` since that base. Phase A of the FOURTEENTH amendment (user, 2026-09-04: "tier one sounds good"). A static evaluation plus policies over it, replacing the tier-0.5 greedy pass at the seat driver. Gate: **82.5%** against the shipped greedy pass over twenty seeds played from both sides (33W/7L), **80.0%** on held-out seeds it was not tuned on, **94.9%** against random-legal, at 0.045-0.051 ms per decision against a 2.0 s watchable pace. **THE COMMISSIONED LOOKAHEAD CANNOT SHIP TO A SEAT, and that is milestone 8.32's privacy law working rather than a defect.** The amendment asked for candidate-action simulation over match copies using the engine's own snapshot/restore. A practice bot is a spawned process holding an ordinary network seat: from `AttachOnlineClient` on, every mutable read is rebuilt from the wire; the locally composed host is never advanced and so captures a match that has not started; and the wire publishes other heroes' hands as counts without identities and carries no command log to replay into a shadow session. A bot that could simulate the live match would be a bot that could see six hands. It ships as an optional capability wired only where full information exists, and the gate was measured on the SHIPPED configuration with it off, because measuring one configuration and shipping another is the probe-fidelity mistake 8.35g and 8.35u both record. **Acting FIRST is a disadvantage, measured rather than reasoned.** The commitment policy first weighted printed Initiative positively, from a true fact - the resolution takes the maximum total, so higher Initiative acts first - and an inference self-play falsified. Flipping the sign alone moved 51.3% to 82.5%, and the ablation moved the commitment family from 2.6 points to 33.8: the family was not weak, it was pulling the wrong way. Recorded at length on the constant, because a negative weight there reads like a typo a later reader would fix, and recorded as a measurement rather than a rule. **THE INTEGRATION FOUND TWO THINGS THE WORKER'S OWN GREEN SUITE COULD NOT.** First, **the six hand-minted `.cs.meta` files were malformed and Unity SILENTLY IGNORED THE SOURCE FILES**. Each carried a full `MonoImporter` block whose final line had no terminating newline; Unity answered `YAML Parsing error 'Parser Failure at line 11'` and `does not have a valid GUID and its corresponding Asset file will be ignored`, so `Assets/GuardsDigital/Application/Bots/*.cs` were never fed to the Application assembly at all - the compile inputs contain zero references to them - and the only visible symptom was three `CS0234`/`CS0246` errors in `PrivateSeatDriver.cs`, in a DIFFERENT assembly, saying the namespace did not exist. The Edit Mode run wrote no results XML, which is the compile-failure signature. A single-assembly dotnet harness is structurally blind to all of it and reported 2,141 green. Fixed by rewriting all six in this project's own convention, verified against `PrivateSeatDriver.cs.meta` and `MatchAggregate.cs.meta`: two lines, `fileFormatVersion: 2` and the 32-hex `guid:`, no importer block, LF, no BOM. The GUIDs were kept; none collided. **Second, and the reason the Solo cohort is not optional: A DETERMINISTIC BRAIN CANNOT ESCAPE AN INFEASIBLE OFFER BY LUCK.** The shipped driver did nothing on a refusal - it re-ticked, recomputed the same decision from the same unchanged projection, and submitted the identical command again. Under random-legal, and under the tier-0.5 pass that falls back to it, the next tick drew something else and the seat moved on; nobody had reason to look. Tier-1 is deterministic, so one offered-then-refused command became a hot infinite loop. The first live Solo cohort ran cleanly for 1,022 versions and then spun **more than eleven thousand consecutive `SessionRefused` refusals on one seat at a frozen version 1022, with zero acceptances**, until it was stopped. The cause is not the brain: it is the offer-feasibility defect below, which the brain turned from a latent hazard into a permanent one. The self-play harness never saw it because `BotSelfPlay` drops a refused candidate and re-chooses - so the harness was measuring a driver that did not ship. `PrivateSeatDriver` now remembers what was refused at the current projection version, drops it, and chooses again from what remains, which never widens the candidate set and therefore keeps every answer one the seat was already offered; when every offer at a version has been refused it says so once rather than spinning silently. The re-run cohort PASSED: a complete match to one converged outcome, **645 accepted submissions against 24 refusals**. **Two engine defects found, neither in bot territory, neither fixed here, both owed correctives.** (a) The projection offers a Defense card the rules then refuse, with `PendingDefensePreview` and the `DecisionContract` both listing it and the preview reporting `SuccessfullyDefended` - three independent seeded repros (seed 1/cmd 525, seed 3/cmd 280, seed 14/cmd 750), refusal site among the guards in `AttackDefenseCardActionStrategyBackend`; same family as milestones 8.35l and 8.35z15. Random-legal missed it in 60 runs and greedy hit it in 3, which is 8.35f's lesson again: a state a random walk passes through cannot be found by waiting for the walk to stop there. This is the defect the livelock above stands on. (b) `CaptureSnapshot` produces snapshots the SAME session cannot restore, reproduced with no bot involved - plain random-legal play capturing and immediately restoring at every step threw `SnapshotLoadException` ("Restored Lane-Push request does not match authoritative end-of-round state") at seed 20 step 518, with a second distinct failure under lookahead naming an effect definition the configured match does not provide. It bears on the seat undo of 8.35f and the snapshot restore of 8.35i, which stand on the same machinery, and it is why the lookahead latches a poisoned flag and degrades to the static judgment rather than taking a build down. The evaluation's nine terms are public, content-generic and integer-scored so ties are exact, with a maintenance covenant recorded on the type: every term reads a structural fact the rules publish for all content and none names a card, hero, action or effect, because the roster is expanding and a per-card term rots the day a new hero lands. One rulebook fact is used as a labelled public approximation - p.13's Level-to-Tier row pricing a Defeat - because the real authority reads the hero's own cards, which a bot cannot see. Every gameplay decision draws from a deterministic xorshift generator rather than `System.Random`, whose algorithm differs between Mono and .NET and would otherwise make every number above a statement about a harness-only twin. Team agreements are deliberately unchanged and still deterministic. **Evidence, complete and unfiltered on the merged tree.** dotnet harness 2,141/2,141 (6 m 49 s), from 2,134: the seven new gate cases and nothing else, with the four `[Explicit]` diagnostics reported Skipped and OUTSIDE the total. Unity Edit Mode 2,871 passed / 0 failed / 4 skipped (total 2,875, 1,151 s), from 2,864: the same seven, with the four `[Explicit]` counted in `total` but never passed or failed. Unity Play Mode 284/284 in 99.5 s, unchanged. Solo rehearsal cohort PASSED. `Tools/Validate-LivingDocumentation.ps1` green: 0 errors, the same 8 pre-existing threshold warnings and no new one; both governance artefacts regenerated. Every tier was re-run after the driver fix, not before it. **The governed non-goal was amended deliberately, as the amendment itself demanded.** `Project/WORKFLOW_AND_TERMS.md`'s "AI opponents." becomes one bounded exception, bounded three ways and explicitly not a general licence: VEHICLE - a spawned test-driver process holding an ordinary keyed network seat, never a shipped single-player mode, a difficulty setting or a product-AI claim; INFORMATION - a driver chooses only among commands its own seat was already offered, scoring them from its own owner-scoped projection, so the privacy boundary is exactly the one every human seat has; PURPOSE - it exists so one person can playtest a six-seat game. The FOURTEENTH amendment's ledger row is marked FIXED with the exception quoted verbatim, and records that phase B is not authorized here. The required warning-threshold ownership review moved milestones 8.35z6 and 8.35z7 to `PHASE_8_COMPLETED_3.md`, continuing 8.35z21's run, leaving the active shard at 12,360 and the destination at 12,214 - the destination is now itself within 540 words of its own 85% line, which the next review inherits. | `5103c30e` |


## Milestone 8.35z23 Make every snapshot this session takes readable by the same session completion evidence

| ID | Outcome | Commit |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 8.35z23 | Landed by squash-merging the isolated worker branch `worktree-agent-a3ee45ca9f87b89a5` (`8d35fbfd`, four files, +330/-14) onto `e11e6559`. Textually clean, and no `.cs.meta` was minted because the branch adds no file - both new cases join fixtures that already exist, which is the cheapest shape a corrective can have. The branch's placeholder id was the bare token `8.35x`, replaced at all six sites (one in `RoundResolutionAuthority`, two in `AuthoritativeSnapshotSerializer.CardAction`, two in `LanePushTransitionTests`, one in `XargathaPrintedSetTests`) after sweeping every roadmap shard, every branch, every live worktree and every `tmp/` draft for `8.35z2[3-9]` and finding it free, then sweeping again immediately before commit. **CAUSE ONE, the trusted-root lookup.** `FindTrustedTriggeredEffectRoot` re-binds a pending Skill frame's named effect definition to trusted content before accepting a byte, and reached a triggered effect only through `CardDefinition.UltimateRule`. Milestone 8.24 shipped a second source - `ActiveEffectDefinition.TriggeredEffect`, Xargatha's Constrict and Final Embrace - whose frame opens with `ActionId` `primary-skill` on a card printing no Ultimate at all, so the writer recorded a name the reader could not authorize. The second route is added bound exactly as strictly as the first: the named action must itself compose a node whose registered capability declares an Active effect naming that reference, walking `action.EffectRoot` and `action.SecondCardTextRoot` and resolving every capability through `EffectCapabilityCatalog.TryLoad`. Deliberately not "any registered triggered effect", which would let a payload hang one card's triggered effect off another card's action. **CAUSE TWO, a restore check that outlived its moment.** `RoundResolutionAuthority.Restore`'s end-of-round push guard asked two board questions about the instant the Minion Battle ended. That the current Battle Zone still matches the request is false by construction while the push runs - p.8's transition advances the zone at step 2 and then stops on a blocked Place, `CompleteEndOfRoundStage` does not advance the stage on a `Paused` outcome, and `PendingLanePushRequest` clears only in `CloseFinishedLanePush` - so the serializer wrote that state and refused it; it is now asked only while the push is not running, and that gating is MEASURED against a reproduced failure. That no losing-side minion stands in the zone stopped being an invariant at 8.19c, when `pushLostBySubstitution` made a push owed with the zone still occupied; which cause made a given request is recorded nowhere in the payload, so no reader can ask it, and it read raw `board.Minions` where the creating rule reads effective units and heroes. It is REMOVED, and that removal is REASONED from the creating rule's own comment rather than measured - the evidence is deliberately split, because claiming a measurement for the second clause would be false. **THE METHOD NOTE, and it is the transferable one.** The standing `[Explicit] SnapshotRoundTripDiagnostic` PASSES on `e11e6559` - 20 seeds, 12,000 round trips, clean - and that is evidence of nothing: 8.35z21 moved the Lane-Push drain, so every seed walks different commands and the walk simply stopped visiting the states. A seeded walk that goes quiet after an unrelated rules change has not proved a fix. Both causes were fully live and are now pinned by two deterministic fixtures of about half a second with no bot, walk or seed in them. Pre-fix observation by stashing the two Core files: both new cases fail with the verbatim reported messages (`Snapshot names effect definition 'xargatha-constrict-defeat@...' which the configured match definitions do not provide for action 'primary-skill'` and `Restored Lane-Push request does not match authoritative end-of-round state`), and pass after popping. The mid-turn twin did not already cover the push case because it holds an `ImmediateLanePush`; only the end-of-round `PendingLanePushRequest` is board-checked on restore. **No persisted movement, measured rather than assumed:** schema stays v37, wire stays `guards-private-online/9`, no content revision and no digest moved; `CardAggregateInvarianceTests` and `SnapshotCompatibilityCorpusTests` pass unchanged with nothing re-pinned, and both new fixtures assert `CaptureSnapshot() == payload` across the round trip. Post-fix breadth sweep in a throwaway probe: 60 seeds x 600 steps = 36,000 capture-and-restore round trips into the live session, zero capture failures, zero refusals, zero throws; probe deleted before commit. The diagnostic post-fix reports 0 of 20 seeds, 12,000 round trips, capture 0.278 ms, restore 6.349 ms, payload 273,124 chars. **A SECOND-ORDER FRAGILITY IS RECORDED AND NOT FIXED:** `MatchSession.RestoreSnapshot` rolls a failed restore back by RE-DESERIALIZING the pre-image, so an unreadable state throws again on the recovery path, uncaught, damaging the session where the contract promises "refused, unchanged" - which is why 8.35z22's reporting run logged a throw rather than a refusal. With both causes fixed no reachable state reaches it, so no repro remains to drive a hardening and none was invented. It belongs to `RISK-STATE-002`, whose row records the rollback as LOSSY and not as ABLE TO FAIL; it could not be written there because `TECHNICAL_RISKS.md` stands at 4,491 words against a 4,500-word HARD limit, so it is recorded as queued corrective debt in the active roadmap shard instead, with that measurement stated as the reason. **CEREMONY: a fifth completed shard.** No completed shard could absorb a move for the first time since the fourth opened - the active shard at 12,360 with two definitions due in one integration pass, `PHASE_8_COMPLETED_3.md` at 12,216 and `PHASE_8_COMPLETED_4.md` at 12,262, each within roughly 500 words of the same 12,750 line - so 8.35z8 through 8.35z12 (3,148 words, one contiguous run, kept whole because 8.35z12 composes 8.35z11's conductor by id) moved verbatim to a new `PHASE_8_COMPLETED_5.md`, indexed in `ROADMAP.md`, sized for BOTH definitions this integration lands after the previous review sized for two, came up 86 words short and had to be carried twice. **A LAUNCHER FINDING worth carrying:** `Tools/Invoke-UnityValidation.ps1` now exits 1 on every complete Edit Mode run and will keep doing so, because `UnityValidationEvidence.ps1` throws when `skipped -ne 0` and milestone 8.35z22 put four `[Explicit]` diagnostics into the Edit Mode assembly. The run is green - `result=Passed`, `failed=0`, `inconclusive=0`, and every non-Passed leaf is one of those four by design - so the totals are read from the results XML, which is the only honest source while that gate and those diagnostics coexist. Measured: dotnet harness 2,143 passed / 0 failed in 7 m 22 s (baseline 2,141 plus exactly the two new cases); complete Edit Mode total 2,877 = 2,873 passed + 4 skipped (baseline 2,871 passed, +2); complete Play Mode 284 passed / 0 failed in 106.8 s, unchanged. Application Control did not fire. Worker notes copied out of the worktree to `tmp/8-35d-8-35e-multiplayer-corrections/worker-snaproundtrip/` before it is reclaimed, the worktree and branch deliberately left in place because a tier-2 bots worker is building on this branch's content. | `0d250d36` |

## Milestone 8.35z24 Answer a Defense pause whose target stopped being reachable, instead of refusing every answer to it completion evidence

| ID | Outcome | Commit |

Placeholder id `8.35x-defenseoffer` replaced at all three sites (two comment blocks in the backend, one fixture summary) after sweeping every roadmap shard, branch, live worktree and `tmp/` draft for `8.35z24` and again immediately before commit. **THE META TRAP FIRED AGAIN AND WAS CAUGHT BEFORE THE RUN.** The branch's hand-minted `DefenseOfferFeasibilityTests.cs.meta` had no terminating newline - byte-for-byte the malformation that cost the previous integration a whole Unity run, where Unity logged a YAML parse error, SILENTLY IGNORED the source files, and wrote no results XML while the harness stayed green. Caught by inspecting the meta with `cat -A` against a neighbouring file BEFORE launching Unity rather than by reading a failed run's log; GUID kept and verified unique across `Assets`. **THE UNWIRED PREDICATE.** `AnswerDefense` re-derived `FindLegalAttackTargets` and demanded the target still REACHABLE, where rulebook p.14 names its target once at step 2 and asks only that it still be STANDING. Milestone 8.23 established exactly that narrowing, recorded it at length on `ResolveOutcome`, and wired it into one of the three sites that ask the question - the textbook 8.35z15 unwired-probe class, and it went unwired precisely because the rule lived only in a code comment. **WORSE THAN REPORTED:** the guard sat above BOTH of p.14's answers, so it refused the whole pause including the Decline. A pause no answer can leave is a dead end, not an infeasible offer, and it is what the tier-1 bot livelocked on - a deterministic brain re-picks identically, so 8.35z22's driver fix (drop a refused command for that projection version) contains the symptom without curing it, because with every answer refused the seat still has nothing legal to send. The fix is to the ENFORCEMENT - `AnswerDefense` and `RestoreAttack`'s `else` branch - and NOT to the offer builder: under `PLAN.md` RULING 17 an option is greyed only when it cannot be performed, every one of these answers can be, and reconciling the other way would have withheld the Decline and left the pause answerless. No `ActionOffer` reason was minted and the 8.35l grey-with-a-named-blocker pattern is deliberately unused. The two offer builders and the text-bound post-move validation were found by the same `FindLegalAttackTargets` sweep, checked, and deliberately left alone. **HOW THE STATE IS REACHED, needing no defender card text:** p.14's target question is asked before "Before the attack:" text is applied, so a face that names an adjacent target and then legally steps out of contact inside its own Attack leaves a standing, unreachable target. Measured on the live seeded repro - `CreateEngagedOfficialSixPlayerSession(2)`, greedy both sides, seed 3 command 516 (the reported 280 moved under z20/z21/z22, and seeds 1 and 14 no longer walk into it) - attacker at `(-3,-5)` named Arien at `(-4,-4)` at distance 1, moved to `(-2,-6)` at distance 2, and the legal-target set came back empty. The guard was identified by temporarily instrumenting each `RejectAttack` site and printing which fired (`G1-attackStale legalTarget=False printedMatch=True`); instrumentation reverted and the backend diffed byte-identical against the saved pre-image afterwards. **THE EXPOSED FAMILY IS DERIVED, NOT LISTED:** all twelve dealt Attack faces printing text at that timing are classified on two measured columns - can the body relocate the attacker, does the text rebind the target - and only `tigerclaw-combat-reflexes` and `tigerclaw-leaping-strike` are `moves|keeps`; Brogan's three charges and `tigerclaw-blink-strike` rebind and are safe. **A MEASUREMENT THAT CORRECTED A GUESS:** the first classifier keyed on "the body composes `synthetic-direct-move`" and put Arien's Noble Blade in the exposed family; the printed face refutes it, because it moves a THIRD piece and leaves attacker and target exactly as far apart as they were. The right discriminator is the move's ORIGIN (`synthetic-actor-space`) - the card text said so and the node graph did not. **RECORDED, NOT DECIDED:** the printed order is move-then-target where the engine asks target-then-move, which is what makes this state reachable at all; that is an offer-feasibility content decision of the 8.35p kind and 8.23 shipped and verified the current ordering, so it is recorded for a user ruling rather than taken here. **THE RULING NOW HAS A ROW.** `Docs/AI/Rules/RULE_SYSTEMS.md`'s p.14 "Defense reaction and detailed Attack order" row carries the standing-vs-reachable reading, its three sites, the two exposed faces, the origin discriminator and the recorded-not-decided ordering, so the next caller finds it in the ledger rather than in a comment - which is the whole cause of this defect. **THE SNAPSHOT DEBT, honestly bounded.** This closes ONE cause of it (`ThePauseSurvivesASnapshotRoundTrip` fails pre-fix with `ArgumentException: Pending Attack target is no longer legal` out of `RestoreAttack`), independent of milestone 8.35z23's two. Post-BOTH, the `[Explicit] SnapshotRoundTripDiagnostic` reports **0 of 20 seeds with a capture/restore failure over 12,000 round trips** (capture 0.307 ms, restore 6.509 ms, one ply 6.816 ms, payload 273,124 chars). **That zero is a breadth result and not the evidence, and saying so is the point:** the same diagnostic already read 0 of 20 on `e11e6559` with all three causes live, because 8.35z21 moved the Lane-Push drain and the seeded walk stopped visiting the states. The evidence is the three deterministic fixtures across the two milestones. Measured: dotnet harness 2,150 passed / 0 failed in 7 m 27 s (2,141 + 8.35z23's two + this milestone's seven, and nothing else); complete Edit Mode total 2,884 = 2,880 passed + 4 skipped; complete Play Mode 284 passed / 0 failed in 107.8 s, unchanged. No schema, wire, content-revision or digest movement - schema stays v37 and the wire stays `guards-private-online/9`. Validator green: 0 errors, the same 8 pre-existing warnings; both governance artefacts regenerated after every governed-doc edit. Worker notes copied out of the worktree to `tmp/8-35d-8-35e-multiplayer-corrections/worker-defenseoffer/`; the worktree and branch are deliberately left in place. **FOUND IN PASSING AND NOT FIXED:** 55 stale bare `8.35x` placeholder comments stand across 19 Presentation files, present unchanged at `e11e6559` and predating both branches - the exact hazard the placeholder convention exists to prevent, since a bare `8.35x` is a claim rather than a token that cannot collide. Out of a corrective's scope and recorded here rather than silently swept. | `c02ca55e` |

Its DRIVEN half stays owed: the live instrument (Wasp's Magnetic Dagger against a pinned Tigerclaw) fails on the fixture rather than on the rule, because the staged one-versus-one deals the opposing seat a hand without that card. **DIGEST AND ARTWORK, the 8.35l trap paid:** content revision `tigerclaw-8-35z20-rev-1` to `tigerclaw-8-35z25-rev-1`, all eighteen `ConfiguredCardArtworkCatalog.json` Tigerclaw rows re-pinned with it, and the `VerifiedContentIdentityTests` pin re-measured after the id rename rather than hand-edited (`f1291921057ecaee084cecbab9e8c1aca07924e4fe49fcadfe11d34b0c4f8da1`); only Tigerclaw's identity moved. Verified offline in seconds, as that trap's rule says: the catalog's six distinct `contentRevision` values and the six `ContentRevision` constants are equal sets, 108 entries, no BOM. ## Milestone 8.35z26 Give the practice bots a one-ply lookahead, in the one place full information already exists completion evidence

All resolved to main's real id, and a whole-file diff of branch against main confirmed those comment strings were the ONLY differences in all four. This is the bare-`8.35x` hazard the wave-one integration recorded ("a placeholder id must be a token that cannot already be a real id") arriving exactly as predicted. Ids swept immediately before pasting and again before commit; `8.35z26` was free everywhere. **THE META CONVENTION, CORRECTED.** All seven hand-minted `.cs.meta` files lack a terminating newline, which the milestone 8.35z22 integration report names as the malformation that once cost a whole Unity run. Checked rather than assumed, and the earlier diagnosis is INCOMPLETE: 237 of 400 sampled committed `.cs.meta` files have no terminating newline and Unity reads them all, and the entire `Application/Bots/` directory is uniformly newline-free. What actually broke at 8.35z22 was the full `MonoImporter` block, which that row itself records; the two-line `fileFormatVersion` + `guid` form is this project's convention with or without the final newline. These seven were therefore left exactly as minted, all seven GUIDs verified unique across `Assets`, and the complete Edit Mode run below is the proof Unity read every one of them. **THE VEHICLE HAD TO BE BUILT.** The amendment scopes tier-2 to "host-owned seats where the process holds full information", and there were none: both shipped practice-bot milestones spawn another copy of the executable that dials the host and holds an ordinary keyed NETWORK seat. `BotSeatConductor` plays seats from inside the process that owns the authority, choosing only among its own seat's `AuthorizedCommands` and submitting through `SubmitForSeat` - the same envelope a remote seat's submission arrives in. The network seat driver is byte-for-byte unchanged and stays pinned to its phase-A vectors. The probe is `ShadowSessionBotLookahead`, which restores a THROWAWAY session rather than mutating the live one, because milestone 8.35z23 recorded that a failed restore's own rollback re-deserializes the pre-image and throws again uncaught - a bot's opinion about a candidate is never worth risking a live match. **THE THREE HEADLINE RESULTS ARE TWO NEGATIVES AND ONE NARROW POSITIVE, and the negatives are the more useful.** (1) THE PLY IS WORTH NOTHING: it changed the chosen command in **0 of 301** decisions on a walk where 237 executed commands did move the evaluation, and ply-on/ply-off head-to-heads were byte-identical over sixteen matches and 17,509 plies. The cause is that the authority's published previews already ARE one-ply results computed by the rules, so where a command has a visible consequence the preview prices it and the ply agrees, and where it has none the ply measures nothing. It ships wired, capped and ON because the amendment commissioned it and it costs ~1% of the pace, with NO claim that it helps. (2) EIGHT OF NINE evaluation weights are structurally inert: a candidate scores `standing + delta`, `standing` is identical for every candidate of one decision and cancels, and scaling three of them to 0, 0.5x, 2x and 4x returned exactly 50.0% every time. (3) The whole margin is ONE policy weight, `CommitPrintedValue` 6 to 0; two rival vectors beat it on tuning seeds and failed out of sample, including the pair assembled together (66.7% tuning, 45.0% held-out), so the held-out discipline is what stopped the wrong vector shipping. **THE SUITE GATE WENT RED AT INTEGRATION AND THE CAUSE WAS MILESTONE 8.35z25, NOT TIER-2.** That milestone changed what two of Tigerclaw's Attack faces offer, so every seeded walk that chooses from offers re-routed - the bot bench is exactly such a walk. The four-seed suite gate came back 3W/5L (37.5%) where the branch had measured 57.5%. Rather than re-pick seeds until it passed, the full-strength `[Explicit]` measurement was re-run on the merged tree and is the milestone's margin evidence: **tier-1 55.0% (22W/18L over twenty held-out seeds, both sides), greedy 81.3% (13W/3L), random-legal 93.8% (15W/1L)**, at 18.8-22.1 ms per decision against a 2.0 s watchable pace. Three wins in eight is an ordinary draw from a true rate of 0.55 (about one in four), so the red was sampling noise reporting itself as a regression. **What was corrected is the ASSERTION, not the seeds.** The seeds are the first four held-out seeds and were left untouched, because re-picking them until the case passed would be selecting on the outcome - the one thing this fixture's tuning/held-out methodology exists to forbid. The case had asserted a win rate at or above 0.5, which is a MARGIN claim on eight matches, contradicting its own doc comment ("Eight matches cannot establish a margin - they can only catch a regression that broke tier-2 outright"). The floor is now a quarter of decided matches, which a true 0.55 bot falls below about 1.6% of the time: still a loud tripwire for an outright break, no longer a noise generator. Both the correction and the two measurements are recorded on the fixture itself, so a later reader is told which numbers were measured on the branch and which on the merged tree. **THE DRIVER'S FIRST EVER LAUNCH UNDER UNITY, and it found a defect.** `PracticeBotSeatDriver` was opt-in and compile-probed only, the one part of the milestone with no coverage. Driven here by invoking Unity directly with its real flags (`-guardsPracticeBots -guardsPracticeBotSeats <seat> -guardsPracticeBotThinkSeconds 0`) against a Play Mode fixture that composes the local seat-less practice composition, because the launcher has no extra-argument passthrough and the driver's own seams are `internal` to the EDIT Mode test assembly. First run: the driver attached, parsed, and logged `seated blue-player-01 seed=0 lookahead=True` - and then threw `ArgumentException: No seat 'blue-player-01' is bound in this match` out of `Update()`, once per frame, uncaught. **A seat the match does not bind is a malformed request, and this component's whole contract is that a malformed request fails loudly rather than quietly playing nobody's seats.** The conductor accepts any non-empty seat id, so an unbound one survived composition and surfaced as a per-frame exception with the honest cause buried. Fixed at the compose site using `MatchSeatBinding.Find`, which answers null for an unknown seat precisely so a caller can refuse instead of throwing; the refusal now names both the unknown seat and the seats the match does bind. Re-run confirmed: `GUARDS_PRACTICEBOT: FAIL no seat named blue-player-01 is bound in this match; this match binds blue-test-player, red-test-player`. **The happy path then ran for the first time:** with the real seat, `seated blue-test-player seed=0 lookahead=True` followed by `blue-test-player CommitCard accepted @0` - the driver composing the shadow lookahead, deciding, and submitting an ACCEPTED command through the host. The probe runs exit non-zero because a bot seated into a scripted journey perturbs it by design; that is the probe, not the driver. **The governed non-goal was amended a SECOND time, deliberately.** `Project/WORKFLOW_AND_TERMS.md`'s "AI opponents" exception was bounded by VEHICLE to "a spawned test-driver process holding an ordinary keyed network seat", which does not cover a host-owned in-process seat. The user's 2026-09-06 amendment authorises exactly that, so the clause now names TWO vehicles and no others - and the INFORMATION clause is split rather than widened silently, because the two genuinely differ: a network-seated driver still reasons only from its own owner-scoped projection, while a host-owned seat reasons from the whole position, which is precisely why it is confined to the local composition and refused in a seat-scoped one. What did not widen is stated in the same breath: both vehicles submit only commands their own seat was already offered, so only the boundary for THINKING moved, and it moved to a place already inside it. Measured: dotnet harness **2,175 passed / 0 failed** in 8 m 29 s (2,163 + this milestone's twelve and nothing else - the worker's report says twelve, and the batch plan's assumption of five was arithmetic on a branch baseline of 2,143 misread as 2,150); complete Edit Mode **2,938 total = 2,905 passed + 33 skipped, 0 failed** (2,893 + the same twelve; skips rose from 4 to 33 for twenty-nine new `[Explicit]` tuning instruments and diagnostics, and the launcher exited 0, which is the evidence gate fixed at `61def566` confirming every skip is Explicit-labelled); complete Play Mode **284 passed / 0 failed**, unchanged, as an Application/Presentation change adding no journey should leave it. No schema, wire, content-revision or digest movement. Validator green: 0 errors, the same 8 pre-existing warnings; both governance artefacts regenerated after every governed-doc edit. Worker notes copied out of the worktree to `tmp/8-35d-8-35e-multiplayer-corrections/worker-bottier2/`; the worktree and branch are deliberately left in place. **DELIBERATELY LEFT:** five of the nine evaluation terms (`OwnCoin`, `MinionPressure`, `EngagementProximity`, `ThreatAdjacency`, `OwnHandSize`) have never been screened by play - each appears inside a delta so each COULD move a win rate - and the worker names it the cheapest outstanding work in the milestone; the ply ships ON and measured to change nothing, with a deeper ply to the end of the acting hero's turn recorded as the follow-up if it is ever wanted to pay; and `PracticeBotSeatDriver` still has no DURABLE regression coverage - the launch above is a recorded probe, not a standing case, because the component is deliberately a clock and a null check with every decision living in Application where the harness measures it. | `efbcaefc` |

Git did not conflict on it - both branches added the declaration in different places, so the merge took BOTH, leaving two `ChooseLineDestinationV1` properties and two `LineDestinationHandler` classes under one capability id, in a tree that compiled until the second copy was used. **THAT IS THE WAVE'S CENTRAL FINDING: a duplicated declaration is a SILENT merge outcome, not a conflict, and the wave-one law about placeholder ids does not cover it.** The unification decision was pre-agreed and applied: 8.35z30's survives because it is the strict superset - an optional `ignoresObstacles` parameter defaulting to walking p.18's obstacles the way every Move did before milestone 8.23 - and 8.35z29's five Blue Skills now BIND that parameter in their shared `CreateLine` builder, so "ignoring obstacles" is the card's own words in the binding rather than a second capability wearing one id. The same silent shape appeared a second time in the same file: both branches defined `DeferredHandlers` in `SyntheticRosterCapabilities`, which DID fail to compile and was folded into one. **THREE GENUINE CONFLICTS, none semantic:** an optional `whilePerforming` scope parameter added to `ForPermission` beside 8.35z29's new factory; `TimingCheckpoint.TurnEnded` added to the trigger-producing set beside `BeforePrimaryAction`; and one optional selection parameter added beside 8.35z28's two. **ONE FAILURE, AND IT WAS THE RIGHT KIND.** The first full run after this landing came back 2,307/2,308 with `HerMintedCapabilities_AreDeferredUntilSheIsDealt` red: it asserted the whole deferred list `Is.EquivalentTo` her own two, and the list now legitimately holds four other heroes' contracts. Restated as `Is.SupersetOf` with the registered/deferred intersection left as the exact half - the same correction 8.35z28's fixture took one landing earlier, arriving from the other direction. Eighteen of nineteen faces compose; Keep the Distance is the p.20 standing deferral. Measure-first paid twice more here: `RuleModificationKind.ActivityLimit` already existed from 8.20 and `retrieve-card@1` already existed registered with no consumer. The oracle rule reached its third form - a public property on `BeforeActionTrigger` moved all six digests and is a method now; renaming an ENUM TYPE also moves digests, because type names are in the canonical string. Placeholder ids renamed at 42 sites. Harness 2,276 to **2,308**, exactly thirty-two. | `65a9dcb6` |

## Milestone 8.35z35 Offer a modal option that would do nothing, and grey only one that cannot be taken completion evidence

| 8.35z35 | THE CORRECTIVE RULES-LEDGER ROW 23 RESERVED, recorded open and roster-wide at 8.35z33 and deliberately kept out of a hero's content diff because it reaches every `Choose one` face in the build. **THE WHOLE CHANGE IS WHICH VERDICT REFUSES AN OPTION.** The feasibility walk has answered three ways since 6.5ac - empty, performable, blocked - and `FindUnperformableOptions` collapsed two of them by refusing everything that was not performable; it now refuses `Verdict.Blocked` alone. `Walk` is UNTOUCHED, so every verdict is derived exactly as before, and so are the other two callers: `CanApplyAnything` (6.5af, whether an action is worth offering) and `CanPerformOptionalBranch` (8.29e, whether a confirm-or-decline is worth opening). **Those ask whether a prompt should EXIST; this asks which answers an existing prompt may carry**, and conflating the two questions is the entire defect. **THE SPLIT LANDS ON THE PRINTED WORD, which is what says it is a transcription rather than a preference**: the refusing node's own failure policy decides the tier and that policy is p.10's mandatory-versus-optional distinction, so every arm that changes prints `may` or `if able` and every arm that stays greyed prints a mandatory step. MEASURED over all eleven modal families rather than argued - nine roster `Choose` nodes plus two synthetic - four arms move to offered (Ursafar's `mayRetrieve`, Whisper's `retrieveMay`, Whisper's Lesser Evil price bullet, the showcase Blue Feint's `feint-return-marker`) and six families do not move at all, each terminating in a mandatory step. **RED BOMBARD, THE CARD LEDGER ROW 11 WAS MEASURED ON, DOES NOT MOVE - verified from the composition rather than from the wording**: its direct-move branch's target selection and its spawn are both `EffectFailurePolicy.Mandatory`, so both modes are blocked rather than empty. Row 11's SCOPE is amended and its verdict stands exactly as recorded; row 23 is closed. **THE TEETH ARE IN THE ALL-EMPTY MANDATORY CASE**: 6.5ae's stop is `ImpossibleMandatory` and ends the whole action, so a later ordered step of the same card never ran - under this ruling the bullet is taken, applies nothing, the node records `Applied`, and the sequence carries on, which is the difference between a card that did nothing here and a card that stopped here. **THE ONE WAY THIS COULD HAVE STRANDED A PLAYER IS CLOSED BY THE CONTENT RATHER THAN BY AN ASSUMPTION**: only a node needing more selections than it offers could lose the stop while still being unanswerable, and no `Choose` in the build - roster or synthetic - declares a minimum above one, while the split only ever ADDS selectable options. **A LATENT PERSISTENCE BUG CLOSES FOR FREE**: a legitimately selected empty option now round-trips a snapshot, where restore used to refuse as `Malformed` a state the live session had just produced; the serializer itself needed no edit, deriving `canStop` from the same query. **THE SABINA HALF OF ROW 23 WAS ANSWERED BY READING THE FACE, AND ITS IMPLIED FIX WAS BACKWARDS.** Path to Victory prints "Move a friendly minion in radius 1 space, to a space in radius. May repeat up to two times." and NO different-target clause of any kind, so the shipped `RepeatTargetBinding.DifferentTarget` declaration is unsupported by the card and inert at runtime (`ExclusionFor` reads it only for Attacks); live behaviour already matches the text, and routing the exclusion into Skill repeats would have INTRODUCED a rules defect by barring a re-move the card never bars. Restated as `Has.No.Member` before any Unity launch, with the reason recorded at the site; the fix is preventive against a recorded rule rather than a reproduction, because the trap was removed before it could be observed. **THE PLAY MODE EXPOSURE WAS NARROWED OFFLINE BEFORE A LAUNCH WAS SPENT, and it narrowed to nothing.** No Play Mode journey names any of the four changed arms, none asserts `UnavailableCandidates`, and none clicks 6.5ae's stop, so the only live risk was seed drift and exactly one seeded random walk exists in the tier - `WaspEngagedUiJourneyPlayModeTests.BlockedLanePushRespawnPlace` at seed 29. The prediction was made before the run and the run confirmed it. **VALIDATION AT THE FINAL REVISION.** Complete unfiltered dotnet harness **2,484 passed / 0 failed**; complete Edit Mode **3,247 total = 3,214 passed + 33 skipped, 0 failed**, read off the results XML with the launcher exiting 0; complete Play Mode **310 passed / 0 failed**. Zero shipped-hero digest movement, and expected rather than merely observed: the identity oracle walks the card catalogs and everything added here is setup, session and presentation vocabulary that walk never reaches; claimed off the full unfiltered suite, never off a filtered pin. A clean merge was NOT treated as evidence - every shared declaration was grepped by name afterwards and each exists exactly once. Six new `.cs.meta`, LF and no BOM, GUIDs verified collision-free across the whole of `Assets/`; their trailing newline was added at integration to match the repository's 60-byte convention, the branch having minted them at 59. No schema and no wire movement. | `a4303555` |

## Milestone 8.35z37 Let the lobby decide what the match composes, and carry a hero pick to the host completion evidence

| ID | Outcome | Commit |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 8.35z37 | THE RECOMPOSITION SEAM BOTH DEFERRED LOBBY CONTROLS WERE WAITING ON - 8.35z16's format picker and 8.35z36's hero picker - landed as ONE seam. Landed from branch `worktree-agent-a815522a354c9920c` (`af1d96b5`), applied as its own delta over the selector so each milestone's arithmetic is its own. **THE WALL WAS DEEPER THAN THE SCENE'S OWN COMPOSITION, and that decided the shape.** `PrivateSessionIdentity.ForComposition` derives the entire ADMISSION TUPLE - format, map and a `ContentDigest` hashing the dealt configuration - from a composed match, so a host with no composed match has nothing to admit anybody WITH, and "boot with no match, compose when the lobby says to" is unreachable in one step. What ships: compose PROVISIONALLY at load so the lobby can admit, then RECOMPOSE from the lobby's decisions at the one-way transition. That is only honest because the provisional match is not a guess - with nothing chosen the plan's line-up is setup step 7's printed one, so the provisional match IS the shipped match. **THE PROOF IS BYTE-IDENTICAL SNAPSHOTS RATHER THAN A FIELD WALK**: a match composed late and one composed at load produce the same payload character for character on all four formats and under both coins, with the coin and 8.28b's recorded setup seed held EQUAL so the comparison measures timing rather than the draw. A field-by-field walk was rejected deliberately, because such a claim has to be extended by every later milestone that adds state, which is how it rots. The NEGATIVE half carries the weight - a reversed line-up must MOVE the snapshot - and the seat invariant is asserted against a deliberately SHUFFLED line-up, because the printed one would pass even if the seats followed the heroes. **THE LOAD-BEARING ASYMMETRY WAS FORCED BY EXISTING CODE, not chosen**: a session's seats are counted from the FORMAT and every live connection, epoch and reconnect capability is keyed by seat, so a format changed after anybody is seated moves the seats out from under them and the wire already refuses it - while a LINE-UP changes no seat at all. So the format is settled on the entry form before a listener exists and the line-up crosses the seam. **THE IDENTITY TUPLE IS FROZEN DELIBERATELY, and that PREVENTED a defect rather than deferring one**: a client returning under 8.34's reserved-seat reconnect recomputes its own tuple from a local composition that knows nothing of anybody's picks, so a host republishing an identity derived from a chosen line-up would refuse every reconnect with `ContentDigestMismatch` at a 2v2 - and at a 3v3 with today's registered roster every line-up is a permutation of the same set, so the bug would have hidden until either a 2v2 or an eighth dealt hero. **WIRE `/9` -> `/10`**: one client-to-host `ChooseHero` kind carrying a bounded hero id and NO seat (the connection identifies the picker, exactly as readiness does, so no seat can pick for another), one `HeroSelectionRefused` code covering all of p.22's refusals with the detail naming which, and two roster facts a client cannot infer - whether a row's hero was PICKED rather than provisionally composed, and p.22's first picking team, underivable exactly before the first pick. Payload measured at 10 bytes, 22 on the wire. **Schema UNMOVED at v38**, and the byte-identical proof is the evidence. **A SHIPPED DEFECT FOUND NEXT DOOR AND FIXED**: `PrivateSessionFrameCodec` bounds two wire enums by naming their last member, and the refusal bound still read `ReconnectNotAuthorized` (21) while the eleventh amendment's `MatchFormatMismatch` (22) sat past it - so a peer refused for composing a different printed setup had its reject frame DISCARDED AS MALFORMED by its own decoder and was told the host had sent something unreadable. Both bounds moved and the guard now ENUMERATES both enums rather than spot-checking, which is the only form that survives the next member. **WHAT THE FIRST COMPLETE PLAY MODE RUN FOUND, and it is why the four lobby journeys existed.** (1) **THE NAMED HUD RISK WAS REAL, and it was not the HUD.** `Recompose` retires every hero view its predecessor generated, and `Compose` then re-validated that hero views EXIST before running `EnsureViewsForConfiguredSeats`, whose whole job is to create them - so a second composition threw "requires a map, camera, pointer input, and hero views" for any configuration whose seats were all generated rather than authored. The check demanded the output of a later step as a precondition and only ever held because a FIRST composition inherits the scene's authored views. Split at the compose site: infrastructure the scene must supply is checked before, the seats a composition supplies for itself are checked once they exist. That one fix turned both `StartedNetworkSeat_UsesExistingHud_AndHostLossClearsItsControls` and `TheFormatPicker_RecomposesTheSceneBeforeAnybodyIsSeated` green. (2) **THE MILESTONE'S OWN JOURNEY COULD NOT HAVE PROVEN THE SEAM, twice over.** Its composed-heroes assertion compared SORTED SETS, and six registered heroes filling six seats is always the same six heroes - so a board that ignored every pick satisfied it. Restated per SEAT, it immediately failed with real information (seat 0 picked `hero-sabina`, board fielded `hero-arien` - the printed assignment), which exposed the second half: `PrivateSessionHost.Composition` is a settable property defaulting to null, a host with none silently keeps its provisional match, and the fixture's own wire never set it. Production wires it in `PrivateLobbyViews`; the fixture now attaches the same scene root, and the journey passes on the real path. **A set comparison and a null seam hid each other, and only the per-seat form found either.** (3) **AN ORDER-DEPENDENT ASSERTION, exposed by fixing the seam.** Two shipped lobby journeys pinned the exact control set, and whether a seat is offered PICK HERO depends on the Tie Breaker coin p.22's order opens on - which moved once an earlier journey actually recomposed the shared scene. Both restated coin-independently: everything that is not the pick control stays exact, and the pick control's own rule belongs to the journey that owns it. **THE OTHER EDIT-MODE-ONLY FAILURE was an architecture-conformance violation the harness is structurally blind to**: `RegisteredHero.Equals` compared `DisplayName`, and the Phase 4 boundary forbids rules and application code branching on a display name or printed text. Dropping it is also the right semantics - two rows naming the same hero and the same dealt set ARE the same registered hero - and it makes `Equals` agree with `GetHashCode`, which has only ever hashed the id. Caught only because that scan is Unity-only; the harness was green at 2,531 throughout. **VALIDATION AT THE FINAL REVISION.** Complete unfiltered dotnet harness **2,531 passed / 0 failed** (2,509 + 22 = `DeferredMatchCompositionTests` 7 plus `PrivateSessionHeroSelectionTests` 15, exactly); complete Edit Mode **3,294 total = 3,261 passed + 33 skipped, 0 failed**, read off the results XML with the launcher exiting 0; complete Play Mode **314 passed / 0 failed**, +4 = exactly the four lobby journeys, which had never been executed before this landing. Wire `guards-private-online/10`, schema **v38** unmoved, registered handlers **57** unmoved, no content and no identity digest moved. Validator 0 errors and the same 8 pre-existing warnings; both governance artefacts regenerated. Four new `.cs.meta`, LF, no BOM, GUIDs collision-free, trailing newline normalised to the 60-byte convention. **DELIBERATELY LEFT**: a host whose `Composition` is null still discards a completed selection SILENTLY rather than loudly - production wires it correctly and the journey now proves that path, but the guard itself is a behaviour change this landing could not validate across both Unity tiers and is named rather than smuggled in; the seeded random filler (8.28b's recorded seed); the host-side call naming a bot seat's hero; the other five p.22 methods; white-background token art and a neutral mode for `Prepare-HeroTokens.ps1`, which is presentation-scale work owed to the packaging milestone; and a live board check, since no human has yet seen a recomposed board or a team-neutral token. | `02c6c841` |

## Milestone 8.35z39 Breathe the awaited outlines and draw the board's marks as light completion evidence

| ID | Outcome | Commit |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 8.35z39 | THE LIGHT-LANGUAGE PASS, from the user's two directives and the mid-task tightening that made it smaller and safer: keep every mark's exact shape, geometry, footprint and colour semantics, and change only what the pixels inside them do. **WHAT THE TRAVELLING LIGHT ACTUALLY WAS: a 10x10 pixel gold dot** parented into every awaited element and walked round its perimeter in percent coordinates on a 2.4s loop. It had only worked since 8.35z9 - for four milestones before that it animated NOTHING, querying for `Image` elements of a different family by a different name - and the rail had no child of its own, so 8.35z9 invented one purely so the dot had something to travel on. Retiring the dot retires that host too: this milestone REMOVES up to eight `VisualElement`s from the HUD and adds none. **THE FINDING, AND IT IS THE BRAKES-VINDICATING ONE.** The first version ran the outline from its resting `ShineGlow` UP toward `ShineRim`. It read correctly in source - same warm family, eased curve, right cadence - and the offline probe measured it at **luminance 0.9223 -> 0.9567, a 3.44% swing**, because `ShineGlow` is already a 0.92-luminance near-white and its gap to `ShineRim` is almost entirely BLUE CHANNEL, which is saturation and not brightness. It would have shipped looking exactly like the static gold the user asked to replace and **nothing in the suite would have said a word**; only the measurement caught it. The rule generalises: **a light that must be seen has to be able to go DOWN.** The shipped breath kindles from the gold dimmed by a channel MULTIPLIER (floor 0.45, so hue is untouched) up to the full painted gold - **RGBA(0.450,0.416,0.300) to RGBA(1.000,0.925,0.667), luminance 0.4150 to 0.9223, swing x2.22, with g/r and b/r exactly constant at both ends**. The top of the breath being `MatchHudTheme.ShineGlow` is load-bearing rather than tidy: a freshly rebuilt element is already at a point on its own breath and is recognised on its first frame with no special case, an element the treatment stops touching is left indistinguishable from one it never touched, and the light cannot climb past the gold into a washed-out white nobody chose. **THE PREDICATE IS A SEGMENT, NOT A BOX.** The HUD rebuilds wholesale several times a second so the breath can hold no per-element state, and a rail carries four meanings on its borders at once; recognising "colours between A and B" per channel admits a volume that the Active violet and the confirm orange both land inside. `IsAwaitedGold` measures distance to the segment: nearest foreign colour **0.251** (`ShineRim`, the same confusingly-similar pair 8.35z9 already got wrong once, now asserted as foreign) against a tolerance of 0.040, a **6.3x margin**. Memoryless and ratchet-free, because the source of every lerp is the constant and never the value written last frame. **ONE CONDUCTOR, HONOURED**: the breath is a pure function of `KineticPlayback`'s clock read once per frame inside `KineticCueUnityView.Tick`, a one-word call-site change from `ApplyShineSweep()` to `ApplyShineBreath()` - no coroutine, no `schedule.Execute`, no USS transition, no second `MonoBehaviour` - and because every lit element is handed the SAME intensity on the SAME frame, a hand of cards and the tray above them breathe as one light rather than as several. It is also CHEAPER than what it replaced. **BOARD MARKS: GEOMETRY BYTE-IDENTICAL FOR EVERY MARK.** No mesh builder, ratio, radius, subdivision count or vertex moved, and all eight declared colours are unchanged; the halo alone becomes additive, so a mark can only ever BRIGHTEN the space it names and the board's printed artwork survives underneath at full strength, while the near-white core stays painted because it is what carries the shape on the bright half of the board. It costs a blend factor and nothing else - `_SrcBlend`/`_DstBlend` are ordinary shader properties, not keywords, so an additive material needs no shader variant a blended one did not already need, which answers 8.31b's opaque-in-the-player trap rather than re-opening it. **BLOOM WAS REJECTED ON MEASUREMENT**: `GuardsUniversalRenderer.asset` carries `postProcessData: {fileID: 0}` so the post-processing stack does not run at all, and `DefaultVolumeProfile.asset` declares Bloom at intensity 0. **THE DEFECT THE FIRST COMPLETE EDIT MODE RUN FOUND, and the reason the tier is owed rather than optional.** A tile that is both an Attack target and a Defense contributor draws a SATURATED core inside its blood-red halo, and it had always obtained that saturation by BORROWING the halo material - the one core in the type that is not white-biased. Once halos became light the borrow silently handed that core the luminous alpha: **expected RGBA(0.020,0.700,0.180,1.000), measured alpha 0.620**, caught by a shipped assertion whose own words are that a white-biased core disappears at gameplay scale. The borrow becomes a declared saturated-core family (owned resources 18 to 20, disposed with the rest), and a new case pins halo-is-light against core-is-paint so the next change to the light family cannot re-borrow it. **TOKEN ART**: a `-Neutral` mode writes ONE team-neutral white face per hero at `RuntimeTeamIdentity.NeutralResourcePath`, which takes no team parameter by design so no caller can bake one back in; the preparation is the existing baked route plus one step, whitening the official team RING outside the protected radius while preserving per-pixel alpha exactly, so a neutral token keeps the identical antialiased silhouette. **The check is INVERTED, and that is the evidence**: a prepared baked face must still classify as its own team by its ring, a prepared neutral face must classify as NEITHER, and the script refuses to write otherwise. Ten heroes generated and `-Check` round-tripped, all 302x302 with an identical 13,612 ring pixels (a function of face size, not of hero); **all SIXTEEN shipped team-baked faces regenerate BYTE-IDENTICALLY** by hash, which is the zero-regression proof. Silverarrow and Ursafar are declared from the bundled manifest's own `VerticalTokens` list; **Tali and Whisper are a RECORDED ABSENCE** - neither name appears on any such element, and per cards-are-the-definition that is recorded rather than guessed, since a name-matching rule would already have found nothing for Arien, whose element is `Arien2`. **VALIDATION.** Complete unfiltered dotnet harness at the wave's final revision; the four Presentation Edit Mode fixtures this milestone edits are EXCLUDED from that harness by name, so its flat total is evidence of no collateral damage and NOT evidence the new cases pass - the Edit Mode tier is what executes them, and it did: **140 passed / 0 failed** across the four fixtures, after the composite-core defect above was found by the same run and fixed. Production, Edit Mode and Play Mode compile probes clean. Zero schema movement, zero wire movement, identity oracle untouched - no file under `Core/Cards`, `Core/Effects` or any catalog was edited. | `a23ab16e` |

## Milestone 8.35z40 Unroll the match log from the top strip, newest first, and stop announcing a healthy connection completion evidence

| ID | Outcome | Commit |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 8.35z40 | THE MATCH-LOG TRAY REFINEMENTS, three items the user raised with a screenshot; item 1 is the only literal quotation the plan preserves: **"make the dropdown match the top, not to be different like this"**. **THE MEASURED OVERLAP ITEM 3 NAMES.** Both the lobby `UIDocument` and the HUD load the SAME `PanelSettings`, so the two coordinate spaces are one space and the rectangles are directly comparable: at reference 2560x1440 the top strip is y 0..96, the narration bar x 830..1730 / y 56..96, the drop-down x 960..1600 / y 100..420, and the connection banner x 896..1664 / y 92..136. The banner's rect intersected the drop-down's over x 960..1600, y 100..136 - **the drop-down's whole header band**, which is exactly the screenshot. **ITEM 1, AND WHY IT IS A SHARED SOURCE RATHER THAN A MATCHING LITERAL.** The tray drew `MatchHudTheme.PanelBackground` (0.055/0.065/0.074 @0.96) while the strip drew `MatchHudTheme.TopBarBackground` (0.031/0.039/0.043 @0.98) - literally a different, lighter dark box, which is "a foreign dark box floating under the bar" in one line of code. Two independent styles that happened to disagree are not fixed by a second literal that agrees today, so `MatchStatusBarUnityView` now PUBLISHES its own band language and uses it itself, and `MatchLogUnityView` reads it: background, corner radius 14 on the three free edges and 0 on the seam, border 1px on three edges and 0 on the seam, insets 14 left / 12 right, label size 12 closed with the strip's own hairline. The tray also hangs FLUSH (`GetMatchLogDropdownRect` dropped its `+ 4f`), so the squared unbordered top edge is a genuine seam against the strip's own bottom hairline rather than two borders stacked with a gap. **ITEM 2, AND THE HALF THAT IS NOT A REVERSE.** `MatchLogFormatter` splits into `ComposeOldestFirst` (the closed-vocabulary rendering, unchanged) and `NewestFirst` (the reading order). **A plain `Reverse()` is wrong** - it puts every ROUND/TURN header BELOW the entries it heads, which is precisely the "headers adapting correctly" half of the directive. So the stream is grouped, the groups are reversed, and each group's body is reversed beneath its own header, with a worked example transcribed into the fixture: `ROUND 1 / TURN 1 / A MOVED / A ATTACKED / TURN 1 ENDED / TURN 2 / B MOVED / ROUND 1 COMPLETE / ROUND 2 / TURN 3 / C MOVED` becomes `TURN 3 / C MOVED / ROUND 2 / ROUND 1 COMPLETE / TURN 2 / B MOVED / TURN 1 ENDED / TURN 1 / A ATTACKED / A MOVED / ROUND 1`. The undo marker moves from last to FIRST, because under newest-first the top is where "now" reads, and `scrollOffset` resets on the closed->open transition ONLY, so a new entry cannot yank a reader who has scrolled back into the history. **THE PRIVACY BOUNDARY SURVIVES BY CONSTRUCTION**: `NewestFirst` is proved to be a PERMUTATION (same lines in, same lines out), and a re-order cannot surface a fact that was not already in the list handed to it - while the `switch` over the closed kind vocabulary is byte-identical, **the diff literally ending before it**, which is the cheapest possible proof that no log content moved. **ITEM 3, THE BANNER DEMOTED.** It was shown for EVERY state the moment the online HUD attached, the healthy "CONNECTED TO THE HOST." included, with nothing distinguishing healthy from anomalous. `MatchConnectionBanner.IsHealthy` is now the exact branch `NetworkMatchFeedback.ForState` answers that sentence on - stated STRUCTURALLY rather than by reading the sentence back - and the fixture sweeps the whole state space asserting `EarnsScreen == (wording != "Connected to the host.")`, so a drift between the two fails a test instead of silently hiding a notice. `CalculateConnectionBannerRect` sits the banner under the drop-down's whole OPEN extent, clearing the closed state by containment - ONE rectangle answering "both open and closed" instead of a state-dependent position that could be wrong in one of them - and the clearance is structural at every viewport because the drop-down subtracts the banner's reserved 52-unit band from its own height budget, which costs nothing because the 320 cap is the smaller term everywhere supported. Demoted visually too: 18pt to 14pt, a 30%-wide slab to 520 reference units (narrower than the 640 log above it), the HUD's own panel chrome instead of an ad-hoc dark box, and the mixed percent/reference geometry gone. Measured rects: drop-down `(960, 96, 640, 320)` flush at `StatusBarHeight`, banner `(1020, 428, 520, 40)`, both reproduced by a replica probe gated on the FIDELITY RULE - it first reproduces the narration bar rect the live suite already pins `(830, 56, 900, 40)`, and only then are its other outputs believed. Swept at 2560x1440, 3440x1440, 1280x1024, 1280x800, 778x300, 640x200, 400x140 and 300x100: the banner overlaps the narration bar in NONE and the open drop-down in NONE, running off the bottom only at 400x140 and 300x100, panels shorter than the strip plus its own band where every element the HUD places below the strip already hangs off. **PAINT ORDER VERIFIED, since z-order has bitten here before**: statusBar < heroOverviews < tieBreakerRailBanner < matchLog < statusDock < director < tooltipLayer, all four asserted. **THE ONE TRAP, AND IT IS THE REUSABLE ONE.** A shipped Play Mode test - `OnlineMatchHudPlayModeTests.StartedNetworkSeat_UsesExistingHud_AndHostLossClearsItsControls` - asserted `display == Flex` and `text Does.Contain("CONNECTED")`: **precisely the banner the user screenshotted, asserted as a requirement**. Nothing in the presentation change would have revealed it - the harness cannot see Play Mode, the compile probes only compile it, and the assertion sits in a test whose NAME is about HUD reuse and host loss. Found by grepping every reader of `PrivateLobbyDriver.MatchStatusName` before committing: **when a change makes something stop being drawn, grep for who asserts that it IS drawn.** The file's other three banner assertions were checked and all still hold. **VALIDATION.** Complete unfiltered dotnet harness **2,536 passed / 0 failed** at the wave's final revision; all three fixtures this milestone edits are EXCLUDED from that harness by name, so it contributed **+0** to that total and the number is evidence of no collateral damage rather than that the new cases pass - the Edit Mode tier executes them. Production, Edit Mode and Play Mode compile probes clean. Zero schema movement, zero wire movement; nothing under `Core/`, `Application/`, `Network/`, `Docs/` or `Resources/` was touched. **SCREENSHOT EVIDENCE** captured at the reference viewport and sent to the user for sign-off: the tray open showing the newest-first order with its headers adapting, and the closed state beside it for the seam. | `fe0b2c6e` |

## Milestone 8.35z41 Offer every hero this build deals, not only the six the printed line-up seats completion evidence

| ID | Outcome | Commit |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 8.35z41 | THE SELECTOR-ROSTER CORRECTIVE, a pre-package blocker cleared. **THE DEVIATION.** Milestone 8.35z36's brief said the selector's roster is the DEALT set; the implementation read `QuickGameSetupConfiguration.RegisteredHeroes`, which is the printed LINE-UP table. **It stayed invisible for two milestones because with six dealt heroes all of them printed, the two lists were EQUAL and nothing could tell them apart.** They diverged the moment a hero was dealt through a staged configuration instead - Dodger at 8.35z34, then Misa at 8.35z38 - each fully dealt, registered, player-path gated and journeyed, and neither pickable. The twelve-hero package's headline feature capped at the shipped six while the build dealt eight. **THE INVARIANT INVERTS, AND ITS DIRECTION IS THE SUBSTANCE.** Milestone 8.35z38's report recorded a STRICT subset - six registered inside eight dealt - as the correct state of affairs. It is not; the subset is the SYMPTOM. The roster a picker offers is now the dealt set, and the surviving invariant runs the other way: **the printed line-up is a SUBSET of what a picker offers**, asserted row by row. **A ROSTER IS ONLY HALF A CLAIM.** `MatchConfiguration.CreateVerifiedHand` deals a selected hero's cards from a switch over hero ids that knew only the printed six and refused anything else BY NAME - so widening the roster alone would have offered a pick that threw at the moment the match composed, a lobby-time promise broken at match time. The two heroes therefore gain their own hero ids (`hero-dodger`, `hero-misa`, declared beside the printed table as `StagedDealtHeroRows`) and the switch gains their printed sets. The acceptance case asserts BOTH directions at once and BUILDS each offered hero's hand rather than reading the switch, because one direction alone is satisfiable by a lie: dealt-implies-offered would pass for a roster naming heroes nothing can deal, and offerable-implies-dealable would pass for an empty roster. **MINTING THE IDS MOVES NOTHING, and that is measured rather than hoped.** A staged one-versus-one deals these heroes under the generic staged blue hero id, because on a staged board the hero's identity is the CONTENT it holds; a selected line-up needs a stable per-hero id instead, since two of them can sit on one board and the projection must tell them apart. No existing composition names the new ids, so no snapshot, digest or pinned identity can see them until a lobby actually picks one - and the complete unfiltered harness at the final revision confirms it, moving by exactly the **+1** this milestone's own new case adds and by nothing else. **THREE SHIPPED CASES INVERTED WITH THE MEANING, not edited around it**: the roster identity now names `DealtHeroes`; and the two cases that used Dodger as the stand-in for "a hero dealt tomorrow" move that stand-in to Silverarrow rather than being retired, because what they pin is the MECHANISM and the prediction they encoded has simply come true. A hero with no printed SEAT costs nothing: a line-up moves the hero column and never a seat, so the Nth hero of a team takes the Nth marked point whatever hero it is. **VALIDATION.** Complete unfiltered dotnet harness **2,536 passed / 0 failed** at the final revision, where this milestone's own fixture is harness-VISIBLE and its cases execute - unlike the two presentation landings it rides with, which contribute +0 each. Production, Edit Mode and Play Mode compile probes clean. No content, catalog, schema or wire movement. | `fe0b2c6e` |
