## Milestone 8.35z43 Roll a refused restore back with the graphs it displaced, not by re-reading them completion evidence

| ID | Outcome | Commit |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 8.35z43 | THE STRUCTURAL HARDENING BATCH, discharging the corrective debt milestone 8.35z23 recorded against `RISK-STATE-002` plus four items queued behind it. **THE REAL DEFECT: THE ROLLBACK RAN THE DESERIALIZER ON THE FAILURE PATH.** `MatchSession.RestoreSnapshot` caught a failed restore and rolled back by RE-DESERIALIZING the pre-image - and a failed restore is precisely the situation where the state being described may be one this build cannot read. The second throw escaped uncaught, **after `aggregate.Restore` had already replaced the graphs**, leaving the live session holding a half-finished restore where the session's own contract promises "restore refused, unchanged". That is the mechanism 8.35z22's reporting run logged as a throw rather than a refusal. **THE FIX NEEDED NO NEW TYPE, AND WHY IT IS AVAILABLE HERE IS THE PART WORTH RECORDING.** The aggregate already carried `Capture()`/`Adopt()`, the graph-set detach/attach pair the replay path uses, and that pair IS a rollback that cannot fail: **`Restore` REPLACES the graphs - it deserializes a whole new set and hands it to one assignment site - and never mutates the ones it displaces, so the old graphs are still intact and reinstalling them is an ASSIGNMENT.** A command by contrast mutates its graphs IN PLACE, and for that a serialized pre-image really is all that is left, which is why `MatchAggregate.Rollback` stays exactly as it was for `Execute`. A failed restore now degrades to refused-and-unchanged with a typed refusal (`SnapshotRejected` or `EngineFault`), and the reported cause is the ORIGINAL failure rather than whatever the rollback met. **EXTENDED TO `Reconstruct` (Undo/Restart) - same defect, same two lines**: `Adopt(first)` replaces graphs identically, so its catch carried the identical fragility, and hardening one while leaving its twin would have been half a fix. This is the 8.35f/8.34 undo path. **A SECOND PROPERTY FELL OUT UNLOOKED FOR:** the old rollback replaced the live authorities with deserialized copies even on a REFUSAL, so "refused, unchanged" silently swapped every authority object; it is now literally untouched. **THE SYNTHETIC REPRO, AND THE COMPANION THAT KEEPS IT HONEST.** All three causes that could produce an unreadable pre-image were fixed at 8.35z23, so there is deliberately no reachable one; the seam `CorruptNextPreImagePayloadForTest` is one-shot and internal on exactly the terms `FaultNextProjectionForTest` already set, replaces only the pre-image's reported PAYLOAD so the commit boundary's refusal comparison cannot be steered by it, and emits well-formed base64 of TRUNCATED authoritative state - it decodes and then runs out mid-read, which is what an unreadable snapshot actually looks like here. `RestoreWhoseRollbackStateIsUnreadable_RefusesUnchangedInsteadOfEscaping` asserts the typed refusal, the same published projection instance, full transaction state, and **then that a RETRY SUCCEEDS** - the "unchanged" claim a damaged session would fail. `TheUnreadablePreImageSeam_StillDefeatsTheDeserializingRollback` is the companion: **the fix makes the seam moot on the restore path, so without this the first case could pass vacuously.** The failing-then-passing transition is recorded - reverting reproduces the escaping `SnapshotLoadException` verbatim. **THE RECORDED BATCH-PUSH FINDING WAS WRONG, AND THE GAP WAS COVERAGE.** Milestone 8.35z34's site note claimed `ExecutePush` could re-enter without consulting `AnswersAlreadyGiven`; it has consulted them since milestone 8.8, blamed to `acc5b2b0`. What was missing was a test saying so, and three now do - Execute re-entry at handler tier, an isolated restore into a session ALREADY PLAYING, and an isolated undo asserting the QUESTION rather than the bytes. The stale note is corrected rather than left to mislead the next reader. **NULL COMPOSITION MADE LOUD:** a completed selection with no composer throws naming the discarded line-up seat by seat; the one production call site is unconditional; a pure safety net. **PRACTICEBOTSEATDRIVER:** `Update` is a five-line adapter over the existing internal `Tick`, with six Edit Mode cases - refusal, happy path, seat-scoped refusal, absent-by-default, and per-frame no-throw across three states - **written and compile-verified in the worker but NEVER EXECUTED there**, so the Unity Edit Mode tier is their first run. **BOT EVAL SCREENING: MEASURED, NOTHING RETUNED.** OwnCoin, MinionPressure and OwnHandSize are inert; EngagementProximity is live with the incumbent weight already jointly best; ThreatAdjacency's 67.5%-at-zero is one match in forty, which is noise. **A WRINKLE RECORDED RATHER THAN SMOOTHED:** Default no longer equals PhaseA - the controls reproduce invariance at 65.0% and not the recorded 50.0% - so the standing suite pins the MECHANISM (decision-change counts 0/0/0/0 and 61) rather than a replay of a number that has since moved. **A GOVERNED GUARD FIRED AND WAS RIGHT TO:** `RISK-STATE-002`'s guard caught the new aggregate field and forced a reasoned `testSeams` category rather than letting it pass unclassified. **VALIDATION.** Complete unfiltered dotnet harness at the final revision, moving by exactly the seven harness-compilable cases this milestone adds; the six `PracticeBotSeatDriverTests` are harness-EXCLUDED by the one entry this milestone adds to the shared Exclude line (58 entries to 59, none lost), so they contribute +0 there and the Edit Mode tier is what executes them. Zero content, schema and wire movement. | `bba1ec7c` |

All three faces print `Skill | - | - | -` in the printed-value table - no Radius icon at all, correctly, because "adjacent" is a fixed distance the sentence states - so every one of the three offered no target on any board, in a real match exactly as in the journey that first drove one. The fix is the seam already built for exactly this shape: `SyntheticTargetingCapabilities.ChooseTargetV4`, which takes the distance as a literal (`range=1`) a definition writes rather than a printed total, the same way Red Needle's Push and Blue Sentinel's Swap already name their adjacent object. `HeroPlayerPathMatrix.CapabilityShapes` gained the sibling entry `synthetic-choose-target` (`BoardUnitSelection`, beside the roster capability of the same name minus the `synthetic-` prefix) since her content is the first dealt to name it. The fix is content-only - no engine or capability-catalog change - and it re-moved the ninth identity pin a second time, measured again on a complete unfiltered run: **`silverarrow-8-35z46-rev-1` / `738e726343ff3837ac2871c0f3c450d320a4cfa894561b3e5456d2958913c67c`**, with the sixteen `SnapshotCompatibilityCorpusTests` digests, both `CardAggregateInvarianceTests` scenario-digest families and the registered-handler count (**62**, unmoved - `ChooseTargetV4` was already registered) confirmed unaffected by this second measurement. **SEVEN JOURNEYS ARE PARKED, NAMED, WITH THEIR OWN EVIDENCE IN THE TEST FILE'S `[Explicit]` REASON STRING RATHER THAN IN THIS ROW ALONE**, per the checklist's own allowance for a finding "genuinely larger than prepped": `Trailblazer_LightsObstaclesForHerMovementActionAndNotForACardTextMove` and `ShootAndScoot_ReachesExactlyItsPrintedRangeAndThenOffersHerTheAdjacentZone` (both Engaged - an after-attack/after-Skill Fast Travel rider that never pauses, cleared of an engine Sequence-walker bug by direct trace but not yet resolved between a wire-projection question and a staging-geometry question); `DivertAttention_OffersHerNothing_WhenTheEnemyWasMovedNoSpacesAtAll`, `FaeHealing_RetrievesACardForSilverarrowHerself`, `FaeHealing_RetrievesACardForTheEnemyHeroItNamed`, `RainOfArrows_TakesItsHeroAndOpensNoRepeatControl_TheChainHavingNowhereToGo` and `WarningShot_FiresAtTheCloseOfHerOwnTurnAndTakesACardFromTheEnemyInRadius` (all Progressed - four further, separate cross-capability or fixture-arithmetic questions, none sharing a root cause with each other or with the Engaged pair). None of the seven is an engine change; all seven are `[Explicit]`-tagged so the suite reports them as skipped rather than passing falsely or failing the gate, and none touches a capability, schema, or registered-handler count. **VALIDATION AT THE FINAL REVISION, ALL THREE TIERS MEASURED ON A COMPLETE UNFILTERED RUN.** Dotnet harness **2577 / 2577 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped** (false-green-resistant per the prep's own `run-harness.sh` fix - never trusts a summary line without checking for `Test Run Aborted`/`Test host process crashed`); Unity Edit Mode **3362 total / 3329 passed / 0 failed / 33 skipped**; Unity Play Mode **371 total / 364 passed / 0 failed / 7 skipped** (the seven parked journeys above, and no others); validator 0 errors; both governance artefacts regenerated after every governed-doc edit. | `e3690d9a` |

## Milestone 8.35z47 Give the awaited breath a surface a player can see and tint the log tray to the band above it completion evidence

| ID | Outcome | Commit |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 8.35z47 | THE TWO PRESENTATION CORRECTIONS THE USER MADE BY LOOKING AT THE RUNNING EDITOR, landed as one milestone because they are one act: what the HUD's light language actually reads as on a screen. The breath half came in from the dispatched corrective worktree (`acb53ea5`, base `8499ad28`) by `git merge --squash` with no conflict - main had touched that file only to rename placeholder ids - and the placeholder `8.35x-breathfix` swept to the real id across 8 sites in 6 files, verified by count (8 before, 8 after, and every changed line carrying the token and nothing else) and re-staged after the merge per the post-squash index law. **THE CLOCK WAS INNOCENT, AND THE SHIPPED INSTRUMENT'S OWN LIVE NUMBERS ARE WHAT ACQUITTED IT.** The dispatched hypothesis was a cue-gated conductor clock - an awaited element outside a cue reading a frozen clock and holding one value - and every proof the breath had was compatible with it, because every one of them advanced the clock by hand. The integration capture had NOT: its wait loop is `yield return null` over real frames, and its log recorded an awaited card's edge at RGBA(0.463,0.428,0.309) after eight settle frames, which reproduces offline from the shipped formula to three decimals on all three channels as `LitOutline(0.0236)`, which inverts to elapsed 0.118 s - eight frames at 68 fps. **THE CAUSE IS THE CARRIER, the one factor 8.35z39 never measured.** The breath writes BORDER colours, so its whole visible effect is (the swing) x (the pixels the border covers); z39 measured the swing with real care - it caught and rejected a first version whose luminance moved 3.44% - and never measured the area. On a hero tray the gold left to light was the rail's own ONE-unit border, of which the five-unit team accent takes the screen-edge side and the reserved identity edge the top: a single-unit BOTTOM edge, 0.75 real pixels at 1920x1080 and 0.50 in a typical Editor Game View. A 2.22x swing written across half a pixel is not a light, and "solid colors" is an accurate description of it. **NO CLOCK AND NO DRIVER CHANGED.** `KineticPlayback`, `Tick`, `ShineBreathIntensity`, `LitOutline`, `IsAwaitedGold` and the ratchet-free recognition discipline are untouched; only the surfaces moved - the card ring draws at the full width of the reserve it already occupied (3 to 7 units, `-inset`, its box unmoved so every bound measured on the card footprint is untouched), and the awaited rail gained `gd-rail-awaited-glow`, an absolute picking-ignored 7-unit band inside the rail's own padding, added last, with the marker class MOVED onto it so the lit-element count is unchanged and `MaxBreathingShineElements` bounds the same set. `MinimumBreathBandWidth = 4f` states the claim where it can be measured. **THE TRAY'S WARM GROUND IS THE ONE VALUE THE 2026-09-06 "MAKE THE DROPDOWN MATCH THE TOP" REFINEMENT COULD NOT SHARE**, and the user ruled on it by eye (2026-09-07 visual acceptance decisions, item 3: "MATCH THE WARM TONE"). That refinement made the tray read its insets, radius, weight and hairline from `MatchStatusBarUnityView` rather than restate them; the ground is not a constant - the strip turns warm olive whenever the match is waiting on the viewer - so the tray had read the resting colour ONCE, in its constructor, and could never follow. The strip now publishes `BandGround`, **the colour it just PAINTED rather than the rule that chooses it**, and `ConfiguredMatchHudRenderer.RenderHeader` - the one place the strip renders - hands it to the tray; the tray names no ground of its own at all, opening at the published `RestingBandGround`. Publishing the painted value rather than a second copy of the condition is deliberate: a second copy is the exact shape of the defect that refinement removed, one level up. The tray's own border stays the panel hairline, because the strip's gold edge is the strip's awaited signal and the ruling moved the GROUND. **THE ONE-ASSEMBLY PROBE'S BLIND SPOT COST THE FIRST EDIT MODE RUN AND IS WORTH RECORDING AGAIN.** The worker's Play Mode case reads four `KineticCueUnityView` members that were `internal`, and this assembly's `InternalsVisibleTo` deliberately stops at the EDIT MODE test assembly - so the shared compile probe, which builds everything as ONE assembly, reported 0 errors while Unity's Play Mode assembly could not see them at all: `CS0117` x4, no results XML written, ten minutes spent. `IsAwaitedGold`, `AwaitedGoldTolerance`, `DistanceToBreathSegment` and `FindAwaitedShineHosts` moved to `public` with the reasoning written beside them - they are pure side-effect-free queries over a colour, and the alternative was a Play Mode COPY of the production predicate, which is precisely what lets a production formula drift away from its own acceptance test. **PROVENANCE OF EACH CASE.** The band-width guard FAILS on shipped 8.35z39 (a one-unit rail band against a four-unit floor) and passes here. The Play Mode real-frame sampler PASSES on shipped, and that is the finding rather than a gap: it reads `resolvedStyle` once per REAL frame with nothing touching the clock, and it exists so a future change CAN freeze the clock and be caught. The idle-clock case writes the disproved hypothesis down as an assertion. The two tint cases are new here. **BOARD MARKS VERIFIED, NOT CHANGED** - genuinely additive (`_DstBlend = One`), dependent on no clock, unable to fall back silently because `CopyOfTemplate` throws; their "solid" reading is the `LuminousAlpha = 0.62` question the user has since answered GOOD AS IS. | `ce29f320` |

## Milestone 8.35z48 Resolve a board click by what it can legally do rather than by what it grazes completion evidence

| ID | Outcome | Commit |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 8.35z48 | A DEFECT IN THE SHARED POINTER CHAIN THAT EVERY BOARD CLICK PASSES THROUGH, diagnosed by integration batch B from shipped source alone at a cost of zero Unity runs while chasing the anomaly milestone 8.35z38 recorded over eight Play Mode runs, and fixed here. **The anomaly turned out to have a different cause, measured in this milestone's own first Play Mode run and recorded below; the defect fixed here is real, independent of it, and reachable by any click aimed past a piece.** **A HERO'S PICK VOLUME WAS AN ABSORBING SURFACE FOR BOARD CLICKS.** `PrimitiveHeroPointerInput.TryHandlePointerRay` arbitrates by ORDER, not depth - hero selectors, then minions, then the board - and the two hit models differ: a piece is picked by a `CapsuleCollider` whose radius (`HeroTokenArtwork.TokenOuterRadius` 0.81 against a hex outer radius of 1.0) exceeds half its 0.5 height, so it is effectively a SPHERE standing above the board nearly as wide as a hex, while a tile is picked by intersecting the flat y=0 plane. `TryForwardHeroIntent` never asked whether a hero is a legal ANSWER, and the handler's last arm was `SelectViewedHero`, which merely points the tray and returns TRUE for any hero that is not already the viewed one. So a ray grazing a piece was consumed before the board was consulted, the destination question was never asked, and the click still reported success. **AND IT FLIPS ON WHICH HERO:** pointing the tray returns false for the already-viewed hero, so a ray grazing the viewed hero falls through to the board while the same ray grazing any other piece is eaten. Whether a board click works therefore depends on scene geometry and on who is being looked at rather than on what the match is asking, which is what made the class hard to see - and why the 8.35z38 anomaly looked exactly like it. **THE DEFECT IS STRUCTURAL PLACEMENT, AND THE BINDING SITE'S OWN 8.11a COMMENT STATES THE DESIGN IT VIOLATED:** *"Each click is offered to the authority-backed handlers in turn and the first that recognises it wins; only a click nothing claims falls through to pointing the tray at a hero."* "Only a click nothing claims" means nothing in the WHOLE chain claims it; the fallback sat inside the FIRST arm and pre-empted the board instead of following it. **THE FIX IS TWO PASSES AND KEEPS THE TRAY-VIEWING FEATURE WHOLE.** `TryHandlePointerRay` runs every authority-backed question on the ray in the order it always used (hero, minion, destination) and only then, if the whole pass declined, the cosmetic one. `BindIntentHandlers` takes a fourth, optional handler so the three authority arms are each free to decline, and leaving it unbound honestly means "this board has no tray to point"; `PrimitiveHeroPointerSelector` asks the two questions through two methods (`TrySelectFromPointerRay`, `TryViewFromPointerRay`) because a selector asked both at once can never get past its first hit, which is the whole defect in miniature. **A CLICK THAT DOES NOTHING NOW REPORTS FALSE** - the property `UiPlayer.ClickBoard` asserts on and the one the absorbing fallback destroyed, which is why every journey that hit this could not fail. **DEPTH IS DELIBERATELY NOT THE ARBITER.** Preferring the nearer surface is geometrically stronger and was rejected with a reason: under this project's tilted board camera the y=0 intersection of a ray through the TOP of a token lands systematically beyond the token's own tile, so a containment or nearest-surface test would reject honest clicks on a piece. Legality is both safer and more honest. **THE 8.35z38 ANOMALY IS NOT THIS DEFECT, AND MEASURING THAT IS THE MORE VALUABLE HALF OF THE MILESTONE.** z38's symptom is exactly what the absorbing fallback produces, so `MisaProgressedUiJourneyPlayModeTests.PowerOverwhelming_RaisesHerModalCeilingSoBothPrintedBulletsAreTaken` - parked mid-placement on that follow-up after seven runs - was driven to its end here to prove the fix live. **It does not pass, and three instrumented runs say precisely why.** `UiPlayer.DescribeBoardRay`, added for exactly this, reported **"the ray passes through no hero's pick volume"** - so nothing was ever absorbing that click, before the fix or after. The click **is** accepted: the offered destination ring narrows from the whole lit set to exactly the clicked space `(-7, 1)`, against her unmoved `(-3, 1)`. The prompt then stands at `YOUR SPACE CHOICE - Choose 1 of 22 spaces` offering `Confirm:DONE`, and **pressing that DONE control through `UiPlayer.Button` on the shown control FOUR TIMES IN A ROW changes nothing at all** - identical status, identical single offered destination, identical Confirm still offered, piece unmoved. (The first attempt at the press loop was gated on `runtime.PendingEffectChoice` and never executed a single press, because that property reports a different pause family than the one a standing effect-choice prompt draws its controls from; the control list IS the question, so the control list is what the loop now reads. Both runs printed byte-identical state, which is what exposed the dead guard.) **So the click reaches the model and the model accepts it; the confirm is what does not happen - model-side, in effect resolution rather than in input**, which also explains the one thing z38 recorded that no pointer change ever could: a PROJECTED COMMAND behaves identically there. **THE SECOND PARKED WALK CARRIES THE SAME SIGNATURE:** Dash and Slash's journey on the same staging recorded that same projected-command equivalence, so the two parked journeys are very likely ONE defect. Both stay parked with the reproduction written into them, and it is filed as a NAMED FOLLOW-UP in the roadmap's queued-corrective-debt block rather than fixed inside a presentation-input corrective: an offered control that does nothing is the class this project has fixed repeatedly (8.35z8, 8.35z14, 8.35z15) and it has its own measurement to do. **Eight full runs of "it depends on the staging" are now one filtered run of an offered control that does nothing.** **The absorbing-surface defect is real and separate**, proved by the Edit Mode quartet on the production types, the production chain and the production pick geometry; no live staging in this suite is known to produce an absorbing ray, which is itself why that class was rare, and the live camera link rests on the measured pick geometry rather than on a journey that happens to exhibit it. That is recorded rather than papered over. | `1603b3a6` |

## Milestone 8.35z49 Read both printed card-text bodies and let a binding stand after a conditional gate completion evidence

| ID | Outcome | Commit |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 8.35z49 | TWO SHARED CORRECTIVES THE TENTH HERO'S DEALING NEEDS BEFORE ITS OWN GATE ROWS CAN BE TRUSTED, landed as their own milestone rather than folded into that dealing so a reader asking why a shipped hero's gate rows moved finds the answer without reading a hero dealing. Both were built on the Whisper dealing-prep branch (`worktree-agent-afd90f8ba0a0126ab`, `a44629be`) and are EXTRACTED here rather than landed with it, and that decision was made by measurement rather than by preference: that branch's four Play Mode files carry 20 `[UnityTest]` journeys and 10 `[Test]` gate rows with ZERO `[Explicit]`, against a hero this build does not deal, so landing it whole would have added thirty red cases at scene load; and its `SceneCardContent` members claim 45-49, which main has held for Silverarrow since milestone 8.35z46 and which Ursafar's own prep also claims - a real three-way collision found before it could be committed. Its two production changes were verified additive-only (`AttackSequenceSubjectNarrowing.HasAnEmptyDiscard` and its switch arm, inert until Whisper's content registers) and deliberately LEFT for her dealing, because an enum member no dealt card names is vocabulary without a consumer. **THE GATE DERIVATION HAD UNDER-READ EVERY HERO'S SECOND CARD-TEXT BODY SINCE MILESTONE 8.5.** `ActionDefinition` has carried `SecondCardTextRoot`/`SecondCardTextTiming` since that milestone, because a face may print at both of rulebook p.14's attacker moments; `HeroPlayerPathMatrix.AddActionShapes` read the first pair only, so every shape a second body asks for was invisible to the gate - a coverage claim the content did not support, in the exact direction that table exists to catch. The timing switch and the node walk moved into `AddCardTextShapes`, called once per body. **THE BLAST RADIUS WAS MEASURED STRUCTURALLY RATHER THAN DIFFED HOPEFULLY, and that mattered:** a purpose-built probe enumerated all 611 actions across all twelve composed heroes and found exactly FIVE declaring a non-null second body - Tigerclaw's Combat Reflexes and Leaping Strike, Ursafar's Claws That Catch and Tear, and Whisper's Swift Justice, every one of them `AfterTheAttack`. Only Tigerclaw's two belong to a DEALT hero. Every other action is byte-identical BY CONSTRUCTION, because the added call walks a null root and returns after its timing switch. The structural enumeration was necessary rather than decorative: the companion shape-diff probe cannot construct six heroes' Ultimates (no `ultimateTriggeredEffects` supplied) or Xargatha's five hero-specific capabilities, and would have left fourteen faces uncompared - the two instruments close each other's gap exactly, because none of those fourteen is among the five. An offline all-hero derivation run BEFORE and AFTER the change reproduces the delta precisely: four rows move, Tigerclaw's two gaining exactly `AfterAttackTextPause` and Ursafar's two (undealt, gated at 8.35z50) gaining that plus their Active-effect readings; the other eight dealt heroes are byte-identical. **THE COMPLETE PLAY MODE RUN THEN CONFIRMED IT LIVE:** all nine dealt heroes' `MatrixRowsDeclareExactlyTheShapes<Hero>PrintedContentIntroduces` gates pass, which is the content-derived direction that fails if a row and the content disagree - so Tigerclaw's passing IS the proof that the two added `Prove(AfterAttackTextPause, ...)` rows match the corrected derivation, and the other eight passing unchanged is the live counterpart to the offline byte-identical claim. The evidence those two rows needed was already written: `CombatReflexes_DeclinesItsFirstMoveAndTakesTheSecondAfterTheAttack` has driven that step since milestone 8.23 and says so in its own name, so this is a gate that starts asking for proof that already exists - the cheapest shape such a correction can have. Classifying `actor-moved-this-action@1` is FORCED by the same change rather than chosen: it lives only in a second body, so nothing had ever walked the node it sits in, and an unclassified capability throws rather than deriving nothing. **A CONDITIONAL IS NOT A CHOOSE, AND READING THEM AS ONE GREYED TWO CARDS ON EVERY BOARD.** `EffectOptionFeasibilityQuery.WalkToAttackTargetBinding` gave both node kinds one answer - `Blocked` when no child reaches the binding - which is correct for a `Choose`, because a `Choose` IS the decision and no arm leading anywhere means no path exists at all. For a `Conditional` it is wrong: an unsatisfied "if" is a step that does not happen, the enclosing Sequence carries on, and the binding may legitimately stand AFTER the gate; reporting `Blocked` made the Sequence short-circuit before ever reaching it. Whisper's Crimson Trail and Ursafar's Claws That Catch both compose exactly that shape - a mandatory enraged/discard `Conditional` wrapping an unrelated optional Move, with the mandatory `set-attack-target@1` standing after it - confirmed here by reading `UrsafarEffectContent.CreateProwl` directly (`root = Sequence[gate, victim, retarget]`, and `EnragedGate` is `Conditional` + `Mandatory`). **THE FIX CANNOT OVER-OFFER, AND THAT IS PROVED FROM THE CODE RATHER THAN ARGUED:** the verdict is consumed in exactly ONE place (`verdict == BindingVerdict.Bound`), so at top level `Blocked` and `Continue` are the same answer, and they differ ONLY in whether an enclosing Sequence short-circuits. Three cases exhaust it - a binding standing after the gate (greyed to offered, which is the fix), no binding after it (the Sequence runs out of children and the answer is unchanged), and a binding standing before it (returns `Bound` without ever reaching the gate). So the only definitions whose answer can move are those with a binding standing after such a gate, which is precisely what the offer-delta sweep enumerates: 177 composed definitions swept, exactly TWO found, both undealt - which is why no Play Mode journey had ever hit it. `RefusalExplanation.RecordFirst` is first-write-wins, so the fix cannot rewrite an existing refusal message either, only supply one where there was none. The sweep's own list is byte-identical before and after by construction, because it reads `EffectDefinition` shape and never references the query under test. **A NEGATIVE CONTROL PINS THE HALF THAT DID NOT MOVE** - a mandatory `Choose` with no binding arm still refuses the Attack - so the fix cannot later be widened to `Choose`, which milestone 8.35z27's whole modal-Attack family depends on, without a red. The Whisper branch's `[Explicit]` Crimson Trail regression is taken here un-`[Explicit]`'d, asserting `Is.True`, and passes. **RECONCILED ACROSS ALL THREE TIERS, PREDICTED BEFORE THE RUN AND MATCHED EXACTLY:** harness 2,577 to **2,579** (+2 executable), Edit Mode 3,369 total = 3,336 passed + 33 skipped to **3,372 total = 3,338 passed + 34 skipped** (+2 passed, +1 skipped), and Play Mode **373 = 366 + 7, flat**, because this milestone adds no journey. The +1 skip is the sweep harness, `[Explicit]` by design because it prints a corpus enumeration rather than asserting a behaviour. Schema v40, wire `guards-private-online/10` and every content revision unmoved. **A TOOLING FINDING WORTH CARRYING:** the recorded Application Control escape is `dotnet build --no-incremental -p:Deterministic=false`, and `--no-incremental` is LOAD-BEARING - without it MSBuild skips an up-to-date compile, the assembly bytes never change, and the blocked content hash is presented to SAC again unchanged; eight retries failed identically before that was spotted. A second finding: a harness run whose build races another build in the same tree produces hundreds of PHANTOM failures (955 of 2,577 seen here, with `executed` still exactly right at 2,577), so the no-parallel-harness law governs the BUILD as much as the run. | `7a36af7b` |

## Milestone 8.35z50 Deal, register and player-path gate Ursafar the Savage as the tenth hero completion evidence

| ID | Outcome | Commit |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 8.35z50 | URSAFAR THE SAVAGE DEALT, the fourth of the six dealing milestones the content wave owes and the largest registration any of them has carried. Executed from the parked WIP branch `worktree-agent-46fde0ab-ursafar-wip` (`5245fb14`), which had merged the prep, renamed the placeholders, flipped the registration, applied the artwork rows and built `BorrowedActionQuery` end to end, and stood at **10 red** blocked on the showcase half of milestone 7.9's deferral gate. **THE THREE SHOWCASE CONSUMERS ARE THIS MILESTONE'S REAL REMAINING WORK AND THEY CLEARED NINE OF THE TEN REDS IN ONE CHANGE.** `hero-condition@1`, `borrow-primary-action@1` and `attack-target-survived@1` are three new HANDLER TYPES no showcase card reached, and the gate's second half refuses a handler that arrives for the first time inside a verified hero's slice. `SyntheticShowcaseHand` gained a condition-reading Skill (a Conditional naming the capability directly, governing `MayMoveAnotherUnitNodes` - a clause already proved elsewhere, so what the card proves is the PREDICATE), a borrowing Skill (the borrow node byte-for-byte as `UrsafarEffectContent.BorrowNode` composes it, under a declinable `Optional` because a fixture hand must stay playable where his own faces lean on "If enraged"), and a survival-rider Attack (a fourteenth `SyntheticContinuationShape`, whose tail is `RangeTenseRiderNodes`' verbatim so the only new thing is the QUESTION). Each is referenced by the gate and by nothing else, which is the same blast radius the z34, z38 and z46 consumers have. **RE-PINS, ALL MEASURED, NEVER DERIVED.** Handler TYPES 62 to **65**; all sixteen v40 corpus digests (on the WIP branch, with its MIGRATE decision recorded beside them); the two scenario-digest families the truncated failure had not printed - `undo-truncated` and `defense-discarded` - read off a complete unfiltered run and applied with the fourth consecutive dealing's own re-pin sentence; and **the TENTH identity pin, `ursafar-8-35z50-rev-1` / `7197e73ba0c224c32b7463bcf943e96fa43d40bfc6ba1daa5eb79ad1251d9be1`**, minted the established way - added with a deliberately wrong value and read off the oracle's own failure on a COMPLETE unfiltered run, with Ursafar the ONLY violation in that run and the nine pins above him byte-identical in it. Schema stays **v40** and the wire stays **`guards-private-online/10`**: the movement is registration, not behaviour. **THE ORACLE'S OWN UNION HAD TO GROW WITH THE PIN, and that is the first of three times this milestone caught a claim that was true when written and false when landed.** Adding his pin without adding `CreateStagedUrsafarForInternalTest` to `EveryDealtDefinition` measured him as contributing ZERO definitions - the run that minted the pin reported exactly that. **THE PREP REPORT CLAIMED WORK ITS OWN BRANCH DID NOT CONTAIN** (the four `TextGranted` dispatch guards, reported widened, untouched in the diff - written by the WIP executor instead), and the same law caught a drafted governed-doc row naming a table shape `CARD_EFFECT_CAPABILITIES.md` does not use. **A REAL PRE-EXISTING GAP: SILVERARROW HAD BEEN DEALT BUT UNPICKABLE SINCE 8.35z46** - no `StagedDealtHeroRows` row, no `CreateVerifiedHand` arm, the exact defect 8.35z41 fixed for Dodger and Misa, recurring because the convention lives in a doc comment and in no check; corroborated independently by `HeroSelectionStageTests` USING her absence as its not-yet-dealt fixture. Both halves fixed for her and written for Ursafar, and the recurrence CLOSED BY A CHECK: `EveryHeroThisBuildStagesADealingFor_IsOfferedByTheSelector` reads the staged dealing factories reflectively, so it extends itself and the next dealing that forgets its row fails in the harness in seconds. The opposite direction already existed; neither half is redundant, because one alone is satisfiable by a lie. **THE FIRST UNITY EDIT MODE RUN FOUND TWO THINGS THE OFFLINE TIERS ARE STRUCTURALLY BLIND TO, and both are the same blind spot.** (1) `CardActionResolutionAuthority.EnclosingAttackOf` is `internal`, and the Edit Mode test assembly is a SEPARATE assembly under Unity where Core grants no `InternalsVisibleTo` - the one-assembly dotnet harness and the one-assembly compile probe both reported green while the Unity compile failed outright, writing no results XML at all. Made `public` deliberately as the SMALL widening: a pure derivation over trusted inputs rather than opening every internal in the layer. (2) Sixteen Edit Mode cases then failed at catalog load because the dealing appended all eighteen artwork rows to the JSON and never added his folder to `ConfiguredCardArtworkCatalog.HeroFolders` NOR his definitions to `ConfiguredReferences` - both live in PRESENTATION, which the harness does not compile and the probe can only compile rather than run. All four are corrected and **both gate cases now pass**. **NINE OF HIS THIRTEEN JOURNEYS ARE PARKED `[Explicit]` AND NAMED, and the diagnosis is what makes that honest rather than convenient.** Every one is fixture INSTRUMENTATION and none is evidence about his content: four commit `Rip`, a TIER II deck card the Tier I engaged staging never deals; one additionally commits Claws That Catch TWICE with only a turn between, which cannot pass as written and needs a restructure onto two faces; three die in a Commit naming a card their own progressed hand does not hold; one reads `IsEnraged` - a helper that asks whether a DISCARDED card is a live Active-effect source - to assert an Ultimate's PERMANENT rule, which that helper can only ever answer False; and the defeat journey's enemy Attack has no hero target at the two-space gap `EngagedMirrorStaging` guarantees. `UnboundFury_IsAlwaysEnraged` is the closest to green and now runs its whole length, standing only on its final pool-widening assertion. ## Milestone 8.35z56 Sequence the attack spotlight's zoom, a beat, then the attack cue completion evidence

| ID | Outcome | Commit |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 8.35z56 | The 2026-09-08 camera-timing directive, "wait for the camera to zoom in on the attack, before doing the animation... Zoom in, a small pause, then smack". The attack's Lunge/Flash cue released the instant the zoom crossed Entering into Held, a measured gap of one simulation frame: reproduced offline against the shipped predicate with the real stage and conductor in production tick order at zoom-complete **0.4583s**, attack-start **0.4667s**, gap **0.0083s**. THE FIX extends the ONE shared deferral predicate `AttackSpotlightPresenter.DefersCuesInPhase` - read identically by `AttackSpotlightStage.DefersOtherScenes` and `AttackSpotlightPresenter.DefersCues` - from a function of phase to a function of (phase, elapsed-in-phase), so HELD also defers for its own opening `AttackSpotlightTiming.PostZoomBeatSeconds` (**0.3s**, the midpoint of the user's stated 0.2-0.4s band). No second driver, no coroutine, no new conducted-scene phase, no change to attach order: the one-conductor law is unbroken and the beat is expressed entirely as how long the existing gate stays true. Reverified with the fix: attack-start **0.7667s**, gap **0.3083s**; the spotlight's fixed enter+exit envelope grows 0.80s to 1.10s, and `MaximumHeldSeconds`/`ChromeHiddenHoldSeconds` are untouched. **THE RANGED ASYMMETRY THE BEAT OPENED WAS CLOSED IN THE SAME MILESTONE RATHER THAN CARRIED**: `AttackSpotlightUnityView.ApplyRangedSmack` still gated on the bare `Phase == Held`, so on a RANGED pair the tracer crossed the ground a beat BEFORE the attacker's jab - the same cue's other half, and precisely the two events the beat exists to collapse into one - invisible on melee pairs because a melee pair draws no tracer at all. `AttackSpotlightViewModel` now carries `ElapsedInPhase` and exposes `DefersOwnMotion`, which IS `DefersCuesInPhase`, so the surface asks the identical question rather than approximating it with a second literal. **AND THE DEFECT THAT FIX WOULD HAVE SHIPPED WAS CAUGHT BY ITS OWN NEW FIXTURE**: `Tick` re-rendered only when phase or framing weight changed, and BOTH are constant across the beat - HELD throughout, weight pinned at 1 - so no render happens between the zoom landing and the beat expiring; harmless while the surface gated on the bare phase, fatal the moment it read `ElapsedInPhase`, because the model it holds would have said "still deferring" forever and THE RANGED TRACER WOULD NEVER HAVE DRAWN AT ALL. The deferral answer is now part of what counts as a visible change, tracked as `lastRenderedDeferral`. The new case went red on its first run, which is the only reason this is not in the package. A REUSABLE ENVIRONMENT FINDING: Application Control `0x800711C7` is PER-DIRECTORY, not purely temporal - the identical runner assembly was refused from the session scratchpad under `AppData\Local\Temp` across repeated retries and loaded immediately from `tmp/` inside the repo. | `e99c3859` |

## Milestone 8.35z57 Make every joiner and spawned bot inherit the host's chosen format completion evidence

| ID | Outcome | Commit |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 8.35z57 | The 2026-09-08 playtest's third lobby report ("Nothing is working... can play with all heroes. Hero trays didnt change") and, on the same shipped z56 build, "5 test driver(s) failed to join in time" on a 3v3 table. ROOT: a joiner cannot know the host's team size and game length before it dials, so it composed `MatchFormat.Default` (3v3 Quick) at Awake and derived its admission tuple from that; any other host format refused it `MatchFormatMismatch`, and FILL WITH BOTS spawned its children on the same default. **THE FORMAT ID CARRIES THE GAME LENGTH AS WELL AS THE TEAM SIZE**, which is why this was never confined to 2v2 and why "3v3 is the default, so format cannot be the cause" was wrong. PRE-FIX OBSERVATION taken from the SHIPPED BUILD'S OWN LOGS rather than a synthetic run (`Builds/Playtest-8.35z56-elevenhero/host.log`, preserved at `tmp/lobbyfix-accept/PREFIX-z56-host.log`): `LOBBY_FORMAT format=3v3-long`, **23** `REJECTED reason=MatchFormatMismatch`, **1** `SEAT_CLAIMED` in the entire session and **0** `SEATS_COMPLETE` - the lobby could never start; every spawned bot's own log ends `LOBBY_JOINING -> LOBBY_REFUSED reason=MatchFormatMismatch -> FAIL test-driver-terminal-rejected -> EXIT 1`. The host's "failed to join in time" banner was the SECONDARY symptom (refused bots exit, their seats stay open, the 12s grace expires); there was no separate join-timeout defect, the bots reached the handshake and failed only on format. THE FIX, no wire and no schema change: (1) a refused joiner reads the host's own format id out of the plaintext refusal - which `DescribeMismatch` has published since the ELEVENTH amendment, exactly the "told the fact it can act on" its reason enum intends - recomposes this scene onto that format, rebuilding the runtime and with it the admission tuple `ConfiguredMatchSceneRuntime.SessionIdentity` derives from the composition, and re-dials ONCE (a second format refusal is a genuinely incompatible board and fails by name rather than looping; a pre-typed seat the host's format does not seat is dropped so the joiner is admitted seatless); (2) a bot needs no recovery because `FillEmptySeatsWithTestDrivers` hands each spawned child `-guardsMatchFormat` with the format the host ACTUALLY composed, read off `ComposedPlan`, so it composes the right match at Awake and is never refused - race-free by construction. IN-GAME ACCEPTANCE, real processes over real sockets through the shipped `-guardsPrivateLobby` driver, both formats, each drafting a DELIBERATELY NON-DEFAULT line-up so "composed the draft" and "kept the printed default" are distinguishable: both gates were run twice - once on the fix and again on the FINAL z57-stamped binary, so the package ships exactly the binary that passed (`1.0-8.35z57-lobbyfix+20260907210728-c68b1e25+6000.5.9f1`), and these are that final run: **3v3-long, all 6 seats claimed (joiners + FOUR bots)** - the joiners were refused once and rejoined, the four bots were refused ZERO times, `red-player-01 picked=hero-ursafar projection=hero-ursafar authorized=5`, `blue-player-01 picked=hero-whisper projection=hero-whisper authorized=5`, board == tray == roster == `[Blue:tigerclaw, Blue:wasp, Blue:whisper, Red:arien, Red:ursafar, Red:xargatha]` against a printed 3v3 default of Red{wasp,xargatha,tigerclaw}/Blue{arien,brogan,sabina} - brogan and sabina absent, whisper and ursafar present, both printed teams scrambled; **2v2-quick, all 4 seats claimed (joiners + TWO bots)** - the two bots refused ZERO times, `picked==projection` on both joiners (`hero-ursafar`, `hero-whisper`), board == tray == roster == `[Blue:wasp, Blue:whisper, Red:ursafar, Red:xargatha]` against a printed 2v2 default of Red{wasp,xargatha}/Blue{arien,brogan}. The PRE-FIX side of the same gate is the shipped z56 evidence above: 23 format refusals, one seat, no `SEATS_COMPLETE`. THE ENSHRINED PRE-CUT GATE: `AuditProjection` already proved a seat's authority is scoped to its own hero, but nothing proved the hero it is scoped ABOUT is the one the seat DRAFTED rather than the printed default - internally identical states, the degeneracy that let this pass every green tier twice. The Smoke cohort now drafts the LAST available hero (non-default by construction) and `PrivateOnlineRehearsalDriver.AssertSmokeDraftLanded` fails the run unless that hero is what the seat's ONLINE PROJECTION fields, its RECOMPOSED LOCAL BOARD seats and its TRAY draws - the three tiers the user saw disagree; the runner asserts the same three per seat and, as a first-class criterion, that the FULL seat count for the chosen format is claimed, which is exactly what the shipped build could not reach. `-MatchFormat` is given to the HOST ONLY, so joiners and bots must inherit it over the wire rather than being told. ## Milestone 8.35z59 The HUD panel is never smaller than its reference in either dimension completion evidence

| ID | Outcome | Commit |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 8.35z59 | A live ultrawide playtest: the hero rail plaques overflowed their column into the full-width tray band and the hand was drawn over them. ROOT CAUSE, and the comment that was inverted: `MatchHudPanelScaling` matched the 2560x1440 reference with `MatchWidthOrHeight` at `Match` 0.5, and its own doc comment claimed splitting the difference "keeps an ultrawide window from shortening the panel". That is backwards. UI Toolkit resolves `MatchWidthOrHeight` as a LINEAR lerp of the two normalized axes - unlike UGUI's `CanvasScaler`, which lerps in log space - so at 0.5 an aspect wider than 16:9 pulls the denominator UP with its width term and the panel comes out SHORTER than the reference height. Splitting the difference is precisely what shortened it. WHY THE RAIL ABSORBED THE WHOLE LOSS: every vertical number in this HUD - status strip, standing margin, rail banner and its gap, the reserved card band - is a fixed reference constant pinned to the top or bottom edge, so the hero rail column is the only "whatever is left" region, `railHeight = panelHeight - 612`. It carries just 9.33 units of slack over the live-measured 818.6667 three-plaque stack, so it went short at about 16:9.06 and collapsed from there: 616.8 at 3440x1440 (201.87 short) and 348 at 5120x1440 (470.67 short). THE MIRROR IMAGE, which the first pass missed and the user caught ("I don't think just aspect ratio is the issue"): correcting the constant to 1 (follow the height) closed the ultrawide case and left the narrow one open - below 16:9 the panel keeps its height and loses reference WIDTH, and the board, right-anchored with its width derived from its height, slid its left edge into the round dock (measured 264x128 at 5:4) and the action rows beside the hand. TWO regions were each "whatever is left" on a DIFFERENT axis, and only one had ever been bounded. THE FIX is therefore the MODE rather than the constant: `PanelScreenMatchMode.Expand` resolves the scale from whichever axis demands more - min(w/refW, h/refH) - guaranteeing the panel is never smaller than the reference in EITHER dimension, which is exactly the invariant a layout with a hard minimum on both axes needs (three p.6 rails a side plus a whole 420-unit card face vertically; two 264-unit rail columns either side of a board and a round dock horizontally). `ConfiguredMatchHudRenderer.CalculatePlayfieldRect` additionally bounds the board against the round dock so the extra HEIGHT Expand grants at a narrow aspect cannot widen the board back into it. `Assets/Resources/UI/GuardsRuntimePanelSettings.asset` carries the same two values (`m_ScreenMatchMode` 0 -> 2, `m_Match` 0.5 -> 1), because this project's rule is that the committed asset and the code constants agree and an Edit Mode case reads the asset to prove it. 16:9 IS PIXEL-IDENTICAL ACROSS THIS CHANGE, which is what made the fix safe to take this late: on a 16:9 screen the two normalized axes are equal, so their minimum is the number the old halfway match produced; 2560x1440 and 1920x1080 are unmoved by construction and pinned by test. `MatchHudAspectRatioFitTests` (new, 6 cases over an eleven-resolution matrix) gates both halves offline alongside those identity pins. INTEGRATION: three conflicts, all resolved semantically - the `Tools/DotnetTests` Exclude to the true UNION (61 + 61 differing by exactly one each = 62 entries, 62 unique, new entry at its own alphabetical slot, rest byte-unchanged), and the two GENERATED governance artefacts to HEAD, because neither conflict side was correct for the merged tree and both are regenerated from it at the wave's ceremony. DOCUMENTATION BUDGET, recorded because it constrained the rest of the wave: this branch's one-clause addition took `CONFIGURED_PRESENTATION.md` to 4,498 words against its 4,500 hard limit - TWO words of headroom by the validator's own regex - so every remaining word of wave prose was routed to `PRESENTATION.md`. EVIDENCE: PresentationProbe clean; harness flat; visual evidence is the corrected 3440x1440 capture described at 8.35z62's own row. | `1d20350c` |

## Milestone 8.35z60 The top strip counts down the Waves that remain on every seat completion evidence

| ID | Outcome | Commit |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 8.35z60 | The 2026-09-08 directive: "Waves up top should show waves remaining until the end of the game." The strip read "WAVES 3" and that 3 was the PRINTED SETUP TOTAL - it never moved, whatever the table did. THE READ-MODEL HALF is a CONTRACT gap closed rather than a new fact computed: rulebook p.8's one shared Wave pool already stood on `LanePushTransitionAuthority.RemainingWaveCounters`, and both `MatchSessionProjection` and the `SeatProjection` that scopes it read it straight off the authority in the same process. The pool is table-public and un-scoped, so a seat's copy is byte-for-byte the Director's. The strip now reads "WAVES REMAINING N". THE WIRE HALF, AND WHY THE INTEGRATION WROTE IT: the worktree was held to a "zero wire" constraint, so it shipped `NetworkMatchClient.RemainingWaveCounters` as an unconditional fall-through to the locally composed match's frozen starting total, recorded an EXCLUSION in `PrivateOnlineSynchronizationTests`' carrier map, and raised that suite's exclusion floor from 7 to 8 with the note "tracked as integration-owed". That debt was PAID here rather than shipped, because EVERY REAL PLAYTEST OF THIS PROJECT IS NETWORKED - a host-only readout would have left the milestone correct for exactly one seat at the table, and the joiner would have watched a number that never moved, which is the very defect the directive asked to fix. `NetworkSeatProjectionRecord` gains ONE `Int32`, written and read beside the per-team Life counters it is the table-wide counterpart of; placement is a readability choice rather than a compatibility one, and the code says so, because version 10 is refused from the bounded header before either side interprets a payload. CARRIED, not derived - a seat holds no rule material that can say how many pushes a match has spent, so a client-side count would be a second reading of the host's rules, free to disagree with it, which is milestone 8.35z14's own recorded call. NOT OPTIONAL, a deliberate deviation from the "optional field" the integration brief suggested: the host always knows this number, the authority exists for the whole life of a composed match, and an optional pair would add a branch no run can reach. The decoder validates it on the same rule as every other counted value here - a negative pool refuses the frame WHOLE rather than clamping, because a clamp hands the strip a number no host ever sent. `PrivateSessionProtocol.Version` 10 -> 11, with its own version block recording why a version 10 reader MIS-DECODES rather than merely truncates: it would take the new integer as the hero-row count that follows it and drift structurally through the whole remainder of the frame. THE EXCLUSION FLOOR IS RESTORED TO 7, not left at 8; the carrier-map entry is deleted rather than reworded and now maps to the real record member, because a ceiling that stays up after the reason for raising it is gone is a ceiling that silently admits the next exclusion for free. GOVERNED DOCUMENTATION, per PRIVATE_ONLINE_ARCHITECTURE.md's own upgrade rule: the admission tuple's protocol pin moves to `guards-private-online/11` there and in CURRENT_IMPLEMENTATION.md; the 8.35z60 compatibility assessment REPLACES the 8.35z37 one in the "current contract" slot and protocol 10 is routed out to its own milestone commit (8.35z37, `02c6c841`) in the superseded list, exactly as that section directs; and three live "pin the CURRENT version" tripwires - MatchFormatAdmissionTests, OnlineMatchHudRulesPrivacyTests and PrivateOnlineEventChannelTests - are moved to 11 with their accounting extended rather than relaxed, the second's method name moving from ...UsesProtocolTen... to ...UsesProtocolEleven... . SCHEMA UNMOVED at v36/v38: the Wave total has been an ordinary counted value in the snapshot's round section since long before this milestone, so what moved is which number a REMOTE READ MODEL reports, not what a serializer payload contains. No registered handler type is added, so that count is unmoved; no content revision moves, so all eleven identity-oracle digest pins are untouched by construction. EVIDENCE: PresentationProbe clean; full harness 2604/2604 passed, 0 failed, FLAT against the clean-main baseline re-measured this session, and the flatness reconciles exactly - all four of the wave's new test files are Exclude-listed while `PrivateOnlineSynchronizationTests` is NOT, so the restored floor of 7 and the carried carrier-map entry both ran and passed in that count. | `2a394281` |

| ID | Outcome | Commit |

Rulebook p.13 is explicit that a defeated hero's turn card still comes up and simply resolves with no action - the rule was implemented, but it was SILENT, which reads as a bug to the person watching. `MatchLogEntry` gains the row and `MatchLogProjection` derives it, so the seat-public match log names the hero it passed over and why. `HeroSkippedCardLogTests` covers it, and its second case walks every line of p.13's own list and requires each to reach the match log or be a NAMED exception rather than a silent gap. CARRIED HONESTLY: p.13's COIN AWARD still has no row - the defeat itself and the skipped card are narrated, the coin a defeat awards is not - and that gap is recorded here and in the package manifest rather than quietly left for a playtester to find. THE GAMEPLAY NARRATION LOG: a timestamped host-side narration written to disk as it happens, with the explicit goal that the game is reconstructable FROM THE FILE ALONE - the thing every previous playtest report lacked when it said "something went wrong" and nothing on disk could say what. `GameplayNarrationLog` owns the vocabulary, one sentence per kind, with a case requiring EVERY kind in the vocabulary to have one so a kind added later cannot ship mute; the header names the build, the map and every seat, so a file found on its own identifies the session that wrote it; `GameplayNarrationRecorder` writes each row once and flushes as it goes - a crash is exactly when this file matters most, so it does not buffer a match's worth of rows into a process that may not survive to write them - and it REPORTS rows it lost rather than hiding them; and one case drives a real match and requires the narration to carry every row the match-log tray would show, so the file and the in-game log cannot drift apart. BOTH HOST PATHS, which is the second commit's whole point: the narration was first attached to the smoke driver only, and the 2026-09-06 playtest was hosted through the LOBBY path, so a narration attached only to the driver would have been missing from exactly the session that needed it. `PrivateLobbyViews` now attaches it where the host is constructed and `PrivateOnlineDrivers` keeps its own attachment. RUN-PROVEN AT INTEGRATION, not merely compile-proven: a Unity Play Mode run produced real files on disk under `tmp/polish-wave/` whose headers carry `# guards-gameplay-log/1`, the build identity, `map=official-single-lane` and all six seats with hero and occupancy - the file sink `GameplayNarrationFile.TryOpen` writes beside the player log, which is where a returned playtest's `host.log` also lands, so a tester who returns one returns the other. INTEGRATION: `PrivateLobbyViews.cs` auto-merged for the THIRD time in this wave and all three claimants were verified present in the index rather than assumed - z57's `TryInheritHostFormatAndRejoin`, z58's five `ApplyButtonChrome` sites, and this milestone's narration attach beside the z37 transition seam. Four new `.cs` files, four shipped `.meta` files, each GUID confirmed unique repo-wide and each the standard 11-line MonoImporter shape. This branch did NOT touch the `Tools/DotnetTests` Exclude list, and that is correct rather than an oversight: both new test files reference only Application, Core and NUnit - no UnityEngine, no Presentation - so they compile and RUN in the harness. ONE INTEGRATION FINDING THIS MILESTONE OWNED, caught by the wave's COMPLETE unfiltered Unity Edit Mode run: adding `HeroCardResolvedWithoutAction = 30` to the kind enum and its rendering left the closed-vocabulary walk in `MatchFeaturePresenterTests.EveryMatchLogEntryKind_RendersItsOwnLineOrHeader` unswept, so it counted 29 cases against 30 kinds and failed - which is exactly the silent gap that case's count assertion exists to catch, and it caught it. The missing case is added. The harness could not have caught this either: `MatchFeaturePresenterTests` is exclude-listed there, so only a complete Unity Edit Mode run reaches it, and this milestone's own new files ARE compiled there, which is why its +8 below was green while this stayed hidden. EVIDENCE: PresentationProbe clean; full harness 2612/2612 passed, 0 failed, exactly +8 against the 2604 clean-main baseline, and every one of the eight is named - `GameplayNarrationLogTests`' six (TheLog_AnswersWhyASeatWasPassedOver, EveryKindInTheVocabulary_HasASentence, TheHeader_NamesTheBuildTheMapAndEverySeat, TheRecorder_WritesEachRowOnceAndFlushesAsItGoes, TheRecorder_ReportsRowsItLostRatherThanHidingThem, ADrivenMatch_NarratesEveryRowTheTrayWouldShow) and `HeroSkippedCardLogTests`' two (Page13sUnresolvedCardLine_WritesARowNamingTheHeroItPassedOver, EveryLineOfPage13sList_ReachesTheMatchLogOrIsANamedException). ## Milestone 8.35z62 The transition redraws every surface and a refused format press says so completion evidence

| ID | Outcome | Commit |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 8.35z62 | STOP-THE-LINE corrective after the user played the shipped 8.35z57 build and it was STILL broken in the real UI flow. Two defects, both reproduced before they were fixed, both from the SAME family: A SILENT EARLY RETURN. THE LAW THIS ADDS: acceptance must drive THE SAME PATH THE USER'S FINGER DOES - the z57 rehearsal gate passed and the user's session broke anyway, because the gate drives `-MatchFormat` launch arguments and driver-protocol picks while the user drives a PANEL BUTTON and the pick UI. DEFECT B - THE ONE-WAY TRANSITION REBUILT THE BOARD BUT NOT THE HUD. `ConfiguredMatchHudRenderer.Build()` opened with `if (root != null) return;` - once-only for its WHOLE body. That is right for the panel, because instantiating `PanelSettings` again throws away the live panel every surface is drawn on; it is wrong for everything below it, because `ConfiguredMatchSceneRuntime` constructs a NEW view for every surface it owns - hands, round, undo/restart, diagnostics, minion choice, hero overviews, match log - and hands them to `Configure` on EVERY composition. At the lobby's one-way transition the scene recomposes, `Configure` swapped in the new views, called `Build()`, and `Build()` returned immediately: the new roots were never added to the tree, and the PREVIOUS composition's views stayed on screen wired to the PREVIOUS composition's presenters. The board is a separate view rebuilt by `Compose`, which is exactly why it looked right while everything around it was stale. THE FIX: `Build()` splits into `EnsurePanelBuilt()` (once) and `ComposeSurfaces()` (every time); `ComposeSurfaces` already began with `root.Clear()` and re-added every child from the views it currently holds, so it was ALREADY idempotent and had simply never been reachable a second time, and `Build()` has exactly one caller, `Configure`, which runs once per composition, so this trades no per-frame work. HOW IT WAS FOUND, because the method matters more than the fix: the projections were already proven right, so no model-tier assertion could see this, and a new Play Mode case queried the actual panel and printed all three tiers in one line. Before the fix: `readModel=[dodger,misa,sabina,silverarrow,ursafar,whisper]`, rails drawn `[arien,brogan,sabina,tigerclaw,wasp,xargatha]`, `railRoots=1`, `viewRootInTree=False`, `seat=blue-player-01`. The read model held the PICKED line-up, the seat was bound, and the CURRENT runtime's rail view root was not in the panel at all - the previous composition's was, still holding the printed demo six. That is the user's report exactly: correct tokens on the map, six strangers in the trays. After the fix the same line reads `viewRootInTree=True` with the picked heroes drawn. DEFECT A - THE HOST PANEL'S FORMAT PRESS WAS SWALLOWED: `CycleMatchFormat` and `ChooseMatchSetup` both opened with a guard that returned SILENTLY once hosting had begun, so the user pressed 2V2, got a six-seat table, and their host log carries NO `LOBBY_FORMAT` line at all - nothing logged, nothing drawn, indistinguishable from a dead button. THE GUARD IS CORRECT AND IS KEPT: a session's seats are counted from the format, and every connection, epoch and reconnect capability the host holds is keyed by seat, so a format changed after anybody can hold a seat would move the seats out from under them - `MatchSceneCompositionRoot.ComposeForLineup` states this rule already. The defect was never the refusal; it was the SILENCE. `RefuseFormatChangeIfUnavailable` refuses on exactly the same condition and NAMES it, to the marker stream (`LOBBY_FORMAT_REFUSED reason=AlreadyHosting|NoComposedMatch|NoSuchPrintedSetup`) and to the entry form the player is looking at; pressing the format already composed now says so too. THE STRANDED SEAT, explained rather than hand-waved: the escalation asked how `ordinal=7 seat=red-player-03` could exist in a 2v2 match, and it could not and did not - Defect A means the 2V2 PRESS NEVER APPLIED, so that session was the composed 3v3 default throughout, six seats of which red-player-03 is an ordinary one, and the preserved host log agrees on every count (`claimed=1/6` through `6/6`, `SEATS_COMPLETE claimed=6/6`). No seat was stranded and no submission was wrongly accepted; the 7 `UnexpectedMessage` refusals followed by one `SUBMISSION_ACCEPTED version=1` are a client submitting either side of the match-started transition. THE 24 REFUSALS likewise are seats picking OUT OF TURN in p.22's alternating order, and they looked pathological only because accepted picks were invisible beside them: the host has raised `SeatHeroChosen` since 8.35z37 and `PrivateOnlineDrivers`' marker switch simply had no case for it, so a returned log recorded every REFUSED pick and not one accepted one. A log that records only failures cannot be reasoned from; `HERO_PICKED ordinal= seat= hero= team=` closes it. A REGRESSION THIS MILESTONE ALMOST SHIPPED, recorded because the near-miss is the lesson: the first draft of the transition hardening made a null line-up REFUSE TO ATTACH, which would have stranded the player in a lobby whose match had already started - and the seam's own original remark says a null line-up should leave this process on the match it has rather than compose something else and call it the same match. It now attaches exactly as before and merely says so (`LOBBY_TRANSITION_LINEUP_ABSENT`). The team-size mismatch path, which previously skipped the recomposition and attached the provisional runtime anyway, now ADOPTS the host's format, derivable from the roster's line-up with no new message, the same way 8.35z57 derives it from the plaintext refusal. EVIDENCE: PresentationProbe clean, re-run after every edit; `PrivateLobbyPlayModeTests` 16/16 passed, 0 failed, the same suite that carried the failing rendered-tier case before the fix. Zero schema and zero wire movement; protocol /11 unmoved from 8.35z60. | `3608b90b` |


## Milestone 8.35z63 Show a live effect on the board and let its aura travel with the hero completion evidence

| ID | Outcome | Commit |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 8.35z63 | The user's directive: "when a hero has an active effect, we need to show it. Same purple color we use in hero tray to show active cards should higlight the effect target. For example. If tigerclaw has effect of his silver he should have pruple higlight. If it is an active effect in radius, we need to paint the hexes in that radius that same color." ONE COLOUR, ALREADY IN THE THEME: `MatchHudTheme.ActiveEffectAccent` - milestone 8.5b's violet, worn since by a card sourcing a live Active effect in the tray - is now what the board draws too. No second purple was minted and no literal promoted; both new consumers read that one field, and that file is NOT in this branch's diff, which is the check that no copy was made. They differ only in alpha and by the board's own two established idioms: a thin mark is ADDED LIGHT (milestone 8.35z39), a whole-hex area is a TINT (milestone 8.11a) so the artwork reads through it. WHAT IS DRAWN: a hero carrying an effect wears a violet annulus on its board token (`ActiveEffectHeroMarker`, the acting-hero shape one ring wider); an effect with a radius paints its disk in the same violet, centred on its carrier's CURRENT hex every time the board rebinds - which is rulebook p.17's "an Active effect's Radius moves with its hero" for free, because nothing is stored. DERIVED, NOT WIDENED: `ActiveEffectVisibility` reads only what `IMatchReadModel` already carries - the live source-card ids the trays have read since 8.5b, the card's owner, the Active-effect definitions that card's content declares, the controller's current space, and the projected per-icon item count rulebook p.16 raises a printed Radius by. Zero schema, zero wire, zero Core change, and seat-agnostic, so all six seats and any spectator see the same marks. THE FINDING THE SWEEP TURNED UP - WASP'S STATIC BARRIER: its affected scope is `ControllerHero`, because the rules it carries are hers, but the card reads "spaces in radius count as obstacles", a disk of SPACES reached through `ObstacleOverlays`. A scope-only reading ringed Wasp and left the barrier invisible. `DrawsADisk` is now asked of the whole definition and mirrors Core's own `ValidateDisk` union exactly. THE SWEEP: all 61 registered definitions across the eleven heroes are placed - 40 RING, 18 DISK, 3 RING+DISK, 0 unplaced - taken from an Explicit table-printing case rather than hand-written, and `ActiveEffectVisibilityTests` walks the catalog so a scope this layer cannot place becomes a red test rather than a hero silently rendering nothing. Sabina ships no Active effect at all, which is a fact about her printed set rather than a gap. THE AURA GLIDES WITH ITS CARRIER: the second commit hands the disk the conductor's own displacement while a token slides, translate-only in x/z with an exact hex-lattice settle, so tinted hexes travel under a moving hero instead of jumping at the end of the move. LAYERING, stated so nothing later re-derives it: the aura is its own tile layer at local Y 0.010, under the perimeter (0.015), the pieces (0.03) and the six-corner marks (0.075/0.085). It never touches `IsHighlighted` - the retired choice fill a dozen journeys assert is false on every destination they click. Gold still means "waiting on you", violet means "an effect is running", and where both apply both are drawn. THE ID, recorded rather than resolved silently: this branch was dispatched as 8.35z62, and that id was ALREADY TAKEN by the wave's stop-the-line corrective, which had landed at `3608b90b` with its roadmap definition, archive section and all five status strings already written. Renumbering a milestone whose ceremony is complete is churn with no reader benefit, and the landing order is chronologically honest as it stands - the four merges, then the corrective, then this - so this milestone took 8.35z63 and the placeholder was renamed to that rather than to the dispatched id. INTEGRATION: this branch was based on this wave's own 8.35z60, so the collisions its dispatch predicted with the button branch (`UnityMatchFeatureViews.cs`, `MatchFeaturePresenters.cs`) were already reconciled in its own history and did not arise; the merge was clean with NO conflicts. The two seams that could still have collided were verified in the index rather than assumed: this milestone's `MatchSceneCompositionRoot.cs` change coexists with 8.35z62's transition work, which lives in `ConfiguredMatchHudRenderer` and `PrivateLobbyViews` and is in neither diff, and 8.35z62's own two fixes are present and intact - `composedChildren` in the renderer and `IgnorePointerThroughout` in the HUD views. The `Tools/DotnetTests` Exclude resolves to 64 entries, 64 unique, exactly one added (`ActiveEffectBoardTreatmentTests.cs`); `ActiveEffectVisibilityTests.cs` is deliberately NOT excluded, because the derivation is Unity-free and belongs in the fast harness where every disk's exact hex set is proven against `RadiusQuery` offline - which also puts it under Core's architecture rules for the first time. Four new `.cs` files, four shipped `.meta` files, every GUID confirmed unique repo-wide and each the standard 11-line MonoImporter shape; the placeholder was renamed across 24 occurrences in 12 files, all on added lines. EVIDENCE: Tools/PresentationProbe clean, 0 errors. Full harness 2629/2629 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped (7m50s) - exactly +16 against this wave's own 2613, the count this branch reported in its own tree (2604 -> 2620) carried onto a base that had already gained 9. No content, catalog or digest file is touched, so all eleven identity-oracle pins are unchanged by construction. CARRIED HONESTLY: the user's judgement is still wanted on the two alphas (tint 0.28, ring 0.62) - both are the board's existing tuning knobs and neither was moved - and the mid-slide aura frame the capture recipe asks for is not among the screenshots this wave shipped. | `44c4ecaa` |
